“I will do anything, milor,” she replied quietly, “to help my old man.”

“Well, then,” said Sir Percy Blakeney in that optimistic, lighthearted yet supremely authoritative tone of which he held the secret, “you and Rosette remain here and wait for the gendarmes. When they come, say nothing; behave with absolute meekness, and let them search your place from end to end. If they ask you about your husband say that you believe him to be at his workshop. Is that clear?”

===Chinese===

小屋里静了片刻，忧伤与创痛的天使在这些忠贞勇敢的灵魂头顶盘旋；随后，英国人那开朗的、充满生气与欢乐的嗓音再度响起——他已经站直了高大魁梧的身子，此刻把一只温暖的手搭在老人肩上：

“我看哪，我的好勒内格尔，”他说，“要想骗过那帮魔鬼、保住您这条脖颈，咱俩可没有多少工夫好耽搁了。喂，petite maman，”他转向老妇人，补了一句，“您能拿出点勇气来吗？”

“我什么都肯做，milor，”她平静地答道，“只要能帮上我的老伴。”

“那么，好极了。”珀西·布莱克尼爵士说道，用的是那种只有他才拿捏得来的口气——乐观、轻松，却又带着说一不二的威严，“您和罗塞特留在家里，等宪兵来。他们来了，什么也别说；拿出十足的温顺，让他们把这屋子从这头搜到那头。要是他们问起您丈夫，就说您估摸着他在作坊里。听明白了吗？”

===Original===

“Quite clear, milor,” replied petite maman.

“And you, Père Lenègre,” continued the Englishman, speaking now with slow and careful deliberation, “listen very attentively to the instructions I am going to give you, for on your implicit obedience to them depends not only your own life but that of these two dear women. Go at once, now, to the Rue Ste. Anne, round the corner, the second house on your right, which is numbered thirty-seven. The porte-cochère stands open, go boldly through, past the concierge’s box, and up the stairs to apartment number twelve, second floor. Here is the key of the apartment,” he added, producing one from his coat pocket and handing it over to the old man. “The rooms are nominally occupied by a certain Maître Turandot, maker of violins, and not even the concierge of the place knows that the hunchbacked and snuffy violin-maker and the meddlesome Scarlet Pimpernel, whom the Committee of Public Safety would so love to lay by the heels, are one and the same person. The apartment, then, is mine; one of the many which I occupy in Paris at different times,” he went on. “Let yourself in quietly with this key, walk straight across the first room to a wardrobe, which you will see in front of you. Open it. It is hung full of shabby clothes; put these aside, and you will notice that the panels at the back do not fit very closely, as if the wardrobe was old or had been badly put together. Insert your fingers in the tiny aperture between the two middle panels. These slide back easily: there is a recess immediately behind them. Get in there; pull the doors of the wardrobe together first, then slide the back panels into their place. You will be perfectly safe there, as the house is not under suspicion at present, and even if the revolutionary guard, under some meddlesome sergeant or other, chooses to pay it a surprise visit, your hiding-place will be perfectly secure. Now is all that quite understood?”

“Absolutely, milor,” replied Lenègre, even as he made ready to obey Sir Percy’s orders, “but what about you? You cannot get out of this house, milor,” he urged; “it is watched, I tell you.”

===Chinese===

“全明白了，milor。”petite maman 答道。

“至于您，勒内格尔老爹，”英国人接着说，这会儿讲得缓慢而字斟句酌，“我马上要交代的这番吩咐，您要一字不漏地听仔细了，因为您能不能不折不扣地照办，不光关系到您自己的性命，也关系到这两位亲爱的女人。现在立刻动身，到拐角外的圣安娜街（Rue Ste. Anne）去，右手第二幢房子，门牌三十七号。那道可供车马进出的大门（porte-cochère）敞开着，您大胆进去，走过门房的小屋，上楼梯到二楼十二号房间。这是那套房间的钥匙。”说着他从大衣口袋里掏出一把钥匙，递到老人手里。“这几间屋子名义上住着一位做小提琴的迪朗多师傅（Maître Turandot），连那儿的门房都不知道：那个吸着鼻烟的驼背制琴匠，和公安委员会恨不得捉拿归案的、专爱多管闲事的红花侠（the Scarlet Pimpernel），本是同一个人。所以说，这套房间是我的；是我在巴黎不同时期落脚的多处住所之一。”他继续说，“您用这把钥匙悄悄开门进去，径直穿过第一间屋子，走向正对面的一口大衣柜。把它打开。柜里挂满了破旧的衣裳；把它们拨到一边，您就会看到柜子后壁的板子拼得不怎么严实，仿佛柜子年头久了，或是当初做得粗糙。把手指伸进当中两块板中间的小缝里。板子很容易往两边推开：后面紧接着就是一个夹层。钻进去；先把柜门合拢，再把后壁的板子推回原位。您在那里面万无一失，因为这幢房子眼下不在嫌疑之列；就算革命卫队碰上哪个好事的中士，要来个突击检查，您的藏身之处也稳稳当当。这些全都听明白了吗？”

“句句明白，milor。”勒内格尔回答，一边已准备遵照珀西爵士的吩咐行事，“可您怎么办？您出不了这幢房子，milor，”他急切地说，“我跟您说，这房子有人盯着呢。”

===Original===

“*La!*” broke in Blakeney, in his lighthearted way, “and do you think I didn’t know that? I had to come and tell you about Pierre, and now I must give those worthy gendarmes the slip somehow. I have my rooms downstairs on the ground floor, as you know, and I must make certain arrangements so that we can all get out of Paris comfortably this evening. The demmed place is no longer safe either for you, my good Lenègre, or for petite maman and Rosette. But wherever I may be, meanwhile, don’t worry about me. As soon as the gendarmes have been and gone, I’ll go over to the Rue Ste. Anne and let you know what arrangements I’ve been able to make. So do as I tell you now, and in Heaven’s name let me look after myself.”

Whereupon, with scant ceremony, he hustled the old man out of the room.

Père Lenègre had contrived to kiss petite maman and Rosette before he went. It was touching to see the perfect confidence with which these simple-hearted folk obeyed the commands of milor. Had he not saved Pierre in his wonderful, brave, resourceful way? Of a truth he would know how to save Père Lenègre also. But, nevertheless, anguish gripped the women’s hearts; anguish doubly keen since the saviour of Pierre was also in danger now.

===Chinese===

“*La*！”布莱克尼带着他那副轻松劲儿插嘴道，“您当我不知道吗？我总得来给您报个皮埃尔的信儿吧，这会儿呢，我还得想法子甩掉那几位尽职的宪兵。您知道，我楼下底层有自己的房间，我得去做些安排，好叫咱们今晚都能舒舒服服地出巴黎。这该死的地方，对您、我的好勒内格尔，对 petite maman 和罗塞特来说，都不再安全了。不过这期间，不管我身在哪儿，都别为我操心。宪兵一走，我就到圣安娜街去，告诉您我都做了哪些安排。所以现在就照我说的做，看在老天爷份上，让我来照应我自己。”

说罢，他也不多客套，连推带搡地把老人送出了房间。

临走前，勒内格尔老爹好歹吻别了 petite maman 和罗塞特。看着这些心地淳朴的人对 milor 的吩咐怀着十足的信任，真叫人感动。他不是以他那神乎其神、智勇双全的法子救出了皮埃尔吗？他自然也有办法救勒内格尔老爹。然而女人们的心仍旧被忧惧攥紧了；这忧惧加倍揪心，因为皮埃尔的救命恩人如今也身陷险境。

===Original===

When Père Lenègre’s shuffling footsteps had died away along the flagged corridor, the stranger once more turned to the two women.

“And now, petite maman,” he said cheerily, as he kissed the old woman on both her furrowed cheeks, “keep up a good heart, and say your prayers with Rosette. Your old man and I will both have need of them.”

He did not wait to say goodbye, and anon it was his firm footstep that echoed down the corridor. He went off singing a song, at the top of his voice, for the whole house to hear, and for that traitor, Jean Baptiste, to come rushing out of his room marvelling at the impudence of the man, and cursing the Committee of Public Safety who were so slow in sending the soldiers of the Republic to lay this impertinent Englishman by the heels.

===Chinese===

等勒内格尔老爹拖着脚步的声响在石板走廊尽头消逝，陌生人又转向那母女俩。

“好了，petite maman，”他吻了吻老妇人两颊的沟壑，欢快地说，“打起精神来，和罗塞特一起祷告吧。您的老伴和我，两个都用得着你们的祷告。”

他没有留下来道别；不多时，走廊里便回响起他坚定有力的脚步声。他放声唱着歌走了，嗓门扯得老大，好让整幢房子都听见，也好让那个叛徒让·巴蒂斯特从屋里冲出来，一面惊诧这人好大的胆子，一面咒骂公安委员会迟迟不动，不赶紧派共和国的士兵来把这个不知天高地厚的英国人捉拿归案。

### II / ### 二

===Original===

A quarter of an hour later half a dozen men of the Republican Guard, with corporal and sergeant in command, were in the small apartment on the fifth floor of the tenement house in the Rue Jolivet. They had demanded an entry in the name of the Republic, had roughly hustled petite maman and Rosette, questioned them to Lenègre’s whereabouts, and not satisfied with the reply which they received, had turned the tidy little home topsy-turvy, ransacked every cupboard, dislocated every bed, table or sofa which might presumably have afforded a hiding place for a man.

Satisfied now that the “suspect” whom they were searching for was not on the premises, the sergeant stationed four of his men with the corporal outside the door, and two within, and himself sitting down in the centre of the room ordered the two women to stand before him and to answer his questions clearly on pain of being dragged away forthwith to the St. Lazare house of detention.

Petite maman smoothed out her apron, crossed her arms before her, and looked the sergeant quite straight in the face. Rosette’s eyes were full of tears, but she showed no signs of fear either, although her shoulder﻿—where one of the gendarmes had seized it so roughly﻿—was terribly painful.

===Chinese===

一刻钟之后，六名共和卫队（Republican Guard）士兵在一名下士和一名中士的带领下，已经进了若利维街那幢出租屋五楼的小套间。他们以共和国的名义强行要求入内，粗暴地推搡 petite maman 和罗塞特，盘问勒内格尔的下落，得了回话还不满足，索性把这个整洁的小家翻了个底朝天：每个碗柜都搜遍，每张床、每张桌子、每张沙发——凡是兴许藏得下一个人的物件——都拆了个七零八落。

中士这时确信他们要抓的“嫌疑分子”并不在屋里，便派下士带四名士兵守在门外，两名守在门内，自己往屋子正中一坐，喝令两个女人站到他跟前，把问题老老实实答个清楚，不然立刻把她们拖去圣拉扎尔拘留所（St. Lazare）。

petite maman 抹平围裙，双臂交叠在胸前，直直地望着中士的脸。罗塞特眼里噙满泪水，却也丝毫没有露出惧色，虽然她的肩膀——被一个宪兵狠狠抓过的地方——疼得厉害。

===Original===

“Your husband, citizeness,” asked the sergeant peremptorily, “where is he?”

“I am not sure, citizen,” replied petite maman. “At this hour he is generally at the government works in the Quai des Messageries.”

“He is not there now,” asserted the sergeant. “We have knowledge that he did not go back to his work since dinnertime.”

Petite maman was silent.

“Answer,” ordered the sergeant.

===Chinese===

“你的丈夫，女公民，”中士劈头喝问，“人在哪儿？”

“我说不准，公民。”petite maman 答道，“这个钟点，他一般都在梅萨热里滨河街（Quai des Messageries）的政府工场里。”

“他现在不在那儿。”中士断言，“我们得到消息，打午饭时候起，他就没回工场去过。”

petite maman 沉默不语。

“回话。”中士喝令。

===Original===

“I cannot tell you more, citizen sergeant,” she said firmly. “I do not know.”

“You do yourself no good, woman, by this obstinacy,” he continued roughly. “My belief is that your husband is inside this house, hidden away somewhere. If necessary I can get orders to have every apartment searched until he is found: but in that case it will go much harder with you and with your daughter, and much harder too with your husband than if he gave us no trouble and followed us quietly.”

But with sublime confidence in the man who had saved Pierre and who had given her explicit orders as to what she should do, petite maman, backed by Rosette, reiterated quietly:

“I cannot tell you more, citizen sergeant, I do not know.”

“And what about the Englishman?” queried the sergeant more roughly, “the man they call the Scarlet Pimpernel, what do you know of him?”

===Chinese===

“我没什么可多说的了，公民中士，”她斩钉截铁地说，“我不知道。”

“你这样死硬，女人，可没给自己捞着半点好处。”他粗声粗气地接着说，“依我看，你丈夫就在这幢房子里，藏在哪个角落。真有必要，我可以领下令来，把每套房间挨个搜到他现身为止；不过真到那步田地，你和你女儿的日子要难过得没法说，你丈夫也要比乖乖跟我们走、不给我们添麻烦倒霉得多。”

可是 petite maman 对那位救了皮埃尔、又明明白白吩咐过她该如何行事的人怀着至高的信任；她有罗塞特在旁撑着，平静地重申：

“我没什么可多说的了，公民中士，我不知道。”

“那个英国人呢？”中士问得更凶了，“人家管他叫红花侠的那个人，你们知道他些什么？”

===Original===

“Nothing, citizen,” replied petite maman, “what should we poor folk know of an English milor?”

“You know at any rate this much, citizeness, that the English milor helped your son Pierre to escape from justice.”

“If that is so,” said petite maman quietly, “it cannot be wrong for a mother to pray to God to bless her son’s preserver.”

“It behooves every good citizen,” retorted the sergeant firmly, “to denounce all traitors to the Republic.”

“But since I know nothing about the Englishman, citizen sergeant﻿—?”

===Chinese===

“什么都不知道，公民。”petite maman 答道，“我们这样的穷苦人，哪会知道一位英国 milor 的事？”

“至少这层你们清楚，女公民：那位英国 milor 帮你儿子皮埃尔逃脱了法网。”

“倘若真是这样，”petite maman 平静地说，“做母亲的求上帝保佑儿子的恩人，总归不算罪过吧。”

“每一个好公民的本分，”中士斩钉截铁地驳道，“就是告发共和国的一切叛徒。”

“可我既然对那位英国人一无所知，公民中士——那叫我怎么告发？”

===Original===

And petite maman shrugged her thin shoulders as if the matter had ceased to interest her.

“Think again, citizeness,” admonished the sergeant, “it is your husband’s neck as well as your daughter’s and your own that you are risking by so much obstinacy.”

He waited a moment or two as if willing to give the old woman time to speak: then, when he saw that she kept her thin, quivering lips resolutely glued together he called his corporal to him.

“Go to the citizen Commissary of the Section,” he commanded, “and ask for a general order to search every apartment in No. 24 Rue Jolivet. Leave two of our men posted on the first and third landings of this house and leave two outside this door. Be as quick as you can. You can be back here with the order in half an hour, or perhaps the committee will send me an extra squad; tell the citizen Commissary that this is a big house, with many corridors. You can go.”

The corporal saluted and went.

===Chinese===

petite maman 耸了耸瘦削的肩膀，仿佛这件事已与她毫不相干。

“再想想吧，女公民。”中士告诫道，“你这么死扛着，赌上的不光是你自己和你女儿的脖颈，还有你丈夫的。”

他等了一两分钟，像是存心给老妇人留个开口的工夫；待到看见她那两片瘦薄发颤的嘴唇执意紧闭，他才把下士叫到跟前。

“到区政委公民那儿去，”他命令道，“讨一道搜查若利维街 24 号全部房间的总令。留咱们两个人，一个守在这幢楼的一楼楼梯口，一个守住三楼楼梯口，再留两个守在这扇门外。越快越好。你带着命令回来，半个钟头尽够了，要不然委员会兴许会给我加派一小队人；告诉区政委公民，这楼房子大，走廊多。去吧。”

下士敬了个礼，去了。

===Original===

Petite maman and Rosette the while were still standing quietly in the middle of the room, their arms folded underneath their aprons, their wide-open, anxious eyes fixed into space. Rosette’s tears were falling slowly, one by one down her cheeks, but petite maman was dry-eyed. She was thinking, and thinking as she had never had occasion to think before.

She was thinking of the brave and gallant Englishman who had saved Pierre’s life only yesterday. The sergeant, who sat there before her, had asked for orders from the citizen Commissary to search this big house from attic to cellar. That is what made petite maman think and think.

The brave Englishman was in this house at the present moment: the house would be searched from attic to cellar and he would be found, taken, and brought to the guillotine.

===Chinese===

这工夫，petite maman 和罗塞特依旧安安静静站在屋子中央，双臂拢在围裙底下，睁大的、惶惶不安的眼睛望着虚空发怔。罗塞特的泪水一颗接一颗，缓缓淌下脸颊，petite maman 却两眼干枯。她在思索，思索着，这一辈子还从没有这样不得不想事的时候。

她想的是那位就在昨天还救了皮埃尔性命的英勇高贵的英国人。坐在她跟前的这个中士，已经向区政委公民讨令，要把这幢大楼从阁楼搜到地窖。正是这个，叫 petite maman 想了又想。

那位勇敢的英国人此刻就在这幢楼里：全楼要从阁楼搜到地窖，他会被搜出来、被抓住、被押上断头台。

===Original===

The man who yesterday had risked his life to save her boy was in imminent and deadly danger, and she﻿—petite maman﻿—could do nothing to save him.

Every moment now she thought to hear milor’s firm tread resounding on stairs or corridor, every moment she thought to hear snatches of an English song, sung by a fresh and powerful voice, never after today to be heard in gaiety again.

The old clock upon the shelf ticked away these seconds and minutes while petite maman thought and thought, while men set traps to catch a fellow-being in a deathly snare, and human carnivorous beasts lay lurking for their prey.

===Chinese===

昨天还甘冒性命救她孩子的人，此刻正处在千钧一发的死地，而她——petite maman——却无力救他。

她时时刻刻都在提心，生怕听见 milor 坚实的脚步声在楼梯或走廊里响起；时时刻刻都在提心，生怕听见一副清亮豪迈的嗓子哼出的英文歌的片段——过了今天，这歌声再也听不见它欢畅的时候了。

架上的老座钟滴答滴答，走过了这每分每秒；petite maman 想着，想着；一面是人们布下罗网，要把一个同胞诱进死地，一面是人形的食人兽埋伏着，只待猎物上钩。

### III / ### 三

===Original===

Another quarter of an hour went by. Petite maman and Rosette had hardly moved. The shadows of evening were creeping into the narrow room, blurring the outlines of the pieces of furniture and wrapping all the corners in gloom.

The sergeant had ordered Rosette to bring in a lamp. This she had done, placing it upon the table so that the feeble light glinted upon the belt and buckles of the sergeant and upon the tricolour cockade which was pinned to his hat. Petite maman had thought and thought until she could think no more.

Anon there was much commotion on the stairs; heavy footsteps were heard ascending from below, then crossing the corridors on the various landings. The silence which reigned otherwise in the house, and which had fallen as usual on the squalid little street, void of traffic at this hour, caused those footsteps to echo with ominous power.

Petite maman felt her heart beating so vigorously that she could hardly breathe. She pressed her wrinkled hands tightly against her bosom.

===Chinese===

又过了一刻钟。petite maman 和罗塞特几乎没挪动过。黄昏的暗影悄悄爬进这间窄屋，把一件件家具的轮廓浸得模糊，将各个墙角裹进昏冥。

中士曾命令罗塞特端一盏灯来。她照办了，把灯搁在桌上，昏黄的光映着中士的腰带和带扣，映着他帽子上别着的三色帽徽。petite maman 想啊想啊，一直想到再也想不动了。

不多时，楼梯上响起一片骚动；沉重的脚步声自下而上，随后又穿过各个楼梯口的走廊。整幢房子本是一片沉寂，这条寒酸的小街到了这个钟点更是照例车马绝迹，愈发衬得那脚步声回荡得不祥而慑人。

petite maman 觉得心口跳得又急又重，连气都透不过来。她把一双皱纹纵横的手紧紧按在胸前。

===Original===

There were the quick words of command, alas! so familiar in France just now, the cruel, peremptory words that invariably preceded an arrest, preliminaries to the dragging of some wretched﻿—often wholly harmless﻿—creature before a tribunal that knew neither pardon nor mercy.

The sergeant, who had become drowsy in the close atmosphere of the tiny room, roused himself at the sound and jumped to his feet. The door was thrown open by the men stationed outside even before the authoritative words, “Open! in the name of the Republic!” had echoed along the narrow corridor.

The sergeant stood at attention and quickly lifted his hand to his forehead in salute. A fresh squad of some half-dozen men of the Republican Guard stood in the doorway; they were under the command of an officer of high rank, a rough, uncouth, almost bestial-looking creature, with lank hair worn the fashionable length under his greasy chapeau-bras, and unkempt beard round an ill-washed and bloated face. But he wore the tricolour sash and badge which proclaimed him one of the military members of the Sectional Committee of Public Safety, and the sergeant, who had been so overbearing with the women just now, had assumed a very humble and even obsequious manner.

“You sent for a general order to the sectional Committee,” said the newcomer, turning abruptly to the sergeant after he had cast a quick, searching glance round the room, hardly condescending to look on petite maman and Rosette, whose very souls were now gazing out of their anguish-filled eyes.

===Chinese===

接着便是短促的口令声——唉！这声音在如今的法兰西已是家常便饭；那凶残专横的话语，向来是逮捕的前奏，是要把某个可怜虫——往往全然无辜——拖到那不知宽恕为何物、不知怜悯为何物的法庭面前去的开场白。

小屋里空气窒闷，中士本来已经昏昏欲睡，闻声一激灵，跳了起来。还没等那句威严的“开门！以共和国的名义！”在窄走廊里荡开，门就被守在外面的士兵一把推开了。

中士立正，迅即抬手行礼。门口站着新开来的一小队共和卫队士兵，约莫五六个人；带队的是位高级军官，一个粗野、鄙陋、几乎面目如兽的家伙：油腻腻的折叠礼帽底下，稀疏的头发留到入时的长度，一张没洗干净的浮肿脸，四周围着蓬乱的络腮胡。可他斜挂三色绶带、佩着徽章，标明他是区公安委员会的军事委员之一；方才对两个女人那般盛气凌人的中士，顿时换上一副低声下气、几乎谄媚的模样。

“你向区委员会讨过一道总令。”来人朝屋里飞快扫视一圈后，猛地转向中士说道；他几乎不屑于看 petite maman 和罗塞特一眼，而这两个女人的魂魄，正从满含惊悸的眼睛里往外凝望着。

===Original===

“I did, citizen commandant,” replied the sergeant.

“I am not a commandant,” said the other curtly. “My name is Rouget, member of the Convention and of the Committee of Public Safety. The sectional Committee to whom you sent for a general order of search thought that you had blundered somehow, so they sent me to put things right.”

“I am not aware that I committed any blunder, citizen,” stammered the sergeant dolefully. “I could not take the responsibility of making a domiciliary search all through the house. So I begged for fuller orders.”

“And wasted the Committee’s time and mine by such nonsense,” retorted Rouget harshly. “Every citizen of the Republic worthy of the name should know how to act on his own initiative when the safety of the nation demands it.”

===Chinese===

“讨过，指挥官公民。”中士回答。

“我不是什么指挥官。”对方生硬地说，“我叫鲁热（Rouget），国民公会（Convention）兼公安委员会的委员。你向区委员会讨搜查总令，他们觉得你多半出了岔子，所以派我来把事情理顺。”

“我不知道自己出了什么岔子呀，公民。”中士哭丧着脸、结结巴巴地说，“全楼挨户搜查，这个责任我担不起。所以我才请求更周全的命令。”

“还拿这种蠢话来糟蹋委员会的时间、糟蹋我的时间。”鲁热厉声驳道，“共和国的公民，但凡配得上这个名号的，在国家安危需要的时候，都该知道怎么当机立断。”

===Original===

“I did not know﻿—I did not dare﻿—” murmured the sergeant, obviously cowed by this reproof, which had been delivered in the rough, overbearing tones peculiar to these men who, one and all, had risen from the gutter to places of importance and responsibility in the newly-modelled State.

“Silence!” commanded the other peremptorily. “Don’t waste any more of my time with your lame excuses. You have failed in zeal and initiative. That’s enough. What else have you done? Have you got the man Lenègre?”

“No, citizen. He is not in hiding here, and his wife and daughter will not give us any information about him.”

“That is their lookout,” retorted Rouget with a harsh laugh. “If they give up Lenègre of their own free will the law will deal leniently with them, and even perhaps with him. But if we have to search the house for him, then it means the guillotine for the lot of them.”

===Chinese===

“我不知道——我不敢——”中士嗫嚅着，显然被这顿训斥镇住了；这训斥用的正是那班人特有的粗声恶气、盛气凌人的腔调——他们一个不落，全是从阴沟里爬上这新造国家里显赫权位、肩挑重任的。

“闭嘴！”对方断喝一声，“别再拿你那些站不住脚的托词浪费我的时间。你在干劲和决断上都失了职。这就够了。你还干了些什么？勒内格尔那个人，你拿到了没有？”

“没有，公民。他不曾藏在这儿，他老婆和女儿也不肯供出他半点消息。”

“那是他们自作自受。”鲁热冷笑一声驳道，“他们要是自愿交出勒内格尔，法律会从轻发落他们，没准儿连他也从轻。可要是非得我们搜楼揪人，那就等于送他们一伙通通上断头台。”

===Original===

He had spoken these callous words without even looking on the two unfortunate women; nor did he ask them any further questions just then, but continued speaking to the sergeant:

“And what about the Englishman? The sectional Committee sent down some spies this morning to be on the lookout for him on or about this house. Have you got him?”

“Not yet, citizen. But﻿—”

“*Ah ça*, citizen sergeant,” broke in the other brusquely, “meseems that your zeal has been even more at fault than I had supposed. Have you done anything at all, then, in the matter of Lenègre or the Englishman?”

===Chinese===

他说出这些冷血的话时，甚至不曾朝那两个倒霉的女人看上一眼；这会儿也不再问她们什么，只管对中士继续说道：

“还有那个英国人呢？区委员会今早派了几名密探，守在这幢房子里里外外留意他。你抓到他没有？”

“还没有，公民。不过——”

“*Ah ça*（哎呀呀），公民中士，”对方粗暴地打断他，“照这么看，你的干劲比我料想的还要不济。这么说，勒内格尔的案子也好，英国人的案子也好，你到底做过半件事没有？”

===Original===

“I have told you, citizen,” retorted the sergeant sullenly, “that I believe Lenègre to be still in this house. At any rate, he had not gone out of it an hour ago﻿—that’s all I know. And I wanted to search the whole of this house, as I am sure we should have found him in one of the other apartments. These people are all friends together, and will always help each other to evade justice. But the Englishman was no concern of mine. The spies of the Committee were ordered to watch for him, and when they reported to me I was to proceed with the arrest. I was not set to do any of the spying work. I am a soldier, and obey my orders when I get them.”

“Very well, then, you’d better obey them now, citizen sergeant,” was Rouget’s dry comment on the other man’s surly explanation, “for you seem to have properly blundered from first to last, and will be hard put to it to redeem your character. The Republic, remember, has no use for fools.”

The sergeant, after this covert threat, thought it best, apparently, to keep his tongue, whilst Rouget continued, in the same aggressive, peremptory tone:

“Get on with your domiciliary visits at once. Take your own men with you, and leave me the others. Begin on this floor, and leave your sentry at the front door outside. Now let me see your zeal atoning for your past slackness. Right turn! Quick march!”

===Chinese===

“我已对您说过了，公民。”中士气冲冲地顶了回去，“我认定勒内格尔还在楼里。至少一个钟头之前他没出去过——我就知道这么多。我本想把这楼整个搜一遍，因为保险能在别的哪套房间里搜出他来。这楼里的人都是通家之好，遇事总是互相帮衬着逃脱法网。可那个英国人不归我管。委员会的密探奉命盯他，他们向我报了信，我再去执行逮捕就是。盯梢的活计一项也没派给我。我是个当兵的，领了命令才照办。”

“很好，那这会儿你最好就服从命令，公民中士。”鲁热对对方这番强头倔脑的辩白冷冷评了一句，“瞧你从头错到尾，要挽回名声只怕够呛。记住，共和国可不留蠢材。”

中士挨了这句绵里藏针的威吓，看样子觉得还是闭嘴为妙；鲁热则拿同样的咄咄逼人、专横武断的口气接着说：

“挨户查抄，即刻动手。带上你自己的人，剩下的留给我。就从这一层查起，再派你的哨兵到大门外守着。现在让我瞧瞧，你的干劲怎么补上你从前的懈怠。向右转！开步——走！”

===Original===

Then it was that petite maman spoke out. She had thought and thought, and now she knew what she ought to do; she knew that that cruel, inhuman wretch would presently begin his tramp up and down corridors and stairs, demanding admittance at every door, entering every apartment. She knew that the man who had saved her Pierre’s life was in hiding somewhere in the house﻿—that he would be found and dragged to the guillotine, for she knew that the whole governing body of this abominable Revolution was determined not to allow that hated Englishman to escape again.

She was old and feeble, small and thin﻿—that’s why everyone called her petite maman﻿—but once she knew what she ought to do, then her spirit overpowered the weakness of her wizened body.

Now she knew, and even while that arrogant member of an execrated murdering Committee was giving final instructions to the sergeant, petite maman said, in a calm, piping voice:

“No need, citizen sergeant, to go and disturb all my friends and neighbours. I’ll tell you where my husband is.”

===Chinese===

就在这时，petite maman 开口了。她想了又想，如今可算明白自己该怎么做了；她知道，那个残忍的、没有人性的恶棍马上就要在走廊楼梯间上下奔走，挨家挨户叫门，闯进每一套房间。她知道救了她皮埃尔的那个人就藏在这楼里的什么地方——会被搜出来、拖上断头台；因为她知道，这场可憎的革命的全部当权者都铁了心，再不容那个众人切齿的英国人逃掉一回。

她又老又衰，个子小，身板薄——所以人人都唤她 petite maman——可她一旦明白了自己该做什么，那股精神气便能压倒干瘦躯体里的衰弱。

此刻她明白了。就在那个万人唾骂的杀人委员会的嚣张委员给中士交代最后指示的当口，petite maman 用一种平静而尖细的嗓音开了口：

“用不着，公民中士，去搅扰我的各位朋友和街坊了。我来告诉你们，我丈夫在哪儿。”

===Original===

In a moment Rouget had swung round on his heel, a hideous gleam of satisfaction spread over his grimy face, and he said, with an ugly sneer:

“So! you have thought better of it, have you? Well, out with it! You’d better be quick about it if you want to do yourselves any good.”

“I have my daughter to think of,” said petite maman in a feeble, querulous way, “and I won’t have all my neighbours in this house made unhappy because of me. They have all been kind neighbours. Will you promise not to molest them and to clear the house of soldiers if I tell you where Lenègre is?”

“The Republic makes no promises,” replied Rouget gruffly. “Her citizens must do their duty without hope of a reward. If they fail in it, they are punished. But privately I will tell you, woman, that if you save us the troublesome and probably unprofitable task of searching this rabbit-warren through and through, it shall go very leniently with you and with your daughter, and perhaps﻿—I won’t promise, remember﻿—perhaps with your husband also.”

===Chinese===

鲁热霍地转过身，脏污的脸上漾开一丝狰狞的得色，带着丑恶的狞笑说道：

“哟！想通了，是吧？好啊，快说！想给自己捞点好处，就麻利点儿。”

“我得替我女儿着想。”petite maman 声音虚弱、带着几分絮叨地说，“我也不愿为着我一个人，害得全楼的街坊不得安生。他们平日都是些和睦的好街坊。要是我说出勒内格尔在哪儿，你们能担保不去搅扰他们、把楼里的兵都撤走吗？”

“共和国从不许诺。”鲁热粗声粗气地答道，“共和国的公民尽本分，休想图什么报偿。本分有亏，便要受罚。不过私下里我可以告诉你，女人：你要是省了我们把这座兔子窝里里外外翻个遍的麻烦——这营生既费力，多半也捞不着什么好处——那你和你女儿会得到很宽大的处置，没准儿——记住，我可不担保——没准儿连你丈夫也是。”

===Original===

“Very good, citizen,” said petite maman calmly. “I am ready.”

“Ready for what?” he demanded.

“To take you to where my husband is in hiding.”

“Oho! He is not in the house, then?”

“No.”

===Chinese===

“很好，公民。”petite maman 平静地说，“我准备好了。”

“准备什么？”他问。

“带你们到我丈夫藏身的地方去。”

“哦嗬！这么说，他不在楼里？”

“不在。”

===Original===

“Where is he, then?”

“In the Rue Ste. Anne. I will take you there.”

Rouget cast a quick, suspicious glance on the old woman, and exchanged one of understanding with the sergeant.

“Very well,” he said after a slight pause. “But your daughter must come along too. Sergeant,” he added, “I’ll take three of your men with me; I have half a dozen, but it’s better to be on the safe side. Post your fellows round the outer door, and on my way to the Rue Ste. Anne I will leave word at the gendarmerie that a small reinforcement be sent on to you at once. These can be here in five minutes; until then you are quite safe.”

===Chinese===

“那他在哪儿？”

“在圣安娜街。我带你们去。”

鲁热朝老妇人飞快投去一道狐疑的目光，又和中士交换了一个心照不宣的眼色。

“很好。”他略停了停，说，“不过你女儿也得跟着。中士，”他又说，“我带你三个人走；我手头有六个人，可稳妥起见还是多些好。把你的人都布置在大门外四周，我去圣安娜街的路上，会到宪兵队留话，叫他们即刻给你派一小支援兵过来。援兵五分钟就能到；在那之前你万无一失。”

===Original===

Then he added under his breath, so that the women should not hear: “The Englishman may still be in the house. In which case, hearing us depart, he may think us all gone and try to give us the slip. You’ll know what to do?” he queried significantly.

“Of course, citizen,” replied the sergeant.

“Now, then, citizeness﻿—hurry up.”

Once more there was tramping of heavy feet on stone stairs and corridors. A squad of soldiers of the Republican Guard, with two women in their midst, and followed by a member of the Committee of Public Safety, a sergeant, corporal and two or three more men, excited much anxious curiosity as they descended the steep flights of steps from the fifth floor.

Pale, frightened faces peeped shyly through the doorways at sound of the noisy tramp from above, but quickly disappeared again at sight of the grimy scarlet facings and tricolour cockades.

The sergeant and three soldiers remained stationed at the foot of the stairs inside the house. Then citizen Rouget roughly gave the order to proceed. It seemed strange that it should require close on a dozen men to guard two women and to apprehend one old man, but as the member of the Committee of Public Safety whispered to the sergeant before he finally went out of the house: “The whole thing may be a trap, and one can’t be too careful. The Englishman is said to be very powerful; I’ll get the gendarmerie to send you another half-dozen men, and mind you guard the house until my return.”

===Chinese===

随后他又压低嗓子补了一句，不让两个女人听见：“那英国人兴许还在楼里。果真如此，他听见我们动身，只当我们全走了，就会想法子溜。该怎么做，你明白？”他意味深长地问。

“明白，公民。”中士答道。

“那么，女公民——快走。”

石头楼梯和走廊上，又一次响起沉重的脚步声。一队共和卫队士兵，当中夹着两个女人，后面跟着公安委员会的一名委员、一个中士、一个下士外加两三个人，从五楼一段段陡峭的台阶往下走，惹得人人侧目、个个悬心。

听见头顶上嘈杂的脚步声，一张张苍白惊惶的脸怯生生地从各家门口探头张望；可一瞧见那污渍斑斑的红色军服镶边和三色帽徽，便又飞快缩了回去。

中士和三名士兵留在楼内楼梯脚下把守。随后鲁热公民粗声粗气地下令出发。看守两个女人、捉拿一个老头子，竟要差不多一打人手，这事儿看着未免蹊跷；可正如那位公安委员会委员临出楼门前附耳对中士说的：“这整桩事兴许是个圈套，再怎么小心也不为过。听说那英国人厉害得很；我会叫宪兵队再给你派六个人来，你给我守住这楼，等我回来。”

### IV / ### 四

===Original===

Five minutes later the soldiers, directed by petite maman, had reached No. 37 Rue Ste. Anne. The big outside door stood wide open, and the whole party turned immediately into the house.

The concierge, terrified and obsequious, rushed﻿—trembling﻿—out of his box.

“What was the pleasure of the citizen soldiers?” he asked.

“Tell him, citizeness,” commanded Rouget curtly.

===Chinese===

五分钟后，一行士兵由 petite maman 引路，到了圣安娜街 37 号。临街的大门敞着，整队人当即拐进了楼里。

门房吓坏了，一面打着躬一面冲出他那间小屋——浑身直哆嗦。

“公民士兵们有何贵干？”他问。

“你说给他听，女公民。”鲁热简短地命令道。

===Original===

“We are going to apartment No. 12 on the second floor,” said petite maman to the concierge.

“Have you a key of the apartment?” queried Rouget.

“No, citizen,” stammered the concierge, “but﻿—”

“Well, what is it?” queried the other peremptorily.

“Papa Turandot is a poor, harmless maker of volins,” said the concierge. “I know him well, though he is not often at home. He lives with a daughter somewhere Passy way, and only uses this place as a workshop. I am sure he is no traitor.”

===Chinese===

“我们要去二楼 12 号房间。”petite maman 对门房说。

“那套房你有钥匙吗？”鲁热问。

“没有，公民。”门房结结巴巴地说，“可是——”

“嗯，可是什么？”对方蛮横地追问。

“迪朗多老爹是个可怜巴巴、与世无争的做小提琴的师傅。”门房说，“我跟他熟得很，虽说他不常在家。他住在帕西那边，跟一个女儿过活，这地方只当个作坊使。我担保他不是叛徒。”

===Original===

“We’ll soon see about that,” remarked Rouget dryly.

Petite maman held her shawl tightly crossed over her bosom: her hands felt clammy and cold as ice. She was looking straight out before her, quite dry-eyed and calm, and never once glanced on Rosette, who was not allowed to come anywhere near her mother.

As there was no duplicate key to apartment No. 12, citizen Rouget ordered his men to break in the door. It did not take very long: the house was old and ramshackle and the doors rickety. The next moment the party stood in the room which a while ago the Englishman had so accurately described to Père Lenègre in petite maman’s hearing.

There was the wardrobe. Petite maman, closely surrounded by the soldiers, went boldly up to it; she opened it just as milor had directed, and pushed aside the row of shabby clothes that hung there. Then she pointed to the panels that did not fit quite tightly together at the back. Petite maman passed her tongue over her dry lips before she spoke.

===Chinese===

“这个嘛，很快便见分晓。”鲁热干巴巴地说。

petite maman 把披巾紧紧交掩在胸前：一双手又湿又冷，冷得像冰。她直望着前方，眼里干干的，神色镇定，一次也不曾瞥罗塞特一眼——罗塞特被拦着，不许靠近母亲半步。

12 号房间没有备用的钥匙，鲁热公民便命令手下把门砸开。这费不了多少工夫：这楼又老又破，门也摇摇晃晃。不多时，一队人已经站在那间屋子里——不久之前，英国人当着 petite maman 的面，向勒内格尔老爹分毫不差地描述过的正是它。

那口大衣柜就在眼前。petite maman 被士兵们团团围着，径直朝它走过去；她照 milor 吩咐过的那样打开柜门，把挂着的那些破旧衣裳拨到一边，然后指向柜子后壁拼得不甚严实的板子。开口之前，petite maman 用舌头舔了舔干涩的嘴唇。

===Original===

“There’s a recess behind those panels,” she said at last. “They slide back quite easily. My old man is there.”

“And God bless you for a brave, loyal soul,” came in merry, ringing accent from the other end of the room. “And God save the Scarlet Pimpernel!”

These last words, spoken in English, completed the blank amazement which literally paralysed the only three genuine Republican soldiers there﻿—those, namely, whom Rouget had borrowed from the sergeant. As for the others, they knew what to do. In less than a minute they had overpowered and gagged the three bewildered soldiers.

===Chinese===

“那些板子后面有个夹层。”她终于开口了，“板子一推就开。我的老伴就在里面。”

“上帝赐福给你，你这勇敢忠义的灵魂！”屋子另一头传来一句欢快嘹亮的话，“上帝保佑红花侠！”

末后这句话是用英语说的；当场真正如遭雷击、整个人僵住的，只有那三名货真价实的共和军士兵——也就是鲁热从中士那里借来的那三个——这句话把他们那全然的茫然惊愕补了个十足。至于其余的人，早知道该做什么：不到一分钟，三个晕头转向的士兵就被他们制住，堵上了嘴。

===Original===

Rosette had screamed, terror-stricken, from sheer astonishment, but petite maman stood quite still, her pale, tear-dimmed eyes fixed upon the man whose gay “God bless you!” had so suddenly turned her despair into hope.

How was it that in the hideous, unkempt and grimy Rouget she had not at once recognised the handsome and gallant milor who had saved her Pierre’s life? Well, of a truth he had been unrecognisable, but now that he tore the ugly wig and beard from his face, stretched out his fine figure to its full height, and presently turned his lazy, merry eyes on her, she could have screamed for very joy.

The next moment he had her by the shoulders and had imprinted two sounding kisses upon her cheeks.

“Now, petite maman,” he said gaily, “let us liberate the old man.”

===Chinese===

罗塞特吓了一大跳，惊叫出声；petite maman 却一动不动地站着，苍白噙泪的眼睛紧紧盯着那个男人——他那一声欢畅的“上帝赐福给你！”，就这样骤然间把她的绝望化成了希望。

在那个面目可憎、蓬头垢面、满身污垢的鲁热身上，她怎么就没一眼认出那位救了她皮埃尔性命的俊朗英武的 milor 呢？说真的，他那时实在是认不出来；可这会儿，他从脸上扯下丑陋的假发和假胡子，把挺拔的好身架舒展到十足的高度，随即又把那双慵懒快活的眼睛转向她，她高兴得险些叫出声来。

转眼间，他已抓住她的双肩，在她两颊上响响亮亮地印下两个吻。

“好啦，petite maman，”他欢快地说，“咱们去放了老人家吧。”

===Original===

Père Lenègre, from his hiding-place, had heard all that had been going on in the room for the last few moments. True, he had known exactly what to expect, for no sooner had he taken possession of the recess behind the wardrobe than milor also entered the apartment and then and there told him of his plans not only for père’s own safety, but for that of petite maman and Rosette who would be in grave danger if the old man followed in the wake of Pierre.

Milor told him in his usual lighthearted way that he had given the Committee’s spies the slip.

“I do that very easily, you know,” he explained. “I just slip into my rooms in the Rue Jolivet, change myself into a snuffy and hunchback violin-maker, and walk out of the house under the noses of the spies. In the nearest wine-shop my English friends, in various disguises, are all ready to my hand: half a dozen of them are never far from where I am in case they may be wanted.”

===Chinese===

勒内格尔老爹待在藏身处，方才屋里这一阵动静，他一字不落全听见了。其实他本来就知道等着的会是什么：他前脚刚钻进衣柜后面的夹层，milor 后脚也进了这套房间，当场就把自己的全盘打算讲给他听——不光为着老爹自身的安全，也为着 petite maman 和罗塞特：老头子若跟着皮埃尔的后尘而去，她们俩就要大难临头了。

milor 用他一贯那副轻松口气告诉他，自己已经甩掉了委员会的密探。

“这在我易如反掌，你知道。”他解释道，“我溜进若利维街我那几间屋子，把自己变成一个吸着鼻烟的驼背制琴匠，就从密探们的鼻子底下走出楼去。最近的那家酒馆里，我的英国朋友们化着各式各样的装，个个听我差遣：总有六个人不远不近地跟着我，以备不时之需。”

===Original===

These half-dozen brave Englishmen soon arrived one by one: one looked like a coal-heaver, another like a seedy musician, a third like a coach-driver. But they all walked boldly into the house and were soon all congregated in apartment No. 12. Here fresh disguises were assumed, and soon a squad of Republican Guards looked as like the real thing as possible.

Père Lenègre admitted himself that though he actually saw milor transforming himself into citizen Rouget, he could hardly believe his eyes, so complete was the change.

“I am deeply grieved to have frightened and upset you so, petite maman,” now concluded milor kindly, “but I saw no other way of getting you and Rosette out of the house and leaving that stupid sergeant and some of his men behind. I did not want to arouse in him even the faintest breath of suspicion, and of course if he had asked me for the written orders which he was actually waiting for, or if his corporal had returned sooner than I anticipated, there might have been trouble. But even then,” he added with his usual careless insouciance, “I should have thought of some way of baffling those brutes.”

===Chinese===

这六位勇敢的英国人不多时便一个个到齐了：一个像是运煤工，一个像是穷酸乐师，第三个像是马车夫。可他们个个昂首阔步走进楼来，转眼都在 12 号房间里聚齐。在这里换上新的行头，不多时，一小队共和卫队看上去就跟真的一模一样了。

勒内格尔老爹自己都承认，虽说 milor 变成鲁热公民的整个过程他都亲眼瞧着，可他几乎不敢相信自己的眼睛——那变化实在太彻底了。

“让你们受了这么大的惊吓，petite maman，我心里真是过意不去。”milor 这会儿和颜悦色地收了尾，“可要把您和罗塞特接出楼去、又把那个蠢中士和他的一部分人留在原地，我实在想不出别的法子。我不想在他心里勾起哪怕一丝一毫的疑心；当然，他要是当真向我要他正等着的那纸书面命令，或是他的下士回来得比我预计的早，那就可能麻烦。不过真到那时候，”他以他惯常那种毫不在意的洒脱劲儿补了一句，“我总会想出什么法子来对付那帮畜生的。”

===Original===

“And now,” he concluded more authoritatively, “it is a case of getting out of Paris before the gates close. Père Lenègre, take your wife and daughter with you and walk boldly out of this house. The sergeant and his men have not vacated their post in the Rue Jolivet, and no one else can molest you. Go straight to the Porte de Neuilly, and on the other side wait quietly in the little café at the corner of the Avenue until I come. Your old passes for the barriers still hold good; you were only placed on the ‘suspect’ list this morning, and there has not been a hue and cry yet about you. In any case some of us will be close by to help you if needs be.”

“But you, milor,” stammered Père Lenègre, “and your friends﻿—?”

“*La*, man,” retorted Blakeney lightly, “have I not told you before never to worry about me and my friends? We have more ways than one of giving the slip to this demmed government of yours. All you’ve got to think of is your wife and your daughter. I am afraid that petite maman cannot take more with her than she has on, but we’ll do all we can for her comfort until we have you all in perfect safety﻿—in England﻿—with Pierre.”

Neither Père Lenègre, nor petite maman, nor Rosette could speak just then, for tears were choking them, but anon when milor stood nearer, petite maman knelt down, and, imprisoning his slender hand in her brown, wrinkled ones, she kissed it reverently.

===Chinese===

“现在嘛，”他换上更斩钉截铁的口气收了尾，“当务之急，是赶在城门关闭之前出巴黎。勒内格尔老爹，带上您的妻子和女儿，大大方方走出这楼去。中士和他的人没有撤走若利维街的岗位，旁人便谁也搅扰不着你们。径直到讷伊门（Porte de Neuilly）去，出了门，在大道拐角那家小咖啡馆里安安静静等我。你们过城关用的旧通行证还作数；你们是今天早上才列上‘嫌疑’名单的，还没有为你们闹出追捕的风声来。无论如何，需要的话我们自有人就在左近接应。”

“可是您呢，milor，”勒内格尔老爹嗫嚅道，“还有您的朋友们——？”

“*La*，老兄，”布莱克尼轻快地回了一句，“我不是早跟您说过，千万别为我跟我的朋友们操心吗？要甩开你们这个该死的政府，我们的路子多着呢。您只消惦记您的妻子和女儿就是。只怕 petite maman 随身带不了比她身上更多的东西了，不过我们一定尽力让她舒舒服服，直到把你们全家安顿得妥妥帖帖——在英国——和皮埃尔团聚。”

勒内格尔老爹、petite maman、还有罗塞特，一时都说不出话来，泪水哽住了他们；不多时 milor 站得近了些，petite maman 跪了下来，把他那双修长的手攥进自己褐色皱纹的手里，恭恭敬敬地吻了它。

===Original===

He laughed and chided her for this.

“ ’Tis I should kneel to you in gratitude, petite maman,” he said earnestly, “you were ready to sacrifice your old man for me.”

“You have saved Pierre, milor,” said the mother simply.

A minute later Père Lenègre and the two women were ready to go. Already milor and his gallant English friends were busy once more transforming themselves into grimy workmen or seedy middle-class professionals.

===Chinese===

他笑了，为这个嗔怪她。

“该下跪道谢的是我呀，petite maman。”他恳切地说，“为了我，您是打算舍出您的老伴了。”

“您救了皮埃尔，milor。”做母亲的只简单地说了这么一句。

一分钟后，勒内格尔老爹和两个女人已整装待发。milor 和他那些英武的英国朋友们，已经又忙着把自己变回满身油污的工匠，或是衣着寒酸的中产先生了。

===Original===

As soon as the door of apartment No. 12 finally closed behind the three good folk, my lord Tony asked of his chief:

“What about these three wretched soldiers, Blakeney?”

“Oh! they’ll be all right for twenty-four hours. They can’t starve till then, and by that time the concierge will have realised that there’s something wrong with the door of No. 12 and will come in to investigate the matter. Are they securely bound, though?”

“And gagged! Rather!” ejaculated one of the others. “Odds life, Blakeney!” he added enthusiastically, “that was a fine bit of work!”

===Chinese===

12 号房间的门在这三位好人身后终于合上，托尼勋爵（my lord Tony）便问他的首领：

“这三个倒霉的士兵怎么办，布莱克尼？”

“哦！他们撑上二十四小时不成问题。到不了饿死的份上，到时候门房自会发现 12 号的门不对劲，进来查看究竟。不过，他们捆结实了吗？”

“嘴也堵上了！那还用说！”另一个人朗声答道。“老天在上，布莱克尼！”他兴冲冲地补上一句，“这活儿干得真漂亮！”

## A Question of Passports / ## 护照问题

===Original===

Bibot was very sure of himself. There never was, never had been, there never would be again another such patriotic citizen of the Republic as was citizen Bibot of the Town Guard.

And because his patriotism was so well known among the members of the Committee of Public Safety, and his uncompromising hatred of the aristocrats so highly appreciated, citizen Bibot had been given the most important military post within the city of Paris.

He was in command of the Porte Montmartre, which goes to prove how highly he was esteemed, for, believe me, more treachery had been going on inside and out of the Porte Montmartre than in any other quarter of Paris. The last commandant there, citizen Ferney, was guillotined for having allowed a whole batch of aristocrats﻿—traitors to the Republic, all of them﻿—to slip through the Porte Montmartre and to find safety outside the walls of Paris. Ferney pleaded in his defence that these traitors had been spirited away from under his very nose by the devil’s agency, for surely that meddlesome Englishman who spent his time in rescuing aristocrats﻿—traitors, all of them﻿—from the clutches of Madame la Guillotine must be either the devil himself, or at any rate one of his most powerful agents.

“*Nom de Dieu!* Just think of his name! The Scarlet Pimpernel they call him! No one knows him by any other name! and he is preternaturally tall and strong and superhumanly cunning! And the power which he has of being transmuted into various personalities﻿—rendering himself quite unrecognisable to the eyes of the most sharp-seeing patriot of France, must of a surety be a gift of Satan!”

But the Committee of Public Safety refused to listen to Ferney’s explanations. The Scarlet Pimpernel was only an ordinary mortal﻿—an exceedingly cunning and meddlesome personage it is true, and endowed with a superfluity of wealth which enabled him to break the thin crust of patriotism that overlay the natural cupidity of many Captains of the Town Guard﻿—but still an ordinary man for all that! and no true lover of the Republic should allow either superstitious terror or greed to interfere with the discharge of his duties which at the Porte Montmartre consisted in detaining any and every person﻿—aristocrat, foreigner, or otherwise traitor to the Republic﻿—who could not give a satisfactory reason for desiring to leave Paris. Having detained such persons, the patriot’s next duty was to hand them over to the Committee of Public Safety, who would then decide whether Madame la Guillotine would have the last word over them or not.

===Chinese===

比博（Bibot）对自己十分有把握。共和国从来不曾有过、往后也绝不会再有第二位像城防军（Town Guard）公民比博这样的爱国公民。

由于他的爱国热忱在公安委员会（Committee of Public Safety）各位委员当中有口皆碑，他对贵族毫不妥协的仇恨又备受赏识，公民比博便得到了巴黎（Paris）城内最重要的一处军事职位。

他统管着蒙马特门（Porte Montmartre）——单凭这一点，就足见上头何等器重他，因为请您相信，蒙马特门里里外外干的通敌卖国勾当，比巴黎任何别的地段都要多。上一任指挥官费尔内（Ferney）公民，就因为放走了一整批贵族——清一色共和国的叛徒——任他们溜出蒙马特门、在巴黎城墙之外寻得安身之地，被送上了断头台。费尔内分辩说，这些叛徒是被魔鬼的手段从他眼皮底下摄走的，因为那个终日价忙着搭救贵族——全是叛徒，一个不落——让他们逃脱断头台夫人（Madame la Guillotine）魔爪的爱管闲事的英国人，若不是魔鬼本人，至少也必定是魔鬼最得力的代理人之一。

“*Nom de Dieu!*（我的上帝啊！）想想他那个名号吧！人家管他叫红花侠（the Scarlet Pimpernel）！谁都不知道他还有别的名字！他身高力强得超乎自然，狡诈多智更非人力所能及！他还能变化出种种不同的相貌身形——叫全法国眼力最尖的爱国者也认他不出——这本事必定是撒旦所赐！”

可是公安委员会不肯听费尔内的辩解。红花侠不过是个凡人——诚然，是个极端狡诈、爱管闲事的角色，而且富得流油，单凭钱财就能砸穿许多城防军队长蒙在天生贪财之心上的那层薄薄的爱国假面——可终究不过是个凡人！共和国真正的热爱者，谁也不该让迷信的恐惧或者贪财之心妨碍自己履行职责；在蒙马特门，这职责便是扣下任何一个说不出充分理由却想离开巴黎的人——贵族也好，外国人也好，或是别的什么共和国的叛徒也好。扣下这等人之后，爱国者下一步的职责，便是把他们移交公安委员会，再由委员会定夺，断头台夫人要不要对他们说最后一句话。

===Original===

And the guillotine did nearly always have the last word to say, unless the Scarlet Pimpernel interfered.

The trouble was, that that same accursed Englishman interfered at times in a manner which was positively terrifying. His impudence, certes, passed all belief. Stories of his daring and of his impudence were abroad which literally made the lank and greasy hair of every patriot curl with wonder. ’Twas even whispered﻿—not too loudly, forsooth﻿—that certain members of the Committee of Public Safety had measured their skill and valour against that of the Englishman and emerged from the conflict beaten and humiliated, vowing vengeance which, of a truth, was still slow in coming.

Citizen Chauvelin, one of the most implacable and unyielding members of the Committee, was known to have suffered overwhelming shame at the hands of that daring gang, of whom the so-called Scarlet Pimpernel was the accredited chief. Some there were who said that citizen Chauvelin had forever forfeited his prestige, and even endangered his head by measuring his well-known astuteness against that mysterious League of spies.

But then Bibot was different!

He feared neither the devil, nor any Englishman. Had the latter the strength of giants and the protection of every power of evil, Bibot was ready for him. Nay! he was aching for a tussle, and haunted the purlieus of the Committees to obtain some post which would enable him to come to grips with the Scarlet Pimpernel and his League.

===Chinese===

而断头台几乎总有最后一句话要说——除非红花侠插手。

麻烦在于，那个该死的英国人有时插手的方式，着实叫人心惊胆战。他的胆大妄为，说真的，简直匪夷所思。关于他如何艺高人胆大的传闻四处流传，当真叫每一个爱国者那稀疏油腻的头发都惊得打了卷儿。甚至有人窃窃私语——当然不敢太响——说公安委员会里某几位委员，曾与那英国人较量过智谋胆识，结果败下阵来，受尽羞辱，发誓要报仇雪恨；而这仇，千真万确，至今迟迟未报。

众人都知道，委员会里最毫不容情、最不肯通融的委员之一——肖维兰（Chauvelin）公民——就曾在那伙亡命之徒手里蒙受过奇耻大辱，而那伙人公认的首领，正是所谓的红花侠。有人说，肖维兰公民已经把自己那点威望永远赔了个精光，甚至把脑袋也搭了进去，谁叫他拿自己出了名的精明，去跟那个神秘的间谍同盟较量呢。

可比博不一样！

他既不怕魔鬼，也不怕什么英国人。哪怕后者有巨人的膂力、有一切邪魔鬼祟的庇护，比博也照样奉陪。岂止奉陪！他巴不得真刀真枪干上一仗，成日在各个委员会的门庭转悠，只求谋个职位，好同红花侠及其党羽——红花侠同盟（the League of the Scarlet Pimpernel）——短兵相接。

===Original===

Bibot’s zeal and perseverance were duly rewarded, and anon he was appointed to the command of the guard at the Porte Montmartre.

A post of vast importance as aforesaid; so much so, in fact, that no less a person than citizen Jean Paul Marat himself came to speak with Bibot on that third day of Nivose in the year I of the Republic, with a view to impressing upon him the necessity of keeping his eyes open, and of suspecting every man, woman, and child indiscriminately until they had proved themselves to be true patriots.

“Let no one slip through your fingers, citizen Bibot,” Marat admonished with grim earnestness. “That accursed Englishman is cunning and resourceful, and his impudence surpasses that of the devil himself.”

“He’d better try some of his impudence on me!” commented Bibot with a sneer, “he’ll soon find out that he no longer has a Ferney to deal with. Take it from me, citizen Marat, that if a batch of aristocrats escape out of Paris within the next few days, under the guidance of the d﻿—﻿—﻿d Englishman, they will have to find some other way than the Porte Montmartre.”

“Well said, citizen!” commented Marat. “But be watchful tonight﻿ ﻿… tonight especially. The Scarlet Pimpernel is rampant in Paris just now.”

===Chinese===

比博的热忱与坚韧得到了应有的报偿，不久他便受命出任蒙马特门卫队的指挥。

如前所述，这是个非同小可的职位；其分量之重，竟至于共和国元年（the year I of the Republic）雪月（Nivose）三日那一天，连让-保罗·马拉（Jean Paul Marat）公民他老人家都亲自来同比博谈话，为的是叮嘱他务必睁大眼睛，不论男女老幼，一概先疑上一个遍，等他们证明了自己是真正的爱国者，再作分晓。

“谁也不许从你指缝里溜走，比博公民。”马拉神情冷峻、语气恳切地告诫道，“那个该死的英国人狡诈多端、诡计百出，他那股胆大妄为的劲头，连魔鬼本人都要自愧不如。”

“他顶好上我这儿来撒撒野！”比博冷笑道，“他很快就会明白，如今跟他打交道的可不是费尔内了。记住我这话，马拉公民：往后几天里，要是真有一伙贵族在那个该死的英国人带领下逃出巴黎，那他们走的就绝不是蒙马特门。”

“说得好，公民！”马拉称赞道，“不过今夜要留神……今夜尤其要留神。眼下红花侠正在巴黎城里闹得欢。”

===Original===

“How so?”

“The ci-devant Duc and Duchesse de Montreux and the whole of their brood﻿—sisters, brothers, two or three children, a priest, and several servants﻿—a round dozen in all, have been condemned to death. The guillotine for them tomorrow at daybreak! Would it could have been tonight,” added Marat, whilst a demoniacal leer contorted his face which already exuded lust for blood from every pore. “Would it could have been tonight. But the guillotine has been busy; over four hundred executions today﻿ ﻿… and the tumbrils are full﻿—the seats bespoken in advance﻿—and still they come.﻿ ﻿… But tomorrow morning at daybreak Madame la Guillotine will have a word to say to the whole of the Montreux crowd!”

“But they are in the Conciergerie prison surely, citizen! out of the reach of that accursed Englishman?”

“They are on their way, an I mistake not, to the prison at this moment. I came straight on here after the condemnation, to which I listened with true joy. Ah, citizen Bibot! the blood of these hated aristocrats is good to behold when it drips from the blade of the guillotine. Have a care, citizen Bibot, do not let the Montreux crowd escape!”

“Have no fear, citizen Marat! But surely there is no danger! They have been tried and condemned! They are, as you say, even now on their way﻿—well guarded, I presume﻿—to the Conciergerie prison!﻿—tomorrow at daybreak, the guillotine! What is there to fear?”

===Chinese===

“这话怎么讲？”

“旧日的蒙特勒公爵与公爵夫人（ci-devant Duc and Duchesse de Montreux），还有他们一家老小——姐妹、兄弟、两三个孩子、一个教士、外加几名仆人——总共整整一打人，已被判处死刑。明天天一亮就上断头台！只可惜不是今晚。”马拉又补上一句，一脸恶魔似的狞笑，扭曲了那本来就每个毛孔都在往外渗着嗜血欲念的脸，“只可惜不是今晚。断头台这些日子忙得很：今天处决了四百多个……死囚车装得满满的——座位早早预订一空——人还是源源不断地押来。……不过明天一早天一亮，断头台夫人就要对蒙特勒全家老小发话了！”

“可他们肯定关在古监狱（Conciergerie）里呀，公民！那个该死的英国人够不着了吧？”

“若是我没弄错，他们这会儿正在押往监狱的路上。宣判一完我就径直赶到你这儿来了，那场判决我听得满心欢喜。啊，比博公民！这些可恨贵族的血从断头台的铡刀上一滴滴落下来的时候，看着有多痛快。当心啊，比博公民，可别让蒙特勒一家跑了！”

“放心吧，马拉公民！不过哪里就有什么危险！他们已经审过、判过了！正如您所说，他们此刻正在路上——想必看守森严——押往古监狱！——明天天一亮，断头台伺候！还有什么可怕的？”

===Original===

“Well! well!” said Marat, with a slight tone of hesitation, “it is best, citizen Bibot, to be overcareful these times.”

Even whilst Marat spoke his face, usually so cunning and so vengeful, had suddenly lost its look of devilish cruelty which was almost superhuman in the excess of its infamy, and a greyish hue﻿—suggestive of terror﻿—had spread over the sunken cheeks. He clutched Bibot’s arm, and leaning over the table he whispered in his ear:

“The Public Prosecutor had scarce finished his speech today, judgment was being pronounced, the spectators were expectant and still, only the Montreux woman and some of the females and children were blubbering and moaning, when suddenly, it seemed from nowhere, a small piece of paper fluttered from out the assembly and alighted on the desk in front of the Public Prosecutor. He took the paper up and glanced at its contents. I saw that his cheeks had paled, and that his hand trembled as he handed the paper over to me.”

“And what did that paper contain, citizen Marat?” asked Bibot, also speaking in a whisper, for an access of superstitious terror was gripping him by the throat.

“Just the well-known accursed device, citizen, the small scarlet flower, drawn in red ink, and the few words: ‘Tonight the innocent men and women now condemned by this infamous tribunal will be beyond your reach!’ ”

===Chinese===

“唉！唉！”马拉说道，语气里带着一丝迟疑，“如今这年月，比博公民，宁可小心过了头，才最稳妥。”

马拉说话的当儿，他那张素来阴狠毒辣的脸，忽然褪去了那副凶恶得几乎超出人间限度的狰狞之色，凹陷的双颊上漫开一层灰扑扑的——透着恐惧的——色调。他一把攥住比博的胳膊，俯身凑到桌子上，对他咬耳朵道：

“今天公诉人（Public Prosecutor）的话音才落，判决正在宣读，看客们屏息凝神，只有蒙特勒家那位太太和几个女眷孩子还在啼哭呜咽，忽然间，不知打哪儿飞出一小片纸，从人群里飘飘悠悠地落在了公诉人面前的案桌上。他拈起纸片扫了一眼。我瞧见他双颊失色，把纸递到我手上时，手都在发抖。”

“那纸片上写着什么，马拉公民？”比博问，嗓音也压成了耳语，因为一阵迷信的恐惧正扼住他的喉咙。

“就是那个尽人皆知的可恶标记，公民——一朵小小的红花，拿红墨水描的，还附着几行字：‘今夜，被这个无耻法庭枉判的无辜男女，就将远在你够不着的地方了！’”

===Original===

“And no sign of a messenger?”

“None.”

“And when did﻿—”

“Hush!” said Marat peremptorily, “no more of that now. To your post, citizen, and remember﻿—all are suspect! let none escape!”

The two men had been sitting outside a small tavern, opposite the Porte Montmartre, with a bottle of wine between them, their elbows resting on the grimy top of a rough wooden table. They had talked in whispers, for even the walls of the tumbledown cabaret might have had ears.

===Chinese===

“没查出是谁递的？”

“没有。”

“那是什么时——”

“嘘！”马拉断然止住他，“这事休要再提。回你的岗位去，公民，记住——人人皆可疑！一个也不许放走！”

两人方才一直坐在蒙马特门对面一家小酒馆外，中间搁着一瓶葡萄酒，胳膊肘支在粗木桌油腻腻的桌面上。他们说话全用耳语，因为连这破败酒馆的四壁，说不定都长着耳朵。

===Original===

Opposite them the city wall﻿—broken here by the great gate of Montmartre﻿—loomed threateningly in the fast-gathering dusk of this winter’s afternoon. Men in ragged red shirts, their unkempt heads crowned with Phrygian caps adorned with a tricolour cockade, lounged against the wall, or sat in groups on the top of piles of refuse that littered the street, with a rough deal plank between them and a greasy pack of cards in their grimy fingers. Guns and bayonets were propped against the wall. The gate itself had three means of egress; each of these was guarded by two men with fixed bayonets at their shoulders, but otherwise dressed like the others, in rags﻿—with bare legs that looked blue and numb in the cold﻿—the sansculottes of revolutionary Paris.

Bibot rose from his seat, nodding to Marat, and joined his men.

From afar, but gradually drawing nearer, came the sound of a ribald song, with chorus accompaniment sung by throats obviously surfeited with liquor.

For a moment﻿—as the sound approached﻿—Bibot turned back once more to the Friend of the People.

“Am I to understand, citizen,” he said, “that my orders are not to let anyone pass through these gates tonight?”

===Chinese===

他们对面的城墙——在此被蒙马特（Montmartre）大城门豁开一道口子——在冬日午后迅速四合的暮色里阴沉沉地耸峙着，杀气腾腾。一群穿破烂红衬衫的汉子，蓬乱的头上扣着缀了三色帽徽的弗里吉亚帽（Phrygian caps），有的懒洋洋倚着墙，有的成堆坐在满街垃圾的堆顶上，中间架一块粗糙的松木板，污黑的手指间捏着一副油腻的纸牌。枪和刺刀斜倚在墙上。城门本身开有三处出口；每一处都有两个人把守，肩上端着上了刺刀的枪，其余装束与旁人无异，一样破破烂烂——两条光腿在寒气里冻得发青发木——正是革命时期巴黎的无套裤汉（sansculottes）。

比博从座位上站起身，朝马拉点了点头，便到他的部下那边去了。

远处传来一支下流小曲的歌声，还夹着众人齐唱的和声，正渐渐行近，那些嗓子分明都灌饱了黄汤。

歌声渐近的当儿，比博又回过身去，望了一眼那位“人民之友”（the Friend of the People）。

“我是不是可以这样理解，公民，”他说，“给我的命令是今夜不许任何人通过这几道城门？”

===Original===

“No, no, citizen,” replied Marat, “we dare not do that. There are a number of good patriots in the city still. We cannot interfere with their liberty or﻿—”

And the look of fear of the demagogue﻿—himself afraid of the human whirlpool which he has let loose﻿—stole into Marat’s cruel, piercing eyes.

“No, no,” he reiterated more emphatically, “we cannot disregard the passports issued by the Committee of Public Safety. But examine each passport carefully, citizen Bibot! If you have any reasonable ground for suspicion, detain the holder, and if you have not﻿—”

The sound of singing was quite near now. With another wink and a final leer, Marat drew back under the shadow of the cabaret, and Bibot swaggered up to the main entrance of the gate.

“Qui va la?” he thundered in stentorian tones as a group of some half-dozen people lurched towards him out of the gloom, still shouting hoarsely their ribald drinking song.

===Chinese===

“不，不，公民，”马拉答道，“那样我们可不敢。城里还有不少好的爱国者。我们不能妨害他们的自由，否则——”

于是那煽动家自己的惧色——他到底害怕自己亲手放出来的这股人间漩涡——悄悄爬上了马拉那双残忍而锐利的眼睛。

“不，不，”他又斩钉截铁地重申一遍，“公安委员会签发的护照，我们不能置之不理。可你要把每一份护照都细细查验，比博公民！只要有半点说得过去的疑窦，就扣下持照人；要是没有——”

歌声这会儿已经到了近前。马拉又递了个眼色，末了再狞笑一遭，缩回酒馆的阴影里去了；比博则大摇大摆地朝城门正口走去。

“Qui va la?（什么人？）”他声如洪钟地大喝，只见昏暗里跌跌撞撞走来五六个人，嘴里还嘶哑地吼着那支下流的酒歌。

===Original===

The foremost man in the group paused opposite citizen Bibot, and with arms akimbo, and legs planted well apart tried to assume a rigidity of attitude which apparently was somewhat foreign to him at this moment.

“Good patriots, citizen,” he said in a thick voice which he vainly tried to render steady.

“What do you want?” queried Bibot.

“To be allowed to go on our way unmolested.”

“What is your way?”

===Chinese===

走在最前头的那人到了比博公民跟前站住，两手叉腰，两腿大大岔开，竭力想端出一副笔挺的架势，只是这会儿这架势于他显然颇为生疏。

“好爱国者，公民。”他用一口浓浊的酒腔说道，还想把声音放稳，却是枉然。

“你们要干什么？”比博问道。

“放我们安安稳稳赶路。”

“赶往哪儿去？”

===Original===

“Through the Porte Montmartre to the village of Barency.”

“What is your business there?”

This query delivered in Bibot’s most pompous manner seemed vastly to amuse the rowdy crowd. He who was the spokesman turned to his friends and shouted hilariously:

“Hark at him, citizens! He asks me what is our business. *Ohé*, citizen Bibot, since when have you become blind? A dolt you’ve always been, else you had not asked the question.”

But Bibot, undeterred by the man’s drunken insolence, retorted gruffly:

===Chinese===

“出蒙马特门，上巴朗西（Barency）村去。”

“去那儿做什么营生？”

比博拿出最趾高气扬的腔调问出这一句，似乎把这起子吵吵嚷嚷的人逗了个不亦乐乎。那发言人转向他的伙伴，乐乐呵呵地嚷道：

“都听听他说什么，公民们！他问我我们的营生是什么。*Ohé*（喂），比博公民，你打几时起眼睛瞎了？你一向就是个呆子，要不然也问不出这话来。”

可比博不为这醉汉的放肆所动，粗声粗气地顶了回去：

===Original===

“Your business, I want to know.”

“Bibot! my little Bibot!” cooed the bibulous orator now in dulcet tones, “dost not know us, my good Bibot? Yet we all know thee, citizen﻿—Captain Bibot of the Town Guard, eh, citizens! Three cheers for the citizen captain!”

When the noisy shouts and cheers from half a dozen hoarse throats had died down, Bibot, without more ado, turned to his own men at the gate.

“Drive these drunken louts away!” he commanded; “no one is allowed to loiter here.”

Loud protest on the part of the hilarious crowd followed, then a slight scuffle with the bayonets of the Town Guard. Finally the spokesman, somewhat sobered, once more appealed to Bibot.

===Chinese===

“你们的营生，我要知道。”

“比博！我的小比博！”那好酒的演说家这会儿换上了甜腻腻的口吻，“你竟认不得我们啦，我的好比博？我们可个个都认得你呐，公民——城防军的比博队长，是不是，公民们！来，为公民队长欢呼三声！”

五六条粗哑嗓门的叫嚷欢呼平息下去之后，比博二话不说，转向城门边自己的部下。

“把这帮醉鬼轰走！”他下令道，“谁也不许在这儿磨蹭。”

这起子兴高采烈的人跟着大声抗议，随后又同城防军的刺刀小小推搡了一阵。末了，那发言人酒醒了几分，再次向比博陈说。

===Original===

“Citizen Bibot! you must be blind not to know me and my mates! And let me tell you that you are doing yourself a deal of harm by interfering with the citizens of the Republic in the proper discharge of their duties, and by disregarding their rights of egress through this gate, a right confirmed by passports signed by two members of the Committee of Public Safety.”

He had spoken now fairly clearly and very pompously. Bibot, somewhat impressed and remembering Marat’s admonitions, said very civilly:

“Tell me your business then, citizen, and show me your passports. If everything is in order you may go your way.”

“But you know me, citizen Bibot?” queried the other.

“Yes, I know you﻿—unofficially, citizen Durand.”

===Chinese===

“比博公民！你连我和我的伙计们都不认得，眼睛准是瞎了！我再告诉你，你这样阻挠共和国公民履行正当职务，无视人家经这道城门出城的权利——这权利有两名公安委员会委员签署的护照作保——你可是自己在给自己招灾惹祸！”

他这会儿口齿已相当清楚，派头也十足。比博有点被镇住了，又记起马拉的告诫，便客客气气地说：

“那就说说你们的营生吧，公民，护照也拿给我瞧瞧。倘若一切无误，你们便可上路。”

“你是认得我的，对吧，比博公民？”对方问道。

“认得——非官方地认得，迪朗（Durand）公民。”

===Original===

“You know that I and the citizens here are the carriers for citizen Legrand, the market gardener of Barency?”

“Yes, I know that,” said Bibot guardedly, “unofficially.”

“Then, unofficially, let me tell you, citizen, that unless we get to Barency this evening, Paris will have to do without cabbages and potatoes tomorrow. So now you know that you are acting at your own risk and peril, citizen, by detaining us.”

“Your passports, all of you,” commanded Bibot.

He had just caught sight of Marat still sitting outside the tavern opposite, and was glad enough, in this instance, to shelve his responsibility on the shoulders of the popular “Friend of the People.” There was general searching in ragged pockets for grimy papers with official seals thereon, and whilst Bibot ordered one of his men to take the six passports across the road to citizen Marat for his inspection, he himself, by the last rays of the setting winter sun, made close examination of the six men who desired to pass through the Porte Montmartre.

===Chinese===

“你知道我和这儿几位公民，都是给巴朗西的菜农勒格朗（Legrand）公民跑运输的吧？”

“知道，这我知道，”比博留了个心眼说道，“非官方地知道。”

“那么，容我也非官方地告诉你，公民：今晚我们要是到不了巴朗西，明天巴黎的卷心菜和土豆就得断了顿。这下你该如何掂量掂量了吧，公民，扣住我们，可是风险自负。”

“你们的护照，都交出来。”比博命令道。

他刚瞧见马拉还坐在对面酒馆外头，这回正巴不得把责任往这位众望所归的“人民之友”肩上一推。破衣兜里顿时纷纷一阵摸索，掏出盖着官印的乌涂涂的纸片；比博吩咐一名手下把六份护照送到马路对面请马拉公民查验，自己则趁着冬日西沉的最后一缕残照，把这六个想要通过蒙马特门的人上上下下细细打量了一番。

===Original===

As the spokesman had averred, he﻿—Bibot﻿—knew every one of these men. They were the carriers to citizen Legrand, the Barency market gardener. Bibot knew every face. They passed with a load of fruit and vegetables in and out of Paris every day. There was really and absolutely no cause for suspicion, and when citizen Marat returned the six passports, pronouncing them to be genuine, and recognising his own signature at the bottom of each, Bibot was at last satisfied, and the six bibulous carriers were allowed to pass through the gate, which they did, arm in arm, singing a wild carmagnole, and vociferously cheering as they emerged out into the open.

But Bibot passed an unsteady hand over his brow. It was cold, yet he was in a perspiration. That sort of thing tells on a man’s nerves. He rejoined Marat, at the table outside the drinking booth, and ordered a fresh bottle of wine.

The sun had set now, and with the gathering dusk a damp mist descended on Montmartre. From the wall opposite, where the men sat playing cards, came occasional volleys of blasphemous oaths. Bibot was feeling much more like himself. He had half forgotten the incident of the six carriers, which had occurred nearly half an hour ago.

Two or three other people had, in the meanwhile, tried to pass through the gates, but Bibot had been suspicious and had detained them all.

Marat having commended him for his zeal took final leave of him. Just as the demagogue’s slouchy, grimy figure was disappearing down a side street there was the loud clatter of hoofs from that same direction, and the next moment a detachment of the mounted Town Guard, headed by an officer in uniform, galloped down the ill-paved street.

===Chinese===

诚如那发言人所言，他——比博——认得这些人，一个不落。他们都是巴朗西菜农勒格朗公民的运输夫。比博认得每一张脸。他们每天载着一车果蔬进出巴黎。实实在在、的的确确，没有半点可疑的由头；待到马拉公民把六份护照退回来，断定全是真件，每一份底下都有他本人的亲笔签名，比博这才终于放了心。六个醉醺醺的运输夫获准通过城门——他们果真手挽着手走了出去，唱着一支狂野的卡马尼奥拉（carmagnole），一面扯开嗓子欢呼。

可比博抬起一只发颤的手，抹了抹额头。天这么冷，他却出了一身汗。这种事最磨人的神经。他回到酒棚外马拉的桌旁坐下，又要了一瓶新酒。

太阳此刻已经落尽，随着暮色四合，一团潮乎乎的雾气罩上了蒙马特。对面墙根下，打牌的汉子们不时爆出一阵阵地动山摇的咒天骂地。比博自觉心神定多了。六个运输夫那桩事已过去了将近半个钟头，他也忘了个八九不离十。

这中间又有两三个人想要通过城门，可比博起了疑，把他们尽数扣下了。

马拉嘉许了他一番热忱，便向他作别而去。正当那煽动家邋里邋遢、龌里龌龊的身影消失在一条小街口时，同一方向猛然响起一片嘚嘚的马蹄声，转眼间，一小队由一名制服军官带队的城防骑兵，顺着那条坑洼不平的街道疾驰而来。

===Original===

Even before the troopers had drawn rein the officer had hailed Bibot.

“Citizen,” he shouted, and his voice was breathless, for he had evidently ridden hard and fast, “this message to you from the citizen Chief Commissary of the Section. Six men are wanted by the Committee of Public Safety. They are disguised as carriers in the employ of a market gardener, and have passports for Barency!﻿ ﻿… The passports are stolen: the men are traitors﻿—escaped aristocrats﻿—and their spokesman is that d﻿—﻿—﻿d Englishman, the Scarlet Pimpernel.”

Bibot tried to speak; he tugged at the collar of his ragged shirt; an awful curse escaped him.

“Ten thousand devils!” he roared.

“On no account allow these people to go through,” continued the officer. “Keep their passports. Detain them!﻿ ﻿… Understand?”

===Chinese===

骑兵们还没收缰，那军官已先朝比博扬声招呼。

“公民，”他喊道，声音里透着气喘，显见得一路策马狂奔而来，“本区首席政委公民（Chief Commissary of the Section）有话带给你。公安委员会正在缉拿六个人。他们乔装成受雇于一名菜农的运输夫，持的是去巴朗西的护照！……护照是偷来的：那六个人是叛徒——潜逃的贵族——他们的发言人，正是那个该死的英国人，红花侠。”

比博想要开口；他扯了扯破衬衫的领口；一声可怕的咒骂夺口而出。

“一万个小魔鬼！”他咆哮道。

“无论如何不许这些人出去，”军官接着说，“扣下他们的护照。扣下他们！……明白吗？”

===Original===

Bibot was still gasping for breath even whilst the officer, ordering a quick “Turn!” reeled his horse round, ready to gallop away as far as he had come.

“I am for the St. Denis Gate﻿—Grosjean is on guard there!” he shouted. “Same orders all round the city. No one to leave the gates!﻿ ﻿… Understand?”

His troopers fell in. The next moment he would be gone, and those cursed aristocrats well in safety’s way.

“Citizen Captain!”

The hoarse shout at last contrived to escape Bibot’s parched throat. As if involuntarily, the officer drew rein once more.

===Chinese===

比博还在那里大喘气，那军官已厉喝一声“掉头！”，拨回马头，只待照着来路疾驰而回。

“我要去圣德尼门（St. Denis Gate）——格罗让（Grosjean）在那儿当值！”他喊道，“全城命令一律相同。谁也不许出城门！……明白吗？”

他的骑兵们列好了队。下一瞬他就要去了，而那些可恶的贵族也就稳稳当当踏上脱身之路了。

“队长公民！”

这声嘶哑的呐喊，终于从比博干焦的喉咙里挤了出来。那军官像是身不由己，又收了一回缰。

===Original===

“What is it? Quick!﻿—I’ve no time. That confounded Englishman may be at the St. Denis Gate even now!”

“Citizen Captain,” gasped Bibot, his breath coming and going like that of a man fighting for his life. “Here!﻿ ﻿… at this gate!﻿ ﻿… not half an hour ago﻿ ﻿… six men﻿ ﻿… carriers﻿ ﻿… market gardeners﻿ ﻿… I seemed to know their faces.﻿ ﻿…”

“Yes! yes! market gardener’s carriers,” exclaimed the officer gleefully, “aristocrats all of them﻿ ﻿… and that d﻿—﻿—﻿d Scarlet Pimpernel. You’ve got them? You’ve detained them?﻿ ﻿… Where are they?﻿ ﻿… Speak, man, in the name of hell!﻿ ﻿…”

“Gone!” gasped Bibot. His legs would no longer bear him. He fell backwards on to a heap of street debris and refuse, from which lowly vantage ground he contrived to give away the whole miserable tale.

“Gone! half an hour ago. Their passports were in order!﻿ ﻿… I seemed to know their faces! Citizen Marat was here.﻿ ﻿… He, too﻿—”

===Chinese===

“什么事？快讲！——我没了工夫。那个混账英国人这会儿说不定已经到了圣德尼门！”

“队长公民，”比博喘着大气，气息一进一出，活像个在生死关头挣扎的人。“就在这儿！……就在这道城门！……不到半个钟头之前……六个人……运输夫……菜农的伙计……他们的脸，我仿佛张张认得。……”

“对！对！菜农的运输夫，”军官喜动颜色地叫道，“全是贵族……还有那个该死的红花侠。你把他们拿住了？扣下了？……人在哪儿？……说话呀，伙计，看在地狱份上！……”

“跑了！”比博喘着气说。他的两腿再也撑持不住，仰面倒在一堆街上的碎砖烂渣上，就着那低微的地势，把这桩倒霉事的始末一五一十招了出来。

“跑了！半个钟头之前。他们的护照件件无误！……他们的脸我仿佛张张认得！马拉公民当时就在这儿。……他，他也是——”

===Original===

In a moment the officer had once more swung his horse round, so that the animal reared, with wild forefeet pawing the air, with champing of bit, and white foam scattered around.

“A thousand million curses!” he exclaimed. “Citizen Bibot, your head will pay for this treachery. Which way did they go?”

A dozen hands were ready to point in the direction where the merry party of carriers had disappeared half an hour ago; a dozen tongues gave rapid, confused explanations.

“Into it, my men!” shouted the officer; “they were on foot! They can’t have gone far. Remember the Republic has offered ten thousand francs for the capture of the Scarlet Pimpernel.”

Already the heavy gates had been swung open, and the officer’s voice once more rang out clear through a perfect thunderclap of fast galloping hoofs:

===Chinese===

说时迟那时快，那军官又把马猛地兜转过来，坐骑人立而起，前蹄狂乱地刨着空气，咬得嚼子咯咯作响，白沫四下飞溅。

“千百万个恶魔的诅咒！”他厉声叫道，“比博公民，你这桩通敌的罪过，是要拿脑袋来抵的。他们往哪边去了？”

十几只手争先恐后，指向半个钟头前那伙快活的运输夫消失的方向；十几条舌头七嘴八舌、慌里慌张地抢着分说。

“追上去，弟兄们！”军官喊道，“他们是步行的！走不了多远。记住，共和国悬赏一万法郎缉拿红花侠。”

沉重的城门早已敞开，军官的嗓音再一次穿透疾驰的马蹄汇成的滚滚雷鸣，清亮地响了起来：

===Original===

“Ventre à terre! Remember!﻿—ten thousand francs to him who first sights the Scarlet Pimpernel!”

The thunderclap died away in the distance, the dust of four score hoofs was merged in the fog and in the darkness; the voice of the captain was raised again through the mist-laden air. One shout﻿ ﻿… a shout of triumph﻿ ﻿… then silence once again.

Bibot had fainted on the heap of debris.

His comrades brought him wine to drink. He gradually revived. Hope came back to his heart; his nerves soon steadied themselves as the heavy beverage filtrated through into his blood.

“Bah!” he ejaculated as he pulled himself together, “the troopers were well-mounted﻿ ﻿… the officer was enthusiastic; those carriers could not have walked very far. And, in any case, I am free from blame. Citoyen Marat himself was here and let them pass!”

===Chinese===

“Ventre à terre（马腹贴地，全速前进）！记住！——谁先望见红花侠，那一万法郎就归谁！”

雷鸣般的蹄声在远处消散，八十只马蹄扬起的尘土没入了浓雾和黑暗；队长的呼喊又一次穿透饱含雾气的夜空。一声呐喊……一声得胜的欢呼……随后复归寂静。

比博已经晕倒在瓦砾堆上。

同伴们端来酒给他灌了几口。他渐渐还过魂来。希望重新回到心里；待这浓重的酒液一丝丝渗进血液，他的神经很快就稳住了。

“呸！”他定下神来，迸出一句，“骑兵们的坐骑都是骏马……那位军官又劲头十足；那几个运输夫凭两条腿走不了多远。再说，横竖怪不到我头上。Citoyen Marat（公民马拉）本人当时就在这儿，还是他放他们过去的！”

===Original===

A shudder of superstitious terror ran through him as he recollected the whole scene: for surely he knew all the faces of the six men who had gone through the gate. The devil indeed must have given the mysterious Englishman power to transmute himself and his gang wholly into the bodies of other people.

More than an hour went by. Bibot was quite himself again, bullying, commanding, detaining everybody now.

At that time there appeared to be a slight altercation going on, on the farther side of the gate. Bibot thought it his duty to go and see what the noise was about. Someone wanting to get into Paris instead of out of it at this hour of the night was a strange occurrence.

Bibot heard his name spoken by a raucous voice. Accompanied by two of his men he crossed the wide gates in order to see what was happening. One of the men held a lantern, which he was swinging high above his head. Bibot saw standing there before him, arguing with the guard by the gate, the bibulous spokesman of the band of carriers.

He was explaining to the sentry that he had a message to deliver to the citizen commanding at the Porte Montmartre.

===Chinese===

回想整个场面，一阵迷信的寒栗又窜遍他全身：那出城去的六个人的脸，他分明张张认得。魔鬼必定果真赋与了那神秘的英国人一种法力，能把他自己连同他那一伙，整个儿化进别人的躯壳里去。

一个多钟头过去了。比博已然完全恢复本色，吆五喝六、发号施令，如今是见人就扣。

这时，城门另一头似乎起了些许口角。比博觉得有责任过去看看吵嚷的缘由。夜里这个时辰竟有人想进巴黎而不是出巴黎，可谓咄咄怪事。

比博听见一条粗嘎的嗓门提到他的名字。他带着两名手下穿过宽阔的门洞，过去看个究竟。一个手下提着盏灯笼，高举过顶来回晃动。比博看见站在他面前、正与城门卫兵争辩的，正是那伙运输夫里那个好酒的发言人。

他正对哨兵分说，自己有信件要呈交蒙马特门的指挥官公民。

===Original===

“It is a note,” he said, “which an officer of the mounted guard gave me. He and twenty troopers were galloping down the great North Road not far from Barency. When they overtook the six of us they drew rein, and the officer gave me this note for citizen Bibot and fifty francs if I would deliver it tonight.”

“Give me the note!” said Bibot calmly.

But his hand shook as he took the paper; his face was livid with fear and rage.

The paper had no writing on it, only the outline of a small scarlet flower done in red﻿—the device of the cursed Englishman, the Scarlet Pimpernel.

“Which way did the officer and the twenty troopers go,” he stammered, “after they gave you this note?”

===Chinese===

“是一张字条，”他说，“一位骑兵队的军官交给我的。他带着二十名骑兵，正沿着往北去的大道策马疾驰，离巴朗西不远。他们赶上我们六个的时候勒住了马，那军官把这张字条托付给我，要送给比博公民，还给了五十法郎，条件是我今夜务必送到。”

“把字条给我！”比博平平静静地说。

可他伸手接纸时，手却在哆嗦；他的脸因恐惧和狂怒而涨成铁青。

纸上没有字，只有一朵小小红花的轮廓，拿红色勾成——那正是那可诅咒的英国人、红花侠的标记。

“那军官和那二十名骑兵，把这张字条交给你之后，往哪边去了？”他结结巴巴地问。

===Original===

“On the way to Calais,” replied the other, “but they had magnificent horses, and didn’t spare them either. They are a league and more away by now!”

All the blood in Bibot’s body seemed to rush up to his head, a wild buzzing was in his ears.﻿ ﻿…

And that was how the Duc and Duchesse de Montreux, with their servants and family, escaped from Paris on that third day of Nivose in the year I of the Republic.

===Chinese===

“往加来（Calais）那边去了，”对方答道，“不过他们骑的都是千里驹，一点儿也不爱惜。这会儿早已走出一里格（league）开外了！”

比博全身的血液仿佛都涌上了脑袋，耳朵里嗡的一声乱了营。……

蒙特勒公爵与公爵夫人，就这样带着仆人和家眷，于共和国元年雪月三日逃出了巴黎。

## How Jean Pierre Met the Scarlet Pimpernel / ## 让·皮埃尔如何遇见红花侠

===Original===



As told by Himself



===Chinese===



本人自述

### I / ### 一

===Original===



Ah, monsieur! the pity of it, the pity! Surely there are sins which *le bon Dieu* Himself will condone. And if not﻿—well, I had to risk His displeasure anyhow. Could I see them both starve, monsieur? I ask you! and M. le Vicomte had become so thin, so thin, his tiny, delicate bones were almost through his skin. And Mme. la Marquise! an angel, monsieur! Why, in the happy olden days, before all these traitors and assassins ruled in France, M. and Mme. la Marquise lived only for the child, and then to see him dying﻿—yes, dying, there was no shutting one’s eyes to that awful fact﻿—M. le Vicomte de Mortain was dying of starvation and of disease.

There we were all herded together in a couple of attics﻿—one of which little more than a cupboard﻿—at the top of a dilapidated half-ruined house in the Rue des Pipots﻿—Mme. la Marquise, M. le Vicomte and I﻿—just think of that, monsieur! M. le Marquis had his château, as no doubt you know, on the outskirts of Lyons. A loyal highborn gentleman; was it likely, I ask you, that he would submit passively to the rule of those execrable revolutionaries who had murdered their King, outraged their Queen and Royal family, and, God help them! had already perpetrated every crime and every abomination for which of a truth there could be no pardon either on earth or in Heaven? He joined that plucky but, alas! small and ill-equipped army of royalists who, unable to save their King, were at least determined to avenge him.

Well, you know well enough what happened. The counterrevolution failed; the revolutionary army brought Lyons down to her knees after a siege of two months. She was then marked down as a rebel city, and after the abominable decree of October 9th had deprived her of her very name, and Couthon had exacted bloody reprisals from the entire population for its loyalty to the King, the infamous Laporte was sent down in order finally to stamp out the lingering remnants of the rebellion. By that time, monsieur, half the city had been burned down, and one-tenth and more of the inhabitants﻿—men, women, and children﻿—had been massacred in cold blood, whilst most of the others had fled in terror from the appalling scene of ruin and desolation. Laporte completed the execrable work so ably begun by Couthon. He was a very celebrated and skilful doctor at the Faculty of Medicine, now turned into a human hyena in the name of Liberty and Fraternity.

M. le Marquis contrived to escape with the scattered remnant of the Royalist army into Switzerland. But Mme. la Marquise throughout all these strenuous times had stuck to her post at the château like the valiant creature that she was. When Couthon entered Lyons at the head of the revolutionary army, the whole of her household fled, and I was left alone to look after her and M. le Vicomte.



===Chinese===



啊，先生！这有多可怜，多可怜哪！我总以为，有些罪孽，连*le bon Dieu*（仁慈的上帝）亲口也会宽宥。就算他不宽宥吧——唉，我也只好甘冒他的不悦了。难道我能眼睁睁看着他们母子俩活活饿死吗，先生？您倒给评评理！子爵先生已经瘦了，瘦得皮包骨头，那细小娇嫩的骨架几乎要顶破皮肤了。还有侯爵夫人（Mme. la Marquise）！她是一位天使啊，先生！想当年太平岁月，这帮卖国贼和杀人凶手还没统治法国的时候，侯爵先生和侯爵夫人的心思全在这孩子身上；如今却要眼看着他死去——是的，死去，这可怕的事实谁也别想闭眼不认——德莫尔坦子爵先生（M. le Vicomte de Mortain）正在饥饿和疾病里一步步走向死亡。

我们就这样全都挤在两间阁楼里——其中一间比碗柜大不了多少——在皮波街（Rue des Pipots）一栋破败半塌的房子的顶上——侯爵夫人、子爵先生和我——您想想吧，先生！侯爵先生的城堡，想必您知道，坐落在里昂（Lyons）城外。他是位忠贞的高贵绅士；请问，难道您以为他会乖乖听命于那帮可憎的革命者的统治吗？他们谋杀了自己的国王，凌辱了王后和王室，而且，上帝饶恕他们吧！早已犯下了天上人间确实都无法赦免的一切罪行、一切丑行。他投奔了那支英勇然而——唉！——势单力薄、装备寒酸的保王军；这支军队救不了他们的国王，至少铁了心要为他复仇。

后来怎样，您知道得很清楚。反革命失败了；革命军围城两个月，逼得里昂屈膝。她随即被划为叛乱之城；10 月 9 日那道可憎的法令剥夺了她原本的名号，库东（Couthon）又以全城百姓效忠国王为罪名，索取了血腥的报偿，随后那个声名狼藉的拉波特（Laporte）被派了下来，要把叛乱最后一点余烬也彻底踏灭。到那时候，先生，半座城已烧成白地，城里十分之一以上的居民——男女老幼——惨遭冷血屠杀，其余的人也大多在惊恐中逃离了那片废墟与荒凉交织的骇人景象。拉波特把库东如此“得力”开创的可憎事业收了尾。他本是医学院一位大名鼎鼎、医术精湛的大夫，如今打着自由、博爱的旗号，变成了一头人形鬣狗。

侯爵先生随着保王军的残兵散卒，设法逃进了瑞士。可是在那些动荡艰险的日子里，侯爵夫人凭着她天生的刚勇，始终坚守在城堡的岗位上。库东率革命军开进里昂时，她合家的仆役都逃散了，只剩下我一个人照应她和子爵先生。

===Original===



Then one day when I had gone into Lyons for provisions, I suddenly chanced to hear outside an eating-house that which nearly froze the marrow in my old bones. A captain belonging to the Revolutionary Guard was transmitting to his sergeant certain orders, which he had apparently just received.

The orders were to make a perquisition at ten o’clock this same evening in the château of Mortaine as the Marquis was supposed to be in hiding there, and in any event to arrest every man, woman, and child who was found within its walls.

“Citizen Laporte,” the captain concluded, “knows for a certainty that the ci-devant Marquise and her brat are still there, even if the Marquis has fled like the traitor that he is. Those cursed English spies who call themselves the League of the Scarlet Pimpernel have been very active in Lyons of late, and citizen Laporte is afraid that they might cheat the guillotine of the carcase of those aristos, as they have already succeeded in doing in the case of a large number of traitors.”

I did not, of course, wait to hear any more of that abominable talk. I sped home as fast as my old legs would carry me. That selfsame evening, as soon as it was dark, Mme. la Marquise, carrying M. le Vicomte in her arms and I carrying a pack with a few necessaries on my back, left the ancestral home of the Mortaines never to return to it again: for within an hour of our flight a detachment of the revolutionary army made a descent upon the château; they ransacked it from attic to cellar, and finding nothing there to satisfy their lust of hate, they burned the stately mansion down to the ground.

We were obliged to take refuge in Lyons, at any rate for a time. Great as was the danger inside the city, it was infinitely greater on the high roads, unless we could arrange for some vehicle to take us a considerable part of the way to the frontier, and above all for some sort of passports﻿—forged or otherwise﻿—to enable us to pass the various tollgates on the road, where vigilance was very strict. So we wandered through the ruined and deserted streets of the city in search of shelter, but found every charred and derelict house full of miserable tramps and destitutes like ourselves. Half dead with fatigue, Mme. la Marquise was at last obliged to take refuge in one of these houses which was situated in the Rue des Pipots. Every room was full to overflowing with a miserable wreckage of humanity thrown hither by the tide of anarchy and of bloodshed. But at the top of the house we found an attic. It was empty save for a couple of chairs, a table and a broken-down bedstead on which were a ragged mattress and pillow.



===Chinese===



后来有一天，我进城去筹办吃食，恰巧在一家饭馆门外，冷不防听见了一件事，险些把我这把老骨头的骨髓都冻住了。一名革命卫队的上尉正在向他手下的中士传达几道命令，那些命令显然是他刚刚接到的。

命令是当晚十点钟查抄莫尔坦（Mortaine）城堡，因为据料想侯爵就藏匿在那里；而且无论如何，凡在城堡里查见的男女老幼，一概逮捕。

“拉波特公民，”上尉最后说道，“确知那位旧日侯爵夫人和她那崽子还在里头，就算侯爵已经像他那种叛徒的本性一样溜之大吉。那些自称红花侠同盟（the League of the Scarlet Pimpernel）的该死的英国奸细，近来在里昂活动得很厉害，拉波特公民担心，他们会骗过断头台，把那两个贵族佬的性命夺走，就像他们先前对一大批叛徒已经得手的那样。”

这等可憎的话，我自然没有再听下去。我拼着两条老腿能跑多快是多快，飞奔回家。就在当天晚上，天一擦黑，侯爵夫人怀里抱着子爵先生，我背上背一个小包，装着几件必用的东西，我们就这样离开了莫尔坦家的祖宅，从此再没有回去：因为我们出逃还不到一个钟头，一支革命军小队就扑进了城堡；他们从阁楼一路搜到地窖，什么也没搜着，无以消解他们的仇恨，便把那座庄严的府邸烧成了平地。

我们不得不在里昂城里暂且栖身。城里的危险固然大，大路上的危险却更要大上十倍，除非我们能弄到一辆车，送我们走完去边境的一大段路，尤其要弄到几份通行证——伪造的也罢，别的也罢——好让我们通过沿途一道道关卡，那里的盘查严得很。于是我们在城里那些烧毁荒凉的街道间辗转寻个落脚处，可每一栋烧焦的没人要的房子里，都塞满了同我们一样的可怜流民和难民。侯爵夫人累得半死，最后只好将就躲进其中一栋，就在皮波街上。每个房间都挤满了被无政府与流血的潮水冲到这里来的人间残骸。不过在房子顶层我们找到了一间阁楼，里头除了两把椅子、一张桌子和一张破床架，空无一物，床上有一床破褥子和一只枕头。

===Original===



Here, monsieur, we spent over three weeks, at the end of which time M. le Vicomte fell ill, and then there followed days, monsieur, through which I would not like my worst enemy to pass.

Mme. la Marquise had only been able to carry away in her flight what ready money she happened to have in the house at the time. Securities, property, money belonging to aristocrats had been ruthlessly confiscated by the revolutionary government in Lyons. Our scanty resources rapidly became exhausted, and what was left had to be kept for milk and delicacies for M. le Vicomte. I tramped through the streets in search of a doctor, but most of them had been arrested on some paltry charge or other of rebellion, whilst others had fled from the city. There was only that infamous Laporte﻿—a vastly clever doctor, I knew﻿—but as soon take a lamb to a hungry lion as the Vicomte de Mortaine to that bloodthirsty cutthroat.

Then one day our last franc went and we had nothing left. Mme. la Marquise had not touched food for two days. I had stood at the corner of the street, begging all the day until I was driven off by the gendarmes. I had only obtained three sous from the passersby. I bought some milk and took it home for M. le Vicomte. The following morning when I entered the larger attic I found that Mme. la Marquise had fainted from inanition.

I spent the whole of the day begging in the streets and dodging the guard, and even so I only collected four sous. I could have got more perhaps, only that at about midday the smell of food from an eating-house turned me sick and faint, and when I regained consciousness I found myself huddled up under a doorway and evening gathering in fast around me. If Mme. la Marquise could go two days without food I ought to go four. I struggled to my feet; fortunately I had retained possession of my four sous, else of a truth I would not have had the courage to go back to the miserable attic which was the only home I knew.



===Chinese===



就在这里，先生，我们住了三个多星期，到末了子爵先生病倒了，接着便是那样的一些日子，先生，就是叫我最恨的仇人去熬，我也不忍心。

侯爵夫人仓皇出逃时，只带得及当时恰好在家里的那点现钱。证券、产业、凡是贵族的钱财，早叫里昂的革命政府狠心抄没。我们那点微薄的家底很快见了底，剩下的一星半点，还得留着给子爵先生买牛奶和滋补的吃食。我满街奔走寻医问药，可大夫们多半已因这个那个鸡毛蒜皮的叛乱罪名被抓了，其余的也逃出了城。城里只剩那个声名狼藉的拉波特——我知道他医术高明得很——可是把德莫尔坦子爵交到那个嗜血的屠夫手里，还不如把羔羊送到饿狮嘴边。

后来有一天，我们最后一个法郎也花光了，什么都没有了。侯爵夫人已经两天水米未进。我在街角站了一整天讨钱，直到被宪兵赶开，从过路人手里统共只讨到三个苏（sou）。我买了点牛奶，带回家给子爵先生。第二天早晨，我走进大间阁楼，发现侯爵夫人已经饿得昏死过去。

我又在街上讨了一整天的钱，还得躲着巡逻队，饶是这样，也只讨到四个苏。本来或许还能多讨些，只是晌午时分，一家饭馆里飘出的饭菜香熏得我一阵恶心发晕，等我醒过神来，发现自己蜷在一处门洞底下，夜色已经四合。侯爵夫人能两天不吃东西，我就该撑过四天。我挣扎着爬起来；幸好那四个苏还在手里，不然说真的，我怕是鼓不起勇气回那间破阁楼去了——那是我在这世上唯一的家。

===Original===



I was wending my way along as fast as I could﻿—for I knew that Mme. la Marquise would be getting terribly anxious﻿—when, just as I turned into the Rue Blanche, I spied two gentlemen﻿—obviously strangers, for they were dressed with a luxury and care with which we had long ceased to be familiar in Lyons﻿—walking rapidly towards me. A moment or two later they came to a halt, not far from where I was standing, and I heard the taller one of the two say to the other in English﻿—a language with which I am vaguely conversant: “All right again this time, what, Tony?”

Both laughed merrily like a couple of schoolboys playing truant, and then they disappeared under the doorway of a dilapidated house, whilst I was left wondering how two such elegant gentlemen dared be abroad in Lyons these days, seeing that every man, woman and child who was dressed in anything but threadbare clothes was sure to be insulted in the streets for an aristocrat, and as often as not summarily arrested as a traitor.
