However, I had other things to think about, and had already dismissed the little incident from my mind, when at the bottom of the Rue Blanche I came upon a knot of gaffers, men and women, who were talking and gesticulating very excitedly outside the door of a cook-shop. At first I did not take much notice of what was said: my eyes were glued to the front of the shop, on which were displayed sundry delicacies of the kind which makes a wretched, starved beggar’s mouth water as he goes by; a roast capon especially attracted my attention, together with a bottle of red wine; these looked just the sort of luscious food which Mme. la Marquise would relish.

Well, sir, the law of God says: “Thou shalt not covet!” and no doubt that I committed a grievous sin when my hungry eyes fastened upon that roast capon and that bottle of Burgundy. We also know the stories of Judas Iscariot and of Jacob’s children who sold their own brother Joseph into slavery﻿—such a crime, monsieur, I took upon my conscience then; for just as the vision of Mme. la Marquise eating that roast capon and drinking that Burgundy rose before my eyes, my ears caught some fragments of the excited conversation which was going on all around me.



===Chinese===



我一路只顾尽快往前赶——因为我知道侯爵夫人准要急坏了——刚拐进布朗什街（Rue Blanche），就望见两位先生——一望便知是外乡人，因为他们衣着的奢华讲究，是我们里昂人久已见不惯的——正快步朝我走来。过了一两分钟，他们在离我站处不远的地方停住脚，我听见两人中个子高的那个用英语对另一个说——这门语言我还约略懂得几句：“这一回又得手了，是不是，托尼（Tony）？”

两人像一对逃学的学童似的开怀大笑，随后便隐进一栋破房子的大门里去了，剩下我一个人犯嘀咕：这样两位衣冠楚楚的先生，如今怎敢在里昂街头抛头露面？要知道，凡是穿得不像叫花子的，不分男女老幼，在街上准会被当贵族辱骂，十有八九还要就地当作叛徒抓起来。

不过我另有心事，早把这段小插曲丢开了；不料走到布朗什街尽头，却撞见一小群闲人，男男女女，正围着一家熟食店门口，激动万分地嚷着、比划着。起初我没大留意他们说些什么：我的两眼直勾勾粘在店门口，那里摆着各色好吃的，恰恰是能让一个饿得发慌的倒霉叫花子走过时直咽口水的东西；一只烤阉鸡尤其惹我的眼，旁边还有一瓶红葡萄酒；这看着正是侯爵夫人会受用的鲜美吃食。

唉，先生，上帝的诫命说：“不可贪恋！”毫无疑问，当我饥饿的两眼死死盯住那只烤阉鸡和那瓶勃艮第酒的时候，我已经犯下了重罪。我们也都知道加略人犹大的故事，知道雅各的众子把亲兄弟约瑟卖为奴隶的故事——先生，我当时往自己良心上压的，正是这样一桩罪孽；因为正当侯爵夫人吃着烤阉鸡、喝着勃艮第酒的幻象浮现在我眼前时，我的耳朵零零碎碎听到了围在我四周那场激愤议论的只言片语。

===Original===



“He went this way!” someone said.

“No; that!” protested another.

“There’s no sign of him now, anyway.”

The owner of the shop was standing on his own doorstep, his legs wide apart, one arm on his wide hip, the other still brandishing the knife wherewith he had been carving for his customers.

“He can’t have gone far,” he said, as he smacked his thick lips.

“The impudent rascal, flaunting such fine clothes﻿—like the aristo that he is.”



===Chinese===



“他往这边跑了！”有人说。

“不对，往那边！”另一个表示异议。

“反正这会儿连他的影子也不见了。”

店老板站在自家门阶上，两腿叉得开开的，一只手叉着宽腰，另一只手里还挥着那把方才给顾客切肉的刀。

“他跑不远，”他说着，咂了咂厚嘴唇。

“好个不知羞的恶棍，穿得这样招摇——十足一副贵族做派。”

===Original===



“Bah! these cursed English! They are aristos all of them! And this one with his followers is no better than a spy!”

“Paid by that damned English Government to murder all our patriots and to rob the guillotine of her just dues.”

“They say he had a hand in the escape of the ci-devant Duc de Sermeuse and all his brats from the very tumbril which was taking them to execution.”

A cry of loathing and execration followed this statement. There was vigorous shaking of clenched fists and then a groan of baffled rage.

“We almost had him this time. If it had not been for these confounded, ill-lighted streets﻿—”

“I would give something,” concluded the shopkeeper, “if we could lay him by the heels.”



===Chinese===



“呸！这些该死的英国佬！他们个个都是贵族佬！这一个带着一伙党羽，同奸细也差不了多少！”

“领着那挨千刀的英国政府的赏钱，谋害我们所有的爱国志士，抢断头台应得的进项。”

“人家说，旧日的塞尔默兹公爵（Duc de Sermeuse）带着他的一窝崽子，就是他插手从押赴刑场的死囚车上劫走的。”

这话一出口，四下里随即响起一片憎恶咒骂之声。人们狠狠挥舞着攥紧的拳头，接着又是一阵徒劳无功的恼恨哀嚎。

“这回差一点儿就逮住他了。要不是这些该死的、黑灯瞎火的街——”

“要是能把他擒住，”店老板最后说，“我情愿出份彩头。”

===Original===



“What would you give, citizen Dompierre?” queried a woman in the crowd, with a ribald laugh, “one of your roast capons?”

“Aye, little mother,” he replied jovially, “and a bottle of my best Burgundy to boot, to drink confusion to that meddlesome Englishman and his crowd and a speedy promenade up the steps of the guillotine.”

Monsieur, I assure you that at that moment my heart absolutely stood still. The tempter stood at my elbow and whispered, and I deliberately smothered the call of my conscience. I did what Joseph’s brethren did, what brought Judas Iscariot to hopeless remorse. There was no doubt that the hue and cry was after the two elegantly dressed gentlemen whom I had seen enter the dilapidated house in the Rue Blanche. For a second or two I closed my eyes and deliberately conjured up the vision of Mme. la Marquise fainting for lack of food, and of M. le Vicomte dying for want of sustenance; then I worked my way to the door of the shop and accosted the burly proprietor with as much boldness as I could muster.

“The two Englishmen passed by me at the top of the Rue Blanche,” I said to him. “They went into a house﻿ ﻿… I can show you which it is﻿—”

In a moment I was surrounded by a screeching, gesticulating crowd. I told my story as best I could; there was no turning back now from the path of cowardice and of crime. I saw that brute Dompierre pick up the largest roast capon from the front of his shop, together with a bottle of that wine which I had coveted; then he thrust both these treasures into my trembling hands and said:



===Chinese===



“你情愿出什么彩头呢，东皮埃尔（Dompierre）公民？”人群里一个女人挤眉弄眼地笑着问，“出你一只烤阉鸡么？”

“着啊，小妈妈，”他乐乐呵呵地答道，“再饶上一瓶我顶好的勃艮第，祝那爱管闲事的英国佬和他那伙人倒霉透顶，早早踏上断头台的台阶遛一遭。”

先生，我向您担保，那一刻我的心简直停止了跳动。魔鬼就立在我胳膊肘边低声诱哄，而我存心捂死了良心的呼声。我做了约瑟的哥哥们做过的事，做了那桩把加略人犹大逼到绝望悔恨的事。毫无疑问，这一片鼓噪捉拿的，正是我方才亲眼看见走进布朗什街那栋破房子的两位衣冠楚楚的先生。我闭上眼呆了一两秒钟，存心让侯爵夫人饿得昏厥、子爵先生因断粮而垂死的景象浮现在眼前；随后我挤到店门口，鼓起浑身胆量，向那膀大腰圆的店老板开了口。

“那两个英国人刚在布朗什街街口打我跟前过，”我对他说，“他们进了一户人家……我可以指给你们是哪一家——”

转眼间我就被一群尖声喊叫、指手画脚的人围在了当中。我把事情结结巴巴说了一遍；如今这条懦弱与犯罪的路，是再没有回头的机会了。我看见那个蠢货东皮埃尔从店门口抄起最大的一只烤阉鸡，又拿了一瓶我看上了许久的那种酒；随后他把这两件宝贝一股脑塞进我发抖的双手里，说道：

===Original===



“En avant!”

And we all started to run up the street, shouting: “Death to the English spies!” I was the hero of the expedition. Dompierre and another man carried me, for I was too weak to go as fast as they wished. I was hugging the capon and the bottle of wine to my heart; I had need to do that, so as to still the insistent call of my conscience, for I felt a coward﻿—a mean, treacherous, abominable coward!

When we reached the house and I pointed it out to Dompierre, the crowd behind us gave a cry of triumph. In the topmost storey a window was thrown open, two heads appeared silhouetted against the light within, and the cry of triumph below was answered by a merry, prolonged laugh from above.

I was too dazed to realise very clearly what happened after that. Dompierre, I know, kicked open the door of the house, and the crowd rushed in, in his wake. I managed to keep my feet and to work my way gradually out of the crowd. I must have gone on mechanically, almost unconsciously, for the next thing that I remember with any distinctness was that I found myself once more speeding down the Rue Blanche, with all the yelling and shouting some little way behind me.

With blind instinct, too, I had clung to the capon and the wine, the price of my infamy. I was terribly weak and felt sick and faint, but I struggled on for a while, until my knees refused me service and I came down on my two hands, whilst the capon rolled away into the gutter, and the bottle of Burgundy fell with a crash against the pavement, scattering its precious contents in every direction.



===Chinese===



“En avant!（前进）”

于是我们全体顺着街往上跑，一路喊：“英国奸细该死！”我是这趟出征的好汉英雄。东皮埃尔和另一个人架着我，因为我身子太虚，跑不到他们指望的那么快。我把阉鸡和酒瓶紧紧搂在心口；我必须这样搂着，好压住良心那一声声不依不饶的呼喊，因为我自觉是个懦夫——一个卑劣、奸诈、可鄙的懦夫！

等我们赶到那栋房子跟前，我把它指给东皮埃尔看，身后的人群爆出一声得胜的欢呼。顶层一扇窗推开了，两颗人影映着屋里的灯光探了出来，楼下一片欢呼，楼上却回敬了一阵悠长而快活的笑声。

我晕头转向，后来发生的事已记不真切。我只知道东皮埃尔一脚踹开了那栋房子的门，人群跟在他身后蜂拥而入。我总算站住了脚，慢慢从人堆里挤出来。再往后我准是机械地、几乎失去知觉地走着，因为我下一件记得清楚的事，是发现自己又顺着布朗什街飞奔而下，那一片喊杀声落在我身后不远的地方。

我凭着一股盲目的本能，也还死死抱着那只阉鸡和那瓶酒——我出卖人格换来的代价。我虚弱得厉害，一阵阵恶心发晕，可还是挣扎着往前挪了一程，直到两个膝盖再也不听使唤，我双手撑地扑倒下去；阉鸡骨碌碌滚进了阴沟，那瓶勃艮第哗啦一声磕在石板路上，把珍贵的酒浆洒得四下里都是。

===Original===



There I lay, wretched, despairing, hardly able to move, when suddenly I heard rapid and firm footsteps immediately behind me, and the next moment two firm hands had me under the arms, and I heard a voice saying:

“Steady, old friend. Can you get up? There! Is that better?”

The same firm hands raised me to my feet. At first I was too dazed to see anything, but after a moment or two I was able to look around me, and, by the light of a street lantern immediately overhead, I recognised the tall, elegantly dressed Englishman and his friend, whom I had just betrayed to the fury of Dompierre and a savage mob.

I thought that I was dreaming, and I suppose that my eyes betrayed the horror which I felt, for the stranger looked at me scrutinisingly for a moment or two, then he gave the quaintest laugh I had ever heard in all my life, and said something to his friend in English, which this time I failed to understand.

Then he turned to me:



===Chinese===



我趴在那里，凄凄惶惶，绝望已极，几乎动弹不得，忽然听得身后紧跟着响起一串急促而沉稳的脚步声，紧接着一双坚实的手已经架住我的两腋，一个声音说道：

“撑住，老朋友。能站起来吗？来！这样好些么？”

还是那双坚实的手把我搀了起来。起初我晕得什么也看不见，过了一两分钟才能环顾四周；借着头顶正上方一盏街灯的光，我认出了那位高个子、衣着讲究的英国人和他的朋友——方才我把他们出卖给了东皮埃尔的怒火和一群凶暴的乱民。

我只当自己是在做梦；想必我眼里的惊怖露了形迹，因为那陌生人把我细细端详了一两分钟，随即发出我平生听过的最古怪的一阵笑，用英语对他的朋友说了句什么——这一回我没有听懂。

然后他转向我：

===Original===



“By my faith,” he said in perfect French﻿—so that I began to doubt if he was an English spy after all﻿—“I verily believe that you are the clever rogue, eh? who obtained a roast capon and a bottle of wine from that fool Dompierre. He and his boon companions are venting their wrath on you, old compeer; they are calling you liar and traitor and cheat, in the intervals of wrecking what is left of the house, out of which my friend and I have long since escaped by climbing up the neighbouring gutter-pipes and scrambling over the adjoining roofs.”

Monsieur, will you believe me when I say that he was actually saying all this in order to comfort me? I could have sworn to that because of the wonderful kindliness which shone out of his eyes, even through the good-humoured mockery wherewith he obviously regarded me. Do you know what I did then, monsieur? I just fell on my knees and loudly thanked God that he was safe; at which both he and his friend once again began to laugh, for all the world like two schoolboys who had escaped a whipping, rather than two men who were still threatened with death.

“Then it *was* you!” said the taller stranger, who was still laughing so heartily that he had to wipe his eyes with his exquisite lace handkerchief.

“May God forgive me,” I replied.

The next moment his arm was again round me. I clung to him as to a rock, for of a truth I had never felt a grasp so steady and withal so gentle and kindly, as was his around my shoulders. I tried to murmur words of thanks, but again that wretched feeling of sickness and faintness overcame me, and for a second or two it seemed to me as if I were slipping into another world. The stranger’s voice came to my ear, as it were through cotton-wool.



===Chinese===



“我以信义担保，”他用一口地道漂亮的法语说道——倒叫我不由得怀疑，他到底算不算英国奸细——“我真真相信，你就是那个乖巧的滑头，嗯？从那个蠢货东皮埃尔手里弄到了一只烤阉鸡和一瓶酒。他和他那伙酒肉朋友正拿你出气呢，老伙计；他们一边砸那栋房子里剩下的家什，一边骂你撒谎、卖友、坑人——其实我和我朋友早从那房子里溜了，爬上旁边的雨水管，翻过隔壁的屋顶走了。”

先生，我说他讲这一番话，完完全全是为了宽慰我，您肯信么？这一点我可以起誓，因为他眼睛里闪耀着那么动人的慈和，纵然那目光分明带着对我的善意打趣。您可知道我当时做了什么，先生？我扑通跪倒，高声感谢上帝保佑他平安无事；他和他的朋友见了，又放声大笑起来，那模样活脱是两个逃过了鞭子的学童，哪里像两个还被死亡追索着的人。

“这么说，方才*果然*是你！”高个子陌生人说，他还笑得那样畅快，不得不掏出那方精致的蕾丝手帕来拭眼睛。

“愿上帝饶恕我，”我答道。

转眼间他的手臂又揽住了我。我依着他，就像依着一块磐石，因为说真的，我这辈子从不曾领受这样的搀扶——这般沉稳，又这般温柔和善——搭在我肩头。我想低声道谢，可那阵难受的恶心眩晕又涌了上来，有一两秒钟，我恍惚觉得自己正滑进另一个世界。陌生人的声音传到我耳边，仿佛隔着一层棉絮。

===Original===



“The man is starving,” he said. “Shall we take him over to your lodgings, Tony? They are safer than mine. He may be able to walk in a minute or two, if not I can carry him.”

My senses at this partly returned to me, and I was able to protest feebly:

“No, no! I must go back﻿—I must﻿—kind sirs,” I murmured. “Mme. la Marquise will be getting so anxious.”

No sooner were these foolish words out of my mouth than I could have bitten my tongue out for having uttered them; and yet, somehow, it seemed as if it was the stranger’s magnetic personality, his magic voice and kindly act towards me, who had so basely sold him to his enemies, which had drawn them out of me. He gave a low, prolonged whistle.

“Mme. la Marquise?” he queried, dropping his voice to a whisper.



===Chinese===



“这人快饿坏了，”他说，“把他带到你的住处去好不好，托尼？你那儿比我的稳当。过一两分钟他兴许能走，走不动我就背他。”

这话使我神志恢复了几分，我还能有气无力地推辞：

“不，不！我得回去——我一定得回去——好心的先生们，”我喃喃道，“侯爵夫人要急坏了。”

这些蠢话一出口，我就恨不能把自己的舌头咬下来；可是不知怎么，仿佛正是这陌生人那股令人倾倒的人格力量，他那有魔力的嗓音，还有他对我——一个如此卑鄙地把他卖给敌人的人——的和善举动，把这些话从我心底引了出来。他低低地、长长地吹了一声呼哨。

“侯爵夫人？”他问，声音低成了耳语。

===Original===



Now to have uttered Mme. la Marquise de Mortaine’s name here in Lyons, where every aristocrat was termed a traitor and sent without trial to the guillotine, was in itself an act of criminal folly, and yet﻿—you may believe me, monsieur, or not﻿—there was something within me just at that moment that literally compelled me to open my heart out to this stranger, whom I had so basely betrayed, and who requited my abominable crime with such gentleness and mercy. Before I fully realised what I was doing, monsieur, I had blurted out the whole history of Mme. la Marquise’s flight and of M. le Vicomte’s sickness to him. He drew me under the cover of an open doorway, and he and his friend listened to me without speaking a word until I had told them my pitiable tale to the end.

When I had finished he said quietly:

“Take me to see Mme. la Marquise, old friend. Who knows? perhaps I may be able to help.”

Then he turned to his friend.

“Will you wait for me at my lodgings, Tony,” he said, “and let Ffoulkes and Hastings know that I may wish to speak with them on my return?”



===Chinese===



要知道，在这里昂，凡贵族一律被称作叛徒，不经审判就送上断头台，单单说出德莫尔坦侯爵夫人的名姓，本身就是一桩疯狂的罪过；然而——先生，您信也罢，不信也罢——就在那一刻，我心里有一样东西，简直是逼着我向这陌生人敞开心扉；我那样卑鄙地出卖了他，他却以这般温柔、这般仁慈回报我的滔天罪愆。不等我自己明白过来在做什么，先生，我已把侯爵夫人出逃、子爵先生害病的始末根由，一股脑儿倒给了他。他把我拉到一处敞开的门洞底下，同他的朋友一言不发地听我讲，直到我把这段凄惨事一五一十说到了头。

我讲完了，他平静地说：

“带我去见见侯爵夫人吧，老朋友。谁知到呢？兴许我能帮上点忙。”

随后他转向他的朋友。

“托尼，你回我的住处去等我，”他说，“再知会福克斯（Ffoulkes）和黑斯廷斯（Hastings）一声，就说我回来可能有话要同他们讲。”

===Original===



He spoke like one who had been accustomed all his life to give command, and I marvelled how his friend immediately obeyed him. Then when the latter had disappeared down the dark street, the stranger once more turned to me.

“Lean on my arm, good old friend,” he said, “and we must try and walk as quickly as we can. The sooner we allay the anxieties of Mme. la Marquise the better.”

I was still hugging the roast capon with one arm, with the other I clung to him as together we walked in the direction of the Rue des Pipots. On the way we halted at a respectable eating-house, where my protector gave me some money wherewith to buy a bottle of good wine and sundry provisions and delicacies which we carried home with us.



===Chinese===



他说话的口气，就像一个生来发号施令惯了的人；他的朋友立刻听从了他，这叫我暗暗称奇。等那位的身影消失在黑洞洞的街上，陌生人又转向我。

“扶着我的胳膊，好老朋友，”他说，“咱们得尽量走快些。越早解开侯爵夫人的焦心越好。”

我一只胳膊还搂着那只烤阉鸡，另一只挽着他，两人一路朝皮波街的方向走去。半路上我们在一家体面的饭馆前停了停，我的保护人给了我一笔钱，买了一瓶好酒，又置办了各色吃食点心，一并带了回家。

### II / ### 二

===Original===



Never shall I forget the look of horror which came in Mme. la Marquise’s eyes when she saw me entering our miserable attic in the company of a stranger. The last of the little bit of tallow candle flickered in its socket. Madame threw her emaciated arms over her child, just like some poor hunted animal defending its young. I could almost hear the cry of terror which died down in her throat ere it reached her lips. But then, monsieur, to see the light of hope gradually illuminating her pale, wan face as the stranger took her hand and spoke to her﻿—oh! so gently and so kindly﻿—was a sight which filled my poor, half-broken heart with joy.

“The little invalid must be seen by a doctor at once,” he said, “after that only can we think of your ultimate safety.”

Mme. la Marquise, who herself was terribly weak and ill, burst out crying. “Would I not have taken him to a doctor ere now?” she murmured through her tears. “But there is no doctor in Lyons. Those who have not been arrested as traitors have fled from this stricken city. And my little Jose is dying for want of medical care.”

“Your pardon, madame,” he rejoined gently, “one of the ablest doctors in France is at present in Lyons﻿—”

“That infamous Laporte,” she broke in, horrified. “He would snatch my sick child from my arms and throw him to the guillotine.”



===Chinese===



侯爵夫人见我领着一个陌生人走进我们那间破阁楼，她眼中登时露出那种惊怖，我一辈子也忘不了。最后一小截牛油蜡烛在烛窝里摇曳。夫人把瘦削的双臂护在孩子身上，活像一头被追猎的可怜母兽护着崽子。那声恐怖的叫喊在她喉咙里就咽了下去，没等走到嘴边，可我几乎听得见。然而，先生，当陌生人握起她的手对她说话时——啊，说得那样温柔、那样和善——眼看希望的光渐渐照亮她苍白憔悴的脸，那一幕，真叫我这不争气的、几乎碎了的心盛满了欢喜。

“这小病人必须立刻请大夫看，”他说，“之后才谈得上你们的最终安危。”

侯爵夫人自己已虚弱病痛到极点，闻言放声哭了起来。“我何尝不想早些带他去看大夫？”她含泪低语，“可是里昂没有大夫了。没被当叛徒抓走的，也都逃出了这座遭劫的城。我的小若泽（Jose）正因缺医少药在咽气呀。”

“恕我直言，夫人，”他温和地应道，“眼下里昂就有全法国最有本事的大夫之一——”

“那个声名狼藉的拉波特，”她惊恐地打断道，“他会从我怀里夺走我病着的孩子，把他扔上断头台。”

===Original===



“He would save your boy from disease,” said the stranger earnestly, “his own professional pride or professional honour, whatever he might choose to call it, would compel him to do that. But the moment the doctor’s work was done, that of the executioner would commence.”

“You see, milor,” moaned Madame in pitiable agony, “that there is no hope for us.”

“Indeed there is,” he replied. “We must get M. le Vicomte well first﻿—after that we shall see.”

“But you are not proposing to bring that infamous Laporte to my child’s bedside!” she cried in horror.

“Would you have your child die here before your eyes,” retorted the stranger, “as he undoubtedly will this night?”

This sounded horribly cruel, and the tone in which it was said was commanding. There was no denying its truth. M. le Vicomte was dying. I could see that. For a moment or two madame remained quite still, with her great eyes, circled with pain and sorrow, fixed upon the stranger. He returned her gaze steadily and kindly, and gradually that frozen look of horror in her pale face gave place to one of deep puzzlement, and through her bloodless lips there came the words, faintly murmured: “Who are you?”



===Chinese===



“他会治好您孩子的病，”陌生人恳切地说，“他那份职业的自尊，或是职业的体面——随他自己管它叫什么——都会逼着他这么做。可是大夫的活儿一完，刽子手的活儿就开场了。”

“您瞧，milor（爵爷），”夫人悲悲惨惨地呻吟道，“我们是没有指望的了。”

“不，有指望，”他答道，“咱们得先把子爵先生治好——往后的事，再作道理。”

“可您总不至于要把那个声名狼藉的拉波特弄到我孩子的床前来吧！”她惊恐地喊道。

“难道您情愿看着孩子死在您眼前？”陌生人反诘道，“不请大夫，他今夜断然是熬不过去的。”

这话听来残酷骇人，说这话的口气却不容分说。其中的道理也无从否认。子爵先生正在死去。这我看得出来。夫人有一两分钟纹丝不动，一双被痛苦和忧愁围出黑晕的大眼睛，定定地望着陌生人。他沉静而和善地回望着她；渐渐地，她那张苍白脸上吓僵了的惊恐，化作了一种深深的惶惑，透过毫无血色的嘴唇，轻轻吐出了几个字：“您是谁？”

===Original===



He gave no direct reply, but from his little finger he detached a ring and held it out for her to see. I saw it too, for I was standing close by Mme. la Marquise, and the flickering light of the tallow candle fell full upon the ring. It was of gold, and upon it there was an exquisitely modelled, five-petalled little flower in vivid red enamel.

Madame la Marquise looked at the ring, then once again up into his face. He nodded assent, and my heart seemed even then to stop its beating as I gazed upon his face. Had we not﻿—all of us﻿—heard of the gallant Scarlet Pimpernel? And did I not know﻿—far better than Mme. la Marquise herself﻿—the full extent of his gallantry and his self-sacrifice? The hue and cry was after him. Human bloodhounds were even now on his track, and he spoke calmly of walking out again in the streets of Lyons and of affronting that infamous Laporte, who would find glory in sending him to death. I think he guessed what was passing in my mind, for he put a finger up to his lip and pointed significantly to M. le Vicomte.

But it was beautiful to see how completely Mme. la Marquise now trusted him. At his bidding she even ate a little of the food and drank some wine﻿—and I was forced to do likewise. And even when anon he declared his intention of fetching Laporte immediately, she did not flinch. She kissed M. le Vicomte with passionate fervour, and then gave the stranger her solemn promise that the moment he returned she would take refuge in the next room and never move out of it until after Laporte had departed.

When he went I followed him to the top of the stairs. I was speechless with gratitude and also with fears for him. But he took my hand and said, with that same quaint, somewhat inane laugh which was so characteristic of him:

“Be of good cheer, old fellow! Those confounded murderers will not get me this time.”



===Chinese===



他没有直接作答，只从小指上褪下一枚戒指，伸出去给她看。我也看见了，因为我正站在侯爵夫人身旁，摇曳的烛光正正落在那枚戒指上。那是枚金戒指，上面有一朵精工细雕的小花，五片花瓣，鲜红的珐琅质地。

侯爵夫人看看戒指，又抬眼望进他的脸。他点头示意；我望着他的脸，那一刻我的心仿佛当真停止了跳动。我们大家——我们当中哪一个人——没有听说过那位英勇的红花侠（the Scarlet Pimpernel）？他的侠肝义胆，他的舍己为人，我知道得何等透彻——远比侯爵夫人本人透彻！通缉的号令正在缉拿他。人形的猎犬此刻就在追踪他，他却平平静静地说着要再走上里昂街头，去会一会那个以送他赴死为荣耀的、声名狼藉的拉波特。我想他猜着了我心里翻腾的念头，因为他竖起一根手指抵在唇上，又意味深长地指了指子爵先生。

可是看着侯爵夫人如今对他信得这样十足，真叫人心里暖暖的。在他劝说之下，她居然吃了一点东西，喝了些酒——我也只得照样吃喝。甚至过了一会儿，当他明白说出要立刻去把拉波特请来时，她也没有退缩。她满怀炽热地亲了亲子爵先生，然后向陌生人郑重许诺：他一回来，她便躲进隔壁那间屋，拉波特不走，她决不出来。

他走的时候，我一直送他到楼梯口。我感激得说不出话，又为他害怕得说不出话。他却握住我的手，带着他那种特有的古怪而近乎傻气的笑，说道：

“打起精神来，老伙计！那帮该死的杀人凶手，这回可逮不着我。”

### III / ### 三

===Original===



Less than half an hour later, monsieur, citizen Laporte, one of the most skilful doctors in France and one of the most bloodthirsty tyrants this execrable Revolution has known, was sitting at the bedside of M. le Vicomte de Mortaine, using all the skill, all the knowledge he possessed in order to combat the dread disease of which the child was dying, ere he came to save him﻿—as he cynically remarked in my hearing﻿—for the guillotine.

I heard afterwards how it all came about.

Laporte, it seems, was in the habit of seeing patients in his own house every evening after he had settled all his business for the day. What a strange contradiction in the human heart, eh, monsieur? The tiger turned lamb for the space of one hour in every twenty-four﻿—the butcher turned healer. How well the English milor had gauged the strange personality of that redoubtable man! Professional pride﻿—interest in intricate cases﻿—call it what you will﻿—was the only redeeming feature in Laporte’s abominable character. Everything else in him, every thought, every action was ignoble, cruel and vengeful.

Milor that night mingled with the crowd who waited on the human hyena to be cured of their hurts. It was a motley crowd that filled the dreaded proconsul’s antechamber﻿—men, women and children﻿—all of them too much preoccupied with their own troubles to bestow more than a cursory glance on the stranger who, wrapped in a dark mantle, quietly awaited his turn. One or two muttered curses were flung at the aristo, one or two spat in his direction to express hatred and contempt, then the door which gave on the inner chamber would be flung open﻿—a number called﻿—one patient would walk out, another walk in﻿—and in the ever-recurring incident the stranger for the nonce was forgotten.



===Chinese===



不到半个钟头之后，先生，拉波特公民——全法国医术最精湛的大夫之一，也是这场可憎的革命所造出的最嗜血的暴君之一——已经坐在德莫尔坦子爵先生的床边，拿出他全部的本事、全部的学识，同那正夺去孩子性命的可怕疾病搏斗了。至于他此来相救——正如他当着我的面冷笑着说的——是为了把孩子留给断头台。

这一番经过，我是后来才听说的。

原来拉波特每天料理完一天的公事，有个习惯：晚间在自家宅子里应诊。人心里的自相矛盾何其古怪，嗯，先生？这头猛虎每二十四小时里要化身羔羊一个钟头——屠夫摇身变成了良医。英国 milor 把这位可怕人物的古怪脾性揣摩得多透啊！职业的自尊——对疑难杂症的兴趣——随您叫它什么——是拉波特那可憎品性中唯一可取的一点。除此之外，他身上的每一个念头、每一个行止，都卑劣、残忍、睚眦必报。

那天夜里，milor 混在等候那头人形鬣狗治伤的人群里。挤满那位令人胆寒的总督前厅的，是一群三教九流——男男女女，还有孩子——个个自顾不暇，只朝那裹着深色斗篷、静静候轮的陌生人草草瞥上一眼。有一两个朝这贵族咕哝着咒骂，有一两个朝他啐唾沫，以示仇恨和轻蔑；随后通向内室的门猛地掀开——叫一个号——一个病人走出来，另一个走进去——在这周而复始的忙乱里，那陌生人转眼就被忘到了脑后。

===Original===



His turn came﻿—his number being called﻿—it was the last on the list, and the antechamber was now quite empty save for him. He walked into the presence of the proconsul. Claude Lemoine, who was on guard in the room at the time, told me that just for the space of two seconds the two men looked at one another. Then the stranger threw back his head and said quietly:

“There’s a child dying of pleurisy, or worse, in an attic in the Rue des Pipots. There’s not a doctor left in Lyons to attend on him, and the child will die for want of medical skill. Will you come to him, citizen doctor?”

It seems that for a moment or two Laporte hesitated.

“You look to me uncommonly like an aristo, and therefore a traitor,” he said, “and I’ve half a mind﻿—”

“To call your guard and order my immediate arrest,” broke in milor with a whimsical smile, “but in that case a citizen of France will die for want of a doctor’s care. Let me take you to the child’s bedside, citizen doctor, you can always have me arrested afterwards.”



===Chinese===



轮到他了——叫到了他的号——那是名单上最末一个，前厅里如今除了他已空无一人。他走到那位总督的跟前去。当时在屋里站岗的克洛德·勒穆瓦纳（Claude Lemoine）后来告诉我，有那么整整两秒钟，两个人对望着。然后陌生人把头一昂，平静地说：

“皮波街一间阁楼里，有个孩子得了胸膜炎，或是更重的病，快死了。里昂连一个肯去看他的大夫都没有了，孩子会因缺了医术而丧命。您肯去一趟吗，大夫公民？”

据说拉波特迟疑了一两分钟。

“依我看，你这副模样活脱是个贵族，因而也就是个叛徒，”他说，“我倒有半个心思——”

“唤你的卫兵来，把我当场拿下，”milor 带着调皮的笑意接口道，“可那样一来，法兰西的一位公民就要因请不到大夫而死了。让我领您到孩子床前去吧，大夫公民，回头您再叫人抓我不迟。”

===Original===



But Laporte still hesitated.

“How do I know that you are not one of those English spies?” he began.

“Take it that I am,” rejoined milor imperturbably, “and come and see the patient.”

Never had a situation been carried off with so bold a hand. Claude Lemoine declared that Laporte’s mouth literally opened for the call which would have summoned the sergeant of the guard into the room and ordered the summary arrest of this impudent stranger. During the veriest fraction of a second life and death hung in the balance for the gallant English milor. In the heart of Laporte every evil passion fought the one noble fibre within him. But the instinct of the skilful healer won the battle, and the next moment he had hastily collected what medicaments and appliances he might require, and the two men were soon speeding along the streets in the direction of the Rue des Pipots.



===Chinese===



可是拉波特还在迟疑。

“我怎么知道你不是那伙英国奸细里的一个？”他开口道。

“你只管当我是，”milor 不动声色地答道，“然后去看病人吧。”

从来没有一桩险局叫人应付得这般大胆。克洛德·勒穆瓦纳说，拉波特的嘴当时真个张开了，只待喊出那声召唤——把卫队中士唤进屋来，将这个狂妄的陌生人就地拿下。在那千钧一发的一瞬，英勇的英国 milor 生死悬于一线。在拉波特心里，种种邪念正同他仅存的一缕高尚情怀厮拼。但神医的本能终于得胜；下一刻他已匆匆收拾起他可能用得着的药品器械，两个人很快便顺着街道，朝皮波街的方向疾步而去。

---

===Original===



During the whole of that night, milor and Laporte sat together by the bedside of M. le Vicomte. Laporte only went out once in order to fetch what further medicaments he required. Mme. la Marquise took the opportunity of running out of her hiding-place in order to catch a glimpse of her child. I saw her take milor’s hand and press it against her heart in silent gratitude. On her knees she begged him to go away and leave her and the boy to their fate. Was it likely that he would go? But she was so insistent that at last he said:

“Madame, let me assure you that even if I were prepared to play the coward’s part which you would assign to me, it is not in my power to do so at this moment. Citizen Laporte came to this house under the escort of six picked men of his guard. He has left these men stationed on the landing outside this door.”

Madame la Marquise gave a cry of terror, and once more that pathetic look of horror came into her face. Milor took her hand and then pointed to the sick child.

“Madame,” he said, “M. le Vicomte is already slightly better. Thanks to medical skill and a child’s vigorous hold on life, he will live. The rest is in the hands of God.”

Already the heavy footsteps of Laporte were heard upon the creaking stairs. Mme. la Marquise was forced to return to her hiding-place.



===Chinese===



那一整夜，milor 和拉波特一同守在子爵先生床边。拉波特只出去过一趟，去取他还要用的药品。侯爵夫人趁这个空当从藏身之处跑出来，好看孩子一眼。我看见她握住 milor 的手，把它按在自己心口，默默致谢。她跪下来，恳求他走，把她和孩子交给命去。他哪里肯走？可她恳求得那样切，最后他说：

“夫人，请容我向您言明：就算我甘愿扮演您派给我的那个懦夫角色，此刻我也无能为力。拉波特公民来这栋房子，有他卫队六名精兵护送。他把这些人留驻在这扇门外的楼梯口上了。”

侯爵夫人发出一声恐怖的惊呼，那张可怜的脸上又一次布满惊惶。milor 拉起她的手，指了指病着的孩子。

“夫人，”他说，“子爵先生已经略有起色。托医术的福，也托孩子生命力旺盛的福，他会活下来的。其余的，就在上帝手里了。”

已经听得见拉波特沉重的脚步踏上吱呀作响的楼梯。侯爵夫人只得回到她的藏身之处。

===Original===



Soon after dawn he went. M. le Vicomte was then visibly easier. Laporte had all along paid no heed to me, but I noticed that once or twice during his long vigil by the sickbed his dark eyes beneath their overhanging brows shot a quick suspicious look at the door behind which cowered Mme. la Marquise. I had absolutely no doubt in my mind then that he knew quite well who his patient was.

He gave certain directions to milor﻿—there were certain fresh medicaments to be got during the day. While he spoke there was a sinister glint in his eyes﻿—half cynical, wholly menacing﻿—as he looked up into the calm, impassive face of milor.

“It is essential for the welfare of the patient that these medicaments be got for him during the day,” he said dryly, “and the guard have orders to allow you to pass in and out. But you need have no fear,” he added significantly, “I will leave an escort outside the house to accompany you on your way.”

He gave a mocking, cruel laugh, the meaning of which was unmistakable. His well-drilled human bloodhounds would be on the track of the English spy, whenever the latter dared to venture out into the streets.

Mme. la Marquise and I were prisoners for the day. We spent it in watching alternately beside M. le Vicomte. But milor came and went as freely as if he had not been carrying his precious life in his hands every time that he ventured outside the house.



===Chinese===



天亮后不久他就走了。那时子爵先生分明见好。拉波特始终没把我放在眼里，可是我注意到，在他守在病榻边的漫长时间里，有一两次，他浓眉底下那双黑眼睛，朝侯爵夫人蜷缩躲藏的那扇门飞快地、疑心地扫了一下。当时我心里就毫不怀疑：他的病人是谁，他知道得清清楚楚。

他向 milor 吩咐了几句——白天还得置办几种新药。他说话的时候，眼里闪着一种阴冷的光——半是讥诮，全是威吓——一边抬眼望着 milor 那张平静无波的脸。

“这些药今天白天务必给他备齐，病人的安危所系，”他干巴巴地说，“卫兵已奉命放你进出。不过你不必害怕，”他意味深长地补了一句，“我会在这房子外头留下一队护兵，一路上陪着你了。”

他发出一声讥诮而残忍的笑，其中的意味再明白不过。只要那英国奸细胆敢冒险上街，他那些训练有素的人形猎犬就会盯死他的踪迹。

侯爵夫人和我做了一天的囚徒。这一天我们轮班守在子爵先生床边。milor 却进进出出，来去自如，仿佛他每次跨出这栋房子，并不是把宝贵的性命托在手掌心里似的。

===Original===



In the evening Laporte returned to see his patient, and again the following morning, and the next evening. M. le Vicomte was making rapid progress towards recovery.

The third day in the morning Laporte pronounced his patient to be out of danger, but said that he would nevertheless come again to see him at the usual hour in the evening. Directly he had gone, milor went out in order to bring in certain delicacies of which the invalid was now allowed to partake. I persuaded Madame to lie down and have a couple of hours’ good sleep in the inner attic, while I stayed to watch over the child.

To my horror, hardly had I taken up my stand at the foot of the bed when Laporte returned; he muttered something as he entered about having left some important appliance behind, but I was quite convinced that he had been on the watch until milor was out of sight, and then slipped back in order to find me and Madame here alone.

He gave a glance at the child and another at the door of the inner attic, then he said in a loud voice:

“Yes, another twenty-four hours and my duties as doctor will cease and those of patriot will recommence. But Mme. la Marquise de Mortaine need no longer be in any anxiety about her son’s health, nor will Mme. la Guillotine be cheated of a pack of rebels.”



===Chinese===



傍晚拉波特回来看他的病人，第二天早晨又来，第二天夜里再来。子爵先生的病飞快地好转起来。

第三天早晨，拉波特宣告他的病人已脱离危险，但又说，晚上到了往常的钟点，他还是要来。他一走，milor 便出门去置办几样病人如今可以受用的鲜美吃食。我劝夫人躺下，到里间阁楼好好睡上两个钟头，由我留下看护孩子。

叫我惊恐的是，我刚在床脚站定，拉波特就回来了；他进门时咕哝了一句落下了什么要紧器械的话，可我心里透亮：他一直在暗处守着，直到 milor 走得没了影，才溜回来，好撞见我和夫人单独在这里。

他朝孩子望了一眼，又朝里间阁楼的门望了一眼，然后朗声说道：

“是啊，再过二十四小时，我做大夫的差事就完了，做爱国者的差事就要重新开场。德莫尔坦侯爵夫人不必再为她儿子的健康操心，Mme. la Guillotine（断头台夫人）也决不会再让一伙叛党从她手里溜掉了。”

===Original===



He laughed, and was on the point of turning on his heel when the door which gave on the smaller attic was opened and Mme. la Marquise appeared upon the threshold.

Monsieur, I had never seen her look more beautiful than she did now in her overwhelming grief. Her face was as pale as death, her eyes, large and dilated, were fixed upon the human monster who had found it in his heart to speak such cruel words. Clad in a miserable, threadbare gown, her rich brown hair brought to the top of her head like a crown, she looked more regal than any queen.

But proud as she was, monsieur, she yet knelt at the feet of that wretch. Yes, knelt, and embraced his knees and pleaded in such pitiable accents as would have melted the heart of a stone. She pleaded, monsieur﻿—ah, not for herself. She pleaded for her child and for me, her faithful servant, and she pleaded for the gallant gentleman who had risked his life for the sake of the child, who was nothing to him.

“Take me!” she said. “I come of a race that have always known how to die! But what harm has that innocent child done in this world? What harm has poor old Jean-Pierre done, and, oh﻿ ﻿… is the world so full of brave and noble men that the bravest of them all be so unjustly sent to death?”

Ah, monsieur, any man, save one of those abject products of that hideous Revolution, would have listened to such heartrending accents. But this man only laughed and turned on his heel without a word.



===Chinese===



他笑着，正要转身走，通向小阁楼的那扇门开了，侯爵夫人出现在门槛上。

先生，我从没见她比此刻在这灭顶的悲恸中更美过。她的脸白得像死人脸，一双睁大的眼睛直直盯着那个人形怪物——他竟说得出那样狠毒的话来。她穿着一身寒酸的破旧衣裳，一头浓密的栗色头发在头顶挽成冠冕模样，看起来却比任何王后都更有王后的气派。

可是先生，她那样骄傲的一个人，竟跪倒在那恶棍的脚前。是的，跪下了，抱住他的双膝，用那样凄惨的声调哀告，就是石头人听了也要心软。她哀告的，先生——啊，不是为她自己。她为她的孩子哀告，为我——她忠心的老仆人——哀告，也为那位侠义的绅士哀告；那孩子与他非亲非故，他却为孩子把性命都豁上了。

“把我带走吧！”她说，“我出身的家族，向来懂得怎样赴死！可那无辜的孩子在这世上做过什么恶？可怜的老让·皮埃尔又做过什么恶？还有，唉……莫非这世上英武高尚的人已经多得容不下了，偏要把他们当中最英武的一位，这样不公道地送上死路么？”

啊，先生，只要是个人——只要不是那场丑恶革命造出来的那种下贱货色——听了这样肝肠寸断的哀告，谁会不动心。可这个人只是笑了笑，一言不发，转身走了。

---

===Original===



Shall I ever forget the day that went by? Mme. la Marquise was well-nigh prostrate with terror, and it was heartrending to watch the noble efforts which she made to amuse M. le Vicomte. The only gleams of sunshine which came to us out of our darkness were the brief appearances of milor. Outside we could hear the measured tramp of the guard that had been set there to keep us close prisoners. They were relieved every six hours, and, in fact, we were as much under arrest as if we were already incarcerated in one of the prisons of Lyons.

At about four o’clock in the afternoon milor came back to us after a brief absence. He stayed for a little while playing with M. le Vicomte. Just before leaving he took Madame’s hand in his and said very earnestly, and sinking his voice to the merest whisper:

“Tonight! Fear nothing! Be ready for anything! Remember that the League of the Scarlet Pimpernel have never failed to succour, and that I hereby pledge you mine honour that you and those you care for will be out of Lyons this night.”

He was gone, leaving us to marvel at his strange words. Mme. la Marquise after that was just like a person in a dream. She hardly spoke to me, and the only sound that passed her lips was a quaint little lullaby which she sang to M. le Vicomte ere he dropped off to sleep.

The hours went by leaden-footed. At every sound on the stairs Madame started like a frightened bird. That infamous Laporte usually paid his visits at about eight o’clock in the evening, and after it became quite dark, Madame sat at the tiny window, and I felt that she was counting the minutes which still lay between her and the dreaded presence of that awful man.



===Chinese===



那一天是怎么熬过去的，我怎能忘记？侯爵夫人吓得几乎瘫倒，却还强打精神，变着法子逗子爵先生开心，那光景看着叫人心碎。我们的黑暗里仅有的一线阳光，就是 milor 短暂的露面。外头听得见岗哨整齐的脚步声——他们就是派来把我们严严实实看管起来的。岗哨每六个钟头换一班；说实在的，我们这同已被关进里昂哪座监狱，也没有两样了。

下午四点钟上下，milor 短暂离开后，又回到我们这里。他逗着子爵先生玩了一会儿。临走之前，他握起夫人的手，说得极为恳切，声音低到只剩一丝耳语：

“就在今夜！什么都不要怕！一切都要有所准备！记住，红花侠同盟（the League of the Scarlet Pimpernel）救人从无失手；我在此以名誉为质，担保您和您心里牵挂的人，今夜必出得了里昂。”

他走了，留下我们对他的奇话暗自称奇。打那以后，侯爵夫人就像身在梦里的人。她几乎不同我讲话，唇边唯一的声息，是一支古怪的小摇篮曲——她在子爵先生睡熟之前，轻轻唱给他听。

钟点拖着铅一样的步子挨过去。楼梯上一有响动，夫人就像受惊的小鸟似的惊跳。那个声名狼藉的拉波特，往常总在晚上八点钟上下到；天色黑透之后，夫人坐在小窗前，我觉出她在数着分秒——数着横在她同那个可怕人物照面之间的、还剩下的每一分钟。

===Original===



At a quarter before eight o’clock we heard the usual heavy footfall on the stairs. Madame started up as if she had been struck. She ran to the bed﻿—almost like one demented, and wrapping the one poor blanket round M. le Vicomte, she seized him in her arms. Outside we could hear Laporte’s raucous voice speaking to the guard. His usual query: “Is all well?” was answered by the brief: “All well, citizen.” Then he asked if the English spy were within, and the sentinel replied: “No, citizen, he went out at about five o’clock and has not come back since.”

“Not come back since five o’clock?” said Laporte with a loud curse. “*Pardi!* I trust that that fool Caudy has not allowed him to escape.”

“I saw Caudy about an hour ago, citizen,” said the man.

“Did he say anything about the Englishman then?”

It seemed to us, who were listening to this conversation with bated breath, that the man hesitated a moment ere he replied; then he spoke with obvious nervousness.



===Chinese===



差一刻八点，我们听见楼梯上照常响起那沉重的脚步。夫人像挨了一击似的跳起来。她奔到床边——简直像失了魂似的——把仅有的一条破毯子裹在子爵先生身上，一把将他抱进怀里。外头听得见拉波特同岗哨说话的沙哑嗓音。他照例问一声：“一切正常么？”对方简短地答：“一切正常，公民。”他又问那英国奸细在不在里头，哨兵答道：“不在，公民，他五点钟上下出去的，到现在还没回来。”

“五点钟出去到现在还没回来？”拉波特大声咒骂了一句，“*Pardi!*（天哪）但愿那个蠢货科迪（Caudy）没有放跑了他。”

“我一个钟头前还见过科迪，公民，”那人答道。

“那他提到那个英国人的动静没有？”

我们屏息听着这场问答，那人回答之前似乎迟疑了片刻；随后他开口时，分明带着慌张。

===Original===



“As a matter of fact, citizen,” he said, “Caudy thought then that the Englishman was inside the house, whilst I was equally sure that I had seen him go downstairs an hour before.”

“A thousand devils!” cried Laporte with a savage oath, “if I find that you, citizen sergeant, or Caudy have blundered there will be trouble for you.”

To the accompaniment of a great deal more swearing he suddenly kicked open the door of our attic with his boot, and then came to a standstill on the threshold with his hands in the pockets of his breeches and his legs planted wide apart, face to face with Mme. la Marquise, who confronted him now, herself like a veritable tigress who is defending her young.

He gave a loud, mocking laugh.

“Ah, the aristos!” he cried, “waiting for that cursed Englishman, what? to drag you and your brat out of the claws of the human tiger.﻿ ﻿… Not so, my fine ci-devant Marquise. The brat is no longer sick﻿—he is well enough, anyhow, to breathe the air of the prisons of Lyons for a few days pending a final rest in the arms of Mme. la Guillotine. Citizen sergeant,” he called over his shoulder, “escort these aristos to my carriage downstairs. When the Englishman returns, tell him he will find his friends under the tender care of Doctor Laporte. En avant, little mother,” he added, as he gripped Mme. la Marquise tightly by the arm, “and you, old scarecrow,” he concluded, speaking to me over his shoulder, “follow the citizen sergeant, or﻿—”



===Chinese===



“实话说，公民，”他说，“科迪当时认定那英国人在房子里，而我呢，也同样确准，我一个钟头前亲眼看见他下了楼。”

“一千个魔鬼！”拉波特恶狠狠地赌咒喊道，“要叫我查出你，中士公民，或是科迪误了事，有你们好受的！”

他又骂骂咧咧了一大串，忽然抬起靴子，一脚踹开我们阁楼的门，随即在门槛上站定，双手插在马裤口袋里，两腿大叉开着，同侯爵夫人打了个照面——此刻迎着他的她，活像一头护崽的真母虎。

他放声讥笑起来。

“啊，贵族佬们！”他嚷道，“在等那个该死的英国人呢，是不是？等他把你和你那崽子从人形老虎的爪子里捞出去。……想得倒美，我好心的旧日侯爵夫人。崽子的病是好了——好歹够结实，可以在里昂的监狱里透几天风，然后到 Mme. la Guillotine（断头台夫人）的怀抱里去享最后的安息。中士公民，”他扭头喊道，“把这些贵族佬押到楼下我的马车跟前去。那英国人回来时，告诉他，他的朋友们正承蒙拉波特大夫的悉心照料呢。En avant（前进），小妈妈，”他补了一句，一面紧紧钳住侯爵夫人的胳膊，“还有你，老稻草人，”他扭头冲我说了最后一句，“跟在中士公民后头，不然——”

===Original===



Mme. la Marquise made no resistance. As I told you, she had been, since dusk, like a person in a dream; so what could I do but follow her noble example? Indeed, I was too dazed to do otherwise.

We all went stumbling down the dark, rickety staircase, Laporte leading the way with Mme. la Marquise, who had M. le Vicomte tightly clasped in her arms. I followed with the sergeant, whose hand was on my shoulder; I believe that two soldiers walked behind, but of that I cannot be sure.

At the bottom of the stairs through the open door of the house I caught sight of the vague outline of a large barouche, the lanterns of which threw a feeble light upon the cruppers of two horses and of a couple of men sitting on the box.

Mme. la Marquise stepped quietly into the carriage. Laporte followed her, and I was bundled in in his wake by the rough hands of the soldiery. Just before the order was given to start, Laporte put his head out of the window and shouted to the sergeant:

“When you see Caudy tell him to report himself to me at once. I will be back here in half an hour; keep strict guard as before until then, citizen sergeant.”



===Chinese===



侯爵夫人没有反抗。我方才说过，打黄昏起，她就像身在梦里；我除了学她那高贵的榜样，还有什么法子？说真的，我也晕得分不清东西了。

我们一行磕磕绊绊，摸下那道黑洞洞、摇摇晃晃的楼梯，拉波特领着路，同侯爵夫人并排——她把子爵先生紧紧搂在怀里。我跟着中士走，他的手按在我肩上；我记得身后好像还有两个士兵跟着，不过这一点我不敢说准。

到了楼梯脚，透过房子敞开的大门，我瞥见一辆大型四轮马车影影绰绰的轮廓，车灯把微弱的光投在两匹马的尻部，也投在车座上并坐的两个人身上。

侯爵夫人悄没声地上了车。拉波特跟着她上去，我也被士兵们粗手粗脚地推搡着塞了进去。正要吩咐起车，拉波特把头探出车窗，朝中士喊道：

“见到科迪，叫他立刻到我那儿报到。我半个钟头后回来；在那之前，照旧严加看守，中士公民。”

===Original===



The next moment the coachman cracked his whip, Laporte called loudly, “En avant!” and the heavy barouche went rattling along the ill-paved streets.

Inside the carriage all was silence. I could hear Mme. la Marquise softly whispering to M. le Vicomte, and I marvelled how wondrously calm﻿—nay, cheerful, she could be. Then suddenly I heard a sound which of a truth did make my heart stop its beating. It was a quaint and prolonged laugh which I once thought I would never hear again on this earth. It came from the corner of the barouche next to where Mme. la Marquise was so tenderly and gaily crooning to her child. And a kindly voice said merrily:

“In half an hour we shall be outside Lyons. Tomorrow we’ll be across the Swiss frontier. We’ve cheated that old tiger after all. What say you, Mme. la Marquise?”

It was milor’s voice, and he was as merry as a schoolboy.

“I told you, old Jean-Pierre,” he added, as he placed that firm hand which I loved so well upon my knee, “I told you that those confounded murderers would not get me this time.”



===Chinese===



转眼间车夫甩了个响鞭，拉波特高喊一声“En avant!（前进）”，笨重的大马车便顺着坑洼不平的街道轱辘轱辘驶去了。

车厢里一片寂静。我听得见侯爵夫人对着子爵先生轻声细语，心里惊异她竟能这样出奇地平静——不，是这样快活。忽然，我听见了一个声音，那一声可真叫我的心停了跳。那是一阵古怪而悠长的笑声，我原以为这辈子再也听不到的了。声音来自马车的角落，就在侯爵夫人座位旁边——她正在那儿又温柔又快活地哄着孩子。一个和善的声音欢欢喜喜地说：

“再过半个钟头，我们就出里昂城了。明天我们就越过瑞士边境。咱们到底把那只老老虎骗过了。您说是不是，侯爵夫人？”

那是 milor 的声音，他快活得像个学童。

“我早说过吧，老让·皮埃尔，”他补了一句，一面把他那只我深爱着的坚实的手，放在我的膝头上，“我早说过，那帮该死的杀人凶手，这回逮不着我。”

===Original===



And to think that I did not know him, as he stood less than a quarter of an hour ago upon the threshold of our attic in the hideous guise of that abominable Laporte. He had spent two days in collecting old clothes that resembled those of that infamous wretch, and in taking possession of one of the derelict rooms in the house in the Rue des Pipots. Then while we were expecting every moment that Laporte would order our arrest, milor assumed the personality of the monster, hoodwinked the sergeant on the dark staircase, and by that wonderfully audacious coup saved Mme. la Marquise, M. le Vicomte and my humble self from the guillotine.

Money, of which he had plenty, secured us immunity on the way, and we were in safety over the Swiss frontier, leaving Laporte to eat out his tigerish heart with baffled rage.



===Chinese===



想想看，不到一刻钟之前，他还顶着那个可憎的拉波特那副狰狞行头，站在我们阁楼的门槛上，我竟没有认出他来！他花了两天工夫，四处搜罗同那个恶棍身上相似的旧衣裳，又悄悄占下了皮波街那栋房子里一间无人过问的破屋。随后，就在我们时时刻刻提心吊胆、只等拉波特下令抓人的当口，milor 扮成了那怪物的模样，在漆黑的楼梯上把中士骗了个正着，凭这一手胆大包天的绝招，把侯爵夫人、子爵先生连同我这一把贱骨头，从断头台底下救了出来。

他有的是钱，一路买通了各处关节；我们平平安安越过了瑞士边境，只留下拉波特一个人，去捶胸咬齿地咽他那恶虎捕空的怒气。

## “Needs Must﻿—” / ## 「情非得已——」

### I / ### 一

===Original===

The children were all huddled up together in one corner of the room. Etienne and Valentine, the two eldest, had their arms round the little one. As for Lucile, she would have told you herself that she felt just like a bird between two snakes﻿—terrified and fascinated﻿—oh! especially by that little man with the pale face and the light grey eyes and the slender white hands unstained by toil, one of which rested lightly upon the desk, and was only clenched now and then at a word or a look from the other man or from Lucile herself.

But Commissary Lebel just tried to browbeat her. It was not difficult, for in truth she felt frightened enough already, with all this talk of “traitors” and that awful threat of the guillotine.

Lucile Clamette, however, would have remained splendidly loyal in spite of all these threats, if it had not been for the children. She was little mother to them; for father was a cripple, with speech and mind already impaired by creeping paralysis, and maman had died when little Josephine was born. And now those fiends threatened not only her, but Etienne who was not fourteen, and Valentine who was not much more than ten, with death, unless she﻿—Lucile﻿—broke the solemn word which she had given to M. le Marquis. At first she had tried to deny all knowledge of M. le Marquis’ whereabouts.

===Chinese===

孩子们全都缩在房间的一角。艾蒂安和瓦朗蒂娜——两个年长的——用胳膊搂着最小的那个。至于吕西尔，她自己也说得上来的：她只觉得像一只夹在两条蛇之间的小鸟——吓得魂飞魄散，却又移不开眼——哦！尤其是怕那个面色苍白的小个子男人：淡灰色的眼睛，一双纤长白皙、不曾沾过劳作污痕的手，其中一只轻轻搭在书桌上，只是偶尔——另一个人或是吕西尔自己说了句什么话、递了个什么眼色时——才倏然攥紧。

而勒贝尔政委（Commissary Lebel）一味想吓服她。这并不难，因为说实在的，满耳朵“叛徒”长“叛徒”短，再加上断头台那骇人的威胁，她早已怕得够了。

然而，若不是为了孩子们，任凭这些威吓，吕西尔·克拉梅特（Lucile Clamette）本可以死守忠贞、绝不动摇。她就是孩子们的小妈妈：父亲已经残废，不断蔓延的瘫痪夺走了他的言语和神志；妈妈生小约瑟芬的时候便去世了。而如今这些恶魔所威胁的不仅是她，还有不满十四岁的艾蒂安、刚过十岁没多久的瓦朗蒂娜——都要处死，除非她——吕西尔——背弃自己对侯爵先生许下的郑重诺言。起初她还矢口否认，说全然不知侯爵先生的下落。

===Original===

“I can assure M. le Commissaire that I do not know,” she had persisted quietly, even though her heart was beating so rapidly in her bosom that she felt as if she must choke.

“Call me citizen Commissary,” Lebel had riposted curtly. “I should take it as a proof that your aristocratic sentiments are not so deep-rooted as they appear to be.”

“Yes, citizen!” murmured Lucile, under her breath.

===Chinese===

“我可以向政委先生保证，我真的不知道。”她一直安安静静地咬定这一点，尽管心口怦怦狂跳，跳得她几乎要窒息。

“叫我政委公民。”勒贝尔生硬地回敬，“你要肯这么叫，我倒可以当作一个凭据，证明你那套贵族情调，还没有表面看上去那么根深蒂固。”

“是，公民！”吕西尔低声应道。

===Original===

Then the other one, he with the pale eyes and the slender white hands, leaned forward over the desk, and the poor girl felt as if a mighty and unseen force was holding her tight, so tight that she could neither move, nor breathe, nor turn her gaze away from those pale, compelling eyes. In the remote corner little Josephine was whimpering, and Etienne’s big, dark eyes were fixed bravely upon his eldest sister.

“There, there! little citizeness,” the awful man said, in a voice that sounded low and almost caressing, “there is nothing to be frightened of. No one is going to hurt you or your little family. We only want you to be reasonable. You have promised to your former employer that you would never tell anyone of his whereabouts. Well! we don’t ask you to tell us anything.

“All that we want you to do is to write a letter to M. le Marquis﻿—one that I myself will dictate to you. You have written to M. le Marquis before now, on business matters, have you not?”

“Yes, monsieur﻿—yes, citizen,” stammered Lucile through her tears. “Father was bailiff to M. le Marquis until he became a cripple and now I﻿—”

===Chinese===

这时另外那个人——那个生着淡色眼睛和纤长白手的人——朝书桌探过身来。可怜的姑娘顿觉有一股无形的大力将自己紧紧攫住，攥得那么紧，她动弹不得，喘不上气，目光也无法从那双苍白的、不容抗拒的眼睛上移开。远处的角落里，小约瑟芬在低声啜泣，艾蒂安那双黑色的大眼睛，正勇敢地望着长姐。

“好了，好了！小女公民，”那个可怕的男人开口了，声音低沉，几乎算是温存，“没什么好怕的。没有人要伤害你，也不伤害你这一家小的。我们不过要你讲点道理。你从前的主人叫你许下过诺言，永远不把他的下落告诉任何人。好嘛！我们并不要求你告诉我们什么。

“我们只要你给侯爵先生写一封信——内容由我亲自口授。你从前是给侯爵先生写过信的，写的是庄园里的事务，对不对？”

“是，先生（monsieur）——是，公民。”吕西尔噙着泪结结巴巴地说，“父亲残废以前，一直在侯爵先生手下当庄园管家，如今我——”

===Original===

“Do not write any letter, Lucile,” Etienne suddenly broke in with forceful vehemence. “It is a trap set by these miscreants to entrap M. le Marquis.”

There was a second’s silence in the room after this sudden outburst on the part of the lad. Then the man with the pale face said quietly:

“Citizen Lebel, order the removal of that boy. Let him be kept in custody till he has learned to hold his tongue.”

===Chinese===

“别写信，吕西尔！”艾蒂安突然激烈地插进来，“这是这些恶棍设下的圈套，要坑害侯爵先生。”

少年这阵突如其来的发作过后，屋里静了一秒钟。随后，那个面色苍白的男人平静地说：

“勒贝尔公民，下令把这小子带走。先关押起来，等他学会了闭嘴再说。”

===Original===

But before Lebel could speak to the two soldiers who were standing on guard at the door, Lucile had uttered a loud cry of agonised protest.

“No! no! monsieur!﻿—that is citizen!” she implored. “Do not take Etienne away. He will be silent.﻿ ﻿… I promise you that he will be silent﻿ ﻿… only do not take him away! Etienne, my little one!” she added, turning her tear-filled eyes to her brother, “I entreat thee to hold thy tongue!”

The others, too, clung to Etienne, and the lad, awed and subdued, relapsed into silence.

“Now then,” resumed Lebel roughly, after a while, “let us get on with this business. I am sick to death of it. It has lasted far too long already.”

===Chinese===

可是不等勒贝尔向守在门口的两名士兵开口吩咐，吕西尔已经发出一声撕心裂肺的抗议，失声叫了出来。

“不！不！先生！——我是说，公民！”她哀告道，“别把艾蒂安带走。他会住口的。……我向你们保证，他一定会住口的……只求别把他带走！艾蒂安，我的小弟弟！”她转向弟弟，泪眼盈盈地又添了一句，“我求求你，闭上嘴吧！”

其余的孩子也都偎紧了艾蒂安。少年被慑住了，蔫了下去，重新归于沉默。

“好了，”过了片刻，勒贝尔粗声粗气地重新开腔，“咱们接着办正事吧。这码子事我烦透了。拖得也实在太久了。”


===Original===

He fixed his bloodshot eyes upon Lucile and continued gruffly:

“Now listen to me, my wench, for this is going to be my last word. Citizen Chauvelin here has already been very lenient with you by allowing this letter business. If I had my way I’d make you speak here and now. As it is, you either sit down and write the letter at citizen Chauvelin’s dictation at once, or I send you with that impudent brother of yours and your imbecile father to jail, on a charge of treason against the State, for aiding and abetting the enemies of the Republic; and you know what the consequences of such a charge usually are. The other two brats will go to a House of Correction, there to be detained during the pleasure of the Committee of Public Safety. That is my last word,” he reiterated fiercely. “Now, which is it to be?”

He paused, the girl’s wan cheeks turned the colour of lead. She moistened her lips once or twice with her tongue; beads of perspiration appeared at the roots of her hair. She gazed helplessly at her tormentors, not daring to look on those three huddled-up little figures there in the corner. A few seconds sped away in silence. The man with the pale eyes rose and pushed his chair away. He went to the window, stood there with his back to the room, those slender white hands of his clasped behind him. Neither the commissary nor the girl appeared to interest him further. He was just gazing out of the window.

The other was still sprawling beside the desk, his large, coarse hand﻿—how different his hands were!﻿—was beating a devil’s tatoo upon the arm of his chair.

===Chinese===

他一双布满血丝的眼睛盯住吕西尔，瓮声瓮气地接着说：

“现在听我说，小娘们儿，这是我的最后通牒。这位肖维兰公民容你写信，已经是天大的宽大了。要依我的性子，当场就撬开你的嘴。眼下么，你要么立刻坐下，照肖维兰公民的口授把信写出来，要么我就把你、连同你那个无法无天的弟弟和你那白痴老爹一起投进大牢，罪名是危害国家的叛国罪——私通共和国的敌人；这种罪名通常是个什么下场，你自个儿清楚。另外两个小崽子送感化院，关到公安委员会满意为止。这就是我的最后通牒，”他恶狠狠地又强调一遍，“怎么样，你自己挑吧！”

他顿住了。姑娘那两张灰白的脸颊变成了铅色。她伸出舌头，舔了一两次干裂的嘴唇；发根上沁出了汗珠。她无助地望着这两个折磨她的人，不敢去看角落里那三个缩成一团的小小身影。几秒钟在寂静中溜走。淡色眼睛的那个男人站起身，把椅子推开。他走到窗前，背对着房间站在那里，那双纤长的白手交叠在身后。无论是政委还是姑娘，似乎都不再能引起他的兴致。他只管望着窗外。

另外那个还四仰八叉地瘫在书桌旁边，一只粗大的手——他的手跟那人是多么不同啊！——在椅子扶手上敲着烦躁的点子。


===Original===

After a few minutes, Lucile made a violent effort to compose herself, wiped the moisture from her pallid forehead and dried the tears which still hung upon her lashes. Then she rose from her chair and walked resolutely up to the desk.

“I will write the letter,” she said simply.

Lebel gave a snort of satisfaction; but the other did not move from his position near the window. The boy, Etienne, had uttered a cry of passionate protest.

“Do not give M. le Marquis away, Lucile!” he said hotly. “I am not afraid to die.”

But Lucile had made up her mind. How could she do otherwise, with these awful threats hanging over them all? She and Etienne and poor father gone, and the two young ones in one of those awful Houses of Correction, where children were taught to hate the Church, to shun the Sacraments, and to blaspheme God!

“What am I to write?” she asked dully, resolutely closing her ears against her brother’s protest.

===Chinese===

过了几分钟，吕西尔猛地定住心神，抹去惨白额头上的汗湿，拭干还挂在睫毛上的泪珠。随后她从椅子上站起来，毅然走向书桌。

“我写。”她只简单地说了这一句。

勒贝尔满意地哼了一声；另外那人却站在窗边纹丝未动。少年艾蒂安发出一声激愤的抗议。

“别出卖侯爵先生，吕西尔！”他涨红着脸说，“我死都不怕。”

可吕西尔主意已定。这样的威胁悬在全家头上，她还能怎么样呢？她、艾蒂安和可怜的父亲都没了性命，两个小的扔进那种可怕的感化院——在那儿，孩子们被教着仇恨教会、躲避圣事、亵渎上帝！

“要我写什么？”她木然地问，横下心来不再听弟弟的抗议。


===Original===

Lebel pushed pen, ink and paper towards her and she sat down, ready to begin.

“Write!” now came in a curt command from the man at the window. And Lucile wrote at his dictation:

“**Monsieur le Marquis**﻿—We are in grave trouble. My brother Etienne and I have been arrested on a charge of treason. This means the guillotine for us and for poor father, who can no longer speak; and the two little ones are to be sent to one of those dreadful Houses of Correction, where children are taught to deny God and to blaspheme. You alone can save us, M. le Marquis; and I beg you on my knees to do it. The citizen Commissary here says that you have in your possession certain papers which are of great value to the State, and that if I can persuade you to give these up, Etienne, father and I and the little ones will be left unmolested. M. le Marquis, you once said that you could never adequately repay my poor father for all his devotion in your service. You can do it now, M. le Marquis, by saving us all. I will be at the château a week from today. I entreat you, M. le Marquis, to come to me then and to bring the papers with you; or if you can devise some other means of sending the papers to me, I will obey your behests.﻿—I am, M. le Marquis’ faithful and devoted

“Lucile Clamette.”

The pen dropped from the unfortunate girl’s fingers. She buried her face in her hands and sobbed convulsively. The children were silent, awed and subdued﻿—tired out, too. Only Etienne’s dark eyes were fixed upon his sister with a look of mute reproach.

===Chinese===

勒贝尔把笔、墨水和纸推到她面前，她坐下来，准备动笔。

“写吧！”窗边那人这时简短地下了命令。吕西尔便照他的口授写道：

“**侯爵先生**——我们大难临头了。我和弟弟艾蒂安已被逮捕，罪名是叛国。这意味着我们和再不能开口说话的可怜父亲都要上断头台；两个小的要被送进那种可怕的感化院，在那儿，孩子们被教着否认上帝、亵渎神明。只有您一人能救我们，侯爵先生；我跪着恳求您出手。这里的政委公民说，您手里掌握着几份文件，对国家关系重大；还说只要我能劝您交出来，艾蒂安、父亲、我和两个小的便可安然无事。侯爵先生，您从前说过，我可怜的父亲为您效力多年，忠心耿耿，您永远报答不尽。如今您可以报答了，侯爵先生——救我们全家一命。从今天起一星期后，我会在庄园里。我恳求您，侯爵先生，届时来见我，把文件带在身上；您若能想出别的法子把文件送到我手上，我一切听凭您的吩咐。——侯爵先生忠心不贰、感恩不尽的

“吕西尔·克拉梅特。”

笔从不幸的姑娘指间跌落。她双手掩面，抽抽噎噎地哭了起来。孩子们一声不响，又被慑住，又累得没了力气——只有艾蒂安那双黑眼睛，带着无言的责备，一眨不眨地望着姐姐。


===Original===

Lebel had made no attempt to interrupt the flow of his colleague’s dictation. Only once or twice did a hastily smothered “What the﻿—!” of astonishment escape his lips. Now, when the letter was finished and duly signed, he drew it to him and strewed the sand over it. Chauvelin, more impassive than ever, was once more gazing out of the window.

“How are the ci-devant aristos to get this letter?” the commissary asked.

“It must be put in the hollow tree which stands by the side of the stable gate at Montorgueil,” whispered Lucile.

===Chinese===

勒贝尔倒没有想要打断同僚口授的滔滔文句。只有一两回，一声赶忙捂住的“这他妈——！”的惊呼从他唇边漏了出来。这时信已写完、签好，他把信拉到自己面前，撒上吸墨的沙子。肖维兰愈发面无表情，又望着窗外去了。

“这封信怎么送到那两个旧日贵族佬的手上？”政委问道。

“得放进蒙托尔格伊马厩大门旁边那棵空心树里。”吕西尔低声说。

===Original===

“And the aristos will find it there?”

“Yes. M. le Vicomte goes there once or twice a week to see if there is anything there from one of us.”

“They are in hiding somewhere close by, then?”

But to this the girl gave no reply. Indeed, she felt as if any word now might choke her.

===Chinese===

“贵族佬会到那儿去取？”

“会的。子爵先生每星期去一两回，看看我们可有东西留在里面。”

“这么说，他们就藏在附近什么地方？”

这一句姑娘却没有回答。的确，她只觉得此刻再吐一个字，都要把她噎住。


===Original===

“Well, no matter where they are!” the inhuman wretch resumed, with brutal cynicism. “We’ve got them now﻿—both of them. Marquis! Vicomte!” he added, and spat on the ground to express his contempt of such titles. “Citizens Montorgueil, father and son﻿—that’s all they are! And as such they’ll walk up in state to make their bow to Mme. la Guillotine!”

“May we go now?” stammered Lucile through her tears.

Lebel nodded in assent, and the girl rose and turned to walk towards the door. She called to the children, and the little ones clustered round her skirts like chicks around the mother-hen. Only Etienne remained aloof, wrathful against his sister for what he deemed her treachery. “Women have no sense of honour!” he muttered to himself, with all the pride of conscious manhood. But Lucile felt more than ever like a bird who is vainly trying to evade the clutches of a fowler. She gathered the two little ones around her. Then, with a cry like a wounded doe she ran quickly out of the room.

===Chinese===

“哼，管他们藏在哪儿！”那丧尽天良的恶徒接着说，一副残暴无耻的腔调，“如今他们落到咱们手里了——父子俩一锅端。侯爵！子爵！”他又补上一句，朝地上啐了一口，以示对这类头衔的鄙夷，“蒙托尔格伊公民，老子加儿子——不过如此！就凭这个身份，他们也要体体面面地登上大堂，向断头台夫人（Mme. la Guillotine）行礼请安！”

“我们现在可以走了吗？”吕西尔噙着泪讷讷地问。

勒贝尔点头应允。姑娘起身朝门口走去。她唤了唤孩子们，小家伙们便簇拥到她的裙子旁边，像小鸡围着母鸡。只有艾蒂安独自落在后头，满腔怒火，只当姐姐干下了背信弃义的勾当。“女人家根本不懂什么叫荣誉！”他喃喃自语，一派自觉已是男子汉的骄傲。可吕西尔比任何时候都更像一只徒劳挣扎、想躲开捕鸟人利爪的鸟儿。她把两个小的拢在身边，随后，像一头受伤的母鹿般哀鸣一声，快步奔出了房间。


### II / ### 二

===Original===

As soon as the sound of the children’s footsteps had died away down the corridor, Lebel turned with a grunt to his still silent companion.

“And now, citizen Chauvelin,” he said roughly, “perhaps you will be good enough to explain what is the meaning of all this tomfoolery.”

“Tomfoolery, citizen?” queried the other blandly. “What tomfoolery, pray?”

“Why, about those papers!” growled Lebel savagely. “Curse you for an interfering busybody! It was I who got information that those pestilential aristos, the Montorgueils, far from having fled the country are in hiding somewhere in my district. I could have made the girl give up their hiding-place pretty soon, without any help from you. What right had you to interfere, I should like to know?”

===Chinese===

孩子们的脚步声一在走廊尽头消失，勒贝尔便咕哝一声，转向他那始终一言不发的同伴。

“我说，肖维兰公民，”他粗声粗气地说，“如今可否请你行行好，解释解释这一通胡闹究竟是什么意思？”

“胡闹，公民？”对方淡然问道，“请问是什么胡闹？”

“嗐，就是那些文件的事！”勒贝尔凶巴巴地咆哮，“你这多管闲事的家伙，真该天打雷劈！是我得了线报，说那两个瘟神贵族佬蒙托尔格伊父子根本没有逃出国境，就藏在我辖区里的什么地方。不用你搭手，我本来很快就能叫那丫头吐出他们的藏身处。我倒想知道，你有什么权利横插一杠子？”


===Original===

“You know quite well what right I had, citizen Lebel,” replied Chauvelin with perfect composure. “The right conferred upon me by the Committee of Public Safety, of whom I am still an unworthy member. They sent me down here to lend you a hand in an investigation which is of grave importance to them.”

“I know that!” retorted Lebel sulkily. “But why have invented the story of the papers?”

“It is no invention, citizen,” rejoined Chauvelin with slow emphasis. “The papers do exist. They are actually in the possession of the Montorgueils, father and son. To capture the two aristos would be not only a blunder, but criminal folly, unless we can lay hands on the papers at the same time.”

“But what in Satan’s name are those papers?” ejaculated Lebel with a fierce oath.

===Chinese===

“我有什么权利，你心里再清楚不过，勒贝尔公民。”肖维兰泰然自若地答道，“那是公安委员会授予我的权利——我至今还是委员会里一个不称职的委员。是他们派我下来，在一桩对他们事关重大的调查里助你一臂之力。”

“这个我知道！”勒贝尔愠怒地顶了一句，“可你为什么要编出文件这套鬼话？”

“这可不是编造，公民。”肖维兰一字一顿地答道，“文件确确实实存在，就攥在蒙托尔格伊父子手里。不同时拿到文件，光抓那两个贵族佬，非但是失策，简直是罪过的蠢行。”

“可那些文件到底是他娘的什么东西？”勒贝尔狠狠咒骂了一声，脱口问道。


===Original===

“Think, citizen Lebel! Think!” was Chauvelin’s cool rejoinder. “Methinks you might arrive at a pretty shrewd guess.” Then, as the other’s bluster and bounce suddenly collapsed upon his colleague’s calm, accusing gaze, the latter continued with impressive deliberation:

“The papers which the two aristos have in their possession, citizen, are receipts for money, for bribes paid to various members of the Committee of Public Safety by Royalist agents for the overthrow of our glorious Republic. You know all about them, do you not?”

While Chauvelin spoke, a look of furtive terror had crept into Lebel’s eyes; his cheeks became the colour of lead. But even so, he tried to keep up an air of incredulity and of amazement.

“I?” he exclaimed. “What do you mean, citizen Chauvelin? What should I know about it?”

===Chinese===

“动动脑筋，勒贝尔公民！动动脑筋！”肖维兰冷冷地回敬，“依我看，你猜也能猜个八九不离十。”对方气焰正盛，被同事这道平静而诛心的目光一扫，登时泄了气；于是肖维兰从容不迫地接着说了下去：

“公民，那两个贵族佬手里的文件，是一张张银钱收据——保王党奸细为颠覆我们光荣的共和国，向公安委员会形形色色的委员们行贿的凭据。这些事你全知道底细，对不对？”

肖维兰说话的当儿，一丝鬼祟的惊恐爬进了勒贝尔的眼睛；他的脸颊变成了铅色。可即便如此，他还竭力装出一副又惊又疑、全不知情的样子。

“我？”他叫了起来，“你这话什么意思，肖维兰公民？我该知道什么？”


===Original===

“Some of those receipts are signed with your name, citizen Lebel,” retorted Chauvelin forcefully. “Bah!” he added, and a tone of savage contempt crept into his even, calm voice now. “Hériot, Foucquier, Ducros and the whole gang of you are in it up to the neck: trafficking with our enemies, trading with England, taking bribes from every quarter for working against the safety of the Republic. Ah! if I had my way, I would let the hatred of those aristos take its course. I would let the Montorgueils and the whole pack of Royalist agents publish those infamous proofs of your treachery and of your baseness to the entire world, and send the whole lot of you to the guillotine!”

He had spoken with so much concentrated fury, and the hatred and contempt expressed in his pale eyes were so fierce that an involuntary ice-cold shiver ran down the length of Lebel’s spine. But, even so, he would not give in; he tried to sneer and to keep up something of his former surly defiance.

“Bah!” he exclaimed, and with a lowering glance gave hatred for hatred, and contempt for contempt. “What can you do? An I am not mistaken, there is no more discredited man in France today than the unsuccessful tracker of the Scarlet Pimpernel.”

===Chinese===

“那些收据里，有几张就签着你的大名，勒贝尔公民。”肖维兰字字有力地回敬。“呸！”他又补上一声，平稳冷静的嗓音里此刻渗进了凶狠的鄙夷，“埃里奥、富基耶、迪克罗，还有你们这一整伙，脖子都湿到了耳朵根：跟我国的敌人做买卖，同英国通商，四面八方收受贿赂，专干危害共和国安全的勾当。啊！要是依了我，我就让那些贵族佬的仇恨顺其自然地发作。我就让蒙托尔格伊父子和那窝保王党奸细，把你们叛卖和无耻的铁证公之于全天下，再把你们这帮人统统送上断头台！”

他这番话憋着满腔怒火，淡色的眼睛里喷出来的仇恨与轻蔑又是那样凶狠，不由得一阵冰冷的寒战顺着勒贝尔的脊梁骨溜了下去。可即便如此，他也不肯认输；他还想冷笑，还想撑住先前那副横蛮不服的架势。

“呸！”他喝道，阴沉沉地瞪过去一眼，以仇恨还仇恨，以轻蔑还轻蔑，“你又能怎样？要是我没弄错，如今全法兰西再找不出第二个比你更声名扫地的人了——那位追猎红花侠却回回扑空的猎手大人。”


===Original===

The taunt went home. It was Chauvelin’s turn now to lose countenance, to pale to the lips. The glow of virtuous indignation died out of his eyes, his look became furtive and shamed.

“You are right, citizen Lebel,” he said calmly after a while. “Recriminations between us are out of place. I am a discredited man, as you say. Perhaps it would have been better if the Committee had sent me long ago to expiate my failures on the guillotine. I should at least not have suffered, as I am suffering now, daily, hourly humiliation at thought of the triumph of an enemy, whom I hate with a passion which consumes my very soul. But do not let us speak of me,” he went on quietly. “There are graver affairs at stake just now than mine own.”

Lebel said nothing more for the moment. Perhaps he was satisfied at the success of his taunt, even though the terror within his craven soul still caused the cold shiver to course up and down his spine. Chauvelin had once more turned to the window; his gaze was fixed upon the distance far away. The window gave on the North. That way, in a straight line, lay Calais, Boulogne, England﻿—where he had been made to suffer such bitter humiliation at the hands of his elusive enemy. And immediately before him was Paris, where the very walls seemed to echo that mocking laugh of the daring Englishman which would haunt him even to his grave.

===Chinese===

这句讥讽刺中了要害。这回轮到肖维兰失态了，连嘴唇都失了血色。眼里那股义愤填膺的光焰熄灭了，神色变得躲闪而羞惭。

“你说得对，勒贝尔公民。”过了半晌，他平静地说，“你我之间再互相攻讦，不合时宜。我是个声名扫地的人，正如你所说。也许委员会当初就该把我送上断头台，为我的屡屡失手赎罪，那倒更好。至少我不至于像现在这样受罪——一想到仇人得胜，便日日夜夜、时时刻刻咀嚼着羞辱。我恨他，这份仇恨噬咬着我的灵魂。不过，别再说我了。”他若无其事地接着说，“眼下还有比我个人荣辱更要紧的事。”

勒贝尔一时没有再说下去。眼见自己的讥讽见了效，他多半是得意的，尽管怯懦灵魂深处的那份恐惧，仍叫他一阵阵脊背发凉。肖维兰又转向了窗口；他的目光凝望向遥远的远方。窗子朝北开。朝那个方向，一条直线过去，是加来、布洛涅、英国——在那儿，他曾在那个来无影去无踪的敌人手里尝尽苦涩的羞辱。而就在他眼前的，是巴黎——这座城的每一堵墙，仿佛都在回荡着那个胆大包天的英国人的嘲笑，那笑声要纠缠他到进坟墓为止。


===Original===

Lebel, unnerved by his colleague’s silence, broke in gruffly at last:

“Well then, citizen,” he said, with a feeble attempt at another sneer, “if you are not thinking of sending us all to the guillotine just yet, perhaps you will be good enough to explain just how the matter stands?”

“Fairly simply, alas!” replied Chauvelin dryly. “The two Montorgueils, father and son, under assumed names, were the Royalist agents who succeeded in suborning men such as you, citizen﻿—the whole gang of you. We have tracked them down, to this district, have confiscated their lands and ransacked the old château for valuables and so on. Two days later, the first of a series of pestilential anonymous letters reached the Committee of Public Safety, threatening the publication of a whole series of compromising documents if the Marquis and the Vicomte de Montorgueil were in any way molested, and if all the Montorgueil property is not immediately restored.”

===Chinese===

同事的沉默弄得勒贝尔心神不定，他终于粗声粗气地开了口：

“那么，公民，”他说，勉强又挤出一丝冷笑，“既然你眼下还不打算把我们统统送上断头台，可否请你行个好，把事情的底细讲讲清楚？”

“说来倒简单得很，可惜！”肖维兰干巴巴地答道，“蒙托尔格伊父子改名换姓，正是那班得手买通了你们这号人——你们这一整伙——的保王党奸细。我们追查到了本区，查封了他们的田产，把旧庄园翻了个底朝天，搜罗值钱物件等等。两天后，公安委员会收到了一连串瘟神似的匿名信的头一封，威胁说，若是蒙托尔格伊侯爵与子爵受到一丝半点的滋扰，若是蒙托尔格伊家的产业不立即发还，就要公布一大批叫人难堪的文书。”


===Original===

“I suppose it is quite certain that those receipts and documents do exist?” suggested Lebel.

“Perfectly certain. One of the receipts, signed by Hériot, was sent as a specimen.”

“My God!” ejaculated Lebel, and wiped the cold sweat from his brow.

“Yes, you’ll all want help from somewhere,” retorted Chauvelin coolly. “From above or from below, what? if the people get to know what miscreants you are. I do believe,” he added, with a vicious snap of his thin lips, “that they would cheat the guillotine of you and, in the end, drag you out of the tumbrils and tear you to pieces limb from limb!”

===Chinese===

“那些收据和文书，想必确凿是存在的了？”勒贝尔试探着说。

“确凿无疑。其中一张由埃里奥署名的收据，已经作为样品寄来了。”

“我的上帝！”勒贝尔失声叫了出来，抹去额头上的冷汗。

“是啊，你们这帮人都得求个庇佑了，”肖维兰冷冷地回敬，“只求上头保佑，或者求下头保佑，啊？——万一老百姓知道了你们是怎样一伙恶徒。我倒真相信，”他薄薄的嘴唇恶狠狠地一抿，又添了一句，“他们会不等断头台开刃，半路上把你们从死囚车里拖出来，撕成一块一块！”


===Original===

Once more that look of furtive terror crept into the commissary’s bloodshot eyes.

“Thank the Lord,” he muttered, “that we were able to get hold of the wench Clamette!”

“At my suggestion,” retorted Chauvelin curtly. “I always believe in threatening the weak if you want to coerce the strong. The Montorgueils cannot resist the wench’s appeal. Even if they do at first, we can apply the screw by clapping one of the young ones in gaol. Within a week we shall have those papers, citizen Lebel; and if, in the meanwhile, no one commits a further blunder, we can close the trap on the Montorgueils without further trouble.”

===Chinese===

那丝鬼祟的惊恐又一次爬进了政委布满血丝的眼睛。

“谢天谢地，”他喃喃道，“幸亏咱们把克拉梅特那丫头攥在了手心里！”

“那是我的主意。”肖维兰简短地回敬，“我一向信奉一条：要挟制强者，先吓唬弱者。蒙托尔格伊父子架不住那丫头的哀告。就算一时架得住，咱们还能加拧螺丝——抓一个小的扔进牢里。不出一个星期，那些文件就到手了，勒贝尔公民；这期间只要没人再捅娄子，咱们就能兵不血刃地合拢罩住蒙托尔格伊父子的罗网。”


===Original===

Lebel said nothing more, and after a while Chauvelin went back to the desk, picked up the letter which poor Lucile had written and watered with her tears, folded it deliberately and slipped it into the inner pocket of his coat.

“What are you going to do?” queried Lebel anxiously.

“Drop this letter into the hollow tree by the side of the stable gate at Montorgueil,” replied Chauvelin simply.

“What?” exclaimed the other. “Yourself?”

“Why, of course! Think you I would entrust such an errand to another living soul?”

===Chinese===

勒贝尔再没有说什么。过了一会儿，肖维兰回到书桌前，拿起那封可怜的吕西尔含泪写就的信，不慌不忙折好，塞进外衣内袋。

“你打算怎么办？”勒贝尔急切地问。

“把这封信投进蒙托尔格伊马厩大门旁那棵空心树里。”肖维兰简单明了地答道。

“什么？”对方叫了起来，“你亲自去？”

“那还用说，当然！你以为这样的差事，我肯托付给任何一个活人吗？”


### III / ### 三

===Original===

A couple of hours later, when the two children had had their dinner and had settled down to play in the garden, and father been cosily tucked up for his afternoon sleep, Lucile called her brother Etienne to her. The boy had not spoken to her since that terrible time spent in the presence of those two awful men. He had eaten no dinner, only sat glowering, staring straight out before him, from time to time throwing a look of burning reproach upon his sister. Now, when she called to him, he tried to run away, was halfway up the stairs before she could seize hold of him.

“Etienne, *mon petit*!” she implored, as her arms closed around his shrinking figure.

“Let me go, Lucile!” the boy pleaded obstinately.

===Chinese===

两三个钟头后，两个小的吃过了午饭，到园子里安定下来玩耍，父亲也被安顿得舒舒服服，睡上了午觉，吕西尔便把弟弟艾蒂安唤到跟前。自从在那两个可怕的男人面前熬过那段可怕的时光，弟弟就没跟她说过一句话。午饭他也没吃，只是坐在那里阴沉着脸，直勾勾瞪着前方，不时朝姐姐投来灼灼逼人的责备目光。这会儿她一唤他，他扭头就想跑，等她一把抓住他时，他已经跑上楼梯一半了。

“艾蒂安，mon petit（我的小宝贝）！”她恳求着，双臂搂住他瑟缩的身子。

“放开我，吕西尔！”少年倔强地挣扎。


===Original===

“*Mon petit*, listen to me!” she pleaded. “All is not lost, if you will stand by me.”

“All is lost, Lucile!” Etienne cried, striving to keep back a flood of passionate tears. “Honour is lost. Your treachery has disgraced us all. If M. le Marquis and M. le Vicomte are brought to the guillotine, their blood will be upon our heads.”

“Upon mine alone, my little Etienne,” she said sadly. “But God alone can judge me. It was a terrible alternative: M. le Marquis, or you and Valentine and little Josephine and poor father, who is so helpless! But don’t let us talk of it. All is not lost, I am sure. The last time that I spoke with M. le Marquis﻿—it was in February, do you remember?﻿—he was full of hope, and oh! so kind. Well, he told me then that if ever I or any of us here were in such grave trouble that we did not know where to turn, one of us was to put on our very oldest clothes, look as like a barefooted beggar as we could, and then go to Paris to a place called the Cabaret de la Liberté in the Rue Christine. There we were to ask for the citizen Rateau, and we were to tell him all our troubles, whatever they might be. Well! we are in such trouble now, *mon petit*, that we don’t know where to turn. Put on thy very oldest clothes, little one, and run barefooted into Paris, find the citizen Rateau and tell him just what has happened: the letter which they have forced me to write, the threats which they held over me if I did not write it﻿—everything. Dost hear?”

===Chinese===

“mon petit（我的小宝贝），听我说！”她央求道，“只要你肯站在我这边，一切还没有完。”

“一切都完了，吕西尔！”艾蒂安喊道，拼命忍住满眶滚烫的泪水，“荣誉完了。你的背信弃义叫我们全家蒙羞。要是侯爵先生和子爵先生被送上断头台，他们的血就淌在我们头上。”

“只淌在我一人头上，我的小艾蒂安。”她悲伤地说，“可唯有上帝能审判我。那是可怕的抉择：一边是侯爵先生，一边是你、瓦朗蒂娜、小约瑟芬，还有那样孤苦无助的可怜父亲！……这话咱们别再提了。我敢肯定，一切还没有完。我上一回同侯爵先生说话——那是二月份，你还记得吗？——他满怀希望，而且，噢！那样慈和。他是这么跟我说的：倘若我，或者我们这里无论哪一个，遭了大难、走投无路，就穿上最旧的衣裳，尽量扮成赤脚的叫花子，然后到巴黎去，到克里斯蒂娜街（Rue Christine）一家叫“自由酒馆”（Cabaret de la Liberté）的铺子。到那儿就找拉托公民，把咱们的难处，不管什么难处，原原本本讲给他听。瞧！咱们如今正是遭了大难、走投无路了，mon petit。穿上你最旧的衣裳，小家伙，赤着脚跑进巴黎城，找到拉托公民，把今天的事一五一十告诉他：他们逼我写的那封信，我不写他们就悬在我头顶的威胁——一样都别落下。听见了没有？”


===Original===

Already the boy’s eyes were glowing. The thought that he individually could do something to retrieve the awful shame of his sister’s treachery spurred him to activity. It needed no persuasion on Lucile’s part to induce him to go. She made him put on some old clothes and stuffed a piece of bread and cheese into his breeches pocket.

It was close upon a couple of leagues to Paris, but that run was one of the happiest which Etienne had ever made. And he did it barefooted, too, feeling neither fatigue nor soreness, despite the hardness of the road after a two weeks’ drought, which had turned mud into hard cakes and ruts into fissures which tore the lad’s feet till they bled.

He did not reach the Cabaret de la Liberté till nightfall, and when he got there he hardly dared to enter. The filth, the squalor, the hoarse voices which rose from that cellar-like place below the level of the street, repelled the country-bred lad. Were it not for the desperate urgency of his errand he never would have dared to enter. As it was, the fumes of alcohol and steaming, dirty clothes nearly choked him, and he could scarce stammer the name of “citizen Rateau” when a gruff voice presently demanded his purpose.

===Chinese===

少年的眼睛已经亮了起来。一想到自己亲身能做点什么事，挽回姐姐失节带来的奇耻大辱，他便浑身是劲。吕西尔根本不用劝说，他一口就答应去。她叫他换上几件旧衣裳，又往他马裤口袋里塞了一块面包和奶酪。

到巴黎差不多有两里格路，可这一趟奔跑，是艾蒂安平生跑得最欢的一趟。他还是赤着脚跑的，一路上既不觉累，也不觉疼——尽管两周的干旱过后，路面硬邦邦的，烂泥晒成了硬块，车辙裂成一道道口子，把少年的脚割得鲜血淋漓。

直到夜幕降临，他才跑到那家自由酒馆，到了门口却几乎不敢进去。那地方低于街面，像个地窖，从里面升上来的污秽、脏臭和嘶哑的人声，叫这乡下长大的少年望而却步。若不是为了那件十万火急的差事，他死也不敢进去。就这样，酒气和冒着热气的脏衣裳的臭味也几乎把他呛死，等到一个粗哑的嗓音过来盘问来意时，他好不容易才结结巴巴吐出“拉托公民”几个字。


===Original===

He realised now how tired he was and how hungry. He had not thought to pause in order to consume the small provision of bread and cheese wherewith thoughtful Lucile had provided him. Now he was ready to faint when a loud guffaw, which echoed from one end of the horrible place to the other, greeted his timid request.

“Citizen Rateau!” the same gruff voice called out hilariously. “Why, there he is! Here, citizen! there’s a blooming aristo to see you.”

Etienne turned his weary eyes to the corner which was being indicated to him. There he saw a huge creature sprawling across a bench, with long, powerful limbs stretched out before him. Citizen Rateau was clothed, rather than dressed, in a soiled shirt, ragged breeches and tattered stockings, with shoes down at heel and faded crimson cap. His face looked congested and sunken about the eyes; he appeared to be asleep, for stertorous breathing came at intervals from between his parted lips, whilst every now and then a racking cough seemed to tear at his broad chest.

Etienne gave him one look, shuddering with horror, despite himself, at the aspect of this bloated wretch from whom salvation was to come. The whole place seemed to him hideous and loathsome in the extreme. What it all meant he could not understand; all that he knew was that this seemed like another hideous trap into which he and Lucile had fallen, and that he must fly from it﻿—fly at all costs, before he betrayed M. le Marquis still further to these drink-sodden brutes. Another moment, and he feared that he might faint. The din of a bibulous song rang in his ears, the reek of alcohol turned him giddy and sick. He had only just enough strength to turn and totter back into the open. There his senses reeled, the lights in the houses opposite began to dance wildly before his eyes, after which he remembered nothing more.

===Chinese===

这时他才觉出自己又累又饿。他一路没想到停下来，吃一吃细心周到的吕西尔给他备下的那点面包和奶酪。此刻他眼看就要晕倒，忽然一声狂笑从这可怕地方的一头响到另一头，算是给他的怯生生请求的回音。

“拉托公民！”还是那个粗哑嗓门，兴高采烈地喊了起来，“嘿，他不就在那儿嘛！喂，公民！来了个漂漂亮亮的小贵族佬找你。”

艾蒂安把疲惫的目光转向那人指给他看的角落。只见一条大汉四仰八叉地横在长凳上，长长的、有力的四肢直伸在前面。拉托公民身上与其说穿着衣裳，不如说挂着一身破烂：一件脏衬衫，一条破马裤，一双烂袜子，鞋跟磨塌了，红色便帽也褪了色。他那张脸虚胖发紫，眼窝深陷；看样子是睡着了，一两声拉锯似的鼾声不时从张开的嘴唇间滚出来，间或还有一阵撕心裂肺的咳嗽，仿佛要把他那宽阔的胸膛撕开。

艾蒂安看了那人一眼，想到救星竟要从这样一个虚肿的可怜虫身上来，不由自主地打了个寒噤。整个地方在他眼里丑恶、龌龊到了极点。这一切都是什么意思，他弄不明白；他只知道，这仿佛又是一个他和吕西尔落进的可怕圈套，他必须逃离——不惜一切代价地逃离，赶早不赶晚，免得再向这些酒囊饭袋的畜生多出卖侯爵先生一分。再待一刻，他怕自己就要晕倒了。一首醉酒的歌子震得他耳朵嗡嗡响，酒臭熏得他头晕恶心。他只剩下一丝力气，转身踉踉跄跄退回到露天里。一到外头，他的神志便天旋地转，对街屋子里的灯火在他眼前狂舞起来，往后的事，他就什么也不记得了。


### IV / ### 四

===Original===

There is nothing now in the whole countryside quite so desolate and forlorn as the château of Montorgueil, with its once magnificent park, now overgrown with weeds, its encircling walls broken down, its terraces devastated, and its stately gates rusty and torn.

Just by the side of what was known in happier times as the stable gate there stands a hollow tree. It is not inside the park, but just outside, and shelters the narrow lane, which skirts the park walls, against the blaze of the afternoon sun.

Its beneficent shade is a favourite spot for an afternoon siesta, for there is a bit of green sward under the tree, and all along the side of the road. But as the shades of evening gather in, the lane is usually deserted, shunned by the neighbouring peasantry on account of its eerie loneliness, so different to the former bustle which used to reign around the park gates when M. le Marquis and his family were still in residence. Nor does the lane lead anywhere, for it is a mere loop which gives on the main road at either end.

===Chinese===

如今整个乡间，再没有比蒙托尔格伊庄园更荒凉、更凄清的地方了：园子当年何等气派，如今野草丛生；四面围墙倒了塌了；台地满目疮痍；气派的大门锈迹斑斑、破烂不堪。

就在往日好时光里叫做马厩大门的旁边，立着一棵空心树。树不在园子里面，恰在园外，为那条沿着园墙蜿蜒的窄巷挡住午后烈日的炙烤。

树荫清凉宜人，是午后打盹的好去处，因为树下和沿路一带都有一小片绿茵。可每到暮色四合，这条巷子便常空无一人，邻近的庄户人家都躲着它，嫌它阴森冷清——侯爵先生一家还住在庄园里时，园门一带何等热闹，如今却是天差地别。这巷子也不通向什么地方，不过是一条环形岔道，两头都接在大路上。


===Original===

Henri de Montorgueil chose a peculiarly dark night in mid-September for one of his periodical visits to the hollow-tree. It was close on nine o’clock when he passed stealthily down the lane, keeping close to the park wall. A soft rain was falling, the first since the prolonged drought, and though it made the road heavy and slippery in places, it helped to deaden the sound of the young man’s furtive footsteps. The air, except for the patter of the rain, was absolutely still. Henri de Montorgueil paused from time to time, with neck craned forward, every sense on the alert, listening, like any poor, hunted beast, for the slightest sound which might betray the approach of danger.

As many a time before, he reached the hollow tree in safety, felt for and found in the usual place the letter which the unfortunate girl Lucile had written to him. Then, with it in his hand, he turned to the stable gate. It had long since ceased to be kept locked and barred. Pillaged and ransacked by order of the Committee of Public Safety, there was nothing left inside the park walls worth keeping under lock and key.

Henri slipped stealthily through the gates and made his way along the drive. Every stone, every nook and cranny of his former home was familiar to him, and anon he turned into a shed where in former times wheelbarrows and garden tools were wont to be kept. Now it was full of debris, lumber of every sort. A more safe or secluded spot could not be imagined. Henri crouched in the furthermost corner of the shed. Then from his belt he detached a small dark lantern, opened its shutter, and with the aid of the tiny, dim light read the contents of the letter. For a long while after that he remained quite still, as still as a man who has received a stunning blow on the head and has partly lost consciousness. The blow was indeed a staggering one. Lucile Clamette, with the invincible power of her own helplessness, was demanding the surrender of a weapon which had been a safeguard for the Montorgueils all this while. The papers which compromised a number of influential members of the Committee of Public Safety had been the most perfect arms of defence against persecution and spoliation.

===Chinese===

亨利·德·蒙托尔格伊（Henri de Montorgueil）挑了个九月中旬格外漆黑的夜晚，照例来探那棵空心树。将近九点钟，他贴着园墙，悄悄溜下巷子。天上下着细雨，是久旱之后的第一场，虽说雨把路面弄得有些地方又泞又滑，倒也压住了年轻人潜行的脚步声。四下里除了雨点的滴答，万籁俱寂。亨利不时停下，伸长脖颈，浑身的感官都警觉着，像一头被追猎的可怜野兽，捕捉着任何一点可能暴露危险逼近的声息。

跟从前许多回一样，他平安到了空心树下，伸手在老地方摸到了那封不幸的姑娘吕西尔写给他的信。随后，信握在手里，他转向马厩大门。这扇门早已不上锁、不上闩了。庄园遭公安委员会下令劫掠洗劫一空，园墙之内再没有什么值得锁门看管的东西了。

亨利悄然闪进大门，沿着车道往前走。这昔日家园的一石一木、每个角落，他都熟稔于心；不多时他拐进一间棚屋，从前独轮车和园艺工具都存放在那里。如今满屋是瓦砾残渣、各色破烂。再没有比这更安全、更僻静的所在了。亨利蹲进棚屋最深处的角落，从腰带上解下一盏小小的暗灯，拨开灯门，借着那点微弱的光读起信来。读完之后，他久久一动不动，像当头挨了狠狠一记闷棍、失了半个知觉的人。这一击着实不轻。吕西尔·克拉梅特，以她那无助本身战无不胜的力量，正索要交出一件一直以来护佑着蒙托尔格伊父子的武器。那些牵连公安委员会若干位炙手可热委员的文件，本是对付迫害和抄掠最完密的防身利器。


===Original===

And now these were to be given up: Oh! there could be no question of that. Even before consulting with his father, Henri knew that the papers would have to be given up. They were clever, those revolutionaries. The thought of holding innocent children as hostages could only have originated in minds attuned to the villainies of devils. But it was unthinkable that the children should suffer.

After a while the young man roused himself from the torpor into which the suddenness of this awful blow had plunged him. By the light of the lantern he began to write upon a sheet of paper which he had torn from his pocketbook.

“**My Dear Lucile**,” he wrote, “As you say, our debt to your father and to you all never could be adequately repaid. You and the children shall never suffer whilst we have the power to save you. You will find the papers in the receptacle you know of inside the chimney of what used to be my mother’s boudoir. You will find the receptacle unlocked. One day before the term you name I myself will place the papers there for you. With them, my father and I do give up our lives to save you and the little ones from the persecution of those fiends. May the good God guard you all.”

===Chinese===

而如今，这些文件要交出去了：哦！这没有什么可犹豫的。还不曾与父亲商量，亨利就知道文件非交不可。那些革命党人好狠的心计。拿无辜的孩子当人质——这样的主意，只有与魔鬼的奸恶声气相通的脑子才想得出。可要是让孩子们受苦，那是万万不能的。

过了一会儿，年轻人才从这飞来横祸打击下的麻木中振作起来。他借着灯光，在从记事本上撕下的一页纸上动笔写信。

他写道：“**亲爱的吕西尔**：正如你所说，我们欠你父亲、欠你们全家的恩情，本来就无法报答尽。只要我们还有力量救你们，你和孩子们就绝不会受苦。文件就在你知道的那个暗格里——从前我母亲闺房那个壁炉的烟囱里面。你会发现暗格没有上锁。在你指定的日子之前一天，我会亲自把文件放进去。交出这些文件，我父亲和我是交出了性命，来换你和孩子们免遭那些恶魔的毒手。愿仁慈的上帝保佑你们全家。”


===Original===

He signed the letter with his initials, H. de M. Then he crept back to the gate and dropped the message into the hollow of the tree.

A quarter of an hour later Henri de Montorgueil was wending his way back to the hiding place which had sheltered him and his father for so long. Silence and darkness then held undisputed sway once more around the hollow tree. Even the rain had ceased its gentle pattering. Anon from far away came the sound of a church bell striking the hour of ten. Then nothing more.
