The parlour and even Simonne’s room were also filled with people: men, most of whom Molé knew by sight; friends or enemies of the ranting demagogue who lay murdered in the very bath which his casual servant had prepared for him. Everyone was discussing the details of the murder, the punishment of the youthful assassin. Simonne Evrard was being loudly blamed for having admitted the girl into citizen Marat’s room. But the wench had looked so simple, so innocent, and she said she was the bearer of a message from Caen. She had called twice during the day, and in the evening the citizen himself said that he would see her. Simonne had been for sending her away. But the citizen was peremptory. And he was so helpless﻿ ﻿… in his bath﻿ ﻿… name of a name, the pitiable affair!
No one paid much attention to Molé. He listened for a while to Simonne’s impassioned voice, giving her version of the affair; then he worked his way stolidly into the bathroom.
It was some time before he succeeded in reaching the side of that awful bath wherein lay the dead body of Jean Paul Marat. The small room was densely packed﻿—not with friends, for there was not a man or woman living, except Simonne Evrard and her sisters, whom the bloodthirsty demagogue would have called “friend”; but his powerful personality had been a menace to many, and now they came in crowds to see that he was really dead, that a girl’s feeble hand had actually done the deed which they themselves had only contemplated. They stood about whispering, their heads averted from the ghastly spectacle of this miserable creature, to whom even death had failed to lend his usual attribute of tranquil dignity.

===Chinese===

前厅挤满了人，别的屋子也一样；可人挤得最厉害的，是正对着他的那扇门——通浴室的那扇。右边厨房里，不久前他还在让内特·马雷夏尔的连声臭骂下劈柴，此刻他瞥见那女人缩在炉边，脏围裙蒙在头上，正哭着——是啊！为那个可憎的东西哭着；那家伙已在一个可怜的、被引入歧途的姑娘手里偿还了几桩他可恶的罪行，而愤怒已极的暴民此刻还嚷着要把那姑娘撕成碎片。
客厅里、连西莫娜的房间里也全是人：大多是莫莱面熟的男人；有的是那个声嘶力竭的煽动家的朋友，有的是他的仇敌——他此刻横死在他那临时仆人替他备下的浴盆里。人人都在议论凶案的细节，议论那年轻刺客会受什么惩处。西莫娜·埃夫拉尔正被人扯着嗓子责怪，怪她放那姑娘进了马拉公民的房间。可那妞儿看上去那么老实、那么无害，又说是从卡昂带来口信的。她白天来过两回，到晚上，公民亲口说要见她。西莫娜本想打发她走。可公民说一不二。再说他又那样不能动弹……泡在浴盆里……天杀的，这事情真够惨的！
没有谁多理会莫莱。他听了一阵西莫娜慷慨激昂的声音，听她叙说自己眼里的事情经过；随后便木然地挤进了浴室。
他费了好一阵工夫，才挤到那可怕的浴盆边上——让-保罗·马拉的尸身就躺在里面。这间小屋挤得水泄不通——挤的不是朋友，因为除了西莫娜·埃夫拉尔和她的姐妹，世上再没有一个活着的男人或女人，是那嗜血的煽动家肯称作“朋友”的；可他的权势曾是许多人的威胁，如今他们成群结队地赶来，要亲眼看一看他真的死了，看一看一个姑娘柔弱的手，当真做成了他们自己只敢在心里盘算的事。他们四处站着窃窃私语，把头从那具凄惨尸身的可怖景象前别开去——就连死亡，也没能赋予这个可怜虫它惯常的那份安详的尊严。

===Original===

The tiny room was inexpressibly hot and stuffy. Hardly a breath of outside air came in through the narrow window, which only gave on the bedroom beyond. An evil-smelling oil-lamp swung from the low ceiling and shed its feeble light on the upturned face of the murdered man.
Molé stood for a moment or two, silent and pensive, beside that hideous form. There was the bath, just as he had prepared it: the board spread over with a sheet and laid across the bath, above which only the head and shoulders emerged, livid and stained. One hand, the left, grasped the edge of the board with the last convulsive clutch of supreme agony.
On the fourth finger of that hand glistened the shoddy ring which Marat had said was not worth stealing. Yet, apparently, it roused the cupidity of the poor wretch who had served him faithfully for these last few days, and who now would once more be thrown, starving and friendless, upon the streets of Paris.
Molé threw a quick, furtive glance around him. The crowd which had come to gloat over the murdered Terrorist stood about whispering, with heads averted, engrossed in their own affairs. He slid his hand surreptitiously over that of the dead man. With dexterous manipulation he lifted the finger round which glistened the metal ring. Death appeared to have shrivelled the flesh still more upon the bones, to have contracted the knuckles and shrunk the tendons. The ring slid off quite easily. Molé had it in his hand, when suddenly a rough blow struck him on the shoulder.

===Chinese===

这间斗室闷热得难以形容。狭窄的窗子只通往里面的卧室，外头几乎没有一丝空气透进来。一盏恶臭的油灯从低矮的天花板上吊下来，昏黄的光照着死者仰起的脸。
莫莱在那具可怕的形体旁站了一两分钟，沉默而若有所思。浴盆还是他备下的那个样子：一块木板搭在盆上，蒙着布单，只有头和肩膀露在上面，青灰而带着污渍。一只手——左手——以临终剧痛最后一阵痉挛，死死抠着木板的边。
那只手的第四根手指上，那枚贱戒指还在闪着光——马拉说过它不值一偷。可它显然勾起了这可怜虫的贪心；这人忠心伺候了他这几天，如今又要被扔回巴黎街头，忍饥挨饿，无亲无故。
莫莱飞快地、偷偷朝四下扫了一眼。那群跑来对着遇害的恐怖分子幸灾乐祸的人，正各自站着交头接耳，头别向一边，各顾各的心事。他悄悄把手滑到死者的手上，手法灵巧地托起那根闪着金属环的手指。死亡似乎使皮肉在骨头上缩得更紧了，指节收拢，筋腱抽缩。戒指轻轻一捋就下来了。莫莱刚把它攥进手心，肩膀上突然挨了重重一击。

===Original===

“Trying to rob the dead?” a stern voice shouted in his ear. “Are you a disguised aristo, or what?”
At once the whispering ceased. A wave of excitement went round the room. Some people shouted, others pressed forward to gaze on the abandoned wretch who had been caught in the act of committing a gruesome deed.
“Robbing the dead!”
They were experts in evil, most of these men here. Their hands were indelibly stained with some of the foulest crimes ever recorded in history. But there was something ghoulish in this attempt to plunder that awful thing lying there, helpless, in the water. There was also a great relief to nerve-tension in shouting Horror and Anathema with self-righteous indignation; and additional excitement in the suggested “aristo in disguise.”

===Chinese===

“敢偷死人的东西？”一个严厉的声音冲他耳朵吼道。“你是个化了装的贵族佬，还是什么？”
窃窃私语顿时停了。一阵骚动掠过整个屋子。有人叫嚷，有人挤上前来，要看一看这个当场被抓的无耻之徒——他干下的勾当实在骇人。
“偷死人的东西！”
在场的这些人，多半都是作恶的老手。他们的双手沾着史上最卑污的罪行，永远洗不掉。可眼下这桩事——企图劫掠那无助地泡在水里的可怕东西——总有一股盗尸的鬼气。而大呼可怕、口口诛伐，摆出一副义正词严的愤慨，对紧张的神经又是多大的舒泄；再加上“化装的贵族佬”这一层揣测，更添了几分刺激。

===Original===

Molé struggled vigorously. He was powerful and his fists were heavy. But he was soon surrounded, held fast by both arms, whilst half a dozen hands tore at his tattered clothes, searched him to his very skin, for the booty which he was thought to have taken from the dead.
“Leave me alone, curse you!” he shouted, louder than his aggressors. “My name is Paul Molé, I tell you. Ask the citizeness Evrard. I waited on citizen Marat. I prepared his bath. I was the only friend who did not turn away from him in his sickness and his poverty. Leave me alone, I say! Why,” he added, with a hoarse laugh, “Jean Paul in his bath was as naked as on the day he was born!”
“ ’Tis true,” said one of those who had been most active in rummaging through Molé’s grimy rags. “There’s nothing to be found on him.”
But suspicion once aroused was not easily allayed. Molé’s protestations became more and more vigorous and emphatic. His papers were all in order, he vowed. He had them on him: his own identity papers, clear for anyone to see. Someone had dragged them out of his pocket; they were dank and covered with splashes of mud﻿—hardly legible. They were handed over to a man who stood in the immediate circle of light projected by the lamp. He seized them and examined them carefully. This man was short and slight, was dressed in well-made cloth clothes; his hair was held in at the nape of the next in a modish manner with a black taffeta bow. His hands were clean, slender, and claw-like, and he wore the tricolour scarf of office round his waist which proclaimed him to be a member of one of the numerous Committees which tyrannised over the people.

===Chinese===

莫莱拼命挣扎。他力气大，拳头沉。可他很快就被团团围住，两条胳膊死死按住，七八只手撕扯着他那身破衣烂衫，把他搜到了皮肉，找人们认定他从死人身上捞去的赃物。
“放开我，该死的！”他吼得比围攻他的人还响。“我叫保罗·莫莱，告诉你们。去问埃夫拉尔女公民。我伺候过马拉公民。他的浴是我备的。他病里穷里，唯一没躲开他的朋友就是我。我说，放开我！再说，”他哑着嗓子笑了笑，又添一句，“让-保罗泡在浴盆里，跟他落生那天一样，光着呢！”
“倒是真的，”一个翻检莫莱破衣裳翻得最起劲的人说。“他身上什么也没有。”
可疑心一旦起，就不容易消。莫莱的申辩越来越激烈、越来越斩钉截铁。他赌咒说他的证件一应齐全。就带在身上：他自己的身份证件，谁都看得清楚。有人从他口袋里扯了出来；证件潮乎乎的，溅满泥点——几乎没法辨认。证件被递给一个站在灯光正圈里的男人。他抓过去仔细查验。这人矮小瘦削，穿一身剪裁讲究的呢料衣裳；头发在后颈用黑塔夫绸蝴蝶结时兴地束着。一双手干净、细瘦、鹰爪一般；腰间系着三色绶带，表明他是那许多鱼肉人民的委员会中的一员。

===Original===

The papers appeared to be in order, and proclaimed the bearer to be Paul Molé, a native of Besançon, a carpenter by trade. The identity book had recently been signed by Jean Paul Marat, the man’s latest employer, and been countersigned by the Commissary of the section.
The man in the tricolour scarf turned with some acerbity on the crowd who was still pressing round the prisoner.
“Which of you here,” he queried roughly, “levelled an unjust accusation against an honest citizen?”
But, as usual in such cases, no one replied directly to the charge. It was not safe these days to come into conflict with men like Molé. The Committees were all on their side, against the bourgeois as well as against the aristos. This was the reign of the proletariat, and the sansculotte always emerged triumphant in a conflict against the well-to-do. Nor was it good to rouse the ire of citizen Chauvelin, one of the most powerful, as he was the most pitiless members of the Committee of Public Safety. Quiet, sarcastic rather than aggressive, something of the aristo, too, in his clean linen and well-cut clothes, he had not even yielded to the defunct Marat in cruelty and relentless persecution of aristocrats.

===Chinese===

证件看来齐全无误，写明持证人系保罗·莫莱，贝桑松（Besançon）人，木匠营生。身份证最近刚由这人最后的雇主让-保罗·马拉签发，又经本区政委副署。
系三色绶带的人带着几分尖刻，转向仍围住囚犯不肯散的人群。
“你们这里哪一个，”他厉声问道，“诬告了一个老实的公民？”
可是这类事情照例没有人应声认账。这年头同莫莱这路人作对可不安全。各委员会都站在他们那一边，既反资产阶级，也反贵族佬。如今是无产阶级的天下，无套裤汉同有钱人相争，永远是赢家。何况招惹肖维兰更没好处——他是公安委员会里最有权势的一员，也是最心狠的一员。他安静，与其说咄咄逼人不如说冷嘲热讽，一身洁净的衬衣和讲究的衣裳里透着几分贵族气；论对贵族的残忍和赶尽杀绝，连死去的马拉也要让他三分。

===Original===

Evidently his sympathies now were all with Molé, the out-at-elbows, miserable servant of an equally miserable master. His pale-coloured, deep-set eyes challenged the crowd, which gave way before him, slunk back into the corners, away from his coldly threatening glance. Thus he found himself suddenly face to face with Molé, somewhat isolated from the rest, and close to the tin bath with its grim contents. Chauvelin had the papers in his hand.
“Take these, citizen,” he said curtly to the other. “They are all in order.”
He looked up at Molé as he said this, for the latter, though his shoulders were bent, was unusually tall, and Molé took the papers from him. Thus for the space of a few seconds the two men looked into one another’s face, eyes to eyes﻿—and suddenly Chauvelin felt an icy sweat coursing down his spine. The eyes into which he gazed had a strange, ironical twinkle in them, a kind of good-humoured arrogance, whilst through the firm, clear-cut lips, half hidden by a dirty and ill-kempt beard, there came the sound﻿—oh! a mere echo﻿—of a quaint and inane laugh.

===Chinese===

显然，他此刻的同情全在莫莱一边——这个捉襟见肘的可怜仆人，伺候着一个同样可怜的主人。他淡色的深眼睛逼视着人群，人群在他面前退让，缩回角落里，躲开他冷冰冰的威逼目光。就这样，他忽然发现自己与莫莱面对面站着，两人多少离开了众人，紧挨着那只盛着狰狞之物的白铁浴盆。肖维兰手里拿着那叠证件。
“拿着，公民，”他生硬地对对方说。“证件一应齐全。”
说这话时他抬头看着莫莱，因为后者尽管佝偻着肩，个子却高得反常。莫莱从他手里接过证件。就这样，有那么几秒钟，两张脸对视着，眼对着眼——肖维兰忽然觉得一股冷汗顺着脊梁淌下来。他注视的那双眼睛里闪着一种古怪的、讥诮的光，一种好脾气的傲慢；而那两片线条分明、结实的嘴唇——半掩在一部脏兮兮、乱蓬蓬的胡子底下——发出了一阵古怪而傻气的笑声——哦！不过是一丝回响。

===Original===

The whole thing﻿—it seemed like a vision﻿—was over in a second. Chauvelin, sick and faint with the sudden rush of blood to his head, closed his eyes for one brief instant. The next, the crowd had closed round him; anxious inquiries reached his reawakened senses.
But he uttered one quick, hoarse cry:
“Hébert! *A moi!* Are you there?”
“Present, citizen!” came in immediate response. And a tall figure in the tattered uniform affected by the revolutionary guard stepped briskly out of the crowd. Chauvelin’s claw-like hand was shaking visibly.
“The man Molé,” he called in a voice husky with excitement. “Seize him at once! And, name of a dog! do not allow a living soul in or out of the house!”

===Chinese===

这整个——恍如幻象——一秒钟便过去了。肖维兰脑血猛地往头上涌，一阵眩晕恶心，闭了一瞬眼睛。再睁开时，人群已围拢过来；苏醒的感官里灌进了焦急的询问。
可他只发出一声急促嘶哑的喊叫：
“埃贝尔（Hébert）！A moi（到我这儿来）！你在吗？”
“在，公民！”应声立刻传来。一个高大的身影从人群里快步跨出来，穿着革命卫队里流行的那种破旧制服。肖维兰那只鹰爪似的手抖得很明显。
“那个莫莱，”他声音激动得发哑地喊道。“立刻把他拿下！还有，天杀的！不许放一个活人进出这所房子！”

===Original===

Hébert turned on his heel. The next moment his harsh voice was heard above the din and the general hubbub around:
“Quite safe, citizen!” he called to his chief. “We have the rogue right enough!”
There was much shouting and much cursing, a great deal of bustle and confusion, as the men of the Sûreté closed the doors of the defunct demagogue’s lodgings. Some two score men, a dozen or so women, were locked in, inside the few rooms which reeked of dirt and of disease. They jostled and pushed, screamed and protested. For two or three minutes the din was quite deafening. Simonne Evrard pushed her way up to the forefront of the crowd.

===Chinese===

埃贝尔一转身。紧接着，他那粗厉的嗓门便压过四周的喧嚷嘈杂响了起来：
“万无一失，公民！”他朝上级喊道。“那家伙稳稳在我们手里！”
治安队（Sûreté）的队员们关上死了的煽动家住所的各扇房门时，一片叫嚷咒骂，忙乱嘈杂。大约四十来个男人、十几个女人，被锁在那几间散发着污秽与疾病气息的屋子里。他们推推搡搡，尖叫抗议。有两三分钟，那喧嚣震耳欲聋。西莫娜·埃夫拉尔挤到了人群最前头。

===Original===

“What is this I hear?” she queried peremptorily. “Who is accusing citizen Molé? And of what, I should like to know? I am responsible for everyone inside these apartments﻿ ﻿… and if citizen Marat were still alive﻿—”
Chauvelin appeared unaware of all the confusion and of the woman’s protestations. He pushed his way through the crowd to the corner of the anteroom where Molé stood, crouching and hunched up, his grimy hands idly fingering the papers which Chauvelin had returned to him a moment ago. Otherwise he did not move.
He stood, silent and sullen; and when Chauvelin, who had succeeded in mastering his emotion, gave the peremptory command: “Take this man to the depot at once. And do not allow him one instant out of your sight!” he made no attempt at escape.
He allowed Hébert and the men to seize him, to lead him away. He followed without a word, without a struggle. His massive figure was hunched up like that of an old man; his hands, which still clung to his identity papers, trembled slightly like those of a man who is very frightened and very helpless. The men of the Sûreté handled him very roughly, but he made no protest. The woman Evrard did all the protesting, vowing that the people would not long tolerate such tyranny. She even forced her way up to Hébert. With a gesture of fury she tried to strike him in the face, and continued, with a loud voice, her insults and objurgations, until, with a movement of his bayonet, he pushed her roughly out of the way.
After that Paul Molé, surrounded by the guard, was led without ceremony out of the house. Chauvelin gazed after him as if he had been brought face to face with a ghoul.

===Chinese===

“我这是听见了什么？”她专横地质问。“是谁在告莫莱公民？告他什么，我倒要请问？这宅子里每一个人我都担着干系……若是马拉公民还活着——”
肖维兰仿佛对这场混乱、对这女人的抗议一概浑然不觉。他从人堆里挤到前厅一角，莫莱正蹲缩在那里，佝偻着背，污黑的手漫不经心地捻着肖维兰刚才退还给他的证件。此外一动不动。
他站着，沉默而阴郁；等终于按捺住激动的肖维兰发出不容分说的命令：“立刻把这人押到拘押所去。一瞬也不许让他离开你们的视线！”他也没有一点逃跑的意思。
他任凭埃贝尔和那伙人捉住他，带走。他一言不发地跟着走，一点不挣扎。他那壮硕的身躯佝偻得像个老头；仍旧攥着身份证的那双手微微发抖，像一个吓得厉害、全然无助的人。治安队队员们对他手脚很粗，可他一声不吭。抗议全叫埃夫拉尔女人包了，她赌咒说人民不会长久容忍这样的暴虐。她甚至一路硬挤到埃贝尔跟前，狂怒地抬手要打他的脸，又扯着嗓子骂个不停，直到他用刺刀一拨，把她粗暴地推到一边。
此后，保罗·莫莱便被卫兵围着，毫不客气地押出了那所房子。肖维兰目送着他，仿佛刚同食尸鬼打了照面。

### V / ### 五

===Original===

Chauvelin hurried to the depot. After those few seconds wherein he had felt dazed, incredulous, almost under a spell, he had quickly regained the mastery of his nerves, and regained, too, that intense joy which anticipated triumph is wont to give.
In the out-at-elbows, half-starved servant of the murdered Terrorist, citizen Chauvelin, of the Committee of Public Safety, had recognised his arch enemy, that meddlesome and adventurous Englishman who chose to hide his identity under the pseudonym of the Scarlet Pimpernel. He knew that he could reckon on Hébert; his orders not to allow the prisoner one moment out of sight would of a certainty be strictly obeyed.
Hébert, indeed, a few moments later, greeted his chief outside the doors of the depot with the welcome news that Paul Molé was safely under lock and key.

===Chinese===

肖维兰匆匆赶往拘押所。那几秒钟里他感到的晕眩、难以置信、几如中邪，都已过去；他迅速重新驾驭了自己的神经，也重新尝到了胜利在望的人常有的那种强烈的喜悦。
在遇害的恐怖分子那个衣衫褴褛、饿得半死的仆人身上，公安委员会的肖维兰公民认出了他的死敌——那个爱管闲事、胆大包天的英国人，那人惯于借用“红花侠”这个化名掩藏自己的身份。他知道埃贝尔靠得住；他下的那道一瞬也不许让囚犯离开视线的命令，必会得到严格执行。
果然，片刻之后，埃贝尔在拘押所门外迎接他的上级，带来好消息：保罗·莫莱已稳稳地锁进了牢房。

===Original===

“You had no trouble with him?” Chauvelin queried, with ill-concealed eagerness.
“No, no! citizen, no trouble,” was Hébert’s quick reply. “He seems to be a well-known rogue in these parts,” he continued with a complacent guffaw; “and some of his friends tried to hustle us at the corner of the Rue de Tourraine; no doubt with a view to getting the prisoner away. But we were too strong for them, and Paul Molé is now sulking in his cell and still protesting that his arrest is an outrage against the liberty of the people.”
Chauvelin made no further remark. He was obviously too excited to speak. Pushing past Hébert and the men of the Sûreté who stood about the dark and narrow passages of the depot, he sought the Commissary of the Section in the latter’s office.

===Chinese===

“没费事吧？”肖维兰问道，掩饰不住的急切。
“没有，没有！公民，没费事，”埃贝尔飞快答道。“他在这带像是个出了名的无赖，”他接着说，得意地哈哈一笑；“他的一些同伙还想在图雷讷街（Rue de Tourraine）拐角上围挤我们；不用说，是想把囚犯劫走。可他们哪里是我们的对手，保罗·莫莱这会儿正在牢房里生闷气，还嚷嚷说抓他是侵犯人民自由的暴行呢。”
肖维兰没有再说什么。他显然激动得说不出话。他推开堵在拘押所阴暗窄廊里的埃贝尔和治安队员们，径去区政委的办公室找他。

===Original===

It was now close upon ten o’clock. The citizen Commissary Cuisinier had finished his work for the day and was preparing to go home and to bed. He was a family man, had been a respectable bourgeois in his day, and though he was a rank opportunist and had sacrificed not only his political convictions but also his conscience to the exigencies of the time, he still nourished in his innermost heart a secret contempt for the revolutionary brigands who ruled over France at this hour.
To any other man than citizen Chauvelin, the citizen Commissary would, no doubt, have given a curt refusal to a request to see a prisoner at this late hour of the evening. But Chauvelin was not a man to be denied, and whilst muttering various objections in his ill-kempt beard, Cuisinier, nevertheless, gave orders that the citizen was to be conducted at once to the cells.
Paul Molé had in truth turned sulky. The turnkey vowed that the prisoner had hardly stirred since first he had been locked up in the common cell. He sat in a corner at the end of the bench, with his face turned to the wall, and paid no heed either to his fellow-prisoners or to the facetious remarks of the warder.
Chauvelin went up to him, made some curt remark. Molé kept an obstinate shoulder turned towards him﻿—a grimy shoulder, which showed naked through a wide rent in his blouse. This portion of the cell was well-nigh in total darkness; the feeble shaft of light which came through the open door hardly penetrated to this remote angle of the squalid burrow. The same sense of mystery and unreality overcame Chauvelin again as he looked on the miserable creature in whom, an hour ago, he had recognised the super-exquisite Sir Percy Blakeney. Now he could only see a vague outline in the gloom: the stooping shoulders, the long limbs, that naked piece of shoulder which caught a feeble reflex from the distant light. Nor did any amount of none too gentle prodding on the part of the warder induce him to change his position.

===Chinese===

这时已近十点。屈齐尼耶（Cuisinier）政委公民已办完一天的公务，正准备回家睡觉。他是个有家室的人，当年也做过体面的资产阶级；虽然他是个十足的随风倒，为了时势所迫不但牺牲了政治信念，也牺牲了良心，可在心底最深处，他对此刻统治着法兰西的革命暴徒仍怀着一份隐秘的轻蔑。
换了旁人，屈齐尼耶政委公民对这么深更半夜要看囚犯的要求，准会一口回绝。可肖维兰这个人，是谁也驳回不得的；屈齐尼耶一面在乱糟糟的胡子里嘟囔着种种不满，一面还是下令立刻引这位公民去牢房。
保罗·莫莱果真在闹脾气。狱卒赌咒说，囚犯自打进了一般犯人的大牢就几乎没动过窝。他坐在长凳尽头的角落里，脸冲着墙，同狱的犯人也好，看守的插科打诨也好，他一概不理。
肖维兰走上前去，生硬地说了句什么。莫莱固执地拿一边肩膀对着他——一个污黑的肩膀，从罩衫一道大口子里露着肉。牢房这一角几乎全黑；从敞开的牢门透进来的那一线微光，照不进这个肮脏洞穴的偏远一隅。一个小时前，他还在这个可怜虫身上认出了那位精致入骨的珀西·布莱克尼爵士；此刻望着这人，那种神秘而不真实的感觉又攫住了肖维兰。现在他只能看见昏暗里一个模糊的轮廓：佝偻的肩，长长的四肢，还有那截映着远处微光、裸露的肩膀。看守又是戳又是捣，也没能叫他挪动半分。

===Original===

“Leave him alone,” said Chauvelin curly at last. “I have seen all that I wished to see.”
The cell was insufferably hot and stuffy. Chauvelin, finical and queasy, turned away with a shudder of disgust. There was nothing to be got now out of a prolonged interview with his captured foe. He had seen him: that was sufficient. He had seen the super-exquisite Sir Percy Blakeney locked up in a common cell with some of the most scrubby and abject rogues which the slums of indigent Paris could yield, having apparently failed in some undertaking which had demanded for its fulfilment not only tattered clothes and grimy hands, but menial service with a beggarly and disease-ridden employer, whose very propinquity must have been positive torture to the fastidious dandy.
Of a truth this was sufficient for the gratification of any revenge. Chauvelin felt that he could now go contentedly to rest after an evening’s work excellently done.
He gave order that Molé should be put in a separate cell, denied all intercourse with anyone outside or in the depot, and that he should be guarded on sight day and night. After that he went his way.

===Chinese===

“别碰他了，”肖维兰终于没好气地说。“我要看的都看到了。”
牢房闷热得叫人受不了。肖维兰素来娇贵，肠胃浅，厌恶得打了个寒噤，转过身去。跟这个已经落网的仇敌多周旋，如今已捞不到什么。他见过他了：这就够了。他已亲眼看到那位精致入骨的珀西·布莱克尼爵士，锁在一间普通牢房里，同赤贫巴黎贫民窟所能贡献的最卑贱下作的无赖们作伴；显然他在某桩营生里栽了跟头——那营生不但要破衣裳和脏手，还要低声下气伺候一个乞丐似的一身是病的主子，而对这位挑剔的纨绔子来说，光是挨着这么个人，就无异于酷刑。
说实在的，这对任何复仇心都是足够的餍足了。肖维兰觉得，一晚上的差事干得如此漂亮，如今可以心满意足地去安歇了。
他下令把莫莱单独关押，不许与所内外任何人往来，并日夜盯守。之后便自行去了。

### VI / ### 六

===Original===

The following morning citizen Chauvelin, of the Committee of Public Safety, gave due notice to citizen Fouquier-Tinville, the Public Prosecutor, that the dangerous English spy, known to the world as the Scarlet Pimpernel, was now safely under lock and key, and that he must be transferred to the Abbaye prison forthwith and to the guillotine as quickly as might be. No one was to take any risks this time; there must be no question either of discrediting his famous League or of obtaining other more valuable information out of him. Such methods had proved disastrous in the past.
There were no safe Englishmen these days, except the dead ones, and it would not take citizen Fouquier-Tinville much thought or time to frame an indictment against the notorious Scarlet Pimpernel, which would do away with the necessity of a prolonged trial. The revolutionary government was at war with England now, and short work could be made of all poisonous spies.
By order, therefore, of the Committee of Public Safety, the prisoner, Paul Molé, was taken out of the cells of the depot and conveyed in a closed carriage to the Abbaye prison. Chauvelin had the pleasure of watching this gratifying spectacle from the windows of the Commissariat. When he saw the closed carriage drive away, with Hébert and two men inside and two others on the box, he turned to citizen Commissary Cuisinier with a sigh of intense satisfaction.
“There goes the most dangerous enemy our glorious revolution has had,” he said, with an accent of triumph which he did not attempt to disguise.

===Chinese===

第二天上午，公安委员会的肖维兰公民正式通知公诉人富基耶-坦维尔（Fouquier-Tinville）公民：那个世上称为红花侠的危险英国奸细，如今已稳稳在押，必须立即转押阿贝伊监狱，尽快送上断头台。这一回谁也不许再冒风险；既不必再去败坏他那个鼎鼎大名的同盟的名声，也不必再想从他嘴里掏出什么更有价值的情报。此类手法，过去已吃过大亏。
这年头，除了死了的，没有安全的英国人。要给那个臭名昭著的红花侠罗织一份起诉书，免掉冗长审判的麻烦，用不着富基耶-坦维尔公民花多少心思、多少时间。革命政府如今正与英国交战，对付一切毒蝎似的奸细，皆可从速了断。
于是，奉公安委员会之命，囚犯保罗·莫莱被提出拘押所牢房，用一辆密闭马车押往阿贝伊监狱。肖维兰有幸从政委办公室的窗口观赏了这幕惬意的景象。他望着那辆密闭马车驶远——埃贝尔带两个人坐在车里，另外两个坐在车夫座上——便带着一声深深满足的叹息，转向屈齐尼耶政委公民。
“我们光荣的革命所遭遇的最危险的敌人，去了，”他说道，语调里的得意毫不掩饰。

===Original===

Cuisinier shrugged his shoulders.
“Possibly,” he retorted curtly. “He did not seem to me to be very dangerous and his papers were quite in order.”
To this assertion Chauvelin made no reply. Indeed, how could he explain to this stolid official the subtle workings of an intriguing brain? Had he himself not had many a proof of how little the forging of identity papers or of passports troubled the members of that accursed League? Had he not seen the Scarlet Pimpernel, that exquisite Sir Percy Blakeney, under disguises that were so grimy and so loathsome that they would have repelled the most abject, suborned spy?
Indeed, all that was wanted now was the assurance that Hébert﻿—who himself had a deadly and personal grudge against the Scarlet Pimpernel﻿—would not allow him for one moment out of his sight.

===Chinese===

屈齐尼耶耸了耸肩。
“也许吧，”他生硬地回了一句。“我看他不像有什么危险，他的证件也一应齐全。”
对这句话，肖维兰没有作答。是啊，他怎能向这个木头脑袋的官吏解释清楚一颗诡谲头脑的精微运作？伪造身份证件也好，伪造通行证也好，在那个可诅咒的同盟成员眼里算得了什么——这样的证据，他自己见得还少吗？那位精致入骨的珀西·布莱克尼爵士——红花侠本人——戴上过的那些化装，又脏又恶心，连最下贱的被收买的密探都要退避三舍，这他难道没有亲眼见过？
其实，如今唯一要紧的，是确认埃贝尔——他本人同红花侠也有不共戴天的私仇——会一瞬也不让他离开自己的视线。

===Original===

Fortunately as to this, there was no fear. One hint to Hébert and the man was as keen, as determined, as Chauvelin himself.
“Set your mind at rest, citizen,” he said with a rough oath. “I guessed how matters stood the moment you gave me the order. I knew you would not take all that trouble for a real Paul Molé. But have no fear! That accursed Englishman has not been one second out of my sight, from the moment I arrested him in the late citizen Marat’s lodgings, and by Satan! he shall not be either, until I have seen his impudent head fall under the guillotine.”
He himself, he added, had seen to the arrangements for the disposal of the prisoner in the Abbaye: an inner cell, partially partitioned off in one of the guardrooms, with no egress of its own, and only a tiny grated air-hole high up in the wall, which gave on an outside corridor, and through which not even a cat could manage to slip. Oh! the prisoner was well guarded! The citizen Representative need, of a truth, have no fear! Three or four men﻿—of the best and most trustworthy﻿—had not left the guardroom since the morning. He himself (Hébert) had kept the accursed Englishman in sight all night, had personally conveyed him to the Abbaye, and had only left the guardroom a moment ago in order to speak with the citizen Representative. He was going back now at once, and would not move until the order came for the prisoner to be conveyed to the Court of Justice and thence to summary execution.
For the nonce, Hébert concluded with a complacent chuckle, the Englishman was still crouching dejectedly in a corner of his new cell, with little of him visible save that naked shoulder through his torn shirt, which, in the process of transference from one prison to another, had become a shade more grimy than before.

===Chinese===

所幸这一点无须担心。只需向埃贝尔点一句，那人便同肖维兰本人一样上心、一样坚决。
“您把心放到肚子里，公民，”他粗声粗气地起了个誓说。“您一下命令我就猜出了事情的底细。我知道，为一个货真价实的保罗·莫莱，您犯不上费这么大的事。可您放心！从我在死去的马拉公民住处逮住他那一刻起，那该死的英国人一秒钟也没离开过我的视线，我对撒旦起誓！往后也不会，直到我亲眼看见他那颗狂妄的脑袋滚落断头台！”
他又说，囚犯在阿贝伊的安置，是他亲自一手料理的：一间内牢，从一间卫兵室里隔出一半，没有自己的出口，墙上高处只开着一个装了铁栅的小气孔，通外面一条走廊，连一只猫也钻不过去。哦！囚犯看守得严实着呢！公民代表实在无须担心！三四个人——最拔尖、最可靠的——从早晨起就没离开过卫兵室。他本人（埃贝尔）整夜盯着那该死的英国人，亲自把他押到阿贝伊，方才只是为了向公民代表禀报才离开卫兵室。他这马上就回去，押送囚犯出庭受审、随即正法的命令不下来，他绝不挪窝。
埃贝尔得意地嘿嘿一笑，总结道：眼下那英国人还在新牢房的角落里蔫头耷脑地蹲着，除了破衬衫豁口里那截光肩膀，几乎瞧不见别的；那肩膀呢，从一所监狱挪到另一所的路上，又比先前脏了一层。

===Original===

Chauvelin nodded, well satisfied. He commended Hébert for his zeal, rejoiced with him over the inevitable triumph. It would be well to avenge that awful humiliation at Calais last September. Nevertheless, he felt anxious and nervy; he could not comprehend the apathy assumed by the factitious Molé. That the apathy was assumed Chauvelin was keen enough to guess. What it portended he could not conjecture. But that the Englishman would make a desperate attempt at escape was, of course, a foregone conclusion. It rested with Hébert and a guard that could neither be bribed nor fooled into treachery, to see that such an attempt remained abortive.
What, however, had puzzled citizen Chauvelin all along was the motive which had induced Sir Percy Blakeney to play the role of menial to Jean Paul Marat. Behind it there lay, undoubtedly, one of those subtle intrigues for which that insolent Scarlet Pimpernel was famous; and with it was associated an attempt at theft upon the murdered body of the demagogue﻿ ﻿… an attempt which had failed, seeing that the supposititious Paul Molé had been searched and nothing suspicious been found upon his person.
Nevertheless, thoughts of that attempted theft disturbed Chauvelin’s equanimity. The old legend of the crumpled roseleaf was applicable in his case. Something of his intense satisfaction would pale if this final enterprise of the audacious adventurer were to be brought to a triumphant close in the end.

===Chinese===

肖维兰点头，十分满意。他嘉奖了埃贝尔的干劲，同他一起庆贺那必将到来的胜利。去年九月在加来吃的那场奇耻大辱，正好报一报。话虽如此，他心里仍觉焦躁不安；那个假冒的莫莱装出来的死气沉沉，他怎么也想不透。那死气沉沉是装出来的——这一点肖维兰精明得很，猜得到。它预示着什么，他却揣度不出。不过，那英国人必会拼死一逃，这自然是板上钉钉的事。能不能叫这一逃胎死腹中，就看埃贝尔和一支既收买不了也骗不倒的看守了。
不过，始终令肖维兰困惑的，是珀西·布莱克尼爵士甘心给让-保罗·马拉当仆役的动机。这背后无疑藏着那种诡秘的计谋——那个狂妄的红花侠正是以此闻名；与计谋相连的，是对煽动家尸身的一次偷窃企图……企图败露了，因为那个冒牌的保罗·莫莱已被搜过身，身上并未搜出任何可疑的东西。
然而，那桩未遂的偷窃总搅扰着肖维兰的心绪。那句皱了一片玫瑰叶便睡不安枕的老典故，正应在他身上。倘若那胆大包天的冒险家这最后一番事业到头来竟得奏凯，他这份浓烈的快意，终归要褪掉几分颜色。

### VII / ### 七

===Original===

That same forenoon, on his return from the Abbaye and the depot, Chauvelin found that a visitor was waiting for him. A woman, who gave her name as Jeannette Maréchal, desired to speak with the citizen Representative. Chauvelin knew the woman as his colleague Marat’s maid-of-all-work, and he gave orders that she should be admitted at once.
Jeannette Maréchal, tearful and not a little frightened, assured the citizen Representative that her errand was urgent. Her late employer had so few friends; she did not know to whom to turn until she bethought herself of citizen Chauvelin. It took him some little time to disentangle the tangible facts out of the woman’s voluble narrative. At first the words: “Child﻿ ﻿… Chemin de Pantin﻿ ﻿… Leridan,” were only a medley of sounds which conveyed no meaning to his ear. But when occasion demanded, citizen Chauvelin was capable of infinite patience. Gradually he understood what the woman was driving at.
“The child, citizen!” she reiterated excitedly. “What’s to be done about him? I know that citizen Marat would have wished﻿—”
“Never mind now what citizen Marat would have wished,” Chauvelin broke in quietly. “Tell me first who this child is.”

===Chinese===

同一天上午，肖维兰从阿贝伊和拘押所回来，发现有一位访客在等他。一个女人自称让内特·马雷夏尔，求见公民代表。肖维兰认得这女人是他同僚马拉家的打杂女仆，便吩咐立刻放她进来。
让内特·马雷夏尔眼泪汪汪、又惊又怕，一口咬定她的事十万火急。她故去的主子朋友太少；想来想去，她才想起肖维兰公民来。那女人絮絮叨叨讲了一大篇，肖维兰费了些工夫才从里面理出些实在的头绪。起初“孩子……潘坦路……勒里当”这几个词，在他耳中不过是一串不知所云的声音。可只要事情需要，肖维兰公民便有无穷的耐心。渐渐地，他听懂了这女人的来意。
“那孩子，公民！”她激动地翻来覆去说。“他可怎么办哪？我知道马拉公民生前会愿意——”
“马拉公民愿不愿意，眼下不用管，”肖维兰平静地打断她。“先告诉我，这孩子是谁。”

===Original===

“I do not know, citizen,” she replied.
“How do you mean, you do not know? Then I pray you, citizeness, what is all this pother about?”
“About the child, citizen,” reiterated Jeannette obstinately.
“What child?”
“The child whom citizen Marat adopted last year and kept at that awful house on the Chemin de Pantin.”

===Chinese===

“我不知道，公民，”她答道。
“怎么说，你不知道？那么请问，女公民，这么大惊小怪究竟为的什么？”
“为那孩子呀，公民，”让内特固执地又说一遍。
“哪个孩子？”
“马拉公民去年收养、放在潘坦路那所可怕宅子里的那个孩子呀。”

===Original===

“I did not know citizen Marat had adopted a child,” remarked Chauvelin thoughtfully.
“No one knew,” she rejoined. “Not even citizeness Evrard. I was the only one who knew. I had to go and see the child once every month. It was a wretched, miserable brat,” the woman went on, her shrivelled old breast vaguely stirred, mayhap, by some atrophied feeling of motherhood. “More than half-starved﻿ ﻿… and the look in its eyes, citizen! It was enough to make you cry! I could see by his poor little emaciated body and his nice little hands and feet that he ought never to have been put in that awful house, where﻿—”
She paused, and that quick look of furtive terror, which was so often to be met with in the eyes of the timid these days, crept into her wrinkled face.
“Well, citizeness,” Chauvelin rejoined quietly, “why don’t you proceed? That awful house, you were saying. Where and what is that awful house of which you speak?”
“The place kept by citizen Leridan, just by Bassin de l’Ourcq,” the woman murmured. “You know it, citizen.”

===Chinese===

“我倒不知道马拉公民收养过孩子，”肖维兰若有所思地说。
“谁都不知道，”她答道。“连埃夫拉尔女公民也不知道。就我一个人知道。我每个月都得去看那孩子一回。那是个可怜兮兮、遭罪的小崽子，”她接着说，那干瘪的老胸脯里，也许有什么萎缩了的母性在隐隐翻动。“饿得只剩半条命……那双眼睛里的神气啊，公民！叫人看了直想哭！瞧他那瘦骨嶙峋的小身子、那双好看的小手小脚，就知道他压根儿不该被塞进那所可怕的宅子去，在那里——”
她顿住了，那种惊惶躲闪的神色——这些日子在胆小人眼睛里常能见到的那种——悄悄爬上她布满皱纹的脸。
“怎么，女公民，”肖维兰平静地接着说，“怎么不往下讲了？你刚说那所可怕的宅子。你说的那所宅子，在哪儿，是干什么的？”
“勒里当（Leridan）公民开的那处地方，就在乌尔克水渠（Bassin de l'Ourcq）边上，”女人低声说。“您知道的，公民。”

===Original===

Chauvelin nodded. He was beginning to understand.
“Well, now, tell me,” he said, with that bland patience which had so oft served him in good stead in his unavowable profession. “Tell me. Last year citizen Marat adopted﻿—we’ll say adopted﻿—a child, whom he placed in the Leridans’ house on the Pantin road. Is that correct?”
“That is just how it is, citizen. And I﻿—”
“One moment,” he broke in somewhat more sternly, as the woman’s garrulity was getting on his nerves. “As you say, I know the Leridans’ house. I have had cause to send children there myself. Children of aristos or of fat bourgeois, whom it was our duty to turn into good citizens. They are not pampered there, I imagine,” he went on drily; “and if citizen Marat sent his﻿—er﻿—adopted son there, it was not with a view to having him brought up as an aristo, what?”

===Chinese===

肖维兰点了点头。他开始明白了。
“那么，现在告诉我，”他说，带着那副温吞吞的耐心——在他那说不出口的行当里，这耐心屡屡帮了他的忙。“告诉我。去年马拉公民收养了——就算收养了吧——一个孩子，把他安置在潘坦路上勒里当夫妇家里。是不是这样？”
“正是这么回事，公民。还有我——”
“等一下，”他打断了话头，口气严厉了些——这女人的絮叨开始让他心烦。“正如你说的，勒里当家的那所宅子我知道。我自己也有过缘由往那里送孩子。贵族佬的、肥资产阶级的孩子，把他们改造成好公民是我们的本分。那地方可不会娇惯孩子，我想是吧，”他冷冷地接着说；“马拉公民若是把他的——嗯——养子送到那儿去，总不会是指望把他当贵族少爷养吧，嗯？”

===Original===

“The child was not to be brought up at all,” the woman said gruffly. “I have often heard citizen Marat say that he hoped the brat would prove a thief when he grew up, and would take to alcoholism like a duck takes to water.”
“And you know nothing of the child’s parents?”
“Nothing, citizen. I had to go to Pantin once a month and have a look at him and report to citizen Marat. But I always had the same tale to tell. The child was looking more and more like a young reprobate every time I saw him.”
“Did citizen Marat pay the Leridans for keeping the child?”
“Oh, no, citizen! The Leridans make a trade of the children by sending them out to beg. But this one was not to be allowed out yet. Citizen Marat’s orders were very stern, and he was wont to terrify the Leridans with awful threats of the guillotine if they ever allowed the child out of their sight.”

===Chinese===

“压根儿就没打算把他养大成人，”女人粗声说。“我常听马拉公民说，他巴望那崽子长大当个小偷，学酗酒就像鸭子下水一样自然。”
“孩子的父母，你一点也不知道？”
“一点不知道，公民。我每月得去一趟潘坦，看看他，再向马拉公民回话。可我回的永远是那几句。每回见，那孩子都越发像个二流子。”
“马拉公民出钱给勒里当夫妇养这孩子吗？”
“哦，不，公民！勒里当夫妇拿孩子做买卖，打发他们出去讨钱。只是这一个还不许放出去。马拉公民的吩咐严厉得很，他惯会拿断头台吓唬勒里当夫妇，说他们若让孩子离开视线半步，就如何如何。”

===Original===

Chauvelin sat silent for a while. A ray of light had traversed the dark and tortuous ways of his subtle brain. While he mused the woman became impatient. She continued to talk on with the volubility peculiar to her kind. He paid no heed to her, until one phrase struck his ear.
“So now,” Jeannette Maréchal was saying, “I don’t know what to do. The ring has disappeared, and the Leridans are suspicious.”
“The ring?” queried Chauvelin curtly. “What ring?”
“As I was telling you, citizen,” she replied querulously, “when I went to see the child, the citizen Marat always gave me this ring to show to the Leridans. Without I brought the ring they would not admit me inside their door. They were so terrified with all the citizen’s threats of the guillotine.”
“And now you say the ring has disappeared. Since when?”

===Chinese===

肖维兰坐着沉默了一会儿。一线光已穿透他那颗诡谲头脑里黑暗曲折的迷宫。他出神的当儿，那女人等得不耐烦了，又拿她那类人特有的饶舌滔滔讲下去。他全不理会，直到有一句话撞进他耳朵。
“所以这会儿，”让内特·马雷夏尔正说着，“我不知道该怎么办了。戒指不见了，勒里当两口子起了疑心。”
“戒指？”肖维兰截口问。“什么戒指？”
“我不是正跟您说嘛，公民，”她抱怨似的答道，“每回我去看孩子，马拉公民总把这枚戒指交给我，拿给勒里当两口子看。不带着戒指，他们连门也不让我进。公民拿断头台那样那样地吓唬，把他们吓破胆了。”
“现在你说戒指不见了。什么时候的事？”

===Original===

“Well, citizen,” replied Jeannette blandly, “since you took poor Paul Molé into custody.”
“What do you mean?” Chauvelin riposted. “What had Paul Molé to do with the child and the ring?”
“Only this, citizen, that he was to have gone to Pantin last night instead of me. And thankful I was not to have to go. Citizen Marat gave the ring to Molé, I suppose. I know he intended to give it to him. He spoke to me about it just before that execrable woman came and murdered him. Anyway, the ring has gone and Molé too. So I imagine that Molé has the ring and﻿—”
“That’s enough!” Chauvelin broke in roughly. “You can go!”

===Chinese===

“咳，公民，”让内特平平静静地答道，“自打您把可怜的保罗·莫莱抓起来那天起。”
“你这是什么意思？”肖维兰反诘。“保罗·莫莱跟这孩子和戒指有什么相干？”
“不过是这样，公民，昨儿晚上原该是他替我去潘坦的。我还正乐得不用跑一趟。戒指是马拉公民给了莫莱了，我猜。我知道他本来就打算给他。就在那个可恶的女人进来行刺之前，他还跟我提过这事儿。反正，戒指没了，莫莱也没了。所以我想，戒指准在莫莱手里，而且——”
“够了！”肖维兰粗暴地打断她。“你可以走了！”

===Original===

“But, citizen﻿—”
“You can go, I said,” he reiterated sharply. “The matter of the child and the Leridans and the ring no longer concerns you. You understand?”
“Y﻿—y﻿—yes, citizen,” murmured Jeannette, vaguely terrified.
And of a truth the change in citizen Chauvelin’s demeanour was enough to scare any timid creature. Not that he raved or ranted or screamed. Those were not his ways. He still sat beside his desk as he had done before, and his slender hand, so like the talons of a vulture, was clenched upon the arm of his chair. But there was such a look of inward fury and of triumph in his pale, deep-set eyes, such lines of cruelty around his thin, closed lips, that Jeannette Maréchal, even with the picture before her mind of Jean Paul Marat in his maddest moods, fled, with the unreasoning terror of her kind, before the sternly controlled, fierce passion of this man.

===Chinese===

“可是，公民——”
“我说了，你可以走了，”他厉声重申。“孩子、勒里当两口子、戒指，这一切都与你无干了。明白吗？”
“是——是——是，公民，”让内特喃喃应道，吓得魂不附体。
说实在的，肖维兰公民神色的变化，足以吓坏任何一个胆小的人。倒不是他又吼又闹又叫。那不是他的做派。他仍旧像方才一样坐在桌旁，那只瘦削的、活像秃鹫利爪的手，紧扣着椅子扶手。可他那双淡色的深眼睛里，燃烧着那样一种内心的狂怒与得意，那两片抿紧的薄唇周围，刻着那样残忍的纹路，以至于让内特·马雷夏尔——纵然脑子里还存着让-保罗·马拉最癫狂时的模样——也被这个男人森然克制的炽烈狂怒吓跑了，带着她那类人懵懂的恐怖。

===Original===

Chauvelin never noticed that she went. He sat for a long time, silent and immovable. Now he understood. Thank all the Powers of Hate and Revenge, no thought of disappointment was destined to embitter the overflowing cup of his triumph. He had not only brought his arch-enemy to his knees, but had foiled one of his audacious ventures. How clear the whole thing was! The false Paul Molé, the newly acquired menial in the household of Marat, had wormed himself into the confidence of his employer in order to wrest from him the secret of the aristo’s child. Bravo! bravo! my gallant Scarlet Pimpernel! Chauvelin now could see it all. Tragedies such as that which had placed an aristo’s child in the power of a cunning demon like Marat were not rare these days, and Chauvelin had been fitted by nature and by temperament to understand and appreciate an execrable monster of the type of Jean Paul Marat.
And Paul Molé, the grimy, degraded servant of the indigent demagogue, the loathsome mask which hid the fastidious personality of Sir Percy Blakeney, had made a final and desperate effort to possess himself of the ring which would deliver the child into his power. Now, having failed in his machinations, he was safe under lock and key﻿—guarded on sight. The next twenty-four hours would see him unmasked, awaiting his trial and condemnation under the scathing indictment prepared by Fouquier-Tinville, the unerring Public Prosecutor. The day after that, the tumbril and the guillotine for that execrable English spy, and the boundless sense of satisfaction that his last intrigue had aborted in such a signal and miserable manner.
Of a truth Chauvelin at this hour had every cause to be thankful, and it was with a light heart that he set out to interview the Leridans.

===Chinese===

她走了，肖维兰根本没有觉察。他坐了很久，沉默而纹丝不动。现在他明白了。感谢一切仇恨与复仇的势力，他的凯旋之杯满溢，注定再不会掺进失望的苦味了。他不只叫他的死敌跪倒在地，还挫败了他一次胆大包天的奇袭。整个事情多清楚！那个假的保罗·莫莱，马拉家新收的下人，一步步蛀进了主人的信任，为的是从他口中撬出那个贵族孩子下落的秘密。好啊！好啊！我豪侠的红花侠！肖维兰如今全看明白了。像这样让一个贵族的孩子落入马拉这类狡诈恶魔之手的悲剧，这些日子并不稀罕；而肖维兰的禀性与脾性，天生就能理解并赏识让-保罗·马拉这一类的可憎怪物。
而保罗·莫莱——赤贫煽动家家那个污秽下作的仆人，掩着珀西·布莱克尼爵士娇贵真面目的那副可憎面具——为攥住那枚戒指作了最后的拼死努力——有了它，孩子便任他领走。如今计谋败露，他已稳稳锁在牢里——日夜有人盯守。不出二十四小时，他就会被剥去伪装，在百无一失的公诉人富基耶-坦维尔起草的那份犀利起诉书下候审判死。再过一天，这个可憎的英国奸细就该上死囚车、上断头台了，而他的最后一次密谋竟落得如此昭彰、如此凄惨的流产，那份快意，将无边无际。
说实在的，此时此刻的肖维兰实在有一切理由感恩戴德。他动身去见勒里当夫妇时，心里轻快得很。

### VIII / ### 八

===Original===

The Leridans, anxious, obsequious, terrified, were only too ready to obey the citizen Representative in all things.
They explained with much complacency that, even though they were personally acquainted with Jeannette Maréchal, when the citizeness presented herself this very morning without the ring they had refused her permission to see the brat.
Chauvelin, who in his own mind had already reconstructed the whole tragedy of the stolen child, was satisfied that Marat could not have chosen more efficient tools for the execution of his satanic revenge than these two hideous products of revolutionary Paris.
Grasping, cowardly, and avaricious, the Leridans would lend themselves to any abomination for a sufficiency of money; but no money on earth would induce them to risk their own necks in the process. Marat had obviously held them by threats of the guillotine. They knew the power of the “Friend of the People,” and feared him accordingly. Chauvelin’s scarf of office, his curt, authoritative manner, had an equally awe-inspiring effect upon the two miserable creatures. They became absolutely abject, cringing, maudlin in their protestations of goodwill and loyalty. No one, they vowed, should as much as see the child﻿—ring or no ring﻿—save the citizen Representative himself. Chauvelin, however, had no wish to see the child. He was satisfied that its name was Lannoy﻿—for the child had remembered it when first he had been brought to the Leridans. Since then he had apparently forgotten it, even though he often cried after his “*Maman!*”

===Chinese===

勒里当夫妇又急又怕、谄媚巴结，只求事事听从公民代表的吩咐。
他们满脸赔笑地解释说，虽说他们同让内特·马雷夏尔本人熟识，今儿早上这位女公民没带戒指来，他们还是没答应她看那崽子。
肖维兰心里早已把这桩偷孩子悲剧的始末拼凑完整；此刻他确信，要执行他那撒旦式的复仇，马拉再挑不出比这两个革命巴黎的丑类更得力的工具了。
勒里当夫妇贪心、怯懦、爱财如命，只要钱给足了，什么伤天害理的事都肯干；但天底下再多的钱，也别想叫他们把自己的脖子搭进去。马拉显然是用断头台攥着他们的。他们深知“人民之友”的权势，也因此怕他。肖维兰的职权绶带、他生硬威严的做派，对这两个可怜虫有同样的威慑。他们立刻卑躬屈膝、摇尾乞怜，涕泪交流地赌咒表白忠心。他们说，除了公民代表本人，谁也别想看那孩子一眼——有戒指也没用。肖维兰却并不想看那孩子。他已确知孩子姓拉努瓦——刚被送到勒里当家时，孩子还记得自己的名字。后来显然忘了，虽然他常常哭着喊“Maman（妈妈）！”

===Original===

Chauvelin listened to all these explanations with some impatience. The child was nothing to him, but the Scarlet Pimpernel had desired to rescue it from out of the clutches of the Leridans; had risked his all﻿—and lost it﻿—in order to effect that rescue! That in itself was a sufficient inducement for Chauvelin to interest himself in the execution of Marat’s vengeance, whatever its original mainspring may have been.
At any rate, now he felt satisfied that the child was safe, and that the Leridans were impervious to threats or bribes which might land them on the guillotine.
All that they would own to was to being afraid.
“Afraid of what?” queried Chauvelin sharply.
That the brat may be kidnapped﻿ ﻿… stolen. Oh! he could not be decoyed﻿ ﻿… they were too watchful for that! But apparently there were mysterious agencies at work.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

这些解释，肖维兰听得有些不耐烦。孩子于他无足轻重；可红花侠一心要把这孩子从勒里当夫妇的魔爪里救出去；为了这一救，押上了他的全部——并且输掉了！单凭这一点，就足以让肖维兰愿意操办马拉这场复仇了，管它最初的发条是什么。
不管怎样，此刻他已放心：孩子安然无恙；而威胁也好，贿赂也好，只要可能把勒里当夫妇自己送上断头台，他们便刀枪不入。
他们肯承认的只有一样：害怕。
“怕什么？”肖维兰厉声问。
怕那崽子被人绑架……偷走。哦！骗是骗不走的……他们盯得紧着呢！可明摆着有什么神秘的力量在暗中活动……

===Original===

“Mysterious agencies!” Chauvelin laughed aloud at the suggestion. The “mysterious agency” was even now rotting in an obscure cell at the Abbaye. What other powers could be at work on behalf of the brat?
Well, the Leridans had had a warning!
What warning?
“A letter,” the man said gruffly. “But as neither my wife nor I can read﻿—”
“Why did you not speak of this before?” broke in Chauvelin roughly. “Let me see the letter.”

===Chinese===

“神秘的力量！”肖维兰听了放声大笑。那股“神秘的力量”，此刻正在阿贝伊一间不见天日的牢房里烂着呢。还能有什么别的势力为那崽子出力？
不过，勒里当夫妇接到过一封警告信！
什么警告信？
“一封信，”男的瓮声瓮气地说。“只是我跟我婆娘都不识字——”
“怎么不早说？”肖维兰粗暴地打断。“信拿来我看。”

===Original===

The woman produced a soiled and dank scrap of paper from beneath her apron. Of a truth she could not read its contents, for they were writ in English in the form of a doggerel rhyme which caused Chauvelin to utter a savage oath.
“When did this come?” he asked. “And how?”
“This morning, citizen,” the woman mumbled in reply. “I found it outside the door, with a stone on it to prevent the wind from blowing it away. What does it mean, citizen?” she went on, her voice shaking with terror, for of a truth the citizen Representative looked as if he had seen some weird and unearthly apparition.
He gave no reply for a moment or two, and the two catiffs had no conception of the tremendous effort at self-control which was hidden behind the pale, rigid mask of the redoubtable man.
“It probably means nothing that you need fear,” Chauvelin said quietly at last. “But I will see the Commissary of the Section myself, and tell him to send a dozen men of the Sûreté along to watch your house and be at your beck and call if need be. Then you will feel quite safe, I hope.”
“Oh, yes! quite safe, citizen!” the woman replied with a sigh of genuine relief. Then only did Chauvelin turn on his heel and go his way.

===Chinese===

女人从围裙底下摸出一张又脏又潮的纸片。说真的，她自然读不懂上面写的什么，因为那是英文，一首歪诗——肖维兰一见，登时发出一声狂怒的咒骂。
“这信什么时候来的？”他问。“怎么来的？”
“今儿早上，公民，”女人含含糊糊地答道。“我在门外捡的，上头压了块石头，怕风刮跑。这信是什么意思，公民？”她接着问，声音吓得发抖，因为公民代表那副模样，实在像见了什么阴森古怪的鬼魅。
他有一两分钟没有答话。这两个可怜虫哪里想得到，在这位煞神苍白僵硬的面具后面，藏着怎样一番惊人的自制。
“多半没有什么你们需要害怕的意思，”肖维兰终于平静地说。“不过我会亲自去见区政委，叫他派一打治安队员来看守你们的宅子，需要时随叫随到。这样你们就放心了，我想。”
“哦，那敢情好！就放心了，公民！”女人答道，发出一声真心宽慰的叹息。直到这时，肖维兰才转身离去。

### IX / ### 九

===Original===

But that crumpled and soiled scrap of paper given to him by the woman Leridan still lay in his clenched hand as he strode back rapidly citywards. It seemed to scorch his palm. Even before he had glanced at the contents he knew what they were. That atrocious English doggerel, the signature﻿—a five-petalled flower traced in crimson! How well he knew them!
“We seek him here, we seek him there!”
The most humiliating moments in Chauvelin’s career were associated with that silly rhyme, and now here it was, mocking him even when he knew that his bitter enemy lay fettered and helpless, caught in a trap, out of which there was no escape possible; even though he knew for a positive certainty that the mocking voice which had spoken those rhymes on that far-off day last September would soon be stilled forever.
No doubt one of that army of abominable English spies had placed this warning outside the Leridans’ door. No doubt they had done that with a view to throwing dust in the eyes of the Public Prosecutor and causing a confusion in his mind with regard to the identity of the prisoner at the Abbaye, all to the advantage of their chief.

===Chinese===

可勒里当女人给他的那张又皱又脏的纸片，还攥在他捏紧的手心里，他快步朝城里走去。那纸片像要烙焦他的手掌。不等细看，他就知道上面写的是什么。那可憎的英文歪诗，那签名——一朵用猩红描出的五瓣小花！他太熟悉这两样了！
“我们在这儿寻他，我们在那儿找他！”
肖维兰一生最丢脸的时刻，全同这首蠢诗连在一起；此刻它又来了，嘲讽着他——纵然他知道他的死敌已戴镣受困、插翅难逃地落在了陷阱里；纵然他确切无疑地知道，去年九月那个久远的日子里吟出这几句诗的那嘲弄的嗓音，很快就要永远喑哑了。
毫无疑问，是那支可憎的英国奸细大军中的某一个，把警告信放在了勒里当家门口。毫无疑问，他们这样做，是为了迷住公诉人的眼睛，叫他对阿贝伊那个囚犯的身份起疑心，好让他们的首领得利。

===Original===

The thought that such a confusion might exist, that Fouquier-Tinville might be deluded into doubting the real personality of Paul Molé, brought an icy sweat all down Chauvelin’s spine. He hurried along the interminably long Chemin de Pantin, only paused at the Barrière du Combat in order to interview the Commissary of the Section on the matter of sending men to watch over the Leridans’ house. Then, when he felt satisfied that this would be effectively and quickly done, an unconquerable feeling of restlessness prompted him to hurry round to the lodgings of the Public Prosecutor in the Rue Blanche﻿—just to see him, to speak with him, to make quite sure.
Oh! he must be sure that no doubts, no pusillanimity on the part of any official would be allowed to stand in the way of the consummation of all his most cherished dreams. Papers or no papers, testimony or no testimony, the incarcerated Paul Molé was the Scarlet Pimpernel﻿—of this Chauvelin was as certain as that he was alive. His every sense had testified to it when he stood in the narrow room of the Rue des Cordeliers, face to face﻿—eyes gazing into eyes﻿—with his sworn enemy.
Unluckily, however, he found the Public Prosecutor in a surly and obstinate mood, following on an interview which he had just had with citizen Commissary Cuisinier on the matter of the prisoner Paul Molé.
“His papers are all in order, I tell you,” he said impatiently, in answer to Chauvelin’s insistence. “It is as much as my head is worth to demand a summary execution.”

===Chinese===

一想到这疑心可能真的存在，想到富基耶-坦维尔可能当真受骗、怀疑起保罗·莫莱的真实身份，一股冷汗便顺着肖维兰的脊梁流下去。他沿着长得望不到头的潘坦路急急赶路，只在战斗关卡（Barrière du Combat）稍停，去见区政委，当面吩咐派人看守勒里当家宅。等确信此事会迅速办妥，一种按捺不住的焦躁又催着他奔往布朗什街（Rue Blanche）公诉人的住处——只为见见他，同他谈谈，把事情敲实。
哦！他必须确保任何官吏的疑虑、任何畏缩，都拦不住他最珍视的美梦成真。管他证件齐不齐、人证有没有，关在牢里的保罗·莫莱就是红花侠——这一点，肖维兰确信得如同确信自己活着。当他站在科德利埃街那间窄屋里，同他的死敌面对面——眼对着眼——时，他的每一种感官都是这样作证的。
不巧的是，他发现公诉人正是一副又倔又横的脸色——他刚为囚犯保罗·莫莱的事，同屈齐尼耶政委公民谈过一场。
“他的证件一应齐全，我告诉你，”肖维兰再三坚持，他不耐烦地答道。“要求立即处决，我的脑袋也保不住。”

===Original===

“But I tell you that, those papers of his are forged,” urged Chauvelin forcefully.
“They are not,” retorted the other. “The Commissary swears to his own signature on the identity book. The concierge at the Abbaye swears that he knows Molé, so do all the men of the Sûreté who have seen him. The Commissary has known him as an indigent, good-for-nothing lubbard who has begged his way in the streets of Paris ever since he was released from gaol some months ago, after he had served a term for larceny. Even your own man Hébert admits to feeling doubtful on the point. You have had the nightmare, citizen,” concluded Fouquier-Tinville with a harsh laugh.
“But, name of a dog!” broke in Chauvelin savagely. “You are not proposing to let the man go?”
“What else can I do?” the other rejoined fretfully. “We shall get into terrible trouble if we interfere with a man like Paul Molé. You know yourself how it is these days. We should have the whole of the rabble of Paris clamouring for our blood. If, after we have guillotined him, he is proved to be a good patriot, it will be my turn next. No! I thank you!”
“I tell you, man,” retorted Chauvelin desperately, “that the man is not Paul Molé﻿—that he is the English spy whom we all know as the Scarlet Pimpernel.”

===Chinese===

“可我告诉你，他那些证件是伪造的，”肖维兰极力劝道。
“不是，”对方反驳。“政委赌咒说身份证上是他亲笔签名。阿贝伊的门房赌咒说他认得莫莱，见过的治安队员们个个都这么说。政委早就认得他，是个赤贫的、一无所长的蠢大个儿，几个月前因盗窃坐牢放出来后，一直在巴黎街头讨饭。连你那个埃贝尔都承认心里犯嘀咕。公民，你是做了场噩梦，”富基耶-坦维尔末了刺耳地一笑，下了断语。
“可是，天杀的！”肖维兰狂怒地插进来。“你莫非打算放人？”
“不然我还能怎样？”对方烦躁地应道。“动保罗·莫莱这样的人，我们会招来塌天大祸。如今什么光景，你自己也知道。巴黎全城的暴民都会嚷着要我们的血。万一铡了他，又查明他是个好爱国者，下一个就轮到我了。不！多谢你美意！”
“我告诉你，”肖维兰不顾一切地反驳，“这人不是保罗·莫莱——他就是我们都认得的那个英国奸细红花侠。”

===Original===

“*Eh Bien!*” riposted Fouquier-Tinville. “Bring me more tangible proof that our prisoner is not Paul Molé and I’ll deal with him quickly enough, never fear. But if by tomorrow morning you do not satisfy me on the point﻿ ﻿… I must let him go his way.”
A savage oath rose to Chauvelin’s lips. He felt like a man who has been running, panting to reach a goal, who sees that goal within easy distance of him, and is then suddenly captured, caught in invisible meshes which hold him tightly, and against which he is powerless to struggle. For the moment he hated Fouquier-Tinville with a deadly hatred, would have tortured and threatened him until he wrung a consent, an admission, out of him.
Name of a name! when that damnable English spy was actually in his power, the man was a pusillanimous fool to allow the rich prize to slip from his grasp! Chauvelin felt as if he were choking; his slender fingers worked nervily around his cravat; beads of perspiration trickled unheeded down his pallid forehead.
Then suddenly he had an inspiration﻿—nothing less! It almost seemed as if Satan, his friend, had whispered insinuating words into his ear. That scrap of paper! He had thrust it awhile ago into the breast pocket of his coat. It was still there, and the Public Prosecutor wanted a tangible proof.﻿ ﻿… Then, why not.﻿ ﻿… ?

===Chinese===

“Eh Bien（那好吧）！”富基耶-坦维尔反唇相讥。“拿出更实在的证据，证明我们的囚犯不是保罗·莫莱，我收拾他也就是一句话，放心。可要是到明天早上你还说服不了我……我只好放他走他的路。”
一句狠毒的咒骂涌到肖维兰嘴边。他觉得自己像一个人，一路奔一路喘地扑向目标，眼见目标近在咫尺，却忽然被什么东西擒住，缠进一张无形的网里，网收得紧紧的，任他如何挣扎也无济于事。
天杀的！那可恶的英国奸细明明已在他掌心，这人却是个懦种傻瓜，眼看肥肉从指缝里滑掉！肖维兰觉得快要窒息了；瘦削的手指神经质地绞着领巾；汗珠顺着惨白的额角涔涔而下，他也浑然不觉。
忽然间他灵光一现——何止灵光一现！简直像他的朋友撒旦，正贴着他的耳朵甜言蜜语。那张纸片！方才他把它塞进了外衣胸前口袋。它还在那儿，而公诉人正要一件实在的证据……那么，何不……？

===Original===

Slowly, his thoughts still in the process of gradual coordination, Chauvelin drew that soiled scrap of paper out of his pocket. Fouquier-Tinville, surly and ill-humoured, had his back half-turned towards him, was moodily picking at his teeth. Chauvelin had all the leisure which he required. He smoothed out the creases in the paper and spread it out carefully upon the desk close to the other man’s elbow. Fouquier-Tinville looked down on it, over his shoulder.
“What is that?” he queried.
“As you see, citizen,” was Chauvelin’s bland reply. “A message, such as you yourself have oft received, methinks, from our mutual enemy, the Scarlet Pimpernel.”
But already the Public Prosecutor had seized upon the paper, and of a truth Chauvelin had no longer cause to complain of his colleague’s indifference. That doggerel rhyme, no less than the signature, had the power to rouse Fouquier-Tinville’s ire, as it had that of disturbing Chauvelin’s well-studied calm.
“What is it?” reiterated the Public Prosecutor, white now to the lips.

===Chinese===

肖维兰慢慢从口袋里掏出那张脏纸片，思绪还在一点点理顺。富基耶-坦维尔横眉竖眼、一肚子不痛快，半扭着背对他，正闷闷地剔着牙。肖维兰有的是从容。他把纸上的折痕抚平，小心地摊在桌上，正挨着对方的胳膊肘。富基耶-坦维尔从肩头上探眼看了看。
“这是什么？”他问。
“您看见了，公民，”肖维兰温吞吞地答道。“一封信。我想，咱们共同的仇敌红花侠，这样的信您自己也不止一次收到过。”
可公诉人早已一把抓过纸片；说真的，此刻肖维兰再没理由抱怨同僚的冷淡了。那首歪诗，还有那个签名，自有一种本领，能撩起富基耶-坦维尔的火气，正如能搅乱肖维兰苦心修得的镇定一样。
“这是什么？”公诉人又问了一遍，嘴唇都发白了。

===Original===

“I have told you, citizen,” rejoined Chauvelin imperturbably. “A message from that English spy. It is also the proof which you have demanded of me﻿—the tangible proof that the prisoner, Paul Molé, is none other than the Scarlet Pimpernel.”
“But,” ejaculated the other hoarsely, “where did you get this?”
“It was found in the cell which Paul Molé occupied in the depot of the Rue de Tourraine, where he was first incarcerated. I picked it up there after he was removed﻿ ﻿… the ink was scarcely dry upon it.”
The lie came quite glibly to Chauvelin’s tongue. Was not every method good, every device allowable, which would lead to so glorious an end?

===Chinese===

“我说过了，公民，”肖维兰不动声色地答道。“那个英国奸细的一封信。它也是您要我拿的证据——实在的证据，证明囚犯保罗·莫莱不是别人，正是红花侠。”
“可是，”对方声音发哑地叫起来，“这东西您从哪儿弄来的？”
“在保罗·莫莱最先被关押的图雷讷街拘押所他住过的牢房里找到的。他转押之后我捡到的……上面的墨迹还没干透呢。”
这谎话从肖维兰舌尖上溜出来，顺溜极了。只要能通向如此辉煌的结局，什么手段不好，什么伎俩不可用呢？

===Original===

“Why did you not tell me of this before?” queried Fouquier-Tinville, with a sudden gleam of suspicion in his deep-set eyes.
“You had not asked me for a tangible proof before,” replied Chauvelin blandly. “I myself was so firmly convinced of what I averred that I had well-nigh forgotten the existence of this damning scrap of paper.”
Damning indeed! Fouquier-Tinville had seen such scraps of paper before. He had learnt the doggerel rhyme by heart, even though the English tongue was quite unfamiliar to him. He loathed the English﻿—the entire nation﻿—with all that deadly hatred which a divergence of political aims will arouse in times of acute crises. He hated the English government, Pitt and Burke and even Fox, the happy-go-lucky apologist of the young Revolution. But, above all, he hated that League of English spies﻿—as he was pleased to call them﻿—whose courage, resourcefulness, as well as reckless daring, had more than once baffled his own hideous schemes of murder, of pillage, and of rape.
Thank Beelzebub and his horde of evil spirits, citizen Chauvelin had been clear-sighted enough to detect that elusive Pimpernel under the disguise of Paul Molé.

===Chinese===

“怎么不早告诉我？”富基耶-坦维尔问，深陷的眼睛里倏地闪过一丝疑光。
“您先前没向我要实在的证据啊，”肖维兰不紧不慢地答道。“我自己对我断言的事确信不疑，几乎忘了这张要命的纸片的存在。”
的确要命！富基耶-坦维尔以前也见过这样的纸片。那首歪诗他都背熟了，尽管英语于他全然陌生。他痛恨英国人——恨整个民族——怀着那种政治目标相左在危机年代必然激起的刻骨仇恨。他恨英国政府，恨皮特（Pitt）、恨伯克（Burke），连福克斯（Fox）——那个为年轻革命随口辩解的乐天派——也恨。但最恨的，还是那个英国奸细同盟——他乐意这么称呼他们——那些人的勇气、机变和亡命胆色，不止一次搅黄了他那杀人、劫掠、奸淫的可怕图谋。
多亏别西卜（Beelzebub）和他那一群恶鬼，肖维兰公民眼力够尖，识破了保罗·莫莱化装底下那个滑不留手的红花侠。

===Original===

“You have deserved well of your country,” said Tinville with lusty fervour, and gave Chauvelin a vigorous slap on the shoulder. “But for you I should have allowed that abominable spy to slip through our fingers.”
“I have succeeded in convincing you, citizen?” Chauvelin retorted dryly.
“Absolutely!” rejoined the other. “You may now leave the matter to me. And ’twill be friend Molé who will be surprised tomorrow,” he added with a harsh guffaw, “when he finds himself face to face with me, before a Court of Justice.”
He was all eagerness, of course. Such a triumph for him! The indictment of the notorious Scarlet Pimpernel on a charge of espionage would be the crowning glory of his career! Let other men look to their laurels! Those who brought that dangerous enemy of revolution to the guillotine would forever be proclaimed as the saviours of France.

===Chinese===

“你为国家立了大功，”坦维尔嗓门洪亮、热情洋溢地说，重重拍了肖维兰肩膀一下。“要不是你，那可恶的奸细就从我指缝里溜掉了。”
“我把您说服了，公民？”肖维兰干巴巴地回了一句。
“彻底说服了！”对方应道。“这事现在就交给我吧。明天，咱们那位莫莱朋友可要吃一惊了，”他刺耳地大笑一声，又添上一句，“等他发现自己站在审判官前，跟我打照面的时候。”
他当然跃跃欲试。这于他是多大的胜利！以间谍罪名起诉那个臭名昭著的红花侠，将是他一生事业的至高荣耀！让别人去守他们的桂冠吧！谁把这个革命的危险敌人送上断头台，谁就永远被尊为法兰西的救星。

===Original===

“A short indictment,” he said, when Chauvelin, after a lengthy discussion on various points, finally rose to take his leave, “but a scathing one! I tell you, citizen Chauvelin, that tomorrow you will be the first to congratulate me on an unprecedented triumph.”
He had been arguing in favour of a sensational trial and no less sensational execution. Chauvelin, with his memory harking back on many mysterious abductions at the very foot of the guillotine, would have liked to see his elusive enemy quietly put to death amongst a batch of traitors, who would help to mask his personality until after the guillotine had fallen, when the whole of Paris should ring with the triumph of this final punishment of the hated spy.
In the end, the two friends agreed upon a compromise, and parted well pleased with the turn of events which a kind Fate had ordered for their own special benefit.

===Chinese===

“起诉书要短，”他说——肖维兰就种种细节同他长谈之后，终于起身告辞了——“但要狠！我告诉你，肖维兰公民，明天您会头一个向我道贺，贺这一场史无前例的大捷。”
他本来一直主张来一场轰动的大审，再来一场同样轰动的处决。肖维兰却记得，多少回神不知鬼不觉的劫人，就发生在断头台脚下；他倒愿意看他那个滑不留手的仇敌，混在一批叛逆中间悄无声息地正法——那批人正好掩护他的身份，直到铡刀落下，再让全巴黎为这可恨奸细的最终伏法震动欢腾。
最后，两个朋友折中拍板，各自散去，都对命运之神的特意眷顾深感满意。

### X / ### 十

===Original===

Thus satisfied, Chauvelin returned to the Abbaye. Hébert was safe and trustworthy, but Hébert, too, had been assailed with the same doubts which had well-nigh wrecked Chauvelin’s triumph, and with such doubts in his mind he might slacken his vigilance.
Name of a name! every man in charge of that damnable Scarlet Pimpernel should have three pairs of eyes wherewith to watch his movements. He should have the alert brain of a Robespierre, the physical strength of a Danton, the relentlessness of a Marat. He should be a giant in sheer brute force, a tiger in caution, an elephant in weight, and a mouse in stealthiness!
Name of a name! but ’twas only hate that could give such powers to any man!
Hébert, in the guardroom, owned to his doubts. His comrades, too, admitted that after twenty-four hours spent on the watch, their minds were in a whirl. The Citizen Commissary had been so sure﻿—so was the chief concierge of the Abbaye even now; and the men of the Sûreté!﻿ ﻿… they themselves had seen the real Molé more than once﻿ ﻿… and this man in the cell.﻿ ﻿… Well, would the citizen Representative have a final good look at him?

===Chinese===

如此这般放了心，肖维兰回到阿贝伊。埃贝尔人可靠，信得过；可埃贝尔心里也被同样的疑虑侵扰过——那疑虑险些毁了肖维兰的胜局——存着这样的疑虑，他说不定会松了警惕。
天杀的！凡看守那个可恶红花侠的人，都该长上三双眼睛盯他的动静。该有罗伯斯庇尔的机敏头脑，丹东的膂力，马拉的心狠。该是蛮力上的巨人，谨慎上的猛虎，分量上的大象，蹑脚时的老鼠！
天杀的！也只有仇恨，才能把这样的本事赐给一个人！
卫兵室里，埃贝尔承认了自己的疑惑。他的同僚们也承认，二十四小时盯下来，脑子里已是乱麻一团。政委公民当初是那样确信——阿贝伊的总门房至今还确信；还有那些治安队员！……他们自己也不止一次见过真正的莫莱……再说牢里这个人……那么，公民代表要不要最后再好好看一眼？

===Original===

“You seem to forget Calais, citizen Hébert,” Chauvelin said sharply, “and the deadly humiliation you suffered then at the hands of this man who is now your prisoner. Surely your eyes should have been, at least, as keen as mine own.”
Anxious, irritable, his nerves well-nigh on the rack, he nevertheless crossed the guardroom with a firm step and entered the cell where the prisoner was still lying upon the palliasse, as he had been all along, and still presenting that naked piece of shoulder through the hole in his shirt.
“He has been like this the best part of the day,” Hébert said with a shrug of the shoulders. “We put his bread and water right under his nose. He ate and he drank, and I suppose he slept. But except for a good deal of swearing, he has not spoken to any of us.”
He had followed his chief into the cell, and now stood beside the palliasse, holding a small dark lantern in his hand. At a sign from Chauvelin he flashed the light upon the prisoner’s averted head.

===Chinese===

“你好像忘了加来，埃贝尔公民，”肖维兰尖刻地说，“忘了你那时在这人手里吃的奇耻大辱——他如今是你的囚犯了。你的眼睛总该至少同我的一样尖吧。”
他焦躁、易怒，神经几乎绷到了极限，可还是迈着坚定的步子穿过卫兵室，走进了牢房。囚犯仍旧躺在草垫上，同先前一样，破衬衫的窟窿里仍旧露着那截光肩膀。
“大半天他都是这副样子，”埃贝尔耸耸肩说。“我们把面包和水放在他鼻子底下。他吃了喝了，兴许也睡了。可除了骂骂咧咧，他没同我们谁说过一句话。”
他跟着上级进了牢房，此刻站在草垫旁，手里提着一盏小小的遮光提灯。肖维兰一示意，他便把灯光打在囚犯别过去的头上。

===Original===

Molé cursed for awhile, and muttered something about “good patriots” and about “retribution.” Then, worried by the light, he turned slowly round, and with fish-like, bleary eyes looked upon his visitor.
The words of stinging irony and triumphant sarcasm, all fully prepared, froze on Chauvelin’s lips. He gazed upon the prisoner, and a weird sense of something unfathomable and mysterious came over him as he gazed. He himself could not have defined that feeling: the very next moment he was prepared to ridicule his own cowardice﻿—yes, cowardice! because for a second or two he had felt positively afraid.
Afraid of what, forsooth? The man who crouched here in the cell was his arch-enemy, the Scarlet Pimpernel﻿—the man whom he hated most bitterly in all the world, the man whose death he desired more than that of any other living creature. He had been apprehended by the very side of the murdered man whose confidence he had all but gained. He himself (Chauvelin) had at that fateful moment looked into the factitious Molé’s eyes, had seen the mockery in them, the lazy insouciance which was the chief attribute of Sir Percy Blakeney. He had heard a faint echo of that inane laugh which grated upon his nerves. Hébert had then laid hands upon this very same man; agents of the Sûreté had barred every ingress and egress to the house, had conducted their prisoner straightway to the depot and thence to the Abbaye, had since that moment guarded him on sight, by day and by night. Hébert and the other men as well as the chief warder, all swore to that!
No, no! There could be no doubt! There was no doubt! The days of magic were over! A man could not assume a personality other than his own; he could not fly out of that personality like a bird out of its cage. There on the palliasse in the miserable cell were the same long limbs, the broad shoulders, the grimy face with the three days’ growth of stubbly beard﻿—the whole wretched personality of Paul Molé, in fact, which hid the exquisite one of Sir Percy Blakeney, Bart. And yet!﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===

莫莱骂了一阵，又咕哝了些什么“好爱国者”和“报应”之类。后来被灯光搅得心烦，才慢慢转过身，用一双鱼一般浑浊的眼睛望着来客。
那些尖刻的讥诮、得意的嘲讽，本是字字备好的，此刻却冻在肖维兰嘴边。他望着囚犯，望着望着，一种莫测而诡秘的感觉攫住了他。他自己也说不清那感觉：紧接着他便打算嘲笑自己的怯懦——是的，怯懦！因为有那么一两秒钟，他确实感到了害怕。
怕什么，岂有此理？蹲在这牢里的人，是他的死敌红花侠——普天之下他最恨的人，他的死比世上任何生灵的死都更叫他盼望。这人是在被害者身边被擒的——他差一点就骗到了那人的信任。他本人（肖维兰）在那个性命攸关的时刻，直视过冒牌莫莱的眼睛，看见过眼中的嘲弄，见过那份懒洋洋的满不在乎——那正是珀西·布莱克尼爵士的头号招牌。他还听见一声那傻笑的微弱回响，那笑声曾刮得他神经发麻。随后是埃贝尔亲手拿住的这个人；治安队的密探封死了那宅子的每一处出入口，把囚犯径直押进拘押所，又押进阿贝伊，从那一刻起昼夜盯守。埃贝尔、其他队员，还有典狱长，人人都可以为此发誓！
不，不！不可能有疑问！没有疑问！妖术的时代过去了！一个人变不出第二个自己；他不能像鸟儿钻出笼子那样，从自己这副人身里飞出去。可怜牢房草垫上的，还是那长长的四肢、宽阔的肩膀、那张三天没刮、胡茬肮脏的脸——总之，还是保罗·莫莱那整个卑微的人格，底下藏着从男爵珀西·布莱克尼爵士那精致的人格。然而！……

===Original===

A cold sweat ran down Chauvelin’s spine as he gazed, mute and immovable, into those fish-like, bleary eyes, which were not﻿—no! they were not those of the real Scarlet Pimpernel.
The whole situation became dreamlike, almost absurd. Chauvelin was not the man for such a mock-heroic, melodramatic situation. Commonsense, reason, his own cool powers of deliberation, would soon reassert themselves. But for the moment he was dazed. He had worked too hard, no doubt; had yielded too much to excitement, to triumph, and to hate. He turned to Hébert, who was standing stolidly by, gave him a few curt orders in a clear and well-pitched voice. Then he walked out of the cell, without bestowing another look on the prisoner.
Molé had once more turned over on his palliasse and, apparently, had gone to sleep. Hébert, with a strange and puzzled laugh, followed his chief out of the cell.

===Chinese===

肖维兰哑然不动地盯着那双鱼一般浑浊的眼睛，一股冷汗顺着脊梁淌下来——那双眼睛不是，不！它们不是真正的红花侠的眼睛。
整个局面变得恍如梦中，近乎荒唐。肖维兰不是应付这种夸张悲闹场面的人。常识、理性、他惯有的冷静盘算，很快就会重新当家。可此刻他晕了头。无疑他是操劳过度了；又太纵容了兴奋、胜利和仇恨。他转向木立一旁的埃贝尔，用清朗平稳的嗓音丢下几道简短的命令，随即走出牢房，没再看囚犯一眼。
莫莱又翻了个身趴在草垫上，看样子是睡着了。埃贝尔发出一声古怪而困惑的笑，跟着上级走出了牢房。

### XI / ### 十一

===Original===

At first Chauvelin had the wish to go back and see the Public Prosecutor﻿—to speak with him﻿—to tell him﻿—what? Yes, what? That he, Chauvelin, had all of a sudden been assailed with the same doubts which already had worried Hébert and the others?﻿—that he had told a deliberate lie when he stated that the incriminating doggerel rhyme had been found in Molé’s cell? No, no! Such an admission would not only be foolish, it would be dangerous now, whilst he himself was scarce prepared to trust to his own senses. After all, Fouquier-Tinville was in the right frame of mind for the moment. Paul Molé, whoever he was, was safely under lock and key.
The only danger lay in the direction of the house on the Chemin de Pantin. At the thought Chauvelin felt giddy and faint. But he would allow himself no rest. Indeed, he could not have rested until something approaching certainty had once more taken possession of his soul. He could not﻿—would not﻿—believe that he had been deceived. He was still prepared to stake his very life on the identity of the prisoner at the Abbaye. Tricks of light, the flash of the lantern, the perfection of the disguise, had caused a momentary illusion﻿—nothing more.
Nevertheless, that awful feeling of restlessness which had possessed him during the last twenty-four hours once more drove him to activity. And although commonsense and reason both pulled one way, an eerie sense of superstition whispered in his ear the ominous words, “If, after all!”
At any rate, he would see the Leridans, and once more make sure of them; and, late as was the hour, he set out for the lonely house on the Pantin Road.

===Chinese===

起初，肖维兰真想回去见公诉人——同他谈谈——告诉他——告诉他什么？是啊，告诉他什么？说他肖维兰也突然被同样的疑虑缠上了——那疑虑早已折磨过埃贝尔和其他人？说他声称那首定罪的歪诗是在莫莱牢房里找到的，其实是存心撒谎？不，不！这样的招认不但愚蠢，眼下还是危险的，何况他自己尚且不敢相信自己的感官。毕竟，此刻富基耶-坦维尔的心态正合适。保罗·莫莱，管他是谁，稳稳地锁在牢里呢。
唯一的危险，在潘坦路那所宅子的方向。一想到这个，肖维兰便觉眩晕发虚。可他不许自己歇着。说真的，不等到某种近乎确凿的东西重新占据他的心，他也歇不安稳。他不能——也不肯——相信自己受了骗。他仍旧打算拿性命担保阿贝伊那个囚犯的身份。光线的把戏、提灯的一闪、化装的精妙，造成了一瞬的错觉——仅此而已。
然而，过去二十四小时缠着他的那股可怕的焦躁，又一次驱使他行动起来。常识和理性虽然都往一头拉，一种阴森的迷信感却贴着他的耳朵，低声吐出那句不祥的话：“万一呢！”
无论如何，他要再去见勒里当夫妇，把他们的底再摸实一遍；时候虽已夜深，他还是动身往潘坦路上那所孤零零的宅子去了。

===Original===

Just inside the Barrière du Combat was the Poste de Section, where Commissary Burban was under orders to provide a dozen men of the Sûreté, who were to be on the watch round and about the house of the Leridans. Chauvelin called in on the Commissary, who assured him that the men were at their post.
Thus satisfied, he crossed the Barrière and started at a brisk walk down the long stretch of the Chemin de Pantin. The night was dark. The rolling clouds overhead hid the face of the moon and presaged the storm. On the right, the irregular heights of the Buttes Chaumont loomed out dense and dark against the heavy sky, whilst to the left, on ahead, a faintly glimmering, greyish streak of reflected light revealed the proximity of the canal.
Close to the spot where the main Route de Meux intersects the Chemin de Pantin, Chauvelin slackened his pace. The house of the Leridans now lay immediately on his left; from it a small, feeble ray of light, finding its way no doubt through an ill-closed shutter, pierced the surrounding gloom. Chauvelin, without hesitation, turned up a narrow track which led up to the house across a field of stubble. The next moment a peremptory challenge brought him to a halt.
“Who goes there?”
“Public Safety,” replied Chauvelin. “Who are you?”
“Of the Sûreté,” was the counter reply. “There are a dozen of us about here.”

===Chinese===

战斗关卡里侧就是区哨所（Poste de Section），布尔班（Burban）政委奉命派出一打治安队员，在勒里当宅子四周守望。肖维兰顺道进去见了政委，政委向他保证，人已各就各位。
如此放了心，他便穿过关卡，迈开快步，沿潘坦路长长的直道走去。夜色漆黑。头顶翻滚的乌云遮没了月亮的脸，预示着暴风雨。右边，肖蒙高地（Buttes Chaumont）参差的山影在沉沉天幕下又浓又黑地耸出；左边，前方，一道微微闪烁的灰白反光，透出运河的临近。
临近莫城大道与潘坦路相交的路口，肖维兰放慢了脚步。勒里当的宅子就在他左手边；宅子里透出一星微弱的光，想必是从没关严的百叶窗里挤出来的，刺破四周的昏暗。肖维兰毫不迟疑地拐上一条窄径，穿过一片收割后的麦茬地通向那宅子。下一刻，一声不容分说的喝问叫他停住了脚。
“什么人？”
“公安委员会，”肖维兰答道。“你们是什么人？”
“治安队的，”对方回话。“我们一打人分散在这一带。”

===Original===

“When did you arrive?”
“Some two hours ago. We marched out directly after you left the orders at the Commissariat.”
“You are prepared to remain on the watch all night?”
“Those are our orders, citizen,” replied the man.
“You had best close up round the house, then. And, name of a dog!” he added, with a threatening ring in his voice. “Let there be no slackening of vigilance this night. No one to go in or out of that house, no one to approach it under any circumstances whatever. Is that understood?”
“Those were our orders from the first, citizen,” said the man simply.

===Chinese===

“什么时候到的？”
“两个来钟头前。您在政委办留下命令，我们随即就开拔了。”
“准备守一整夜？”
“命令如此，公民，”那人答道。
“那就往宅子跟前收拢些。还有，天杀的！”他添了一句，声音里带着威胁。“今夜警戒不许有半点松懈。任何人不得进出那宅子，无论如何，任何人不得靠近它。听明白了？”
“命令从头就是这样下的，公民，”那人老老实实地答。

===Original===

“And all has been well up to now?”
“We have seen no one, citizen.”
The little party closed in around their chief and together they marched up to the house. Chauvelin, on tenterhooks, walked quicker than the others. He was the first to reach the door. Unable to find the bellpull in the dark, he knocked vigorously.
The house appeared silent and wrapped in sleep. No light showed from within save that one tiny speck through the cracks of an ill-fitting shutter, in a room immediately overhead.
In response to Chauvelin’s repeated summons, there came anon the sound of someone moving in one of the upstairs rooms, and presently the light overhead disappeared, whilst a door above was heard to open and to close and shuffling footsteps to come slowly down the creaking stairs.

===Chinese===

“到目前为止一切无事？”
“没见着一个人，公民。”
这一小队人围着他们的长官，一同朝宅子走去。肖维兰心悬在半空，走得比别人快。他头一个到门口。黑暗里摸不着门铃拉索，他便重重地擂门。
宅子静悄悄的，像是睡熟了。里面不见一点灯火，只有正头顶那个房间的窗上，关不严的百叶板缝里漏出一星微光。
应着肖维兰一声紧一声的叫门，楼上哪间屋子里终于有了动静，跟着头顶那点灯光熄了，楼上一扇门开了又关，一阵拖着脚步的声音慢慢踩下吱呀作响的楼梯。

===Original===

A moment or two later the bolts and bars of the front door were unfastened, a key grated in the rusty lock, a chain rattled in its socket, and then the door was opened slowly and cautiously.
The woman Leridan appeared in the doorway. She held a guttering tallow candle high above her head. Its flickering light illumined Chauvelin’s slender figure.
“Ah! the citizen Representative!” the woman ejaculated, as soon as she recognised him. “We did not expect you again today, and at this late hour, too. I’ll tell my man﻿—”
“Never mind your man,” broke in Chauvelin impatiently, and pushed without ceremony past the woman inside the house. “The child? Is it safe?”
He could scarcely control his excitement. There was a buzzing, as of an angry sea, in his ears. The next second, until the woman spoke, seemed like a cycle of years.

===Chinese===

片刻之后，前门的门闩门杠被卸下，钥匙在生锈的锁眼里涩涩一转，门链在槽里哗啦一响，随后那扇门才慢慢悠悠、小心翼翼地开了。
勒里当家的女人出现在门口。她把一支烛泪纵横的蜡烛高高举过头顶。摇曳的烛光照亮了肖维兰瘦削的身影。
“啊！是公民代表！”女人一认出他便叫出声来。“我们没想到您今天还会再来，还赶这么晚。我去叫我们当家的——”
“用不着叫你们当家的，”肖维兰不耐烦地打断，也不客气，从女人身边挤进屋去。“孩子呢？没事吧？”
他几乎按捺不住激动。耳朵里嗡嗡作响，像一片怒海。女人开口前的那一秒，漫长得像一个轮回。

===Original===

“Quite safe, citizen,” she said placidly. “Everything is quite safe. We were so thankful for those men of the Sûreté. We had been afraid before, as I told the citizen Representative, and my man and I could not rest for anxiety. It was only after they came that we dared go to bed.”
A deep sigh of intense relief came from the depths of Chauvelin’s heart. He had not realised himself until this moment how desperately anxious he had been. The woman’s reassuring words appeared to lift a crushing weight from his mind. He turned to the man behind him.
“You did not tell me,” he said, “that some of you had been here already.”
“We have not been here before,” the sergeant in charge of the little platoon said in reply. “I do not know what the woman means.”
“Some of your men came about three hours ago,” the woman retorted; “less than an hour after the citizen Representative was here. I remember that my man and I marvelled how quickly they did come, but they said that they had been on duty at the Barrière du Combat when the citizen arrived, and that he had dispatched them off at once. They said they had run all the way. But even so, we thought it was quick work﻿—”

===Chinese===

“平安无事，公民，”她安安稳稳地说。“样样都平安。来了这些治安队员，我们真是谢天谢地。先前我们害怕，这话我跟公民代表说过了，我们两口子愁得睡不稳。他们来了之后，我们才敢上床。”
一声深深的宽慰，从肖维兰心底叹出。直到此刻他才明白，自己先前急成了什么样。女人这话仿佛从他心口搬走了一座大山。他转向身后那人。
“你没告诉我，”他说，“你们当中有人已经来过了。”
“我们先前没来过，”带这小队人的中士答道。“我不知道这女人是什么意思。”
“您的人有三个钟头前来过几个，”女人反驳说；“公民代表走后不到一个钟头。我记得我们当家的还直嘀咕他们怎么来得这么快，可他们说，公民到的时候他们正在战斗关卡当值，是公民当场打发他们来的。他们还说是一路跑来的。可就是这样，我们也觉得来得够快——”

===Original===

The words were smothered in her throat in a cry of pain, for, with an almost brutal gesture, Chauvelin had seized her by the shoulders.
“Where are those men?” he queried hoarsely. “Answer!”
“In there, and in there,” the woman stammered, well-nigh faint with terror as she pointed to two doors, one on each side of the passage. “Three in each room. They are asleep now, I should say, as they seem so quiet. But they were an immense comfort to us, citizen﻿ ﻿… we were so thankful to have them in the house.﻿ ﻿…”
But Chauvelin had snatched the candle from her hand. Holding it high above his head, he strode to the door on the right of the passage. It was ajar. He pushed it open with a vicious kick. The room beyond was in total darkness.
“Is anyone here?” he queried sharply.

===Chinese===

话音在她喉咙里憋成一声痛叫——肖维兰几乎是粗暴地一把攥住了她的肩膀。
“那些人在哪儿？”他哑着嗓子问。“回答！”
“在那边屋里，还有那边，”女人结结巴巴地说，指着走廊两边的两扇门，吓得几乎晕过去。“一间屋里三个。这会儿想必都睡了，那么安静。可他们真是我们的主心骨，公民……家里有他们，我们感激不尽……”
可肖维兰已从她手里夺过蜡烛。他把烛台高高举过头顶，大步走向走廊右边那扇门。门虚掩着。他狠狠一脚踹开。里面的房间一团漆黑。
“有人吗？”他厉声问。

===Original===

Nothing but silence answered him. For a moment he remained there on the threshold, silent and immovable as a figure carved in stone. He had just a sufficiency of presence of mind and of will power not to drop the candle, to stand there motionless, with his back turned to the woman and to the men who had crowded in, in his wake. He would not let them see the despair, the rage and grave superstitious fear, which distorted every line of his pallid face.
He did not ask about the child. He would not trust himself to speak, for he had realised already how completely he had been baffled. Those abominable English spies had watched their opportunity, had worked on the credulity and the fears of the Leridans and, playing the game at which they and their audacious chief were such unconquerable experts, they had made their way into the house under a clever ruse.
The men of the Sûreté, not quite understanding the situation, were questioning the Leridans. The man, too, corroborated his wife’s story. Their anxiety had been worked upon at the moment that it was most acute. After the citizen Representative left them, earlier in the evening, they had received another mysterious message which they had been unable to read, but which had greatly increased their alarm. Then, when the men of the Sûreté came.﻿ ﻿… Ah! they had no cause to doubt that they were men of the Sûreté!﻿ ﻿… their clothes, their speech, their appearance﻿ ﻿… figure to yourself, even their uniforms! They spoke so nicely, so reassuringly. The Leridans were so thankful to see them! Then they made themselves happy in the two rooms below, and for additional safety the Lannoy child was brought down from its attic and put to sleep in the one room with the men of the Sûreté.
After that the Leridans went to bed. Name of a dog! how were they to blame? Those men and the child had disappeared, but they (the Leridans) would go to the guillotine swearing that they were not to blame.

===Chinese===

回答他的只有寂静。有一瞬间，他站在门槛上，沉默而僵直，像一尊石雕。他凭着仅剩的一点镇定和意志力，才没有让蜡烛脱手；他背对着那女人和跟进来的队员们，一动不动地站在那里。他不能让他们看见，绝望、狂怒和深重的迷信恐惧，正怎样扭曲他那张惨白脸上的每一道线条。
他没有问孩子。他不敢开口，因为他已经明白，自己输得多么干净。那些可憎的英国奸细窥准了时机，拿捏住勒里当夫妇的轻信和恐惧，耍起他们和那位胆大包天的首领无往不胜的拿手好戏，用一条巧计进了宅子。
治安队员们不大明白眼下的情形，正向勒里当夫妇盘问。男的也证实了他婆娘的话。他们最提心吊胆的当口，正是被人利用的当口。傍晚公民代表走后，他们又收到一封神秘的信，他们读不懂，可越发吓破了胆。后来治安队员们来了……啊！他们哪有理由怀疑那不是治安队员！……那衣着、那谈吐、那做派……您想想，连制服都一模一样！他们说话多和气、多叫人宽心。勒里当夫妇见了他们，真是感激不尽！后来他们便在楼下两间屋里安顿下来，为了加倍保险，拉努瓦家的孩子还从阁楼上抱了下来，放在同一间屋里，同治安队员们一起睡。
之后勒里当夫妇就上床睡了。天杀的！这能怪他们吗？那些人和孩子都无影无踪了，可他们（勒里当两口子）敢对断头台发誓，这不是他们的错。

===Original===

Whether Chauvelin heard all these jeremiads, he could not afterwards have told you. But he did not need to be told how it had all been done. It had all been so simple, so ingenious, so like the methods usually adopted by that astute Scarlet Pimpernel! He saw it all so clearly before him. Nobody was to blame really, save he himself﻿—he, who alone knew and understood the adversary with whom he had to deal.
But these people here should not have the gratuitous spectacle of a man enduring the torments of disappointment and of baffled revenge. Whatever Chauvelin was suffering now would forever remain the secret of his own soul. Anon, when the Leridans’ rasping voices died away in one of the more distant portions of the house and the men of the Sûreté were busy accepting refreshment and gratuity from the two terrified wretches, he had put down the candle with a steady hand and then walked with a firm step out of the house.
Soon the slender figure was swallowed up in the gloom as he strode back rapidly towards the city.

===Chinese===

这些哀哀怨怨的絮叨，肖维兰后来到底听进多少，他自己也说不上来。但这一切是怎么做成的，不用谁来告诉他。一切都那么简单，那么巧妙，同那个精明红花侠惯用的手法如出一辙！一切清清楚楚地在他眼前演过。真要怪罪，谁也怪不着，只怪他自己——只有他一人认得、懂得自己打的是什么样的对手。
可眼前这些人，不该白看一场男人受煎熬的好戏——失望和复仇落空的煎熬。肖维兰此刻受的是什么，将永远是他自己灵魂里的秘密。不多时，等勒里当夫妇沙哑的嗓音在宅子深处哪个角落里消散，治安队员们忙着从两个吓坏的可怜虫手里领酒领赏，他便稳稳放下蜡烛，迈着坚定的步子走出了宅子。
那瘦削的身影迈开大步，急急往城里去，很快便被黑暗吞没了。

### XII / ### 十二

===Original===

Citizen Fouquier-Tinville had returned home from the Palais at a very late hour that same evening. His household in his simple lodgings in the Place Dauphine was already abed: his wife and the twins were asleep. He himself had sat down for a moment in the living-room, in dressing-gown and slippers, and with the late edition of the *Moniteur* in his hand, too tired to read.
It was half-past ten when there came a ring at the front door bell. Fouquier-Tinville, half expecting citizen Chauvelin to pay him a final visit, shuffled to the door and opened it.
A visitor, tall, well-dressed, exceedingly polite and urbane, requested a few minutes’ conversation with citizen Fouquier-Tinville.

===Chinese===

就在当天夜里，富基耶-坦维尔公民从司法宫回到家中，时候已经很晚。他住在多菲纳广场（Place Dauphine）一处简朴寓所，家里人早已上床：妻子和那对双胞胎都睡了。他自己穿着睡袍和拖鞋，手里拿着最新一期的《导报》，在起居室里坐了一会儿，累得一个字也读不进去。
十点半，前门门铃响了。富基耶-坦维尔半猜着是肖维兰公民来做最后一次造访，趿拉着鞋去开了门。
一位来客，高高的个头，衣冠楚楚，礼貌周全，温文和蔼，请求同富基耶-坦维尔公民谈几分钟话。

===Original===

Before the Public Prosecutor had made up his mind whether to introduce such a latecomer into his rooms, the latter had pushed his way through the door into the antechamber, and with a movement as swift as it was unexpected, had thrown a scarf round Fouquier-Tinville’s neck and wound it round his mouth, so that the unfortunate man’s call for help was smothered in his throat.
So dexterously and so rapidly indeed had the miscreant acted, that his victim had hardly realised the assault before he found himself securely gagged and bound to a chair in his own anteroom, whilst that daredevil stood before him, perfectly at his ease, his hands buried in the capacious pockets of his huge caped coat, and murmuring a few casual words of apology.
“I entreat you to forgive, citizen,” he was saying in an even and pleasant voice, “this necessary violence on my part towards you. But my errand is urgent, and I could not allow your neighbours or your household to disturb the few minutes’ conversation which I am obliged to have with you. My friend Paul Molé,” he went on, after a slight pause, “is in grave danger of his life owing to a hallucination on the part of our mutual friend citizen Chauvelin; and I feel confident that you yourself are too deeply enamoured of your own neck to risk it wilfully by sending an innocent and honest patriot to the guillotine.”

===Chinese===
