这老代书人，活脱就是一副老稻草人，与其说像个人，不如说像一只瘦长嶙峋的大鸟：鼻梁上架着那副眼镜，腮边颏下垂着一部稀稀拉拉、长而干瘦的须茬，一路披到胸口，活像一条皱巴巴的灰色围涎。他从头到脚裹着一件带披肩的斗篷大衣，那衣裳从前想必是绿的，如今却五颜六色，连彩虹里都寻不出的颜色也一应俱全。大衣像晨衣似的从上到下扣得严严实实，衣摆底下露出一双特大的脚，蹬着两只从来就不是一双的靴子。他坐在一把摇摇晃晃的草垫椅上，头顶一方凑合搭起的凉棚——四根杆子支着一块破麻布。身前一张桌子，四条腿没有一条囫囵，全靠一捆旧纸旧书勉强垫着，随时要塌似的；桌上摆着一只没了脖儿的半瓶墨水、几页纸和两支鹅毛笔。
姑娘的迟疑，其实不过几秒钟的事。

===Original===

Furtively, like a young creature terrified of lurking enemies, she once more glanced to right and left of her and down the two streets and the river bank, for Paris was full of spies these days﻿—human bloodhounds ready for a few sous to sell their fellow-creatures’ lives. It was middle morning now, and a few passersby were hurrying along wrapped to the nose in mufflers, for the weather was bitterly cold.
Agnès waited until there was no one in sight, then she leaned forward over the table and whispered under her breath:
“They say, citizen, that you alone in Paris know the whereabouts of the English milor’﻿—of him who is called the Scarlet Pimpernel.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

她像个惧怕暗处埋着仇敌的小动物，又偷偷地左右张望了一番，朝那两条街和河岸两头看了看——如今巴黎满城都是密探，是几个苏（sou）就肯出卖同胞性命的人形猎犬。这时已是上午中间时分，天气冷得刺骨，几个赶路的行人围巾直裹到鼻尖，匆匆而过。
阿涅丝等四下里望不见一个人影了，才俯身凑近桌子，压低嗓音悄声说：
“人家说，公民，全巴黎只有您一个人知道那位英国爵爷（English milor'）的下落——就是人家叫作红花侠（the Scarlet Pimpernel）的那位……”

===Original===

“Hush-sh-sh!” said the old man quickly, for just at that moment two men had gone by, in ragged coats and torn breeches, who had leered at Agnès and her neat cap and skirt as they passed. Now they had turned the angle of the street and the old man, too, sank his voice to a whisper.
“I know nothing of any Englishman,” he muttered.
“Yes, you do,” she rejoined insistently. “When poor Antoine Carré was somewhere in hiding and threatened with arrest, and his mother dared not write to him lest her letter be intercepted, she spoke to you about the English milor’, and the English milor’ found Antoine Carré and took him and his mother safely out of France. Mme. Carré is my godmother.﻿ ﻿… I saw her the very night when she went to meet the English milor’ at his commands. I know all that happened then.﻿ ﻿… I know that you were the intermediary.”
“And if I was,” he muttered sullenly as he fiddled with his pen and paper, “maybe I’ve had cause to regret it. For a week after that Carré episode I dared not show my face in the streets of Paris; for nigh on a fortnight I dared not ply my trade﻿ ﻿… I have only just ventured again to set up in business. I am not going to risk my old neck again in a hurry.﻿ ﻿…”
“It is a matter of life and death,” urged Agnès, as once more the tears rushed to her pleading eyes and the look of misery settled again upon her face.

===Chinese===

“嘘——嘘——嘘！”老头儿急忙止住她，因为恰在此时走过去两个男人，外套破破烂烂，裤子千疮百孔，打阿涅丝身边过时，拿眼睛斜瞟她那顶俏丽的小帽和裙子。这时两人已经拐过街角，老头儿也把声音压成了耳语。
“英国人的事，我一概不知。”他咕哝道。
“不，您知道。”她执拗地答道，“可怜的安托万·卡雷（Antoine Carré）东躲西藏、眼看就要被捕的时候，他母亲不敢写信给他，生怕信半路给人截了去，是您给她指点英国爵爷的门路的。后来英国爵爷寻到了安托万·卡雷，把他连同他母亲平平安安带出了法国。卡雷夫人（Mme. Carré）是我的教母……就在她奉英国爵爷之命前去见他的那个晚上，我亲眼见过她。那晚的前前后后我全知道……我知道，您就是居间传话的人。”
“就算我是吧，”他一边摆弄纸笔，一边闷声闷气地咕哝，“只怕我已经尝过苦头了。卡雷那桩事过后，足足一个礼拜，我不敢在巴黎街面上露脸；将近半个月不敢做我的营生……如今好容易才敢重新摆摊。我可不打算这么急着再拿我这条老脖子去冒险……”
“这是人命关天的事啊。”阿涅丝恳求道，泪水又一次涌上她那双哀告的眼睛，愁苦之色重新罩上了她的脸。

===Original===

“Your life, citizeness?” queried the old man, “or that of citizen-deputy Fabrice?”
“Hush!” she broke in again, as a look of real terror now overspread her face. Then she added under her breath: “You know?”
“I know that Mademoiselle Agnès de Lucines is fiancée to the citizen-deputy Arnould Fabrice,” rejoined the old man quietly, “and that it is Mademoiselle Agnès de Lucines who is speaking with me now.”
“You have known that all along?”
“Ever since mademoiselle first tripped past me at the angle of the Pont Neuf dressed in winsey kirtle and wearing sabots on her feet.﻿ ﻿…”

===Chinese===

“是您的性命么，女公民？”老头儿问，“还是公民议员法布里斯（Fabrice）的？”
“嘘！”她又急忙打断，这一回，一脸真正惶恐的神色罩上了她的脸。然后她低声问：“您知道了？”
“我知道，阿涅丝·德·吕西纳小姐（Mademoiselle Agnès de Lucines）是公民议员阿尔努·法布里斯（Arnould Fabrice）的未婚妻，”老头儿不动声色地答道，“也知道此刻跟我说话的，正是阿涅丝·德·吕西纳小姐。”
“您早就知道了？”
“打小姐您头一回穿着粗呢裙子、脚踏木屐，蹦蹦跳跳从新桥拐角我摊前经过的那天起……”

===Original===

“But how?” she murmured, puzzled, not a little frightened, for his knowledge might prove dangerous to her. She was of gentle birth, and as such an object of suspicion to the Government of the Republic and of the Terror; her mother was a hopeless cripple, unable to move: this together with her love for Arnould Fabrice had kept Agnès de Lucines in France these days, even though she was in hourly peril of arrest.
“Tell me what has happened,” the old man said, unheeding her last anxious query. “Perhaps I can help﻿ ﻿…”
“Oh! you cannot﻿—the English milor’ can and will if only we could know where he is. I thought of him the moment I received that awful man’s letter﻿—and then I thought of you.﻿ ﻿…”
“Tell me about the letter﻿—quickly,” he interrupted her with some impatience. “I’ll be writing something﻿—but talk away, I shall hear every word. But for God’s sake be as brief as you can.”

===Chinese===

“可您怎么会知道？”她喃喃地问，又是纳闷，又是不无惊惧，因为他知道的这些事，说不定会给她招来祸殃。她出身良家，单凭这一条，在共和国政府和恐怖时期（the Terror）眼里便是个可疑之人；她母亲是个无望痊愈的残废，动弹不得——这一层，再加上她对阿尔努·法布里斯的爱情，把阿涅丝·德·吕西纳留在了如今的法国，尽管她时时刻刻都有被捕的危险。
“告诉我出了什么事。”老头儿说，没理会她最后那句惶惶的问话，“兴许我能帮上忙……”
“哦！您帮不上——英国爵爷能，只要我们知道他在哪儿，他就一定肯。我一收到那个可怕的人的信，头一个便想到了他——跟着就想到了您……”
“说说那封信——快。”他不耐烦地打断她，“我随便写点什么——你只管讲，我一个字也落不下。可千万看在上帝份上，说得越简短越好。”

===Original===

He drew some paper nearer to him and dipped his pen in the ink. He appeared to be writing under her dictation. Thin, flaky snow had begun to fall and settled in a smooth white carpet upon the frozen ground, and the footsteps of the passersby sounded muffled as they hurried along. Only the lapping of the water of the sluggish river close by broke the absolute stillness of the air.
Agnès de Lucines’ pale face looked ethereal in this framework of white which covered her shoulders and the shawl crossed over her bosom: only her eyes, dark, appealing, filled with a glow of immeasurable despair, appeared tensely human and alive.
“I had a letter this morning,” she whispered, speaking very rapidly, “from citizen Hériot﻿—that awful man﻿—you know him?”
“Yes, yes!”

===Chinese===

他把纸挪近了些，笔蘸上墨水，装出一副听她口授、伏案书写的模样。细碎的雪花开始飘落，在冻硬的地面上铺开一层平整的白毯，行人匆匆来去，脚步声听上去都发闷了。只有近旁那条迟缓的大河，河水轻拍堤岸的声音，打破四下里死一般的寂静。
雪花落在她肩头、落在她胸前交裹的披巾上，这一圈白的映衬之下，阿涅丝·德·吕西纳那张苍白的脸恍若不在人间；只有她那双眼睛——黝黑、哀婉，燃着无尽绝望的光——还紧绷绷地活着，是人的眼睛。
“今天早上我收到一封信，”她压着嗓子，说得飞快，“是埃里奥（Hériot）公民来的——那个可怕的人——您知道他吗？”
“知道，知道！”

===Original===

“He used to be valet in the service of deputy Fabrice. Now he, too, is a member of the National Assembly﻿ ﻿… he is arrogant and cruel and vile. He hates Arnould Fabrice and he professes himself passionately in love with me.”
“Yes, yes!” murmured the old man, “but the letter?”
“It came this morning. In it he says that he has in his possession a number of old letters, documents and manuscripts which are quite enough to send deputy Fabrice to the guillotine. He threatens to place all those papers before the Committee of Public Safety unless﻿ ﻿… unless I.﻿ ﻿…”
She paused, and a deep blush, partly of shame, partly of wrath, suffused her pale cheeks.

===Chinese===

“他从前在法布里斯议员手下当贴身男仆。如今他也当上了国民议会（National Assembly）的议员……此人骄横、狠毒、下流。他恨阿尔努·法布里斯，又口口声声说自己为我神魂颠倒。”
“知道，知道！”老头儿咕哝道，“信上怎么说？”
“信是今早到的。他在信里说，他手里攥着一批旧信件、文书和手稿，足可以把法布里斯议员送上断头台。他威胁说，要把这些文件一古脑儿呈到公安委员会（Committee of Public Safety）去，除非……除非我……”
她顿住了，一阵深红涌上苍白的双颊，一半是羞惭，一半是愤怒。

===Original===

“Unless you accept his grimy hand in marriage,” concluded the man dryly.
Her eyes gave him answer. With pathetic insistence she tried now to glean a ray of hope from the old scarecrow’s inscrutable face. But he was bending over his writing: his fingers were blue with cold, his great shoulders were stooping to his task.
“Citizen,” she pleaded.
“Hush!” he muttered, “no more now. The very snowflakes are made up of whispers that may reach those bloodhounds yet. The English milor’ shall know of this. He will send you a message if he thinks fit.”

===Chinese===

“除非您肯嫁给他，接受他那只肮脏的手。”男人冷冷地把话说完。
她的眼睛替她作了回答。她怀着令人心酸的执拗，想从老稻草人那深不可测的脸上觅出一丝希望。可他只顾俯身写字：手指冻得发青，宽宽的肩膀弓着，一心扑在活计上。
“公民。”她哀求道。
“嘘！”他咕哝道，“这儿不能再多说了。连这些雪花都是窃窃私语凑成的，保不齐就飘进那些猎犬的耳朵里。英国爵爷会知道这件事的。他若觉得妥当，自会捎信给您。”

===Original===

“Citizen﻿—”
“Not another word, in God’s name! Pay me five sous for this letter and pray Heaven that you have not been watched.”
She shivered and drew her shawl closer round her shoulders, then she counted out five sous with elaborate care and laid them out upon the table. The old man took up the coins. He blew into his fingers, which looked paralysed with the cold. The snow lay over everything now; the rough awning had not protected him or his wares.
Agnès turned to go. The last she saw of him, as she went up the Rue Dauphine, was one broad shoulder still bending over the table, and clad in the shabby, caped coat all covered with snow like an old Santa Claus.

===Chinese===

“公民——”
“一个字也别再说了，看在上帝份上！这封信付我五个苏，再求上天保佑，您没叫人盯上梢。”
她打了个寒噤，把披巾往肩上拢了拢，随后仔仔细细数出五个苏，一枚一枚摆在桌上。老头儿收起钱，往冻得像是僵了的手指上呵气。如今什么都盖上了雪；那粗陋的凉棚，既没能遮住他，也没能遮住他的家什。
阿涅丝转身走了。她沿多菲纳街走去，回头望见的最后一眼，是他那宽宽的肩膀仍旧弓在桌子上，那件破旧的带披肩斗篷大衣落满了雪，活像一位老圣诞老人。

### II / ### 二

===Original===

It was half-an-hour before noon, and citizen-deputy Hériot was preparing to go out to the small tavern round the corner where he habitually took his déjeuner. Citizen Rondeau, who for the consideration of ten sous a day looked after Hériot’s paltry creature-comforts, was busy tidying up the squalid apartment which the latter occupied on the top floor of a lodging-house in the Rue Cocatrice. This apartment consisted of three rooms leading out of one another; firstly there was a dark and narrow antechamber wherein slept the aforesaid citizen-servant; then came a sitting-room sparsely furnished with a few chairs, a centre table and an iron stove, and finally there was the bedroom wherein the most conspicuous object was a large oak chest clamped with wide iron hinges and a massive writing-desk; the bed and a very primitive washstand were in an alcove at the farther end of the room and partially hidden by a tapestry curtain.
At exactly half-past seven that morning there came a peremptory knock at the door of the antechamber, and as Rondeau was busy in the bedroom, Hériot went himself to see who his unexpected visitor might be. On the landing outside stood an extraordinary-looking individual﻿—more like a tall and animated scarecrow than a man﻿—who in a tremulous voice asked if he might speak with the citizen Hériot.
“That is my name,” said the deputy gruffly, “what do you want?”

===Chinese===

离正午还有半个钟头，公民议员埃里奥正准备出门，到街角那家小酒馆去——他平日总在那儿用 déjeuner（午餐）。隆多（Rondeau）公民一天拿十个苏的酬劳，照料埃里奥那点儿微不足道的日常起居，此刻正忙着收拾他那套破旧的寓所。这寓所在一栋公寓的顶楼，位于科卡特里街（Rue Cocatrice），一共三间屋子，一间套一间：头一间是又暗又窄的前厅，前述那位公民仆人夜里就睡在那儿；再进去是一间客厅，陈设寥寥，几把椅子、一张居中的桌子、一只铁炉子；最里面是卧室，屋里顶扎眼的东西，是一只用宽铁合页箍着的大橡木箱和一张敦实的写字台；床和一副再简陋不过的洗脸架摆在屋子尽头的一个凹室里，让一幅挂毯帘半遮半掩着。
那天早上七点半整，前厅门上响起一阵不容分说的叩门声；隆多正在卧室里忙，埃里奥便亲自去看看这位不速之客是谁。门外楼梯平台上站着一个模样古怪的人——不像个人，倒像一副又高又会动弹的稻草人——他声音发颤，问能不能同埃里奥公民说几句话。
“我就是埃里奥，”议员粗声粗气地说，“你要干什么？”

===Original===

He would have liked to slam the door in the old scarecrow’s face, but the latter, with the boldness which sometimes besets the timid, had already stepped into the antechamber and was now quietly sauntering through to the next room into the one beyond. Hériot, being a representative of the people and a social democrat of the most advanced type, was supposed to be accessible to everyone who desired speech with him. Though muttering sundry curses, he thought it best not to go against his usual practice, and after a moment’s hesitation he followed his unwelcome visitor.
The latter was in the sitting-room by this time; he had drawn a chair close to the table and sat down with the air of one who has a perfect right to be where he is; as soon as Hériot entered he said placidly:
“I would desire to speak alone with the citizen-deputy.”

===Chinese===

他本想照着老稻草人的脸把门砰地摔上，可后者凭着胆小鬼有时会突然发作的那股愣劲，早已跨进前厅，此刻正不慌不忙、晃晃悠悠地穿过隔壁房间往最里面那间去。埃里奥身为人民代表，又是个最激进的社会民主派，照理谁来求见都该接谈。他一面嘟嘟囔囔骂骂咧咧，一面觉得还是不便破了自己的惯例，迟疑了片刻，便跟上了这位不受欢迎的来客。
来客这时已到了客厅，把一把椅子拉到桌边坐下，那副神气，仿佛天经地义自己就该坐在这儿；埃里奥一进来，他便平平静静地说：
“我想同公民议员单独谈一谈。”

===Original===

And Hériot, after another slight hesitation, ordered Rondeau to close the bedroom door.
“Keep your ears open in case I call,” he added significantly.
“You are cautious, citizen,” merely remarked the visitor with a smile.
To this Hériot vouchsafed no reply. He, too, drew a chair forward and sat opposite his visitor, then he asked abruptly: “Your name and quality?”

===Chinese===

埃里奥又略一迟疑，吩咐隆多把卧室的门关上。
“竖起耳朵听着，说不定我要叫你。”他意味深长地补了一句。
“您可真谨慎，公民。”来客只微微一笑，说了这么一句。
对这句话，埃里奥不屑置答。他也拉过一把椅子，在来客对面坐下，随即劈头问道：“姓甚名谁，干什么的？”

===Original===

“My name is Lépine at your service,” said the old man, “and by profession I write letters at the rate of five sous or so, according to length, for those who are not able to do it for themselves.”
“Your business with me?” queried Hériot curtly.
“To offer you two thousand francs for the letters which you stole from deputy Fabrice when you were his valet,” replied Lépine with perfect calm.
In a moment Hériot was on his feet, jumping up as if he had been stung; his pale, shortsighted eyes narrowed till they were mere slits, and through them he darted a quick, suspicious glance at the extraordinary out-at-elbows figure before him. Then he threw back his head and laughed till the tears streamed down his cheeks and his sides began to ache.

===Chinese===

“小人姓莱平，听候差遣，”老头儿说，“操的营生是替不会写信的人写信，按长短计价，五个苏上下。”
“找我何事？”埃里奥没好气地问。
“出两千法郎，买您当年给法布里斯议员当仆人时从他那儿偷走的那些信件。”莱平回答，语气平静极了。
埃里奥霍地跳起身来，像被什么蜇了一下；他一双近视的浅色眼睛眯成了两道缝，从缝里朝眼前这个捉襟见肘的怪人飞快地、疑疑惑惑地扫了一眼。接着他仰起头大笑，笑得眼泪顺着腮帮子淌，笑得两肋生疼。

===Original===

“This is a farce, I presume, citizen,” he said when he had recovered something of his composure.
“No farce, citizen,” replied Lépine calmly. “The money is at your disposal whenever you care to bring the letters to my pitch at the angle of the Rue Dauphine and the Quai des Augustins, where I carry on my business.”
“Whose money is it? Agnès de Lucines’ or did that fool Fabrice send you?”
“No one sent me, citizen. The money is mine﻿—a few savings I possess﻿—I honour citizen Fabrice﻿—I would wish to do him service by purchasing certain letters from you.”

===Chinese===

“我想，这是一出滑稽戏吧，公民。”他多少定下神来，说道。
“不是滑稽戏，公民。”莱平不动声色地答道，“什么时候您高兴把信件带到我摆摊的地方来——多菲纳街和奥古斯丁滨河道交成的那个拐角上，我的营生就在那儿——那笔钱随时听您支配。”
“钱是谁的？是阿涅丝·德·吕西纳的，还是那个傻瓜法布里斯差你来的？”
“谁也没差我来，公民。钱是我自己的——我攒下的几个体己——我敬重法布里斯公民——想从您手里买下那几封信，也算替他效一份力。”

===Original===

Then as Hériot, moody and sullen, remained silent and began pacing up and down the long, bare floor of the room, Lépine added persuasively, “Well! what do you say? Two thousand francs for a packet of letters﻿—not a bad bargain these hard times.”
“Get out of this room,” was Hériot’s fierce and sudden reply.
“You refuse?”

===Chinese===

埃里奥阴沉着脸，闷声不响，开始在屋里光秃秃的长地板上来回踱步；莱平便又劝道：“怎么样？您意下如何？一包信换两千法郎——这荒年景，不算吃亏的买卖了。”
“给我滚出这间屋子。”埃里奥的回答又凶又突兀。
“您拒绝啦？”

===Original===

“Get out of this room!”
“As you please,” said Lépine as he, too, rose from his chair. “But before I go, citizen Hériot,” he added, speaking very quietly, “let me tell you one thing. Mademoiselle Agnès de Lucines would far sooner cut off her right hand than let yours touch it even for one instant. Neither she nor deputy Fabrice would ever purchase their lives at such a price.”
“And who are you﻿—you mangy old scarecrow?” retorted Hériot, who was getting beside himself with rage, “that you should assert these things? What are those people to you, or you to them, that you should interfere in their affairs?”
“Your question is beside the point, citizen,” said Lépine blandly; “I am here to propose a bargain. Had you not better agree to it?”

===Chinese===

“滚出这间屋子！”
“悉听尊便。”莱平说着，也从椅子上站起身来。“不过临走之前，埃里奥公民，”他又添了一句，声音极平静，“容我告诉您一件事。阿涅丝·德·吕西纳小姐宁可砍下自己的右手，也决不肯让您的手碰它一下，碰一瞬也不成。她也好，法布里斯议员也好，谁也不会按这个价钱去买自己的性命。”
“你算什么东西——你这不中用的老稻草人？”埃里奥反唇相讥，气得几乎发狂，“也配来说这种话？那些人跟你什么相干，你跟他们又什么相干，要你来多管闲事？”
“您这话离题了，公民。”莱平温和地说，“我上这儿来，是为了一桩买卖。您顶好还是答应了吧？”

===Original===

“Never!” reiterated Hériot emphatically.
“Two thousand francs,” reiterated the old man imperturbably.
“Not if you offered me two hundred thousand,” retorted the other fiercely. “Go and tell that, to those who sent you. Tell them that I﻿—Hériot﻿—would look upon a fortune as mere dross against the delight of seeing that man Fabrice, whom I hate beyond everything in earth or hell, mount up the steps to the guillotine. Tell them that I know that Agnès de Lucines loathes me, that I know that she loves him. I know that I cannot win her save by threatening him. But you are wrong, citizen Lépine,” he continued, speaking more and more calmly as his passions of hatred and of love seemed more and more to hold him in their grip; “you are wrong if you think that she will not strike a bargain with me in order to save the life of Fabrice, whom she loves. Agnès de Lucines will be my wife within the month, or Arnould Fabrice’s head will fall under the guillotine, and you, my interfering friend, may go to the devil, if you please.”

===Chinese===

“休想！”埃里奥斩钉截铁，一字一顿。
“两千法郎。”老头儿又不慌不忙重了一句。
“你就是搬出二十万来也休想。”对方勃然大怒，恶狠狠地回敬，“去说给你主子听吧。告诉他们，我——埃里奥——把万贯家财只当粪土，只要能亲眼看着那个法布里斯——普天之下、九幽之下，我最恨的就是他——一步一步登上断头台的台阶，便是天大的赏心乐事。告诉他们，我知道阿涅丝·德·吕西纳厌恶我，我知道她爱他。我知道，不拿他来要挟，我便永远得不到她。可是你错了，莱平公民，”他接着往下说，恨与爱这两股情火仿佛把他攥得越来越紧，他的口气反倒越来越平静；“你要是以为她不肯为了救她心爱的法布里斯而同我做成这笔交易，那你就错了。不出这个月，阿涅丝·德·吕西纳就得做我的妻子；不然，阿尔努·法布里斯的脑袋就得落进断头台下。至于你，我这位爱管闲事的朋友，高兴上哪儿见鬼去就上哪儿见鬼去。”

===Original===

“That would be but a tame proceeding, citizen, after my visit to you,” said the old man, with unruffled sangfroid. “But let me, in my turn, assure you of this, citizen Hériot,” he added, “that Mlle. de Lucines will never be your wife, that Arnould Fabrice will not end his valuable life under the guillotine﻿—and that you will never be allowed to use against him the cowardly and stolen weapon which you possess.”
Hériot laughed﻿—a low, cynical laugh and shrugged his thin shoulders:
“And who will prevent me, I pray you?” he asked sarcastically.

===Chinese===

“公民，那也未免太不过瘾了，我既然登了门，岂能白跑。”老头儿说，那份 sangfroid（沉着冷静）纹丝不动。“不过这回轮到我向你打包票了，埃里奥公民，”他补充道，“德·吕西纳小姐永远不会做你的妻子；阿尔努·法布里斯那条宝贵的性命，也不会了结在断头台下；还有——你手里那件偷来的懦夫武器，永远别想用来对付他。”
埃里奥笑了——一声低低的、冷嘲的笑，耸了耸瘦削的肩膀：
“那么请问，谁来拦我呢？”他讥诮地问。

===Original===

The old man made no immediate reply, but he came just a step or two closer to the citizen-deputy and, suddenly drawing himself up to his full height, he looked for one brief moment down upon the mean and sordid figure of the ex-valet. To Hériot it seemed as if the whole man had become transfigured; the shabby old scarecrow looked all of a sudden like a brilliant and powerful personality; from his eyes there flashed down a look of supreme contempt and of supreme pride, and Hériot﻿—unable to understand this metamorphosis which was more apparent to his inner consciousness than to his outward sight, felt his knees shake under him and all the blood rush back to his heart in an agony of superstitious terror.
From somewhere there came to his ear the sound of two words: “I will!” in reply to his own defiant query. Surely those words uttered by a man conscious of power and of strength could never have been spoken by the dilapidated old scarecrow who earned a precarious living by writing letters for ignorant folk.
But before he could recover some semblance of presence of mind citizen Lépine had gone, and only a loud and merry laugh seemed to echo through the squalid room.

===Chinese===

老头儿没有立刻回答，只朝公民议员跟前挪了一两步，猛然间把整个身子挺得笔直，居高临下地把这个昔日仆佣那委琐龌龊的身影望了片刻。在埃里奥眼里，这个人仿佛整个儿脱胎换骨了；那寒碜的老稻草人霎时间像是一位光彩照人、威势逼人的大人物；他眼中凌空闪下一道睥睨万物的轻蔑、一股至高无上的傲气。埃里奥不明白这番变化——它在他内心的知觉里，比在肉眼上更显豁——只觉得两膝在身下打颤，周身的血都轰然涌回心脏，陷在一种迷信般的恐怖里不能自拔。
不知从哪里，他耳朵里传来两个字：“我拦！”——正是对他那句挑衅问话的回答。这样两个字，出自一个自知手握力量权势的人之口；那个替愚民写信、挣几个辛苦钱的破落老稻草人，断断是说不出来的。
可不等他定下一点神来，莱平公民已经去了，只有一阵响亮的、快活的笑声，仿佛还在那间破屋里回荡。

===Original===

Hériot shook off the remnant of his own senseless terror; he tore open the door of the bedroom and shouted to Rondeau, who truly was thinking that the citizen-deputy had gone mad:
“After him!﻿—after him! Quick! curse you!” he cried.
“After whom?” gasped the man.
“The man who was here just now﻿—an aristo.”

===Chinese===

埃里奥抖落掉自己那点无谓的余悸；他一把拽开卧室的门，朝隆多大喊——隆多心里还真以为议员公民发了疯：
“追他！——追他！快！你这该死的！”他嚷道。
“追谁呀？”那人喘着气问。
“刚才来的那个人——一个贵族佬（aristo）。”

===Original===

“I saw no one﻿—but the Public Letter-Writer, old Lépine﻿—I know him well﻿—”
“Curse you for a fool!” shouted Hériot savagely, “the man who was here was that cursed Englishman﻿—the one whom they call the Scarlet Pimpernel. Run after him﻿—stop him, I say!”
“Too late, citizen,” said the other placidly; “whoever was here before is certainly halfway down the street by now.”

===Chinese===

“我没见着别人——只有那位公共代书人，老莱平——他我熟得很——”
“你这蠢货，真该死！”埃里奥狂怒地咆哮，“方才在这儿的那个人，就是那个该死的英国人——人家管他叫红花侠的那个。追上去——拦住他，听见没有！”
“来不及了，公民。”对方慢条斯理地说，“不管方才来的是谁，这会儿准已走到半条街外了。”

### III / ### 三

===Original===

“No use, Ffoulkes,” said Sir Percy Blakeney to his friend half-an-hour later, “the man’s passions of hatred and desire are greater than his greed.”
The two men were sitting together in one of Sir Percy Blakeney’s many lodgings﻿—the one in the Rue des Petits Pères﻿—and Sir Percy had just put Sir Andrew Ffoulkes au fait with the whole sad story of Arnould Fabrice’s danger and Agnès de Lucines’ despair.
“You could do nothing with the brute, then?” queried Sir Andrew.

===Chinese===

“没有用，福克斯。”半小时后，珀西·布莱克尼爵士（Sir Percy Blakeney）对他的朋友说，“这家伙的仇恨和欲念，比他的贪心更大。”
两人正坐在珀西·布莱克尼爵士众多寓所中的一处——小神父街（Rue des Petits Pères）的那一处——刚才，珀西爵士已把阿尔努·法布里斯身陷险境、阿涅丝·德·吕西纳走投无路的伤心事，对安德鲁·福克斯爵士（Sir Andrew Ffoulkes）和盘托出，令他 au fait（知悉内情）。
“这么说，那畜生那儿你没讨着便宜？”安德鲁爵士问。

===Original===

“Nothing,” replied Blakeney. “He refused all bribes, and violence would not have helped me, for what I wanted was not to knock him down, but to get hold of the letters.”
“Well, after all, he might have sold you the letters and then denounced Fabrice just the same.”
“No, without actual proofs he could not do that. Arnould Fabrice is not a man against whom a mere denunciation would suffice. He has the grudging respect of every faction in the National Assembly. Nothing but irrefutable proof would prevail against him﻿—and bring him to the guillotine.”
“Why not get Fabrice and Mlle. de Lucines safely over to England?”

===Chinese===

“没有。”布莱克尼答道，“行贿他一概不吃；动武也帮不上忙，因为我要的不是把他打翻在地，而是把信拿到手。”
“不过说到底，他就算把信卖了你，回头照样可以告发法布里斯。”
“不，没有真凭实据，他办不到。阿尔努·法布里斯不是一纸告发信就能扳倒的人。国民议会里各派人物，哪怕再不情愿，也都要敬他三分。唯有铁证如山，才能奈何得了他——把他送上断头台。”
“何不把法布里斯和德·吕西纳小姐平平安安送去英国？”

===Original===

“Fabrice would not come. He is not of the stuff that émigrés are made of. He is not an aristocrat; he is a republican by conviction, and a demmed honest one at that. He would scorn to run away, and Agnès de Lucines would not go without him.”
“Then what can we do?”
“Filch those letters from that brute Hériot,” said Blakeney calmly.

===Chinese===

“法布里斯不肯走。他不是流亡分子（émigrés）那块料。他不是贵族；他凭着一腔信念做共和派，而且是天字第一号的老实人（demmed honest）。要他临阵脱逃，他不屑为之；而他不走，阿涅丝·德·吕西纳也决不会独去。”
“那我们还能怎么办？”
“从埃里奥那畜生手里把信偷出来。”布莱克尼平静地说。

===Original===

“Housebreaking, you mean!” commented Sir Andrew Ffoulkes dryly.
“Petty theft, shall we say?” retorted Sir Percy. “I can bribe the lout who has charge of Hériot’s rooms to introduce us into his master’s sanctum this evening when the National Assembly is sitting and the citizen-deputy safely out of the way.”
And the two men﻿—one of whom was the most intimate friend of the Prince of Wales and the acknowledged darling of London society﻿—thereupon fell to discussing plans for surreptitiously entering a man’s room and committing larceny, which in normal times would entail, if discovered, a long term of imprisonment, but which, in these days, in Paris, and perpetrated against a member of the National Assembly, would certainly be punished by death.

===Chinese===

“你是说，撬门入户！”安德鲁·福克斯爵士干巴巴地评了一句。
“不如说，小偷小摸吧。”珀西爵士回敬道，“看守埃里奥住处的那个粗胚，我可以买通，让他今晚趁国民议会开会、公民议员稳稳当当不在家的当口，把我们引进他主子的私室。”
于是这两个人——其中一位是威尔士亲王（the Prince of Wales）最亲密的朋友、伦敦社交界公认的红人儿——便一头商量起潜入他人房间行窃的计划来。这种事若是太平年月败了露，少说也得判长期监禁；而在如今，在巴黎，冲的又是国民议会议员，一旦被擒，必死无疑。

### IV / ### 四

===Original===

Citizen Rondeau, whose business it was to look after the creature comforts of deputy Hériot, was standing in the antechamber facing the two visitors whom he had just introduced into his master’s apartments, and idly turning a couple of gold coins over and over between his grimy fingers.
“And mind, you are to see nothing and hear nothing of what goes on in the next room,” said the taller of the two strangers; “and when we go there’ll be another couple of louis for you. Is that understood?”
“Yes! it’s understood,” grunted Rondeau sullenly; “but I am running great risks. The citizen-deputy sometimes returns at ten o’clock, but sometimes at nine.﻿ ﻿… I never know.”
“It is now seven,” rejoined the other; “we’ll be gone long before nine.”

===Chinese===

隆多公民——他的差事就是照料埃里奥议员的日常起居——正站在前厅里，面对他刚让进主人寓所的两位来客，一双脏手的手指间有一下没一下地捻着两枚金币。
“记着，隔壁屋里的事，你什么也没看见，什么也没听见。”两个生人中个子高的那个说，“等我们走了，另外再赏你两枚路易（louis）。听明白了？”
“是！明白了。”隆多闷闷地哼道，“可我这是提着脑袋办事。议员公民有时十点钟回，有时九点就回……谁说得准。”
“现在七点。”对方答道，“九点以前我们早走了。”

===Original===

“Well,” said Rondeau surlily, “I go out now for my supper. I’ll return in half an hour, but at half-past eight you must clear out.”
Then he added with a sneer:
“Citizens Legros and Desgas usually come back with deputy Hériot of nights, and citizens Jeanniot and Bompard come in from next door for a game of cards. You wouldn’t stand much chance if you were caught here.”
“Not with you to back up so formidable a quintette of stalwarts,” assented the tall visitor gaily. “But we won’t trouble about that just now. We have a couple of hours before us in which to do all that we want. So au revoir, friend Rondeau﻿ ﻿… two more louis for your complaisance, remember, when we have accomplished our purpose.”

===Chinese===

“那好，”隆多没好气地说，“我这就出去吃晚饭。半个钟头后回来，可八点半你们必须滚蛋。”
接着他又冷笑一声，添了一句：
“夜里，勒格罗（Legros）公民和德加（Desgas）公民通常跟议员公民一道回来，让尼奥（Jeanniot）公民和邦帕尔（Bompard）公民又从隔壁过来打牌。你们要是在这儿叫人拿住，可没多少活路。”
“有你给这样一条五位好汉的阵仗撑腰，我们自然没戏。”高个子来客笑吟吟地应道，“不过眼下还犯不上操这份心。我们有一两个钟头的工夫，尽够办完要办的事。那么，au revoir（再见），隆多朋友……事成之后，你这份乖巧，另有两枚路易，记住了。”

===Original===

Rondeau muttered something more, but the two strangers paid no further heed to him; they had already walked to the next room, leaving Rondeau in the antechamber.
Sir Percy Blakeney did not pause in the sitting-room where an oil lamp suspended from the ceiling threw a feeble circle of light above the centre table. He went straight through to the bedroom. Here, too, a small lamp was burning which only lit up a small portion of the room﻿—the writing-desk and the oak chest﻿—leaving the corners and the alcove, with its partially drawn curtains, in complete shadow.
Blakeney pointed to the oak chest and to the desk.

===Chinese===

隆多又嘟囔了几句什么，两位生人却不再理会他；他们已经走进隔壁房间，把隆多撂在前厅里。
珀西·布莱克尼爵士没有在客厅里停留——客厅天花板下吊着一盏油灯，在居中的桌子上方投下一圈微光。他径直穿过客厅，走进卧室。这儿也点着一盏小灯，只照亮屋里一小块地方——写字台和大橡木箱——屋角和那挂着半掩帘子的凹室，都浸在一片漆黑里。
布莱克尼指了指橡木箱和写字台。

===Original===

“You tackle the chest, Ffoulkes, and I will go for the desk,” he said quietly, as soon as he had taken a rapid survey of the room. “You have your tools?”
Ffoulkes nodded, and anon in this squalid room, ill-lit, ill-ventilated, barely furnished, was presented one of the most curious spectacles of these strange and troublous times: two English gentlemen, the acknowledged dandies of London drawing-rooms, busy picking locks and filing hinges like any common house-thieves.
Neither of them spoke, and a strange hush fell over the room﻿—a hush only broken by the click of metal against metal, and the deep breathing of the two men bending to their task. Sir Andrew Ffoulkes was working with a file on the padlocks of the oak chest, and Sir Percy Blakeney, with a bunch of skeleton keys, was opening the drawers of the writing-desk. These, when finally opened, revealed nothing of any importance; but when anon Sir Andrew was able to lift the lid of the oak chest, he disclosed an innumerable quantity of papers and documents tied up in neat bundles, docketed and piled up in rows and tiers to the very top of the chest.

===Chinese===

“你对付箱子，福克斯，写字台归我。”他迅速扫视了一遍屋子，随即低声说道，“家伙什带了没有？”
福克斯点点头。于是，在这间又暗又闷、家徒四壁的破屋子里，呈现出了这个离奇乱世里最古怪的景象之一：两位英国绅士——伦敦客厅里公认的时髦人物——正像两个寻常的穿窬小贼一样，忙着撬锁锉铰链。
两人谁也不说话，屋里静得出奇——只有金属磕着金属的轻响，和两个俯身干活的人深长的呼吸，偶尔打破这寂静。安德鲁·福克斯爵士用锉刀对付橡木箱上的挂锁，珀西·布莱克尼爵士则拿一串万能钥匙开写字台的抽屉。抽屉终于尽数打开，里面却没有要紧东西；可不一会儿，安德鲁爵士掀起了橡木箱的箱盖，露出的却是数不清的文件文书，一扎一扎捆得整整齐齐，编了号，一排排一层层码到箱顶。

===Original===

“Quick to work, Ffoulkes,” said Blakeney, as in response to his friend’s call he drew a chair forward and, seating himself beside the chest, started on the task of looking through the hundreds of bundles which lay before him. “It will take us all our time to look through these.”
Together now the two men set to work﻿—methodically and quietly﻿—piling up on the floor beside them the bundles of papers which they had already examined, and delving into the oak chest for others. No sound was heard save the crackling of crisp paper and an occasional ejaculation from either of them when they came upon some proof or other of Hériot’s propensity for blackmail.
“Agnès de Lucines is not the only one whom this brute is terrorising,” murmured Blakeney once between his teeth; “I marvel that the man ever feels safe, alone in these lodgings, with no one but that weak-kneed Rondeau to protect him. He must have scores of enemies in this city who would gladly put a dagger in his heart or a bullet through his back.”

===Chinese===

“快动手，福克斯。”布莱克尼应着朋友的招呼，拉过一把椅子，在箱子旁边坐下，着手翻检面前这几百扎文件。“要把这些过一遍，咱们的工夫紧巴巴的。”
于是两人一起干了起来——有条不紊，悄然无声——翻检过的纸扎码在身边地上，再从橡木箱里掏出新的。屋里只听见脆纸的窸窣声，偶尔两人中的一个低低咒骂一声——那是又撞见了什么物证，证明埃里奥素有敲诈勒索的癖好。
“挨这畜生挟制的，不止阿涅丝·德·吕西纳一个。”布莱克尼有一回咬着牙咕哝道，“我真纳闷，这人孤零零住在这寓所里，护身的只有那个软骨头隆多，他凭什么觉得安稳。这城里恨他的仇家，少说也几十个，谁不乐意往他心窝里捅一刀，或是朝他后背打一颗子弹。”

===Original===

They had been at work for close on half an hour when an exclamation of triumph, quickly smothered, escaped Sir Percy’s lips.
“By Gad, Ffoulkes!” he said, “I believe I have got what we want!”
With quick, capable hands he turned over a bundle which he had just extracted from the chest. Rapidly he glanced through them. “I have them, Ffoulkes,” he reiterated more emphatically as he put the bundle into his pocket; “now everything back in its place and﻿—”

===Chinese===

两人干了快半个钟头，珀西爵士唇边忽然逸出一声压得飞快的欢呼。
“老天作证，福克斯！”他说，“我相信，要找的东西到手了！”
他一双麻利的手翻着他刚从箱里抽出来的一扎文件，飞快扫了一遍。“拿到了，福克斯。”他把纸扎塞进口袋，又加重语气说了一遍，“现在，东西各归各位——”

===Original===

Suddenly he paused, his slender hand up to his lips, his head turned toward the door, an expression of tense expectancy in every line of his face.
“Quick, Ffoulkes,” he whispered, “everything back into the chest, and the lid down.”
“What ears you have,” murmured Ffoulkes as he obeyed rapidly and without question. “I heard nothing.”
Blakeney went to the door and bent his head to listen.

===Chinese===

他蓦地顿住，纤细的手指抵着嘴唇，头朝门口转过去，脸上每一根线条都绷着紧张的期待。
“快，福克斯。”他悄声说，“东西全放回箱子里，盖上盖。”
“你这耳朵，真是神了。”福克斯一面毫不迟疑、飞快照办，一面低声说，“我可什么也没听见。”
布莱克尼走到门边，侧耳细听。

===Original===

“Three men coming up the stairs,” he said; “they are on the landing now.”
“Have we time to rush them?”
“No chance! They are at the door. Two more men have joined them, and I can distinguish Rondeau’s voice, too.”
“The quintette,” murmured Sir Andrew. “We are caught like two rats in a trap.”

===Chinese===

“三个人上楼来了。”他说，“这会儿到楼梯平台了。”
“来得及冲出去吗？”
“没门儿！已经到门口了。又来了两个人跟他们会合，我还听得出隆多的嗓音。”
“五位好汉到齐了。”安德鲁爵士喃喃道，“咱们是瓮中之鳖，让人家一锅端了。”

===Original===

Even as he spoke the opening of the outside door could be distinctly heard, then the confused murmur of many voices. Already Blakeney and Ffoulkes had with perfect presence of mind put the finishing touches to the tidying of the room﻿—put the chairs straight, shut down the lid of the oak chest, closed all the drawers of the desk.
“Nothing but good luck can save us now,” whispered Blakeney as he lowered the wick of the lamp. “Quick now,” he added, “behind that tapestry in the alcove and trust to our stars.”
Securely hidden for the moment behind the curtains in the dark recess of the alcove the two men waited. The door leading into the sitting-room was ajar, and they could hear Hériot and his friends making merry irruption into the place. From out the confusion of general conversation they soon gathered that the debates in the Chamber had been so dull and uninteresting that, at a given signal, the little party had decided to adjourn to Hériot’s rooms for their habitual game of cards. They could also hear Hériot calling to Rondeau to bring bottles and glasses, and vaguely they marvelled what Rondeau’s attitude might be like at this moment. Was he brazening out the situation, or was he sick with terror?

===Chinese===

话音未落，便听得外间的门清清楚楚地开了，跟着是乱纷纷的人语。布莱克尼和福克斯早已临危不乱，把收拾屋子的收尾活计做得妥妥帖帖——椅子摆正，橡木箱盖合上，写字台的抽屉尽数关严。
“如今只有天照应才能救咱们了。”布莱克尼一面捻低灯芯，一面悄声说，“快，”他又道，“躲到凹室那幅挂毯后头去，听凭星宿保佑。”
两人躲在凹室暗处的帘子后面，一时藏得严严实实，便等着。通客厅的门虚掩着，他们听得见埃里奥和他的朋友们兴冲冲涌进屋来。从乱糟糟的谈笑里，他们很快听明白：议会里的辩论实在乏味透顶，于是暗号一打，这一小伙人便决定转移到埃里奥的住处，玩他们玩惯了的牌。还听得见埃里奥嚷着叫隆多拿酒瓶和杯子来。两人心里暗自纳罕，不知道隆多此刻做何光景——是硬着头皮装糊涂呢，还是吓得魂不附体？

===Original===

Suddenly Hériot’s voice came out more distinctly.
“Make yourselves at home, friends,” he was saying; “here are cards, dominoes, and wine. I must leave you to yourselves for ten minutes whilst I write an important letter.”
“All right, but don’t be long,” came in merry response.
“Not longer than I can help,” rejoined Hériot. “I want my revenge against Bompard, remember. He did fleece me last night.”

===Chinese===

忽然，埃里奥的声音清晰起来。
“诸位自便，朋友们。”只听他说，“这儿有牌，有骨牌，还有酒。我得失陪十分钟，写封要紧的信。”
“行啊，可别磨蹭。”传来快活的回答。
“能多快就多快。”埃里奥应道，“我还得找邦帕尔报一箭之仇呢，记着。昨晚他可把我宰惨了。”

===Original===

“Hurry on, then,” said one of the men. “I’ll play Desgas that return game of dominoes until then.”
“Ten minutes and I’ll be back,” concluded Hériot.
He pushed open the bedroom door. The light within was very dim. The two men hidden behind the tapestry could hear him moving about the room muttering curses to himself. Presently the light of the lamp was shifted from one end of the room to the other. Through the opening between the two curtains Blakeney could just see Hériot’s back as he placed the lamp at a convenient angle upon his desk, divested himself of his overcoat and muffler, then sat down and drew pen and paper closer to him. He was leaning forward, his elbow resting upon the table, his fingers fidgeting with his long, lank hair. He had closed the door when he entered, and from the other room now the voices of his friends sounded confused and muffled. Now and then an exclamation: “Double!” “*Je﻿ ﻿… tiens!*” “*Cinq-deux!*” an oath, a laugh, the click of glasses and bottles came out more clearly; but the rest of the time these sounds were more like a droning accompaniment to the scraping of Hériot’s pen upon the paper when he finally began to write his letter.
Two minutes went by and then two more. The scratching of Hériot’s pen became more rapid as he appeared to be more completely immersed in his work. Behind the curtain the two men had been waiting: Blakeney ready to act, Ffoulkes equally ready to interpret the slightest signal from his chief.

===Chinese===

“那就快去快回。”其中一人说，“趁这工夫，我跟德加把那盘骨牌补上。”
“十分钟，我就回来。”埃里奥说完就算拍板。
他推开卧室的门。屋里灯光极暗。藏在挂毯后面的两人听得见他在屋里走动，自顾自地咒骂。不一会儿，灯光从屋子这头挪到了那头。从两幅帘子的缝隙里，布莱克尼恰好望得见埃里奥的背影：他把灯搁在写字台顺手的角度，脱下大衣，解下围巾，随后坐下，把纸笔拉到跟前。他俯着身子，肘支在桌上，手指摆弄着自己那头长发。他进门时随手带上了门，隔壁朋友们的人声便显得混沌发闷。不时有一两声吆喝透出来：“双！”“*Je… tiens!*（我……成了！）”“*Cinq-deux!*（五二！）”一声咒骂，一阵哄笑，杯盏酒瓶磕碰的脆响；其余时候，这些声响只像一片嗡嗡的背景，衬着埃里奥的笔尖最终落在纸上沙沙的刮擦声——他到底动手写信了。
两分钟过去了，又是两分钟。埃里奥的笔越写越快，像是整个人都沉进了他的活计里。帘子后面，两人一直候着：布莱克尼蓄势待发，福克斯也同样备好了，只等首领最细微的一个暗号。

===Original===

The next minute Blakeney had stolen out of the alcove, and his two hands﻿—so slender and elegant looking, and yet with a grip of steel﻿—had fastened themselves upon Hériot’s mouth, smothering within the space of a second the cry that had been half-uttered. Ffoulkes was ready to complete the work of rendering the man helpless: one handkerchief made an efficient gag, another tied the ankles securely. Hériot’s own coat-sleeves supplied the handcuffs, and the blankets off the bed tied around his legs rendered him powerless to move. Then the two men lifted this inert mass on to the bed and Ffoulkes whispered anxiously: “Now, what next?”
Hériot’s overcoat, hat, and muffler lay upon a chair. Sir Percy, placing a warning finger upon his lips, quickly divested himself of his own coat, slipped that of Hériot on, twisted the muffler round his neck, hunched up his shoulders, and murmuring: “Now for a bit of luck!” once more lowered the light of the lamp and then went to the door.
“Rondeau!” he called. “Hey, Rondeau!” And Sir Percy himself was surprised at the marvellous way in which he had caught the very inflection of Hériot’s voice.

===Chinese===

下一分钟，布莱克尼已经蹑出凹室，两只手——瞧着那么纤细秀气，掐起来却如钢钳——霎时扣上了埃里奥的嘴，把那喊出一半的叫声掐灭在喉咙里。福克斯随即上来收尾，叫这人动弹不得：一条手帕成了顶用的堵嘴布，另一条把脚踝捆了个结实；埃里奥自己的衣袖权充镣铐，床上的毯子绑住双腿，叫他寸步难移。然后两人把这团死沉的肉抬上床，福克斯急急悄声问：“接下来呢？”
埃里奥的大衣、帽子和围巾搭在一把椅子上。珀西爵士把警示的手指抵在唇上，飞快脱下自己的外衣，套上埃里奥那件，围巾往脖子上一绕，肩膀一耸，嘴里咕哝一句“就看这一把运气了！”又把灯光捻低一档，这才走向门口。
“隆多！”他喊道，“喂，隆多！”连珀西爵士自己也惊奇，竟把埃里奥嗓音里的那股腔调学了个惟妙惟肖。

===Original===

“Hey, Rondeau!” came from one of the players at the table, “the citizen-deputy is calling you!”
They were all sitting round the table: two men intent upon their game of dominoes, the other two watching with equal intentness. Rondeau came shuffling out of the antechamber. His face, by the dim light of the oil lamp, looked jaundiced with fear.
“Rondeau, you fool, where are you?” called Blakeney once again.
The next moment Rondeau had entered the room. No need for a signal or an order this time. Ffoulkes knew by instinct what his chief’s bold scheme would mean to them both if it succeeded. He retired into the darkest corner of the room as Rondeau shuffled across to the writing-desk. It was all done in a moment. In less time than it had taken to bind and gag Hériot, his henchman was laid out on the floor, his coat had been taken off him, and he was tied into a mummy-like bundle with Sir Andrew Ffoulkes’ elegant coat fastened securely round his arms and chest. It had all been done in silence. The men in the next room were noisy and intent on their game; the slight scuffle, the quickly smothered cries had remained unheeded.

===Chinese===

“喂，隆多！”牌桌边一个打牌的喊，“议员公民叫你呢！”
他们都围桌坐着：两个埋头打骨牌，另外两个同样全神贯注地观战。隆多趿拉着脚步从前厅出来。油灯昏光下，他那张脸吓得蜡黄。
“隆多，你这蠢货，死哪儿去了？”布莱克尼又喊了一声。
说话间隆多已经进了屋。这一回，用不着暗号，也用不着吩咐。福克斯凭本能便明白，首领这一手大胆的招数若能成功，对他们两人意味着什么。隆多趿拉趿拉朝写字台走去时，他退进了屋里最暗的角落。一切都是眨眼间的事：捆埃里奥堵埃里奥的嘴还没用掉的工夫，这个仆从已经放倒在地，剥了外套，让安德鲁·福克斯爵士那件讲究的外衣反捆住胳膊和胸口，裹成了木乃伊似的一团。全程悄没声儿。隔壁屋里的人正闹哄哄埋头玩牌；这边的轻微扭打、压灭在半截的呼救，全没人理会。

===Original===

“Now, what next?” queried Sir Andrew Ffoulkes once more.
“The impudence of the d﻿—﻿—﻿l, my good Ffoulkes,” replied Blakeney in a whisper, “and may our stars not play us false. Now let me make you look as like Rondeau as possible﻿—there! Slip on his coat﻿—now your hair over your forehead﻿—your coat-collar up﻿—your knees bent﻿—that’s better!” he added as he surveyed the transformation which a few deft strokes had made in Sir Andrew Ffoulkes’ appearance. “Now all you have to do is to shuffle across the room﻿—here’s your prototype’s handkerchief﻿—of dubious cleanliness, it is true, but it will serve﻿—blow your nose as you cross the room, it will hide your face. They’ll not heed you﻿—keep in the shadows and God guard you﻿—I’ll follow in a moment or two﻿ ﻿… but don’t wait for me.”
He opened the door, and before Sir Andrew could protest his chief had pushed him out into the room where the four men were still intent on their game. Through the open door Sir Percy now watched his friend who, keeping well within the shadows, shuffled quietly across the room. The next moment Sir Andrew was through and in the antechamber. Blakeney’s acutely sensitive ears caught the sound of the opening of the outer door. He waited for a while, then he drew out of his pocket the bundle of letters which he had risked so much to obtain. There they were neatly docketed and marked: “The affairs of Arnould Fabrice.”

===Chinese===

“接下来呢？”安德鲁·福克斯爵士又问了一遍。
“凭魔——鬼的厚脸皮，我亲爱的福克斯。”布莱克尼悄声答道，“但愿咱们的星宿别临阵倒戈。来，让我把你拾掇得尽量像隆多——这就成了！套上他的外套——头发抿到脑门上——衣领竖起来——膝盖弯着——好多了！”他端详着几下半拿乔出的变化，安德鲁·福克斯爵士已然判若两人。“如今你只消趿拉着走过这间屋子——喏，你这位本尊的手帕——干净是谈不上的，可顶得上用——走过屋子时擤擤鼻子，正好挡住脸。他们不会留意你——拣暗处走，上帝保佑你——我一两分钟后就跟出来……可别等我。”
他开了门，不等安德鲁爵士提出异议，便把他推了出去，推进那间四个人仍埋头酣战的屋子。借着敞开的门，珀西爵士目送他的朋友紧贴暗处，悄没声儿地趿拉过屋子。转眼的工夫，安德鲁爵士已经穿过去，进了前厅。布莱克尼那双敏锐至极的耳朵，捕捉到外间门开启的声响。他又等了片刻，然后从口袋里掏出那扎他冒了天大风险才到手的信件。信扎得整整齐齐，编着签目，标着一行字：“阿尔努·法布里斯诸事”。

===Original===

Well! if he got away tonight Agnès de Lucines would be happy and free from the importunities of that brute Hériot; after that he must persuade her and Fabrice to go to England and to freedom.
For the moment his own safety was terribly in jeopardy; one false move﻿—one look from those players round the table.﻿ ﻿… Bah! even then﻿—!
With an inward laugh he pushed open the door once more and stepped into the room. For the moment no one noticed him; the game was at its most palpitating stage; four shaggy heads met beneath the lamp and four pairs of eyes were gazing with rapt attention upon the intricate maze of the dominoes.
Blakeney walked quietly across the room; he was just midway and on a level with the centre table when a voice was suddenly raised from that tense group beneath the lamp: “Is it thou, friend Hériot?”

===Chinese===

好啦！今夜他若脱得了身，阿涅丝·德·吕西纳便能得到幸福，从此摆脱埃里奥那畜生的纠缠；接下来，他还得劝她和法布里斯前往英国，前往自由。
眼下的当口，他自身的安危悬于一线；一步失错——牌桌边那些人抬眼一望……呸！真到那步田地，也未必——！
他心里暗笑，又一次推门进了屋。这当儿谁也没有留意他；牌局正到最扣人心弦的节骨眼上；四颗乱蓬蓬的脑袋凑在灯下，八只眼睛都出了神，盯住骨牌摆成的迷魂阵。
布莱克尼不声不响往屋子那头走；刚走到半道，与居中的桌子齐平，灯下那屏气凝神的一伙里忽然有人扬声问：“是你吗，埃里奥朋友？”

===Original===

Then one of the men looked up and stared, and another did likewise and exclaimed: “It is not Hériot!”
In a moment all was confusion, but confusion was the very essence of those hair-breadth escapes and desperate adventures which were as the breath of his nostrils to the Scarlet Pimpernel. Before those four men had had time to jump to their feet, or to realise that something was wrong with their friend Hériot, he had run across the room, his hand was on the knob of the door﻿—the door that led to the antechamber and to freedom.
Bompard, Desgas, Jeanniot, Legros were at his heels, but he tore open the door, bounded across the threshold, and slammed it to with such a vigorous bang that those on the other side were brought to a momentary halt. That moment meant life and liberty to Blakeney; already he had crossed the antechamber. Quite coolly and quietly now he took out the key from the inner side of the main door and slipped it to the outside. The next second﻿—even as the four men rushed helter-skelter into the antechamber he was out on the landing and had turned the key in the door.
His prisoners were safely locked in﻿—in Hériot’s apartments﻿—and Sir Percy Blakeney, calmly and without haste, was descending the stairs of the house in the Rue Cocatrice.

===Chinese===

一个人抬起头直勾勾瞪着，另一个也照样子抬头一看，失声叫道：“这不是埃里奥！”
霎时间满屋大乱，可是乱，恰恰是红花侠那些死里逃生、铤而走险的勾当的精髓所在——那些勾当，于他正如呼吸一般家常。不等那四个人来得及跳起身来，或者弄明白他们的朋友埃里奥出了什么岔子，他已奔过屋子，手已握住门的把手——那扇通往前厅、通往自由的门。
邦帕尔、德加、让尼奥、勒格罗紧追在他脚后，可他拽开房门，一跃过槛，反手砰地摔上门，那一声响亮得出奇，门那边的人被震得顿了一顿。这一顿，于布莱克尼便是性命和自由；他早已穿过前厅，此刻正不慌不忙、沉着地从大门内侧拔出钥匙，捅到外侧去。下一秒——那四个人正没头没脑涌进前厅——他已经到了楼梯平台上，钥匙在门锁里转了一圈。
他的俘虏们被稳稳当当锁在了里面——锁在埃里奥的寓所里——而珀西·布莱克尼爵士，从容不迫、不紧不慢，正沿着科卡特里街那栋房子的楼梯拾级而下。

---

===Original===

The next morning Agnès de Lucines received, through an anonymous messenger, the packet of letters which would so gravely have compromised Arnould Fabrice. Though the weather was more inclement than ever, she ran out into the streets, determined to seek out the old Public Letter-Writer and thank him for his mediation with the English milor, who surely had done this noble action.
But the old scarecrow had disappeared.

===Chinese===

第二天早晨，阿涅丝·德·吕西纳经由一位匿名信使，收到了那包足以连累阿尔努·法布里斯身败名裂的信件。天气比先前更加恶劣，她还是跑到街上，一心要寻到那位老公共代书人，谢谢他居间引线、通到了英国爵爷跟前——这桩侠举，必定是爵爷所为。
可是老稻草人已经不知去向。

## The Traitor / ## 叛徒

### I / ### 一

===Original===

Not one of them had really trusted him for some time now. Heaven and his conscience alone knew what had changed my Lord Kulmsted from a loyal friend and keen sportsman into a surly and dissatisfied adherent﻿—adherent only in name.

Some say that lack of money had embittered him. He was a confirmed gambler, and had been losing over-heavily of late; and the League of the Scarlet Pimpernel demanded sacrifices of money at times from its members, as well as of life if the need arose. Others averred that jealousy against the chief had outweighed Kulmsted’s honesty. Certain it is that his oath of fealty to the League had long ago been broken in the spirit. Treachery hovered in the air.

But the Scarlet Pimpernel himself, with that indomitable optimism of his, and almost maddening insouciance, either did not believe in Kulmsted’s disloyalty or chose not to heed it.

He even asked him to join the present expedition﻿—one of the most dangerous undertaken by the League for some time, and which had for its object the rescue of some women of the late unfortunate Marie Antoinette’s household: maids and faithful servants, ruthlessly condemned to die for their tender adherence to a martyred queen. And yet eighteen pairs of faithful lips had murmured words of warning.

It was towards the end of November, 1793. The rain was beating down in a monotonous drip, drip, drip on to the roof of a derelict house in the Rue Berthier. The wan light of a cold winter’s morning peeped in through the curtainless window and touched with its weird grey brush the pallid face of a young girl﻿—a mere child﻿—who sat in a dejected attitude on a rickety chair, with elbows leaning on the rough deal table before her, and thin, grimy fingers wandering with pathetic futility to her tearful eyes.

In the farther angle of the room a tall figure in dark clothes was made one, by the still lingering gloom, with the dense shadows beyond.

===Chinese===

一段时间以来，他们当中已经没有一个人真正信任他了。究竟是什么把库姆斯特德勋爵（Lord Kulmsted）从一位忠诚的朋友、一个醉心狩猎的运动健将，变成一个乖张不满的党羽——徒有党羽之名——只有上天和他自己的良心知道。

有人说，是缺钱让他心生怨怼。他是个积习难改的赌徒，近来输得极惨；而红花侠同盟有时需要成员在钱财上作出牺牲，事急时甚至要牺牲性命。也有人断言，对首领的嫉妒压倒了库姆斯特德的良知。可以肯定的是，他对同盟许下的效忠之誓，早在心里便已背弃。背叛的阴云在空气中盘旋。

可红花侠本人，凭着他那百折不挠的乐观和那份几乎叫人抓狂的漫不经心，要么根本不信库姆斯特德会不忠，要么就是懒得理会。

他甚至邀他参加眼下的这趟行动——那是同盟近来承头所办的行动中最凶险的一次，目的是营救已故不幸的玛丽·安托瓦内特（Marie Antoinette）宫中的几名妇人：几名女仆和忠仆，只因眷恋殉难的王后，便被无情地处死。然而，十八张忠诚的嘴曾低声说过劝阻的话。

时值 1793 年 11 月底。雨水单调地滴、滴、滴，敲打着贝尔蒂埃街（Rue Berthier）上一座破败空屋的屋顶。寒冬清晨惨淡的光线从没有窗帘的窗口探进来，用它诡异的灰笔触，拂过一个年轻姑娘的苍白面孔——她不过是个孩子——颓然坐在一把摇晃的破椅子上，两肘支在面前粗糙的松木桌上，瘦削肮脏的手指一次次徒劳地抹向含泪的眼睛。

房间深处的角落里，一个穿深色衣服的高大身影，借着尚未散尽的昏暗，与后面浓重的阴影融为一体。

===Original===

“We have starved,” said the girl, with rebellious tears. “Father and I and the boys are miserable enough, God knows; but we have always been honest.”

From out the shadows in that dark corner of the room there came the sound of an oath quickly suppressed.

“Honest!” exclaimed the man, with a harsh, mocking laugh, which made the girl wince as if with physical pain. “Is it honest to harbour the enemies of your country? Is it honest﻿—”

But quickly he checked himself, biting his lips with vexation, feeling that his present tactics were not like to gain the day.

He came out of the gloom and approached the girl with every outward sign of eagerness. He knelt on the dusty floor beside her, his arms stole round her meagre shoulders, and his harsh voice was subdued to tones of gentleness.

“I was only thinking of your happiness, Yvonne,” he said tenderly; “of poor blind papa and the two boys to whom you have been such a devoted little mother. My only desire is that you should earn the gratitude of your country by denouncing her most bitter enemy﻿—an act of patriotism which will place you and those for whom you care forever beyond the reach of sorrow or of want.”

===Chinese===

“我们挨过饿，”姑娘说，眼里含着倔强的泪水，“天知道，爸爸、我和弟弟们已经够苦的了；可我们一直本本分分。”

从房间那个阴暗角落的阴影里，传来一声急忙压住的咒骂。

“本分！”男人喊道，发出一声刺耳的嘲笑，笑得姑娘像挨了打似的瑟缩了一下。“窝藏祖国的敌人算本分吗？本分——”

但他立刻克制住自己，懊恼地咬着嘴唇，觉得眼下这套办法未必能奏效。

他从暗处走出来，一脸热切地凑近姑娘。他跪在她身旁积尘的地板上，双臂悄悄环住她瘦削的肩头，粗厉的嗓音也压成了温柔的调子。

“我只是替你的幸福着想啊，伊冯娜（Yvonne），”他柔声说，“替可怜的瞎眼爸爸着想，替你一直像个小母亲般尽心照料的两个弟弟着想。我唯一的愿望，是让你通过告发国家最凶恶的仇敌来赢得祖国的感激——这一爱国之举，将使你和你在乎的人永远摆脱悲伤与穷困。”

===Original===

The voice, the appeal, the look of love, was more than the poor, simple girl could resist. Milor was so handsome, so kind, so good.

It had all been so strange: these English aristocrats coming here, she knew not whence, and who seemed fugitives even though they had plenty of money to spend. Two days ago they had sought shelter like malefactors escaped from justice﻿—in this same tumbledown, derelict house where she, Yvonne, with her blind father and two little brothers, crept in of nights, or when the weather was too rough for them all to stand and beg in the streets of Paris.

There were five of them altogether, and one seemed to be the chief. He was very tall, and had deep blue eyes, and a merry voice that went echoing along the worm-eaten old rafters. But milor﻿—the one whose arms were encircling her even now﻿—was the handsomest among them all. He had sought Yvonne out on the very first night when she had crawled shivering to that corner of the room where she usually slept.

The English aristocrats had frightened her at first, and she was for flying from the derelict house with her family and seeking shelter elsewhere; but he who appeared to be the chief had quickly reassured her. He seemed so kind and good, and talked so gently to blind papa, and made such merry jests with François and Clovis that she herself could scarce refrain from laughing through her tears.

But later on in the night, milor﻿—her milor, as she soon got to call him﻿—came and talked so beautifully that she, poor girl, felt as if no music could ever sound quite so sweetly in her ear.

That was two days ago, and since then milor had often talked to her in the lonely, abandoned house, and Yvonne had felt as if she dwelt in Heaven. She still took blind papa and the boys out to beg in the streets, but in the morning she prepared some hot coffee for the English aristocrats, and in the evening she cooked them some broth. Oh! they gave her money lavishly; but she quite understood that they were in hiding, though what they had to fear, being English, she could not understand.

===Chinese===

这声音，这恳求，这含情脉脉的眼神，可怜的单纯姑娘如何招架得住。milor（爵爷）是那么英俊，那么和善，那么好。

这一切都那么蹊跷：这些英国贵族不知从何处来到这里，明明有的是钱花，却像是亡命之人。两天前，他们像逃脱法网的罪犯一样求个藏身之处——躲进的正是这座摇摇欲坠的破屋；她伊冯娜带着瞎眼的父亲和两个小弟弟，夜里或是天气恶劣、没法全家站在巴黎街头行乞的时候，就溜进这里栖身。

他们一共五个人，其中一个似乎是首领。他个子非常高，有一双深蓝色的眼睛，快活的嗓音在虫蛀的老椽木间回荡。可是milor——就是此刻还搂着她的这个人——是他们当中最英俊的。头一天夜里，伊冯娜瑟瑟发抖地爬到她平时睡觉的那个墙角时，他就找上了她。

这些英国贵族起初吓着了她，她打算带着全家逃离这座破屋，另找栖身之处；可那位像是首领的人很快便让她安下心来。他看上去那么和善，跟瞎眼爸爸说话那么温存，还跟弗朗索瓦（François）和克洛维斯（Clovis）说些有趣的玩笑话，连她自己也不禁破涕为笑。

可到了夜里晚些时候，milor——她的milor，她很快就这么称呼他了——过来跟她说话，说得那么动听，可怜的姑娘觉得，世上再没有任何音乐能在她耳畔响得这般甜美。

那是两天前的事了。打那以后，milor时常在这座孤寂荒废的屋子里跟她说话，伊冯娜只觉得自己仿佛住在天堂里。她仍旧带着瞎眼爸爸和弟弟们上街乞讨，可早上她给英国贵族们煮热咖啡，傍晚给他们熬汤。哦！他们给起钱来毫不吝惜；她也完全明白他们是在躲藏，只是他们英国人有什么可怕的，她就想不明白了。

===Original===

And now milor﻿—her milor﻿—was telling her that these Englishmen, her friends, were spies and traitors, and that it was her duty to tell citizen Robespierre and the Committee of Public Safety all about them and their mysterious doings. And poor Yvonne was greatly puzzled and deeply distressed, because, of course, whatever milor said, that was the truth; and yet her conscience cried out within her poor little bosom, and the thought of betraying those kind Englishmen was horrible to her.

“Yvonne,” whispered milor in that endearing voice of his, which was like the loveliest music in her ear, “my little Yvonne, you do trust me, do you not?”

“With all my heart, milor,” she murmured fervently.

“Then, would you believe it of me that I would betray a real friend?”

“I believe, milor, that whatever you do is right and good.”

A sigh of infinite relief escaped his lips.

===Chinese===

而现在，milor——她的milor——却在告诉她，这些英国人，她的朋友们，是奸细和叛徒；告诉她，把他们的种种神秘勾当统统禀告罗伯斯庇尔公民（citizen Robespierre）和公安委员会，是她的本分。可怜的伊冯娜又困惑又痛苦，因为milor说的话当然都是真的；可她瘦小的胸膛里，良心在呐喊，一想到要出卖那些好心的英国人，她就觉得可怕极了。

“伊冯娜，”milor用他那亲昵的嗓音低语，那声音在她听来宛如最动听的音乐，“我的小伊冯娜，你是信得过我的，对不对？”

“我全心全意地信你，milor。”她热切地低声说。

“那么，你会相信我会出卖真正的朋友吗？”

“我相信，milor，你无论做什么都是对的、好的。”

一声如释重负的长叹从他唇间逸出。

===Original===

“Come, that’s better!” he said, patting her cheek kindly with his hand. “Now, listen to me, little one. He who is the chief among us here is the most unscrupulous and daring rascal whom the world has ever known. He it is who is called the ‘Scarlet Pimpernel!’ ”

“The Scarlet Pimpernel!” murmured Yvonne, her eyes dilated with superstitious awe, for she too had heard of the mysterious Englishman and of his followers, who rescued aristocrats and traitors from the death to which the tribunal of the people had justly condemned them, and on whom the mighty hand of the Committee of Public Safety had never yet been able to fall.

“This Scarlet Pimpernel,” said milor earnestly after a while, “is also mine own most relentless enemy. With lies and promises he induced me to join him in his work of spying and of treachery, forcing me to do this work against which my whole soul rebels. You can save me from this hated bondage, little one. You can make me free to live again, make me free to love and place my love at your feet.”

His voice had become exquisitely tender, and his lips, as he whispered the heavenly words, were quite close to her ear. He, a great gentleman, loved the miserable little waif whose kindred consisted of a blind father and two half-starved little brothers, and whose only home was this miserable hovel, whence milor’s graciousness and bounty would soon take her.

Do you think that Yvonne’s sense of right and wrong, of honesty and treachery, should have been keener than that primeval instinct of a simple-hearted woman to throw herself trustingly into the arms of the man who has succeeded in winning her love?

Yvonne, subdued, enchanted, murmured still through her tears:

===Chinese===

“这就对了！”他说着，亲切地用手拍了拍她的脸颊。“听我说，小家伙。我们这里那个当首领的，是世上最肆无忌惮、最大胆妄为的恶棍。他就是那个被称作‘红花侠’的人！”

“红花侠！”伊冯娜喃喃道，眼睛因迷信的敬畏而睁得溜圆。她也听说过那个神秘的英国人和他的党羽——他们把贵族和叛徒从人民的法庭公正判处的死刑中救走，而公安委员会的铁腕至今还从未能落到他们头上。

“这个红花侠，”milor过了一会儿诚恳地说，“也是我自己最不肯罢休的死敌。他满口谎言、许下种种诺言，诱我入伙，跟他干那种刺探和背叛的勾当，逼着我做这让我整个灵魂都厌弃的营生。你能救我脱离这可恨的枷锁，小家伙。你能让我重获自由之身，让我自由地去爱，把我的爱放在你的脚下。”

他的嗓音变得说不出的温柔，当他悄声吐出这些美妙的话语时，嘴唇几乎贴着她的耳朵。他，一位尊贵的绅士，竟爱着这个可怜的小流浪儿——她的亲人只有一个瞎眼的父亲和两个半饥半饱的小弟弟，唯一的家就是这间破烂的窝棚，而milor的恩泽和慷慨很快就会把她从这里带走。

难道你们以为，伊冯娜心中对是非对错、对本分与背信的分辨，会比一个纯朴女子的原始本能更敏锐吗？——那本能，就是一旦有男人赢得了她的芳心，便全心信赖地投入他的怀抱。

伊冯娜被降服了，被迷住了，仍旧噙着泪喃喃问道：

===Original===

“What would milor have me do?”

Lord Kulmsted rose from his knees satisfied.

“Listen to me, Yvonne,” he said. “You are acquainted with the Englishman’s plans, are you not?”

“Of course,” she replied simply. “He has had to trust me.”

“Then you know that at sundown this afternoon I and the three others are to leave for Courbevoie on foot, where we are to obtain what horses we can whilst awaiting the chief.”

“I did not know whither you and the other three gentlemen were going, milor,” she replied; “but I did know that some of you were to make a start at four o’clock, whilst I was to wait here for your leader and prepare some supper against his coming.”

===Chinese===

“milor要我做什么呢？”

库姆斯特德勋爵心满意足地从膝上站起身来。

“听我说，伊冯娜，”他说，“那位英国人的计划你都知道，是不是？”

“当然，”她天真地答道，“他不得不信我。”

“那么你知道，今天下午日落时分，我和另外三位要步行前往库尔贝瓦（Courbevoie），在那里设法弄到几匹马，等候首领。”

“我原先不知道您和另外三位先生要去哪儿，milor，”她答道，“可我确实知道你们当中有几位四点钟就要动身，我则要在这儿等你们的首领，备好晚饭等他来。”

===Original===

“At what time did he tell you that he would come?”

“He did not say; but he did tell me that when he returns he will have friends with him﻿—a lady and two little children. They will be hungry and cold. I believe that they are in great danger now, and that the brave English gentleman means to take them away from this awful Paris to a place of safety.”

“The brave English gentleman, my dear,” retorted milor, with a sneer, “is bent on some horrible work of spying. The lady and the two children are, no doubt, innocent tools in his hands, just as I am, and when he no longer needs them he will deliver them over to the Committee of Public Safety, who will, of a surety, condemn them to death. That will also be my fate, Yvonne, unless you help me now.”

“Oh, no, no!” she exclaimed fervently. “Tell me what to do, milor, and I will do it.”

“At sundown,” he said, sinking his voice so low that even she could scarcely hear, “when I and the three others have started on our way, go straight to the house I spoke to you about in the Rue Dauphine﻿—you know where it is?”

===Chinese===

“他说过他什么时候来吗？”

“他没说；不过他告诉过我，他回来时会有朋友跟着——一位夫人和两个小孩子。他们会又饿又冷。我想他们现在处境非常危险，那位勇敢的英国先生是想把他们从这座可怕的巴黎带去一个安全的地方。”

“那位勇敢的英国先生，亲爱的，”milor冷笑着反驳道，“一心干的是可怕的间谍勾当。那位夫人和两个孩子，多半是他手里无辜的工具，就跟我一样；等他用不着他们了，就会把他们交给公安委员会，委员会必然会判他们死刑。伊冯娜，要是你现在不帮我，这也将是我的下场。”

“哦，不，不！”她激动地喊道，“告诉我该做什么，milor，我一定去做。”

“日落时分，”他把声音压得极低，连她也几乎听不见，“等我和另外三位动身上路之后，你就径直去我跟你说过的多菲纳街（Rue Dauphine）上的那座房子——你知道在哪儿吧？”

===Original===

“Oh, yes, milor.”

“You will know the house by its tumbledown portico and the tattered red flag that surmounts it. Once there, push the door open and walk in boldly. Then ask to speak with citizen Robespierre.”

“Robespierre?” exclaimed the child in terror.

“You must not be afraid, Yvonne,” he said earnestly; “you must think of me and of what you are doing for me. My word on it﻿—Robespierre will listen to you most kindly.”

“What shall I tell him?” she murmured.

===Chinese===

“哦，知道，milor。”

“那房子门廊坍塌，顶上插着一面破烂的红旗，你一见就能认出来。到了那儿，推开门，大胆走进去。然后求见罗伯斯庇尔公民。”

“罗伯斯庇尔？”孩子惊恐地喊出声来。

“你千万别怕，伊冯娜，”他恳切地说，“你要想着我，想着你为我做的事。我向你担保——罗伯斯庇尔会非常和颜悦色地听你讲。”

“我该告诉他什么呢？”她喃喃地问。

===Original===

“That a mysterious party of Englishmen are in hiding in this house﻿—that their chief is known among them as the Scarlet Pimpernel. The rest leave to Robespierre’s discretion. You see how simple it is?”

It was indeed very simple! Nor did the child recoil any longer from the ugly task which milor, with suave speech and tender voice, was so ardently seeking to impose on her.

A few more words of love, which cost him nothing, a few kisses which cost him still less, since the wench loved him, and since she was young and pretty, and Yvonne was as wax in the hands of the traitor.

===Chinese===

“就说有一伙神秘的英国人藏在这座屋子里——说他们的首领在他们中间被唤作红花侠。其余的交给罗伯斯庇尔自己去斟酌。你瞧，这多简单？”

这确实非常简单！这孩子也不再畏缩推拒那桩丑恶的差事——milor正用温存的言语、柔和的嗓音，热切地要把这差事压到她身上。

再添几句情话——那于他毫无损失；再添几个吻——损失更是微乎其微，反正这姑娘爱他，反正她年轻貌美。伊冯娜就这样成了叛徒手中任凭搓捏的软蜡。

### II / ### 二

===Original===

Silence reigned in the low-raftered room on the ground floor of the house in the Rue Dauphine.

Citizen Robespierre, chairman of the Cordeliers Club, the most bloodthirsty, most Evolutionary club of France, had just reentered the room.

He walked up to the centre table, and through the close atmosphere, thick with tobacco smoke, he looked round on his assembled friends.

“We have got him,” he said at last curtly.

“Got him! Whom?” came in hoarse cries from every corner of the room.

“That Englishman,” replied the demagogue, “the Scarlet Pimpernel!”

===Chinese===

多菲纳街那座房子的底层，低矮的椽木房间里一片寂静。

罗伯斯庇尔公民——科德利埃俱乐部（Cordeliers Club）主席，法兰西最嗜血、最革命的俱乐部——刚刚重新走进房间。

他走到中央的桌前，透过烟气浓重、浑浊不堪的空气，环视围坐一堂的同志们。

“我们抓到他了。”他终于简短地说道。

“抓到他了！抓到谁？”房间里四面八方响起沙哑的喊声。

“那个英国人，”这位煽动家答道，“红花侠！”

===Original===

A prolonged shout rose in response﻿—a shout not unlike that of a caged herd of hungry wild beasts to whom a succulent morsel of flesh has unexpectedly been thrown.

“Where is he?” “Where did you get him?” “Alive or dead?” And many more questions such as these were hurled at the speaker from every side.

Robespierre, calm, impassive, immaculately neat in his tightly fitting coat, his smart breeches, and his lace cravat, waited awhile until the din had somewhat subsided. Then he said calmly:

“The Scarlet Pimpernel is in hiding in one of the derelict houses in the Rue Berthier.”

Snarls of derision as vigorous as the former shouts of triumph drowned the rest of his speech.

“Bah! How often has that cursed Scarlet Pimpernel been said to be alone in a lonely house? Citizen Chauvelin has had him at his mercy several times in lonely houses.”

===Chinese===

回应他的是一阵长久的吼叫——那吼声，很像笼中一群饿兽忽然被扔进一块鲜肉时的咆哮。

“他在哪儿？”“你们在哪儿抓到他的？”“活的还是死的？”诸如此类的许多问题，从四面八方向说话的人砸去。

罗伯斯庇尔沉着冷静、无动于衷，紧身礼服、利落马裤、蕾丝领结，浑身上下一丝不苟。他等了一会儿，直到喧嚣稍稍平息，才平静地说：

“红花侠就藏在贝尔蒂埃街一座破败的空屋里。”

嘲弄的咆哮同刚才得胜的欢呼一样汹涌，把他后半段话整个淹没了。

“呸！那个该诅咒的红花侠，多少回听说他孤身一人待在僻静屋子里了？肖维兰（Chauvelin）公民就曾在僻静的屋子里好几次把他握在手心里。”

===Original===

And the speaker, a short, thickset man with sparse black hair plastered over a greasy forehead, his shirt open at the neck, revealing a powerful chest and rough, hairy skin, spat in ostentatious contempt upon the floor.

“Therefore will we not boast of his capture yet, citizen Roger,” resumed Robespierre imperturbably. “I tell you where the Englishman is. Do you look to it that he does not escape.”

The heat in the room had become intolerable. From the grimy ceiling an oil-lamp, flickering low, threw lurid, ruddy lights on tricolour cockades, on hands that seemed red with the blood of innocent victims of lust and hate, and on faces glowing with desire and with anticipated savage triumph.

“Who is the informer?” asked Roger at last.

“A girl,” replied Robespierre curtly. “Yvonne Lebeau, by name; she and her family live by begging. There are a blind father and two boys; they herd together at night in the derelict house in the Rue Berthier. Five Englishmen have been in hiding there these past few days. One of them is their leader. The girl believes him to be the Scarlet Pimpernel.”

“Why has she not spoken of this before?” muttered one of the crowd, with some scepticism.

===Chinese===

说这话的人五短身材，粗壮敦实，稀疏的黑发油腻腻地贴在额头上，衬衫领口敞开，露出结实的胸膛和粗糙多毛的皮肤。他煞有介事地朝地上啐了一口，以示轻蔑。

“所以呀，咱们先别急着夸口说抓住了他，罗歇（Roger）公民，”罗伯斯庇尔不动声色地接着说，“我告诉你们那英国人在哪儿。别让他跑了，这就看你们的了。”

房间里热得让人难以忍受。污黑的房梁下，一盏油灯低低地摇曳，把阴森的红光投在三色帽徽上，投在那些仿佛浸满情欲与仇恨的无辜牺牲者鲜血的手上，投在一张张因渴盼着即将到手的野蛮胜利而灼灼发亮的脸上。

“告密的是谁？”罗歇终于问道。

“一个姑娘，”罗伯斯庇尔简短地答道，“名叫伊冯娜·勒博（Yvonne Lebeau）；她一家人靠乞讨过活。有个瞎眼的父亲，还有两个男孩；夜里他们就挤在贝尔蒂埃街那座破屋里过夜。这几天有五个英国人一直藏在那儿。其中一个是他们的首领。那姑娘以为他就是红花侠。”

“她以前怎么不说？”人群里有人嘀咕道，带着几分怀疑。

===Original===

“Frightened, I suppose. Or the Englishman paid her to hold her tongue.”

“Where is the girl now?”

“I am sending her straight home, a little ahead of us. Her presence should reassure the Englishman whilst we make ready to surround the house. In the meanwhile, I have sent special messengers to every gate of Paris with strict orders to the guard not to allow anyone out of the city until further orders from the Committee of Public Safety. And now,” he added, throwing back his head with a gesture of proud challenge, “citizens, which of you will go man-hunting tonight?”

This time the strident roar of savage exultation was loud and deep enough to shake the flickering lamp upon its chain.

A brief discussion of plans followed, and Roger﻿—he with the broad, hairy chest and that gleam of hatred forever lurking in his deep-set, shifty eyes﻿—was chosen the leader of the party.

===Chinese===

“多半是吓坏了。要么就是那英国人花钱堵住了她的嘴。”

“姑娘现在在哪儿？”

“我这就打发她直接回家去，比我们早走一步。有她在，趁我们准备包围那座房子的时候，那英国人应该能安下心来。与此同时，我已派专人赶赴巴黎各处城门，严令守卫：没有公安委员会的进一步命令，任何人都不得出城。好了，”他昂起头，摆出一副傲然挑衅的姿态，补充道，“公民们，今晚你们谁去猎捕那个人？”

这一回，狂喜的嘶吼又响又沉，震得链子上那盏摇曳的油灯直晃。

接着简短地商议了一下行动计划，罗歇——就是那个胸膛宽阔多毛、深陷而狡黠的眼睛里永远潜藏着一丝仇恨的家伙——被选为这队人的头目。

===Original===

Thirty determined and well-armed patriots set out against one man, who mayhap had supernatural powers. There would, no doubt, be some aristocrats, too, in hiding in the derelict house﻿—the girl Lebeau, it seems, had spoken of a woman and two children. Bah! These would not count. It would be thirty to one, so let the Scarlet Pimpernel look to himself.

From the towers of Notre Dame the big bell struck the hour of six, as thirty men in ragged shirts and torn breeches, shivering beneath a cold November drizzle, began slowly to wend their way towards the Rue Berthier.

They walked on in silence, not heeding the cold or the rain, but with eyes fixed in the direction of their goal, and nostrils quivering in the evening air with the distant scent of blood.

===Chinese===

三十个意志坚决、武装精良的爱国者，出发去对付一个人，一个说不定有神通的人。那座破屋里无疑还藏着几个贵族——勒博家那姑娘好像提过一个女人和两个孩子。呸！那算不得什么。三十对一，红花侠好自为之吧。

巴黎圣母院（Notre Dame）高塔上的大钟敲响六点，三十个破衬衫烂马裤的男人，在十一月寒冷的细雨中打着哆嗦，开始缓缓向贝尔蒂埃街进发。

他们默默走着，不理会寒冷和雨水，眼睛死死盯住目标的方向，鼻孔在傍晚的空气里翕动，嗅着远方的血腥气。

### III / ### 三

===Original===

At the top of the Rue Berthier the party halted. On ahead﻿—some two hundred metres farther﻿—Yvonne Lebeau’s little figure, with her ragged skirt pulled over her head and her bare feet pattering in the mud, was seen crossing one of those intermittent patches of light formed by occasional flickering street lamps, and then was swallowed up once more by the inky blackness beyond.

The Rue Berthier is a long, narrow, ill-paved and ill-lighted street, composed of low and irregular houses, which abut on the line of fortifications at the back, and are therefore absolutely inaccessible save from the front.

Midway down the street a derelict house rears ghostly debris of roofs and chimney-stacks upward to the sky. A tiny square of yellow light, blinking like a giant eye through a curtainless window, pierced the wall of the house. Roger pointed to that light.

“That,” he said, “is the quarry where our fox has run to earth.”

No one said anything; but the dank night air seemed suddenly alive with all the passions of hate let loose by thirty beating hearts.

The Scarlet Pimpernel, who had tricked them, mocked them, fooled them so often, was there, not two hundred metres away; and they were thirty to one, and all determined and desperate.

===Chinese===

到了贝尔蒂埃街街口，这一行人停了下来。前方大约再往前两百米的地方，伊冯娜·勒博瘦小的身影——破裙子罩在头上，赤脚在泥泞里啪嗒啪嗒地走——正穿过偶尔摇曳的路灯投下的一片断续光斑，随即又消失在前面墨一样的黑暗里。

贝尔蒂埃街是一条又长又窄、路面糟糕、灯光昏暗的街道，两旁是低矮参差的房屋，背靠城墙防线，因此除了正面，根本无路可进。

街道中段，一座破屋把屋顶和烟囱的残骸鬼影似的直竖向天空。一方小小的黄色灯光，像一只巨眼透过没有窗帘的窗户眨动着，刺破那座房屋的墙壁。罗歇指了指那灯光。

“那儿，”他说，“就是咱们的狐狸钻洞藏身的地方。”

谁也没有作声；但湿冷的夜空中，仿佛顿时挤满了三十颗跳动的心释放出来的种种仇恨的激情。

那个一再耍弄他们、嘲笑他们、愚弄他们的红花侠就在那里，不到两百米开外；他们三十对一，个个志在必得、破釜沉舟。

===Original===

The darkness was intense.

Silently now the party approached the house, then again they halted, within sixty metres of it.

“Hist!”

The whisper could scarce be heard, so low was it, like the sighing of the wind through a misty veil.

“Who is it?” came in quick challenge from Roger.

“I﻿—Yvonne Lebeau!”

===Chinese===

夜黑得伸手不见五指。

这一行人不声不响地逼近那座房子，随后又停了下来，离房子不到六十米。

“嘘！”

那耳语低得几乎听不见，像风穿过雾纱的一声叹息。

“谁？”罗歇迅速低声喝问。

“我——伊冯娜·勒博！”

===Original===

“Is he there?” was the eager whispered query.

“Not yet. But he may come at any moment. If he saw a crowd round the house, mayhap he would not come.”

“He cannot see a crowd. The night is as dark as pitch.”

“He can see in the darkest night,” and the girl’s voice sank to an awed whisper, “and he can hear through a stone wall.”

Instinctively, Roger shuddered. The superstitious fear which the mysterious personality of the Scarlet Pimpernel evoked in the heart of every Terrorist had suddenly seized this man in its grip.

Try as he would, he did not feel as valiant as he had done when first he emerged at the head of his party from under the portico of the Cordeliers Club, and it was with none too steady a voice that he ordered the girl roughly back to the house. Then he turned once more to his men.

===Chinese===

“他在吗？”对方急切地低声问。

“还没有。可他随时可能来。他要是看见房子四周围着一群人，说不定就不来了。”

“他看不见人群。夜黑得像沥青一样。”

“再黑的夜里他也看得见，”姑娘的声音低成了敬畏的耳语，“隔着石墙他也听得见。”

罗歇不由自主地打了个寒战。红花侠的神秘身影在每个恐怖党徒心头唤起的迷信恐惧，此刻突然把他牢牢攥住。

尽管他强打精神，却再也找不回刚带队走出科德利埃俱乐部门廊时那股豪气；他粗声粗气地吩咐姑娘回屋去，嗓音都有些不利索了。随后他又转向手下的人。

===Original===

The plan of action had been decided on in the Club, under the presidency of Robespierre; it only remained to carry the plans through with success.

From the side of the fortifications there was, of course, nothing to fear. In accordance with military regulations, the walls of the houses there rose sheer from the ground without doors or windows, whilst the broken-down parapets and dilapidated roofs towered forty feet above the ground.

The derelict itself was one of a row of houses, some inhabited, others quite abandoned. It was the front of that row of houses, therefore, that had to be kept in view. Marshalled by Roger, the men flattened their meagre bodies against the walls of the houses opposite, and after that there was nothing to do but wait.

To wait in the darkness of the night, with a thin, icy rain soaking through ragged shirts and tattered breeches, with bare feet frozen by the mud of the road﻿—to wait in silence while turbulent hearts beat well-nigh to bursting﻿—to wait for food whilst hunger gnaws the bowels﻿—to wait for drink whilst the parched tongue cleaves to the roof of the mouth﻿—to wait for revenge whilst the hours roll slowly by and the cries of the darkened city are stilled one by one!

Once﻿—when a distant bell tolled the hour of ten﻿—a loud prolonged laugh, almost impudent in its suggestion of merry insouciance, echoed through the weird silence of the night.

Roger felt that the man nearest to him shivered at that sound, and he heard a volley or two of muttered oaths.

===Chinese===

行动方案已在俱乐部里由罗伯斯庇尔主持定下，剩下的只是把它顺利执行到底。

城墙那一面当然没什么可怕的。按照军事规章，那一排房屋的墙壁从平地拔起，既无门也无窗，残破的女儿墙和破烂的屋顶高高耸立，离地足有四十英尺。

那座破屋本身是一排房屋中的一座，有的还住着人，有的早已废弃。因此需要盯住的，是那排房屋的正面。在罗歇的调度下，众人把干瘦的身子紧贴在对面房屋的墙上，此后便无事可做，只有等待。

在黑夜里等待，任细密冰冷的雨水浸透破衬衫和烂马裤，任赤脚被路上的泥浆冻僵——在沉默中等待，任狂躁的心几乎跳到迸裂——等待食物，任饥饿啃噬肚肠——等待酒水，任干渴的舌头粘住上颚——等待复仇，任钟点缓缓流过，任黑暗之城的哭喊一声声归于沉寂！

有一次——远处一口钟敲响十点的时候——一阵响亮而悠长的笑声划破夜空的诡异沉寂，回荡开来，那份快活的没心没肺几乎近于放肆。

罗歇感觉到离他最近的那个人听见这声音直打哆嗦，还听见一两串压低了的咒骂。

===Original===

“The fox seems somewhere near,” he whispered. “Come within. We’ll wait for him inside his hole.”

He led the way across the street, some of the men following him.

The door of the derelict house had been left on the latch. Roger pushed it open.

Silence and gloom here reigned supreme; utter darkness, too, save for a narrow streak of light which edged the framework of a door on the right. Not a sound stirred the quietude of this miserable hovel, only the creaking of boards beneath the men’s feet as they entered.

Roger crossed the passage and opened the door on the right. His friends pressed closely round to him and peeped over his shoulder into the room beyond.

A guttering piece of tallow candle, fixed to an old tin pot, stood in the middle of the floor, and its feeble, flickering light only served to accentuate the darkness that lay beyond its range. One or two rickety chairs and a rough deal table showed vaguely in the gloom, and in the far corner of the room there lay a bundle of what looked like heaped-up rags, but from which there now emerged the sound of heavy breathing and also a little cry of fear.

===Chinese===

“狐狸好像就在附近什么地方，”他低声说，“进屋去。咱们就在洞里等他。”

他带头穿过街道，一部分人跟了上去。

破屋的门虚掩着没上锁。罗歇推门而入。

这里寂静和幽暗笼罩了一切；还有彻底的黑暗，只有右边一扇门的门框边缘透出一线细细的光。这间凄惨的破屋里没有一点声响搅扰它的安宁，只有众人进屋时脚下木板的咯吱声。

罗歇穿过过道，推开右边的门。他的同伴们紧紧围上来，从他肩头探望里面的房间。

地板中央立着一只旧铁皮罐，上面插着一段将尽的牛油烛，它微弱摇曳的光只把光照之外的黑暗衬托得更浓。昏暗中隐约可见一两把摇晃的破椅子和一张粗糙的松木桌，而在房间深处的角落里，躺着一堆像是破布的东西，此时那堆东西里传出沉重的呼吸声，还有一声小小的惊叫。

===Original===

“Yvonne,” came in feeble, querulous accents from that same bundle of wretchedness, “are these the English milors come back at last?”

“No, no, father,” was the quick whispered reply.

Roger swore a loud oath, and two puny voices began to whimper piteously.

“It strikes me the wench has been fooling us,” muttered one of the men savagely.

The girl had struggled to her feet. She crouched in the darkness, and two little boys, half-naked and shivering, were clinging to her skirts. The rest of the human bundle seemed to consist of an oldish man, with long, gaunt legs and arms blue with the cold. He turned vague, wide-open eyes in the direction whence had come the harsh voices.

“Are they friends, Yvonne?” he asked anxiously.

===Chinese===

“伊冯娜，”那堆可怜的破烂里发出虚弱而抱怨的声音，“是英国爵爷们终于回来了吗？”

“不是，不是，爸爸，”一声急促的耳语立刻回答。

罗歇高声咒骂了一句，两个细细的嗓音开始可怜地呜咽起来。

“我看这丫头一直在耍咱们。”其中一个男人狠狠地咕哝道。

姑娘挣扎着站了起来。她蜷缩在黑暗中，两个半裸着身子、浑身发抖的小男孩紧抓着她的裙子。那堆人里剩下的，似乎是个上了年纪的男人，细长的胳膊和腿冻得发青。他把茫然睁大的眼睛转向传来粗厉嗓音的方向。

“他们是朋友吗，伊冯娜？”他不安地问。

===Original===

The girl did her best to reassure him.

“Yes, yes, father,” she whispered close to his ear, her voice scarce above her breath; “they are good citizens who hoped to find the English milor here. They are disappointed that he has not yet come.”

“Ah! but he will come, of a surety,” said the old man in that querulous voice of his. “He left his beautiful clothes here this morning, and surely he will come to fetch them.” And his long, thin hand pointed towards a distant corner of the room.

Roger and his friends, looking to where he was pointing, saw a parcel of clothes, neatly folded, lying on one of the chairs. Like so many wild cats snarling at sight of prey, they threw themselves upon those clothes, tearing them out from one another’s hands, turning them over and over as if to force the cloth and satin to yield up the secret that lay within their folds.

In the skirmish a scrap of paper fluttered to the ground. Roger seized it with avidity, and, crouching on the floor, smoothed the paper out against his knee.

It contained a few hastily scrawled words, and by the feeble light of the fast-dying candle Roger spelt them out laboriously:

===Chinese===

姑娘竭力安慰他。

“是的，是的，爸爸，”她凑到他耳边低语，声音低得几乎听不见，“他们是好公民，本指望在这儿找到那位英国爵爷。他们正为他还没来而失望呢。”

“啊！可他一定会来的，”老人用他那抱怨的腔调说，“今天早上他把那些漂亮的衣裳留在这儿了，他肯定会回来取的。”他细长的手指向房间远处的角落。

罗歇和他的同伴顺着他指的方向望去，看见一把椅子上放着一包叠得整整齐齐的衣裳。他们像一群见着猎物就龇牙低吼的野猫，一齐扑向那包衣裳，你抢我夺，翻来覆去，仿佛要逼着那些呢料和缎子吐出藏在褶皱里的秘密。

争夺之中，一片小纸片飘落到地上。罗歇急切地一把抓起，蹲在地板上，把纸在膝盖上抚平。

纸上有几行潦草涂就的字，罗歇就着即将熄灭的残烛的微光，费力地逐字辨认：

===Original===

“If the finder of these clothes will take them to the crossroads opposite the footbridge which leads straight to Courbevoie, and will do so before the clock of Courbevoie Church has struck the hour of midnight, he will be rewarded with the sum of five hundred francs.”

“There is something more, citizen Roger,” said a raucous voice close to his ear.

“Look! Look, citizen﻿—in the bottom corner of the paper!”

“The signature.”

“A scrawl done in red,” said Roger, trying to decipher it.

===Chinese===

“拾到这包衣裳的人，若能在库尔贝瓦教堂的钟敲响午夜十二点之前，把它们送到直通库尔贝瓦的那座小木桥对面的十字路口，将得到五百法郎的酬谢。”

“还有别的东西，罗歇公民，”他耳边一个嘶哑的声音说。

“看！看，公民——纸的下角！”

“签名。”

“一个红色的潦草签字，”罗歇说，竭力想辨认出来。

===Original===

“It looks like a small flower.”

“That accursed Scarlet Pimpernel!”

And even as he spoke the guttering tallow candle, swaying in its socket, suddenly went out with a loud splutter and a sizzle that echoed through the desolate room like the mocking laugh of ghouls.

===Chinese===

“看起来像一朵小花。”

“那个该死的红花侠！”

话音未落，那截将尽的牛油烛在烛台上晃了晃，噼啦一声嘶响，猛地熄灭了；那声音回荡在凄凉的房间里，像食尸鬼的嘲弄笑声。

### IV / ### 四

===Original===

Once more the tramp through the dark and deserted streets, with the drizzle﻿—turned now to sleet﻿—beating on thinly clad shoulders. Fifteen men only on this tramp. The others remained behind to watch the house. Fifteen men, led by Roger, and with a blind old man, a young girl carrying a bundle of clothes, and two half-naked children dragged as camp-followers in the rear.

Their destination now was the signpost which stands at the crossroads, past the footbridge that leads to Courbevoie.

The guard at the Maillot Gate would have stopped the party, but Roger, member of the Committee of Public Safety, armed with his papers and his tricolour scarf, overruled Robespierre’s former orders, and the party mached out of the gate.

They pressed on in silence, instinctively walking shoulder to shoulder, vaguely longing for the touch of another human hand, the sound of a voice that would not ring weirdly in the mysterious night.

There was something terrifying in this absolute silence, in such intense darkness, in this constant wandering towards a goal that seemed forever distant, and in all this weary, weary fruitless waiting; and these men, who lived their life through, drunken with blood, deafened by the cries of their victims, satiated with the moans of the helpless and the innocent, hardly dared to look around them, lest they should see ghoulish forms flitting through the gloom.

Soon they reached the crossroads, and in the dense blackness of the night the gaunt arms of the signpost pointed ghostlike towards the north.

===Chinese===

又是一段穿行于黑暗荒凉街道的跋涉，细雨——如今已变成雨夹雪——打在衣衫单薄的肩头。这一趟只去了十五个人。其余的留下来看守那座房子。十五个人由罗歇带领，后面还硬拖着一串随行的人：一个瞎眼的老人，一个抱着衣裳包袱的年轻姑娘，还有两个半裸的孩子。

他们这回的目的地，是十字路口的路标，过了直通库尔贝瓦的那座小木桥便是。

马约门（Maillot Gate）的卫兵本想拦下这一行人，但公安委员会成员罗歇亮出他的证件和三色绶带，压下了罗伯斯庇尔先前的命令，这一行人便大步出了城门。

他们默默赶路，本能地肩并着肩走，隐约渴望着能碰到另一只人手，渴望一个不会在神秘之夜发出诡异回响的声音。

这绝对的沉寂、这深重的黑暗、这朝着似乎永远遥不可及的目标不停的跋涉、这令人疲乏又疲乏而一无所获的等待，处处透着骇人；这些人，一生沉醉于鲜血，被受害者的哀号震聋了耳朵，听饱了无助无辜者的呻吟，此刻却几乎不敢四下张望，生怕看见食尸鬼的影子在幽暗中掠过。

很快他们到了十字路口，浓重的夜色里，路标瘦骨嶙峋的臂膀鬼魅似的指向北方。

===Original===

The men hung back, wrapped in the darkness as in a pall, while Roger advanced alone.

“Holà! Is anyone there?” he called softly.

Then, as no reply came, he added more loudly:

“Holà! A friend﻿—with some clothes found in the Rue Berthier. Is anyone here? Holà! A friend!”

But only from the gently murmuring river far away the melancholy call of a waterfowl seemed to echo mockingly:

“A friend!”

===Chinese===

众人裹足不前，像裹在尸衣般的黑暗里，只有罗歇一个人走上前去。

“Holà（喂）！有人吗？”他轻声喊道。

没有人回答，于是他又提高嗓门喊：

“Holà（喂）！一个朋友——带着在贝尔蒂埃街捡到的一包衣裳。有人吗？Holà（喂）！一个朋友！”

但只有远处潺潺的河水那边，一声水鸟的哀鸣仿佛在嘲弄地应和：

“一个朋友！”

===Original===

Just then the clock of Courbevoie Church struck the midnight hour.

“It is too late,” whispered the men.

They did not swear, nor did they curse their leader. Somehow it seemed as if they had expected all along that the Englishman would evade their vengeance yet again, that he would lure them out into the cold and into the darkness, and then that he would mock them, fool them, and finally disappear into the night.

It seemed futile to wait any longer. They were so sure that they had failed again.

“Who goes there?”

The sound of naked feet and of wooden sabots pattering on the distant footbridge had caused Roger to utter the quick challenge.

===Chinese===

就在这时，库尔贝瓦教堂的钟敲响了午夜十二点。

“太迟了。”人们低声说。

他们没有咒骂，也没有怨他们的头领。不知怎么，他们仿佛早就料到那英国人又会一次逃脱他们的报复，料到他会把他们诱到寒冷和黑暗之中，然后嘲笑他们、愚弄他们，最后消失在夜色里。

再等下去似乎也无济于事。他们笃定自己又失败了。

“什么人？”

远处小木桥上赤脚和木鞋啪嗒啪嗒的声响，让罗歇脱口喝问。

===Original===

“Holà! Holà! Are you there?” was the loud, breathless response.

The next moment the darkness became alive with men moving quickly forward, and raucous shouts of “Where are they?” “Have you got them?” “Don’t let them go!” filled the air.

“Got whom?” “Who are they?” “What is it?” were the wild counter-cries.

“The man! The girl! The children! Where are they?”

“What? Which? The Lebeau family? They are here with us.”

“Where?”

===Chinese===

“Holà（喂）！Holà（喂）！你们在那儿吗？”传来响亮而气喘吁吁的回答。

转眼间，黑暗中涌动起快步向前的人影，沙哑的喊声“他们在哪儿？”“你们抓到他们了吗？”“别放走他们！”响成一片。

“抓到谁？”“他们是谁？”“怎么回事？”对方也狂乱地喊了回来。

“那个男人！姑娘！孩子们！他们在哪儿？”

“什么？哪家的？勒博一家？他们跟我们一起在这儿呢。”

“在哪儿？”

===Original===

Where, indeed? To a call to them from Roger there came no answer, nor did a hasty search result in finding them﻿—the old man, the two boys, and the girl carrying the bundle of clothes had vanished into the night.

“In the name of —﻿—﻿—, what does this mean?” cried hoarse voices in the crowd.

The newcomers, breathless, terrified, shaking with superstitious fear, tried to explain.

“The Lebeau family﻿—the old man, the girl, the two boys﻿—we discovered after your departure, locked up in the cellar of the house﻿—prisoners.”

“But, then﻿—the others?” they gasped.

“The girl and the children whom you saw must have been some aristocrats in disguise. The old man who spoke to you was that cursed Englishman﻿—the Scarlet Pimpernel!”

===Chinese===

是啊，在哪儿呢？罗歇喊他们，无人应声，仓促搜索也不见人影——老人、两个男孩，还有抱着衣裳包袱的姑娘，都不见了，消失在了夜色里。

“看在————的份上，这到底是怎么回事？”人群中响起沙哑的喊声。

新来的这些人上气不接下气，吓得发抖，浑身战栗着，拼命想解释清楚。

“勒博一家——老人、姑娘、两个男孩——你们走后，我们发现他们被锁在房子的地窖里——成了囚徒。”

“可是，那——另外那些人呢？”他们喘着气问。

“你们看见的那个姑娘和孩子们，一定是乔装打扮的贵族。跟你们说话的老头，就是那个该死的英国人——红花侠！”

===Original===

And as if in mocking confirmation of these words there suddenly rang, echoing from afar, a long and merry laugh.

“The Scarlet Pimpernel!” cried Roger. “In rags and barefooted! At him, citizens; he cannot have got far!”

“Hush! Listen!” whispered one of the men, suddenly gripping him by the arm.

And from the distance﻿—though Heaven only knew from what direction﻿—came the sound of horses’ hoofs pawing the soft ground; the next moment they were heard galloping away at breakneck speed.

The men turned to run in every direction, blindly, aimlessly, in the dark, like bloodhounds that have lost the trail.

One man, as he ran, stumbled against a dark mass prone upon the ground. With a curse on his lips, he recovered his balance.

===Chinese===

仿佛为了嘲弄地印证这些话，远处突然传来一声悠长而欢快的笑声，回荡不绝。

“红花侠！”罗歇喊道，“穿着破衣裳，光着脚！追呀，公民们，他跑不远！”

“嘘！听！”一个男人突然抓住他的胳膊，低声说。

从远处——只有天知道是从哪个方向——传来马蹄刨动松软地面的声音；转眼间，蹄声疾驰而去，快得不要命。

众人转身朝四面八方跑去，在黑暗里盲目地、无头绪地乱闯，像一群失去了嗅迹的猎犬。

有个人跑着跑着，被地上趴着的一团黑影绊了一下。他嘴里骂着，稳住了身子。

===Original===

“Hold! What is this?” he cried.
